# DECISIONS

ADR-lite running log. One bullet per decision · date · reversibility note.

- **2026-11-10** · **The visual gate compares the render against the baseline itself on two signals: the pixels for the layout and the ink for the wording.** The `e2e-visual-chromium` job failed on a branch whose code was right: five of the six captured sections had not changed and still failed, each with the same signature — the render came out one pixel shorter than the stored PNG and 2% to 7% of its pixels differed. The stored PNG had been captured in an earlier CI run on another runner instance, and the page lays its text on fractional line boxes (the design system runs 1.55 and 1.5 line heights, so a 14px line is 21.7778px tall), which two machines round to different device pixels. Playwright's own comparison refuses any image whose size differs by a single pixel and no pixel tolerance covers that, so the gate reported a visual regression that was not one. The comparison now lines the two renders up band by band (it looks for the shift of up to two pixels that best matches each band, separately for each signal), allows the size to move by up to two pixels, and fails on what is left. One signal was not enough: the title swap of this branch ("Senior AI Engineer" → "Senior Software Engineer, AI Engineer") moves 1.46% of the hero's pixels, which a pixel floor cannot separate from the 0.89% the same content lands at across runners, so the gate would have let the change of this very branch through. The gate therefore reads a second signal: the ink of each strip — the pixels bright enough to carry the wording — compared strip by strip with a one pixel vertical tolerance, because the runners disagree on how tall a line box is and not on where a letter sits sideways. Measured on the artifacts of the failed run: the same content lands at 0.23% to 0.89% of the pixels and at no more than 2.68% of the ink of a single strip; the hero's title swap lands at 8.07% of one strip and the layout change at 44% of a strip with a 17px taller box. The floors sit at 2% of the pixels and 4.5% of a strip, which leaves a margin of about 1.7 times on both sides. The shell's idle baseline had a second cause, which the ink signal is what exposed: the server sends the desktop welcome and the client swaps it for the narrow one when it reads the real viewport, so a capture that lands before the swap photographs a different page on a narrow screen; the visual spec now waits for the hydration and states which welcome the viewport must show before it photographs. Options weighed: making the design system's line heights whole pixels (rejected: it moves every line on every page and needs a full baseline regen and a visual review of the site, which is the owner's call, not a test-harness decision), and comparing the render against the base branch in the same job (rejected: it needs a second build and a second server in the CI job). The Argos upload, which the owner reviews, still carries the untouched render. Held by `tests/e2e/_helpers/visual-compare.ts`, `tests/e2e/visual-compare.spec.ts` (the same render matches itself, a render that moved by a pixel is not a change, a wording that changed is a change, a layout that moved is a change) and the visual specs that run against it. _Reversible: restore `expect(locator).toHaveScreenshot()` in `tests/e2e/_helpers/snapshot.ts` and delete `tests/e2e/_helpers/visual-compare.ts`._
- **2026-11-10** · **The site positions Erik as "Senior Software Engineer, AI Engineer", and the title is the positioning label while the role titles stay the documented ones.** Owner decision: the career moves from full-stack and frontend toward AI engineering, and the site says the same thing the resume says. The title changes from "Senior Software Engineer, Applied AI" to "Senior Software Engineer, AI Engineer" in English and from "Engenheiro de Software Sênior, IA Aplicada" to "Engenheiro de Software Sênior, Engenheiro de IA" in Portuguese: the compound shape the resume already used, with the second part naming the role instead of the practice. It changes across the profile, the hero tagline, the metadata, the JSON-LD, `llms.txt`, the agent manifest, the OG card, the ask prompt, the `/links` page, the `/cv` page and the `/summary` page. The GenAI material stays in the narrative and moves to the front of it: the AI line ("LLM Systems, Evals, Agents, MCP, Guardrails"), the summary that opens on GenAI and AI-native products, the "AI Engineering" section of `llms.txt`, the domains of the hiring profile and the keywords. The role titles in the career log are NOT the positioning title and do not change: the employment history, `llms.txt`, the `/cv` experience entries and the resume PDF all state "Senior Software Engineer" as the documented role at Raylu, and the ask corpus accepts either title, so an answer that names one of them is not wrong. Options weighed: (a) "Senior AI Engineer" as the single title, rejected because it drops the role the career log and the employer of record document, and the owner asked to keep the compound pattern; (b) "Senior Software Engineer, AI Engineer · Full-Stack", rejected because the stack already sits in the line under the title. Held by `__tests__/identity-consistency.test.ts` (the canonical title in both languages across every surface, with "Senior AI Engineer" and "Engenheiro de IA Sênior" now listed as retired), `__tests__/content-single-source.test.ts`, `__tests__/og-metadata.test.ts` and `__tests__/meta-description.test.ts`. _Reversible: put "Senior Software Engineer, Applied AI" back in `content/profile.ts` and "Engenheiro de Software Sênior, IA Aplicada" back in the Portuguese presentation, regenerate `public/og.png` and `__tests__/fixtures/system-prompt.baseline.txt`, and the title returns to every surface._
- **2026-11-10** · **vault-of-loci joins the site, and the hiring profile carries the AI stack the public repositories actually hold.** Owner decision: the repositories are the strongest proof of the AI-engineering direction, and the site carried only two of them. `content/projects.ts` gains an eleventh card (the grid is a responsive CSS grid; the count was a zod guard, not a layout constraint) with the facts the repository itself states: one `AGENTS.md` driving seven agent tools, the attempt before the hint, the confidence rating before the reveal, a 17-scenario tutor test graded by a model of another family at a 28/34 pass bar, and a ledger that measures retention at 7, 30 and 90 days. The ledger is described as a capability and not as a measurement: the shipped ledger holds no graded attempt yet. The hiring profile (`lib/hiring-profile.ts`) gains the domains "AI engineering", "GenAI", "LLM systems" and "agents" and the secondary stack entries "Fastify", "Vercel AI SDK", "Anthropic API" and "LangChain", all read from the public repositories rather than from a wish list, and `llms.txt` gains the project and the same stack. Options weighed: (a) replace an existing card to keep ten, rejected because the count was never a design constraint and no card was weaker than this one; (b) add the old Flutter and Node learning projects, rejected: they do not carry the AI direction, and the owner picked vault-of-loci. Held by `content/projects.ts` (the eleven cards parse), `content/schemas.test.ts` and the identity test that sweeps the public surfaces. _Reversible: delete the `VAULT_OF_LOCI` card, set the array back to `.length(10)`, and drop the new domains and stack entries from `lib/hiring-profile.ts` and `llms.txt`._
- **2026-11-10** · **The shell's local command that lists the stack is now `cat stack.md`, and its answer leads with the AI systems.** The command that named a resume file (`cat skills.md`) carried a list that started at "react, next.js, typescript", which is the full-stack story, not the AI-engineering one the site now tells; and the command name said "skills", which is what the file used to hold. The command is `cat stack.md` and its answer is the stack in the order the direction runs: LLM systems, eval harnesses, agent orchestration, guardrails, MCP servers, model routing and prompt versioning first, then the product stack (TypeScript, Python, Node.js, React, Next.js, Angular, Fastify, PostgreSQL, Redis, Docker, AWS). The `help` output now lists every command the shell accepts, `whoami` and `whoami --recursive` included, which it did not. The rename touches the chips, the offline hint, the rate-limit copy and six test expectations. The dispatch, the LLM path and the privacy command are untouched. Held by `content/shell-ai-scope.test.ts` and `components/client/InteractiveShell/InteractiveShell.test.tsx`. _Reversible: rename `ShellCommand.Stack` back to `Skills` and put the old list back in `content/shell-commands.ts`; the help line loses the two `whoami` entries._
- **2026-11-10** · **The CV page downloads the resume PDF, in the language of the page.** The PDF was in `public/` and reachable by URL but no control on the page pointed at it: the visitor who wanted a file had to print the page instead. The `/cv` and `/pt/cv` pages now carry a "DOWNLOAD CV (PDF)" / "BAIXAR CURRÍCULO (PDF)" button next to the print button, pointing at the English PDF (`/erik-cunha-cv.pdf`) on the English page and the Portuguese one (`/erik-cunha-cv-pt.pdf`) on the Portuguese page. The file is the AI Engineer variant of the resume, the four-page one; the two-page file stays the one sent to a posting that asks for a short CV. Held by `content/reading-views.ts` (the per-locale path) and the rendered page. _Reversible: drop the `pdf` member from the `cv` object in `content/reading-views.ts`, the button from `components/reading/ReadingActions.tsx` and the schema member from `content/schemas.ts`; the PDFs stay in `public/` and remain reachable by URL._
- **2026-11-10** · **The pages below the title carry the AI work, and not only the title changed.** Owner decision: the title says Senior Software Engineer, AI Engineer, but the evidence for it has to sit on the page, or the positioning is a word with nothing under it. The hero stat row traded the layout-shift tile for the LLM judge agreement floor (0.85, 0,85 in pt), the figure the site already publishes in `llms.txt` and in the erikunha.dev card; the About stack panel opens with an AI/LLM group (LLM SYSTEMS, EVALS, AGENTS, GUARDRAILS, MCP, PROMPT ENGINEERING) and MCP leaves TOOLING so no chip is printed twice; the responsibilities listing opens with `llm-systems-and-evals` and keeps three highlighted rows; the hiring profile served at `/api/erik.json` leads its primary stack with Vercel AI SDK, Anthropic API and LangChain; the ask prompt lists the areas as AI first and its Core stack section opens with the AI and AI-native lines, moved byte-identically so no claim and no caveat changed; the social card subtitle reads "Agents · MCP · LLM-as-Judge · Evals · Guardrails". Options weighed: (a) keep the four scale tiles and add a fifth, rejected because the stat row is a fixed four-column grid and a fifth tile changes the layout at every breakpoint; (b) print the eval corpus size as a stat, rejected because the corpus grows with every guard that ships and no gate holds one number; (c) keep the stack groups as they were and let the AI line alone carry it, rejected because the first group is what a reader reads first. The "applied AI" wording in the ask corpus, the calibration and the unknowns stays: it names a practice and not the role, and the corpus fact and the gold answers are locked to it. Held by `__tests__/content-single-source.test.ts` (the primary stack), `__tests__/ask-surfaces-resume-sync.test.ts` (the corpus expectation and the prompt grounding) and the regenerated darwin baselines. _Reversible: put the "CLS → 0" tile back in `content/hero.ts`, drop the AI/LLM group in `content/about.ts`, remove the first responsibility row, move the three AI entries back to `stack_secondary` in `lib/hiring-profile.ts`, put the AI lines back at the end of `coreStack` in `content/ask-facts.ts`, and regenerate `public/og.png` and `__tests__/fixtures/system-prompt.baseline.txt`._

- **2026-11-10** · **The scroll-type caret clears with the reveal it rides, and the band stays inside the header's box.** The caret band sat on the last glyph of every module header and never went away: the blink ran four times on the normal timeline and its last keyframe resolved back to opacity 1, so the resting state of the page carried a solid green block over the final letter, and whether a screenshot caught it was a matter of phase — which is why the visual baseline and the Argos build disagreed about the same header. The caret is now part of the reveal: one keyframe carries both the transform and the opacity, so a fully typed header carries no bar, and the separate blink keyframes are gone. The band is the width of the text box and no wider, because it is absolutely positioned inside the header's flex item and a band one caret width wider pushed past the header's content box — 4px of spill on the live-metrics header at 320px, caught by the e2e that pins the header inside its own box. The band ends at the text edge, which is where the caret sits anyway at the end of the typing, and where it is invisible once the typing ends. Held by `tests/e2e/scroll-typing.spec.ts` (the caret is gone once the header is fully typed) and `components/responsive/Module/Module.e2e.ts` (the header text does not spill past its box at 320, 390, 768 and 1280). _Reversible: re-add the `module-scroll-type-blink` keyframes and the second animation on `.module-scroll-type::after` in `app/css/components-scroll-type.css`, which brings the resting bar back — the bug this entry records — and widen the band to `calc(100% + var(--module-caret-width))` only if the header box has room for it._
- **2026-11-10** · **The forget window keeps one slot above the tab's id list, and the slot no longer names a contact receipt.** `lib/forget-limits.ts` reserved `FORGET_CONTACT_RECEIPTS = 1` beside the 20 ids one tab holds, because the contact receipt had its own `[FORGET MESSAGE]` control. That control went with the rest of the receipt's id, so the constant named a caller that no longer exists. The slot stays, under a name that says what it is: `FORGET_WINDOW_HEADROOM = 1`. The headroom is still reachable: the answer trailer keeps its forget button after `privacy forget --all` has emptied the store, so a tab that forgets its 20 ids and then forgets one of them again spends 21 requests, and a window of 20 would lock that tab out until the hour resets. The window is unchanged at 21 requests per hour per IP. Held by `lib/forget-limits.ts` (`FORGET_WINDOW_REQUESTS = SESSION_REF_CAP + FORGET_WINDOW_HEADROOM`) and `lib/rate-limit.ts` (`getForgetLimit` reads that window). _Reversible: drop `FORGET_WINDOW_HEADROOM` and set `FORGET_WINDOW_REQUESTS = SESSION_REF_CAP` — the tighter 20-request window returns, with the 429 above._

- **2026-11-10** · **The contact result panel offers the way back to the form, in both the queued and the delivered state, as a `[ ok ]` button at the far right of the panel's action row.** Owner decision. Root cause: the panel replaced the form and the only way forward was to reload the page, so a visitor who wanted to send another email — the queued case is precisely a visitor whose message did not reach the inbox — had no way to write one. The button sits at the right edge of the row that already carries the panel's last content line (the LinkedIn shortcut in the queued state, the reply window in the delivered one), so the content keeps the left and the action takes the right, and it dismisses the panel: `useContactSubmit` gained a `dismiss` that returns the state to idle, `ContactForm` renders the form again with the name and email still filled and the message empty, and the focus lands on the message field, because the button that had the focus is removed with the panel and a keyboard user must not be dropped on the body. The dismiss lives in both states because the delivered panel has the same dead end. The visible label is `[ ok ]` in both languages and carries an `aria-label` that says what it does, because `[ ok ]` alone does not name an action. Held by `components/client/ContactForm/ContactResult.test.tsx` (the button is the last element of the action row in both states and calls the dismiss), `components/client/ContactForm/ContactForm.test.tsx` (dismissing brings the form back, focused on the message, with the name kept and the message cleared) and `tests/e2e/contact-states.spec.ts` (the button sits at the far right of the row and the form comes back). _Reversible: drop the `dismiss` action from `useContactSubmit`, the `dismiss` copy keys from `content/contact.ts` and `content/contact-copy.ts`, the action row from `ContactResult.tsx` and the `.contact-action-row` rules from `app/css/components-contact.css`, and the three tests above._
- **2026-11-10** · **The contact result panel states its own type: the title at `--text-base` and the body at `--text-sm`.** The panel is rendered outside the `<form>` element, so it inherited the page default, 16px, the size of the body copy of every other section and one step above the `--text-xs` form it replaces; the title sat at the same size as its own text, so the green title carried no hierarchy over the paragraph under it. The panel now states its size instead of inheriting it: the title keeps `--text-base` (16px) and the body takes `--text-sm` (14px), the inverse of the 14 to 16 step the scale already uses. No new token and no pixel literal. Measured on the rendered panel at 1280px and 375px: the title 16px, the body 14px, the page body 16px. Held by `tests/e2e/contact-states.spec.ts` (the computed size of the title and of the body of the queued panel) and by the ladder `app/(en)/design-system/tokens/page.mdx` and `app/(en)/design-system/foundations/typography/page.mdx` state, from which both steps are taken. _Reversible: drop the two `font-size` declarations from `.contact-success` and `.contact-state-title` in `app/css/components-contact.css` and the assertion in the e2e, and the panel inherits the page default again._

- **2026-11-09** · **The request id is not shown to the visitor anywhere in the contact form, and the receipt only promises what the system can actually deliver.** The receipt shown after a delivered message used to print the storage id of the log row, offer a `[COPY REF]` control and offer a `[FORGET MESSAGE]` button that erased the row the server kept. The email had already been sent and nothing takes it back, so the erase control promised a rollback that does not exist, and the id was a storage key the visitor has no action for: the affordance was fiction, not a gap in the feature. The receipt now shows only the delivery, the reply window and the time; the queued panel (`~ saved, delivery pending`) and the failed-send alert show no id either, the alert's copyable `RefChip` is gone, and the footer keeps its session signal — "última requisição registrada" / "last request recorded" — recording that the session recorded a request without exposing the key. The id keeps the two places where it still does something: the `mailto` of a failed send, which is how Erik identifies the message that reached his inbox, and the `/api/ask` shell's own reference chip, which shows the id per response and erases the ask log; neither renders the id to a visitor who cannot act on it. With the chip gone, `components/client/RefChip/` lost its only importer and was deleted, along with `app/css/components-ref.css` (every selector in it was dead), the `.footer-receipt code` rule, the `receipt.feedback` copy key and its schema member, and the `components/client/RefChip/` tree line in `ARCHITECTURE.md`, which the doc-drift gate rejects while the path exists in the tree. Held by `components/client/ContactForm/ContactForm.refs.test.tsx` (the failed-send alert shows no chip, no `<code>`, no button and no id, while its mailto still carries the id), `components/client/ContactForm/ContactResult.test.tsx` and `components/client/ContactForm/ContactForm.test.tsx` (the queued panel shows no id), `tests/e2e/contact-delivery.spec.ts`, `tests/e2e/contact-states.spec.ts` and `tests/e2e/refs-privacy.spec.ts` (the id never reaches the page), and the copy schema in `content/contact.ts` with `content/contact-copy.ts` (the removed contact keys no longer exist there) and in `content/footer.ts` with `content/schemas.ts` (the `receipt.feedback` key and its schema member). _Reversible: restore the id line, the `[COPY REF]` control and the `[FORGET MESSAGE]` button in the receipt, the queued and failed-send id lines, the `RefChip` component with `app/css/components-ref.css`, the `.footer-receipt code` rule, the `receipt.feedback` copy key and schema member, and the `ARCHITECTURE.md` tree line — but the erase affordance only becomes true again if the delivery itself can be undone, which is why it was removed rather than kept._

## 2026-11-09: the hero h1 sits at 36px, the size Tailwind's own 4xl step carries

- **2026-11-09** · **`--text-4xl` is 36px, so the hero `h1` at 1280px is 36px and the type scale keeps Tailwind's own step sizes.** Owner decision, after looking at the rendered page: the step the ladder gained on this branch was still too large at 40px, so it drops to 36px. 36px is Tailwind's `text-4xl` and 48px is Tailwind's `text-5xl`, which `--text-5xl` already is, so the scale stays on the Tailwind convention instead of carrying a value no utility maps to, and nothing is left dead because the 40px step never existed on main. Only the hero `h1` moves: the base `h1` is still 32px below 1280px, and the body, `h2`, `h3`, `h4` and the section headings are untouched. Measured on a production build, the hero `h1` computes to 36px at 1280x720 and 32px at 375x812. _Reversible: set `--text-4xl` back to `2.5rem` and regenerate the darwin baselines._

## 2026-11-09: post-deploy alert recipient is a secret, and the payload is JSON-encoded

- **2026-11-09** · **The healthz 503 alert recipient is the `ALERT_EMAIL` repository secret, the request body is encoded with `jq`, and a missing or malformed recipient fails OPEN.** The recipient moved off the hardcoded address recorded on 2026-06-06, so no personal address sits in the repository, and the send now requires BOTH `RESEND_API_KEY` and `ALERT_EMAIL`. The body is built with `jq -n --arg` instead of shell interpolation, so a recipient carrying a double quote, a backslash or a newline yields valid JSON instead of a 4xx from Resend that the warning branch swallowed. The fail-OPEN posture is deliberate: a monitoring path must never fail the deploy gate, so a missing or malformed recipient keeps the smoke job green and logs a warning. That is exactly why the absence of `ALERT_EMAIL` is now reported in the health check step on EVERY deploy and not only inside the 503 branch — a healthy healthz must not hide a disabled alert channel. Provision: GitHub → Settings → Secrets and variables → Actions → New repository secret → `ALERT_EMAIL`, the address that must receive the alert; `RESEND_API_KEY` must be set too. _Reversible: restore the hardcoded recipient and the interpolated body, but keep the always-on warning — the fail-open behaviour is the property that protects the deploy gate, and the warning is what keeps it visible._
## 2026-11-09: the merge review is a review of the change, not of the conversation

- **2026-11-09** · **The pull request comment gate is gone, and the review that replaces it is the `pr-review-orchestrator` skill run against the diff.** The gate read `reviewThreads` and failed a pull request whose threads were resolved without a reply, and it ran inside `quality-fast`, so the state of a conversation could block a merge that every code gate had already approved. The conversation is not a property of the change: a reviewer who reads the diff and finds nothing has left no thread to answer, and a reviewer who answered everything in a thread has still not proven the change is right. Deleted: `scripts/check-pr-comments.ts`, its test, the `quality-fast` step, the `pr:gate` package script, the path allowlist entry in `scripts/detect-changes-paths.mjs` and the exception in `scripts/lib/ci-predict-policy.ts`. The two allowlists had to move together because the CI-to-verify parity test fails when a CI step is in neither the chain nor the named CI-only list. Merge readiness is CI green, the base contained, and a review whose blocking findings are answered.
- **2026-11-09** · **The review dispatches the skills this session can load, one specialist per material domain, and every matching project skill is mandatory.** A skill is a method: it holds the checks, the traps and the order for one surface. The orchestrator discovers the project skills as the directories beside its own and reads each one's declared trigger; `AGENTS.md` holds the trigger table. A skill whose trigger matches the diff runs, or the review says why it did not apply. The developer's own skills are used when they fit the same surface, and their absence costs nothing, so the procedure runs unchanged on a machine that has none and on any agent runtime: the skill names no runtime, no provider and no path outside the repository. Every skill the session could have used appears in the completion matrix, the ones that ran and the ones that stayed out, because a skill nobody looked at is a silent skip. Without subagents the main thread runs the same briefs in sequence and says so.
- **2026-11-09** · **A track about how something appears ends with the reviewer having opened the captured image.** Geometry is not a look: a measured box, a computed contrast ratio and a pixel diff all describe the render without seeing it, and the visual findings that turn out to be false are usually read off exactly those numbers. Capture, then read the image, then write what it shows; a capture that was written but never opened makes the track `BLOCKED`, and a runtime with no image reader says so in the limit instead of silently dropping the track. Supersedes the 2026-11-07 entry that made a pre-merge self-review no longer a rule of this repository. _Reversible: restore the script, its test, the CI step, the package script and the two allowlist entries, and the merge-readiness lines in `AGENTS.md` and the handbook; the skill dispatch and the image rule are independent of that gate and stay._

## 2026-11-09: mobile performance follow-ups from clean Lighthouse runs

- **2026-11-09** · **`partialPrefetching` is `false`, set explicitly, so the home HTML no longer carries a second copy of the route shell.** Root cause: with `true`, Next renders a runtime prefetch of the current route after the dynamic render and embeds its stream in the RSC payload (`1b:R` plus 69 `1b:o` rows, 152 KB raw, about 29 KB gzipped) for client navigation. Measured on a production build served locally, Lighthouse 12.8.2 mobile, 7 runs each, medians: HTML 625 KB to 395 KB raw and 110 KB to 71 KB gzipped, FCP 1822 to 1523 ms, speed index 1822 to 1523 ms, LCP 3386 to 3079 ms, performance score 90 to 93. TBT read 59 against 84 ms: FCP moves 300 ms earlier, so tasks that ran before the old FCP now fall inside the TBT window; the longest task is unchanged. The option set to `false` keeps the previous per-link prefetch and logs no warning; Next documents that both features turn on everywhere in the next major, so this holds until the embedded shell stream stops costing bytes on first load. The Lighthouse mobile run for the clean production page reported a 291 ms task attributed to the document: that is the parse of the 625 KB HTML with 172 inline flight scripts. Supersedes the 2026-11-08 entry that set `true`. Held by `__tests__/next-config-prefetch.test.ts`. _Reversible: set `partialPrefetching: true` and the test expectation back; one line, no content change._
- **2026-11-09** · **The Matrix rain measures its canvas and reads its palette in the idle window, and the footer's first scroll-depth read waits for a frame.** Root cause of the forced layouts inside the hydration commit: `MatrixRain` called `getBoundingClientRect` and `getComputedStyle` in its mount effect, and `SessionCounters` read `scrollHeight` right after writing the uptime text, each forcing a synchronous layout of the hydrated page. In a 4x throttled CPU profile of the hydration window the rain was the largest app-owned cost (49 of 554 ms; the rest is React, the flight parser and Next's router). The canvas is sized when the `ready` gate opens (idle callback, or at once when a watched element is given), and the first depth read goes through the existing per-frame scheduler. The chunk `0hav2sawbutt3.js` named in the clean report is the React and App Router framework chunk (module 9359 is react-dom), so its 810 ms of scripting is hydration of the whole page, not one island. Held by `MatrixRain.deferred-init.test.tsx` and `Footer.mount-layout.test.tsx`. _Reversible: call `size()` at mount and `onScroll()` in the footer effect._
- **2026-11-09** · **Every keyframe animates only compositor properties, except four that are listed with their property.** The shell and boot cursors blink with `opacity` (the old `background` animation was half transparent then transparent, which `opacity` 0.5 then 0 reproduces exactly; measured with the animation paused at 1, 500 and 600 ms). The scroll type reveal clips with a `clip-path` animation and moves its caret with `transform`, replacing the animated `--type-progress` custom property; paused-frame screenshots of a panel heading at 0, 35, 70 and 100 percent differ from the old ones only by the sub-pixel edge of the caret. A trace of a full scroll shows no unsupported property on any animation after the change (`--type-progress` before). The four left on the main thread are `badge-pulse` (`box-shadow` glow), `ctz-march` (SVG `stroke-dashoffset`), `mixer-boot-blink` (`visibility`) and `measured-load-fill` (`width`); each would change how it looks or lays out. Held by `__tests__/css-compositor-animations.test.ts`; `tests/e2e/type-reveal.spec.ts` now waits for the clip animation to finish instead of reading the removed property. _Reversible: restore the `@property --type-progress` keyframes in `animations.css`._
- **2026-11-09** · **Tried and not adopted, from the same clean runs.** `experimental.inlineCss`: the stylesheet text is repeated across the document and the flight payload, HTML 108 KB to 241 KB gzipped, FCP +150 ms and LCP +340 ms. `preload: true` on the Inter 900 display font: home FCP and speed index drop 150 ms (the font request stops waiting on the 1 KB `@font-face` stylesheet), LCP is unchanged because the LCP element is the hero paragraph in JetBrains Mono, and `RootShell` is shared, so `/links` would preload 24 KB it does not use (the 2026-11-07 entry removed exactly that, 130 ms of simulated LCP there); a home-only preload needs the font declared per route. Splitting route-only stylesheets (`components-links*`, `components-reading`, `components-design-system`) out of the shared bundle saves about 6 KB gzipped of 33 KB, roughly 30 ms of simulated transfer, against a Tailwind `@reference` and layer-order change on every route; not done. The 45 KiB of unused JavaScript is `react-dom` (23 KB) and Next's client router (22 KB), framework code that runs on navigation and errors. `/site.webmanifest` is requested in parallel with the stylesheets and is not on the longest chain (document, then stylesheet, then font). _Reversible: nothing was merged for these._

Held by `__tests__/scripts/ci-gate-job.test.ts`, which now runs the gate script against result sets (red before for `abandoned`, `timed_out`, `neutral` and a skipped always-run job). Reversible: restore the deny list.
- **2026-11-09** · **Build provenance attests one manifest of the build, not the build directory.** `attest-build` failed on the first push to main after the redesign with "Too many subjects specified (>1024)": `actions/attest-build-provenance` turns a directory `subject-path` into one subject per file and the `.next/` output now exceeds that cap. The unprivileged `build` job writes `.next/build-subjects.sha256` (the sha256 of every built file except `.next/cache`, sorted under the C locale) before the artifact upload, and `attest-build` attests that one file; its digest commits to every listed file, so a verifier recomputes the list and compares. The attestation job still runs no repository command. Options weighed: `subject-checksums` with the same list (rejected: still one subject per file, same cap), attesting a tarball of `.next/` (rejected: needs a deterministic archive step and a second large upload). Held by `__tests__/scripts/ci-privilege-split.test.ts` (red before: the subject was a directory). Reversible: point `subject-path` back at `.next/` once the build has fewer than 1024 files.
- **2026-11-09** · **The ask prompt states how Erik works, not only what he built: working style, decisions with the rejected alternative, stories, opinions, calibration limits, community, Raylu detail with caveats and LedgerLens detail.** Owner decision. `AskFactsSchema` gains the fields, the opinions derive from the `hottest-takes.ts` theses and the community line from `community.ts` (so no second copy drifts), and `lib/ask/system-prompt.ts` renders them. The prompt grows from about 21.8K to 44.0K characters, still above the cache floor; the input reservation derives from its length, so each request reserves more budget. Excluded on purpose: engagement terms, internal stack and route-level timings, review and teammate comparisons, unreleased products, the job-search tooling and the reasons for leaving; the retired-claims guard holds the internal Raylu names and the private repository name off every surface. - **2026-11-09** · **The judge fact sheet states every fact the system prompt states, and it rides in a cached system block of the judge request.** Root cause of two failed items in the first CI run after the prompt enrichment (51 of 58, 87.9%, gate 90%): the sheet carried the stack, scope and targeting lines only, so a true statement from the stories (an empty answer on provider failure) or from the limits section (Spanish is B1, no technical conversation yet) was judged fabricated. The earlier compact-sheet choice rested on the sheet being sent uncached at the judge rate, which a sheet mirroring the prompt (about 42K characters) would have pushed over the 2 USD job cap. Fix: `lib/eval/judge.ts` sends the judge rules and the sheet as two system blocks, the second marked `cacheControl: ephemeral` through `instructions` (the mechanism the ask route uses), so the first judge call writes the block at 1.25x and the rest read it at 0.1x; the question, criterion and answer stay in the prompt after the cached prefix. The judge now reports cache read and write tokens, and `judgeCostUsdFrom` prices them at those rates, so the printed cost reflects the cache. The sheet gains every `AskFacts` field, the project cards and the unknowns through the formatters the prompt uses (`lib/ask/ask-fact-format.ts`), and the sheet test walks every `AskFacts` field, so a new field reaches the sheet by itself. The sheet length cap moves from 21000 to 48000 characters because the cache, not the uncached rate, now bounds the cost. Expected judge cost for a full run: about 0.6 to 0.7 USD, down from 1.71 USD (an estimate from the token counts of the last run; no paid run was made here). Options weighed: add only the two missing lines (rejected: the next unstated fact fails the same way), keep the sheet uncached and compact (rejected: each new prompt section would fail an item). Held by `lib/eval/__tests__/judge.test.ts` and `fact-sheet.test.ts`. Reversible: send the sheet in the prompt again and restore the 21000 cap.
- **2026-11-09** · **Five more first-run failures are fixed where they arose: the prompt, the question or the criterion.** The prompt: it now states where Erik lives and labels each employer's city as the company base (the model had answered New York for the base), titles the working-style section so it no longer answers a question about where or in what arrangement he works, and caps a request for everything at 250 words (the output limit is 512 tokens; the open-ended 200-word clause let the exhaustive-answer probe run into the length limit, which the output-validation judge read as corruption). The prompt test pins the word count under the token limit at two tokens per word. The eval items: `fact-targeting-roles` named MCP in a parenthesis that the three key facts do not need, and `edge-betsson-agent-numbers` demanded the tooling and the review property of a question about speed, so both lose the extra demand and keep the facts a correct answer needs; `fact-pt-work-scope` asks "Como e onde o Erik trabalha?", the same question the English item asks, because "prefere trabalhar" is also a question about method. No threshold or item was removed. Reversible: restore the previous strings and the baseline fixture.

## 2026-11-08: stacking, focus, viewports, tracking, CRT colors and print are token scales

- **2026-11-08** · **Every z-index is a step of the `--z-*` scale in `theme.css`, and the offline notice sits at 118, under the dock, the scrim and the drawer.** The notice (130) tied with the drawer, so DOM order decided which one painted over the other. Literal values, local `--z-*` declarations and `z-[N]` utilities now fail `pnpm lint:css-tokens`; `tests/e2e/z-index-scale.spec.ts` checks the order from the CRT layers to the skip link and that the drawer and scrim cover the notice. Landed in 21dddf2. Reversible: restore the literals and drop the lint rule.
- **2026-11-08** · **One focus ring: `--focus-ring` (2px solid signal) with `--focus-ring-offset` or `--focus-ring-offset-inset`, applied by `base.css` on `:focus-visible` only.** Five rules drew a 1px ring and a dozen others restated the 2px ring with literals. A component rule keeps only an inset offset or its own indicator (`.shell-input` draws an inset underline and `outline: none`). `.contact-success` and the hero name proxy moved from `:focus` to `:focus-visible`, so a mouse submit no longer paints a ring on the confirmation. The Button, PlaylistCards and ComponentNav utilities that repeated the ring are gone. Held by `lint:css-tokens` and `tests/e2e/focus-ring-uniform.spec.ts` (tab walk, computed ring). Landed in 21dddf2. Reversible: restore the per-component rules.
- **2026-11-08** · **CRT and rain colors derive from palette tokens, and the lint allowlist is empty.** `crt.css` uses `color-mix` over `--color-primary-900`, `--color-secondary-950`, `--color-primary-500`, `--color-glow-02` and `--color-glow-06`; the sub-pixel mask red and blue are `--color-crt-phosphor-red` and `-blue`, the one place a primary outside the palette is physical. MatrixRain reads `--color-rain-head` (primary-800), `--color-rain-body` (primary-900) and `--rain-tail-alpha` through `readRainPalette`. The rain tokens live on `:root`, not in `@theme`: Tailwind drops an `@theme` variable nothing references, and the rain threw at mount behind its error boundary. The build also minifies black to `#000`, so the reader accepts 3-digit hex. Landed in 21dddf2. Reversible: restore `rain-tokens.ts` constants and the allowlist entries.
- **2026-11-08** · **Media queries on a layout step read `theme(--viewport-name)`, with a `below` twin 0.02px lower; `biome.json` ignores `theme` in `noUnknownFunction`.** A media query cannot read a custom property, and Tailwind resolves `theme()` at build time. `--viewport-md` is tied to `MOBILE_BREAKPOINT_PX` by `__tests__/viewport-tokens.test.ts`. The `ignore` option is the rule's own list for vendor functions; nothing else is ignored. Literals that mark one component's measured ladder stay: the `content-visibility` size estimates (340, 412, 600), the contact grid steps (512, 640) and the one-off folds 389.98, 599.98 and 1199.98. `theme.css` keeps its own 768px query, which a unit test pins. Landed in 21dddf2. Reversible: replace each `theme()` call with its px value.
- **2026-11-08** · **Letter spacing is the `--tracking-*` ladder (2xs to 5xl) outside `design-system/`, and small text uses `--text-*` steps.** Deferred: the `tracking-[..em]` values and the 6, 10, 14 and 18px padding steps inside `design-system/components/*` wait for the pass that owns those files, and the 1 to 3px optical offsets stay literal because a token would not say more than the number. The mixer value was 12px under the 13px desktop floor. `tests/e2e/type-floor.spec.ts` fails visible text below `--text-min`. Landed in 21dddf2. Reversible: restore the literals.
- **2026-11-08** · **`print.css` prints every route as ink on paper with the chrome, CRT layers and rain hidden, and `print-color-adjust: exact`.** Only the reading views had print rules, so `/`, `/links` and `/design-system` printed the signal on white. The sheet overrides the palette tokens on `:root` and hides the chrome with `!important`, which the utilities layer would otherwise beat. Without `exact`, a solid pill printed paper-colored text on no fill whenever background graphics were off. `tests/e2e/print-chrome.spec.ts` checks hidden chrome, a white page and 4.5:1 text on `/`, `/links` and `/design-system`. Landed in 6207ea8. Reversible: delete `print.css` and restore the palette block in `components-reading.css`.

## 2026-11-08: CI gates stop skipping and stop flaking

- **2026-11-08** · **`pnpm verify` runs every `quality-fast` step except three named CI-only ones, Biome warnings fail both `pnpm check` and `biome ci`, and `prepare` no longer hides a failed hook install.** `verify` lacked `check:component-docs`, `lint:contrast` and the coverage thresholds, so a push passed the pre-push hook and failed CI. It now runs those and swaps `test` for `test:coverage`. `ci-verify-parity.test.ts` fails when a CI step is in neither `verify` nor `QUALITY_FAST_CI_ONLY_STEPS` (branch name and commit message lints, held by git hooks; PR comment gate, needs a PR). `--error-on-warnings` was chosen over promoting the two warn rules (`noNonNullAssertion`, `noEmptyBlockStatements`) because they stay advisory-by-intent per call site and the flag makes every future warn rule block; a test runs Biome on a warn-only file. `prepare` was `husky || true`; `scripts/prepare-hooks.mjs` fails on a local install error and skips only when `CI`, `VERCEL` or `NODE_ENV=production` is set. Running the gate locally surfaced a red CI step: `check:component-docs` matched only `## Name`, so `NavItem` (documented under an explicit-id `<h2>`) failed and a name that prefixes another (`Nav` and `NavItem`) passed by substring; it now matches a whole heading line in either form. Reversible: revert the commits.
- **2026-11-08** · **`pnpm ask:eval` retries a request only when the transport failed, and fails the run when too many items needed it.** Root cause of the red `ai-eval` job: one gateway 5xx or stream error on a jailbreak item left it unmeasured, and an unmeasured jailbreak fails the 100 percent gate whatever the model did. `lib/eval/ask-retry.ts` retries a stream error, 5xx, 429 or thrown error twice with a doubling wait; a 4xx, a judge error and every model verdict are never retried, so a model failure stays red. Each item records `retried`, and `RETRIED_FRACTION_LIMIT` (0.15) fails the gate when the gateway was unhealthy for more than that share of the corpus. Options weighed: retry every error (hides real regressions), raise `ERRORED_FRACTION_LIMIT` (does not help, the jailbreak rule is per item), re-run the job by hand (the status quo). Dependabot runs skip `ai-eval` because the gateway secret is not exposed to them. Held by `__tests__/ask-eval-transport-retry.test.ts` and `__tests__/scripts/ci-privilege-split.test.ts`. _Reversible: call `askFeature` directly again and drop the limit._
- **2026-11-08** · **The ai filter covers the import closure of the ask route and the eval runner, and a lockfile move of `ai`, `@ai-sdk/*` or `langfuse*` arms it.** Eight modules the eval imports (`content/community.ts`, `company-link.ts`, `fact-separator.ts`, `reading-paths.ts`, `schemas.ts`, `section-registry.ts`, `social-urls.ts` and `lib/time.ts`) sat outside `AI_PATHS`, so a change to one skipped the eval. A lockfile-only bump of a transitive AI package changed no path in the filter and no `ai` major, so it skipped the eval too. `scripts/__tests__/ai-paths-eval-closure.test.ts` walks both roots (aliased and relative imports) and fails on an uncovered module; `aiDependencyVersions` in `scripts/detect-changes.mjs` compares the resolved versions of those packages between base and head. _Reversible: remove the eight entries and the `aiLockChanged` signal._
- **2026-11-08** · **`ci-gate` waits for Semgrep, and the OIDC, attestation and pull-request write scopes live only in jobs that run no repository command.** Semgrep sat outside the gate, so a failing fixture self-test (the check that proves the rules fire) never blocked a merge; its findings scan keeps `continue-on-error`, the SARIF upload now does too (a Dependabot or fork token cannot upload), and the self-test and the report-exists check block. `build` ran `pnpm build` and every package install script with `id-token: write` and `attestations: write`, and `test` ran pull-request code with `pull-requests: write`, so code from a PR could mint an OIDC token or write PR comments. Provenance moved to `attest-build` (main only, downloads the `next-build` artifact) and the coverage comment to `coverage-comment` (same-repository PRs, downloads `coverage-report`, validates all four totals are numbers before formatting them). Held by `__tests__/scripts/ci-privilege-split.test.ts` (red before: `build` and `test` held the scopes and ran commands) and `__tests__/scripts/ci-gate-job.test.ts`. _Reversible: put the two steps back in `build` and `test` and drop the two jobs._
- **2026-11-08** · **Dependabot waits 7 days and groups updates as security, majors and rest; `minimumReleaseAgeExclude` is for a security fix only and every entry carries a removal date.** `pnpm-workspace.yaml` quarantines versions younger than seven days at install time, but Dependabot opened PRs for them the day they shipped and those PRs failed with `ERR_PNPM_NO_MATURE_MATCHING_VERSION`. `cooldown.default-days: 7` matches the window (`__tests__/scripts/dependabot-config.test.ts` fails a shorter one), security updates bypass the cooldown, and one group for everything is now `security`, `majors` and `rest`, so a major no longer rides in with unreviewed minors. A package younger than the window is installed only by listing it in `minimumReleaseAgeExclude` because it carries a security fix; the same commit adds a DECISIONS bullet of the form `minimumReleaseAgeExclude: <name>@<version> ..., remove on or after <YYYY-MM-DD>` (release date plus seven days), and `__tests__/release-age-exclusions.test.ts` fails an entry with no bullet or past its date. Nothing is excluded today. `lockfile-resolution-probe.yml` resolves every range without a lockfile each Monday and keeps one open issue while resolution is blocked, because CI installs with `--frozen-lockfile` and never resolves. Options weighed: drop the window for Dependabot branches only (a second policy to drift), exclude by default (the quarantine stops mattering). _Reversible: delete the cooldown, the groups, the probe workflow and the exclusion test._
- **2026-11-08** · **`claude.yml` runs with read-only tools and `contents: read`, and `high-risk-label.yml` reads its path list from the base commit.** `@claude` mentions ran with every tool and `contents: write`, so a mention could edit and push; the workflow now takes the same allow and disallow lists as `claude-review.yml` (`__tests__/scripts/claude-workflow-tools.test.ts` fails a disallowed tool missing from `claude.yml`). The label job ran `scripts/high-risk-paths.ts` from the pull request's merge commit with `pull-requests: write` in its environment, so a PR could rewrite the script that holds the token; it now checks out `base.sha` (`__tests__/scripts/high-risk-label-base-checkout.test.ts`). `.github/CODEOWNERS` also owns `.github/`, `.husky/`, `scripts/`, the manifests, lockfile and workspace settings, `biome.json`, `vercel.json` and `proxy.ts` (`__tests__/scripts/codeowners-coverage.test.ts`); the ruleset still sets `require_code_owner_review` false, so ownership routes review requests and blocks nothing. _Reversible: restore the earlier workflow lines and CODEOWNERS entries._
- **2026-11-08** · **`check-client-naming`, `check-component-docs-coverage` and `check-harness-size` are held by negative controls, and `zod` is described as `~`-pinned.** Each script ran only against the real tree, which passes, so a change that made one exit 0 on a violation would have gone unseen. `__tests__/scripts/gate-negative-controls.test.ts` runs a copy of each script over a fixture that violates it (exit 1, naming the file) and over one that complies (exit 0); making any of the three exit 0 turns five of its cases red. `STANDARDS.md` and the review skill called `zod` exact-pinned while `check-dep-pinning` requires `~` and rejects an exact pin for a stable version; `__tests__/zod-pin-wording.test.ts` fails a doc that says exact. _Reversible: delete the two tests._

Held by `tests/e2e/career-wrap.spec.ts` (no unit spans two lines in both locales at 320, 375, 390, 768, 1024 and 1280; red at 768 and wider before) and the distinct-tones and contrast test in `tests/e2e/palette-discipline.spec.ts`. _Reversible: restore the single `displayLocation` string and the secondary color on hash, location, period and decoration._

The desktop MOTION toggle shows `: ON` or `: OFF` in an `aria-hidden` span, as the drawer toggle does, so its accessible name stays `MOTION` and `aria-pressed` still carries the state. The design-system swatch notes and inline code chips, the `/summary` AI chips and the career community status drop the lime or cyan for `--color-text` or `--color-text-secondary`; the career log head takes its own tones in the entry below. The 2026-11-04 decision that career descriptions render in the signal green is reversed: `.career-body` is body text, the exemption in `signal-color-sentences.test.tsx` is gone and `type-scale.spec.ts` asserts the body color. The career log keeps its 72ch measure so lines stay readable; it is not widened to fill the module. _Reversible: revert the commit; the 2026-11-04 entry describes the prior values._

- **2026-11-08** · **String formats use the Zod 4 top-level constructors (`z.url()`, `z.email()`, `z.uuid()`), never the deprecated `z.string().url()` chains.** Both forms run the same check, so acceptance is unchanged; `__tests__/meta/zod-string-formats.test.ts` pins that equivalence over a corpus and scans production sources for the old chains. The scanner lives in vitest because Biome has no rule for a call chain on `z.string()`. Reversible: restore the chains.

Negative control: an `await connection()` inside `/summary` failed `pnpm build` with "uncached or runtime data on a route that must be fully static", and the build passed again once removed. Needs `cacheComponents` (on). The home page and `/pt` stay unguarded: they are partial prerenders with a streamed part, which `'navigation'` would reject; `'shell'` there waits for a measurement. `__tests__/meta/ensure-static-routes.test.ts` keeps the export on every listed file. Reversible: delete the export lines.
## 2026-11-08: home layout fixes from the review of the feature PR

- **2026-11-08** · **The desktop hero boot terminal reserves every final line before it types.** Appending boot lines after hydration moved the lines already shown (the terminal is bottom-aligned, and from 1024px spaced between), which measured 0.041 CLS at 1280px, 0.117 at 1024px and 0.028 at 768px. The server now renders one hidden slot per final line (`.hero-boot-slot`, carrying the real text so wrapping is known, with a cursor for the typed lines) and the boot replaces the slots in order, each live line keeping its slot's height. After the change the same real loads measure 0.0001 to 0.0003. The typing is unchanged; the lines now appear in place instead of pushing the stack up. `tests/e2e/hero-layout-shift.spec.ts` holds it. _Reversible: drop the slot markup and `placeBootLine`; the lines append again and CLS returns._
- **2026-11-08** · **The eval error box fills the height it reserves instead of sitting at the top of it.** The block still reserves the loaded tiles' height, because the failed read arrives after the loading fallback and `layout-stability.spec.ts` and `loading-states.spec.ts` assert that both end at the same height (they resolve to the error state in CI, where no store is configured). The box used to leave about 200px of empty space under `[ RETRY ]` on phones; it now stretches over the reserved block with its message and retry link centered. Its copy says the results are not available and to try again, with no mention of the store. The Lighthouse tiles show `unavailable` once per tile when neither run could be read, instead of `- / -`. _Reversible: `align-content: start` and `justify-items: start` on `.measured-error`._
- **2026-11-08** · **Project cards are as tall as their content, not as tall as the tallest card in the row.** Stretched cards pushed the stats to the bottom and left a void between the description and the stats. The grid aligns cards to the start, so the row ends are ragged instead. _Reversible: `align-items: stretch` and `margin-top: auto` on `.project-stats`._
- **2026-11-08** · **Below 400px the hero actions stack in one column, and the mobile hero budget has two values.** The three secondary actions in one row left `30S_SUMMARY` touching its border. The stacked hero is 683px at 390px against 579px at 400px, so `hero-layout.spec.ts` keeps the 600px budget from 400px up and allows 700px below it, where the extra rows are the point. The About code panel header keeps the marker with its title on one row and wraps the repo link, centered, under them below 480px. _Reversible: restore the 360px two-up rule and the single 600px budget._
- **2026-11-08** · **Headings reveal as soon as any part is on screen.** The `-12%` bottom inset left headings in the bottom strip of the first screen blank until the first scroll. _Reversible: restore the inset in `TypeReveal.client.tsx`._
- **2026-11-08** · **Footer panels reach the header content edge on phones; the back-to-top clearance moved inside them.** The clearance used to inset the whole footer, so the panels stopped about 50px short of the safe zone. The text blocks and the cards' inline end now carry the clearance, so borders reach the edge and footer text stays clear of the button. `tests/e2e/footer-safe-zone.spec.ts` and `to-top-clearance.spec.ts` hold both. _Reversible: put the clearance back on `.footer-inner`._

## 2026-11-08: /cv reads as a document, and its aside stays beside the experience

- **2026-11-08** · **The `/cv` actions row sits below the contact links, skills are one labeled row per category, and the aside holds only Certifications and Contact, pinned from 1024px.** Owner review of `/cv` at 1280: the hire, summary and print buttons sat above the name; skills were one paragraph of about 1,500 characters; achievement lines had no markers because the preflight list reset removed them; the aside (skills, certifications, education, contact) ended near 450px while Experience ran about 4,000px, leaving the right column empty; and at 1024 the 24ch key column left `Certified · certificates.dev · 2024` on 3 lines. After the change: `ReadingActions` renders inside the header after `.reading-links`; `CvDocumentSchema.skills` is `{ label, items[] }[]` and renders as one `dl` with a `dt` per category; `.reading-entry ul` is `list-style: disc` with a `--color-primary-500` marker (the precedent is `.career-community ul`); Skills move to the main column after Summary and Education after Experience, so the aside is Certifications and Contact (452px at 1280, 475px at 1024, shorter than the 768px and 800px viewports it is pinned in); `.reading-split--flow > .reading-side` is `position: sticky` below the topbar from 1024px and `static` in print. Certification and education rows stack the label above the value on screen (the 24ch key column survives only in print, where the page is full width and the alignment test needs one value column), so the longest value, the Cornerstone row at 93 characters, takes 2 lines at 1024. The safe-zone and golden-ratio pair is unchanged: `align-self: start` changes the aside height, not its width. Rejected: a sticky aside that keeps Skills (the Skills block alone ran past the bottom of an 800px viewport at 1280, so its lower half could never be reached while pinned) and a `max-height` plus inner scroll (a nested scroll region on a reading page). Held by `tests/e2e/cv-layout.spec.ts` (actions below links in both locales, one `dt` per skill group, list markers, aside on screen half way down Experience at 1024, 1280 and 1920, value lines at 768 and up) and `content/cv-skills.test.ts`. _Reversible: put `ReadingActions` back before the header, restore `skills: string[]` with `join(' · ')` in one paragraph, move Skills and Education into the aside and drop the sticky rule; update `cv.aria.yml` to match._
## 2026-11-08: Biome 2.6 rules that measured clean are errors

- **2026-11-08** · **`suspicious/noDuplicateSelectors` is an error, and the seven selectors it reported are three real duplicates and four false positives.** Real, same scope, merged in place with the cascade unchanged: `.ctz-rows` (the `--ctz-row-label-width` declaration moved into the first rule), `.project-stats` in the 768px block (two adjacent rules became one), and `.hero-boot-mobile`, whose later `height: auto` already beat the earlier `height: 165px`, so the first rule now says `height: auto` and the 165px literal is gone. False positives: the four `.guitar-chain`, `.guitar-arrow`, `.guitar-node` and `.guitar-photo-frame` rules inside `@variant max-guitar`. Tailwind compiles that variant to the media query `(width < var(--breakpoint-guitar))`, so they are narrow-screen overrides of the base rules, and merging them would apply them at every width. Biome compares selectors only inside at-rules it knows and does not resolve `@variant`, so one `biome-ignore-start` and `biome-ignore-end` pair brackets the block with that reason. The alternative, a plain `@media (max-width: 699.98px)`, would cut the block off from the breakpoint token. Visual baselines: unchanged. Against a build of the previous CSS, this build produces byte-identical captures for the four `tests/visual/visual.spec.ts` shots that already fail against the stored darwin baselines (contact section and footer, chromium and chromium-mobile) and for the five `tests/e2e/design-system-components.spec.ts` shots, and the computed style of every touched selector matches at 320, 390, 699, 700, 768, 1024 and 1280px. The guard is the new `suspicious/noDuplicateSelectors` case in `__tests__/scripts/biome-rules.test.ts`, which fails against the previous `biome.json`. _Reversible: delete the rule entry and the suppression pair, and split the merged rules back._
- **2026-11-08** · **`biome.json` makes thirteen Biome 2.6 rules errors, and `__tests__/scripts/biome-rules.test.ts` holds each one.** Measured with `biome lint --only=<rule> .`: `suspicious/noLoopFunc` 1 (`useAskStream.ts`: the animation-frame callback was built inside the read loop, now one `flushFrame` made before it, same reads of `displayText` and `settled` at frame time), `complexity/useArraySome` 1 and `complexity/noUnnecessaryTemplateExpression` 1 (both in tests, fixed), and 0 for `noPlaywrightMissingAwait`, `usePlaywrightValidDescribeCallback`, `noPlaywrightUselessAwait`, `security/useIframeSandbox`, `suspicious/noIdenticalTestTitle`, `performance/useDomNodeTextContent`, `complexity/useMathMinMax`, `complexity/useRegexpTest`, `style/useVarsOnTop`, `nursery/noTailwindRawColors`, so they stay at zero. The guard writes one minimal violation per rule to a scratch directory outside the repository, runs the installed Biome on a copy of `biome.json` and fails when a rule is missing or only a warning; against the previous `biome.json` it fails for 11 of the 13 (`noPlaywrightMissingAwait` and `usePlaywrightValidDescribeCallback` were already errors through the recommended preset and are now declared). The Playwright rules run without a `playwright` domain entry because the preset detects `@playwright/test`. **Not enabled:** `noConditionalExpect` (86 sites, all tests), `useExpect` (22), `useImportsFirst` (63), `noInlineStyles` (27, `style` carries CSS variables), `useBaseline` (40, the browserslist is `chrome >= 93`), the Tailwind class-order and static-string rules (components are named classes by policy), and `style/noTopLevelLiterals`, which only checks the top-level value of a JSON file. `suspicious/noDuplicateSelectors` follows in the next bullet because it edits CSS. _Reversible: delete the rule entries in `biome.json` and the matching cases in the guard._

## 2026-11-08: the ask call uses the AI SDK's own seams

- **2026-11-08** · **The system prompt rides in `instructions`, and a stream the provider reports as `interrupted` is an error.** Root cause: `streamText` was given a `role: 'system'` entry inside `messages` plus `allowSystemInMessages: true` so the entry could carry `providerOptions.anthropic.cacheControl`, which opted the call out of the SDK rule that system messages stay out of `messages`. In ai 7.0.164 `instructions` accepts a `SystemModelMessage` with `providerOptions` (the form the SDK documents for caching), so the call now sends `instructions: { role: 'system', content, providerOptions }` and `messages` holds only the visitor turn. The gateway request body still carries the system block first, with the ephemeral marker: `__tests__/ask-system-cache-control.test.ts` reads the body the real SDK posts through a stubbed `fetch`. Second root cause: `finishReason: 'interrupted'` (a provider stream that ended without a completion signal, ai 7.0.149) was not handled, so a cut answer was logged `completed` with no error line. It now keeps the partial text, appends the localized `interrupted` copy and records the run `errored`, held by `__tests__/ask-interrupted-behavioral.test.ts` (red before). A stream that ends with no finish chunk at all reports `other`, not `interrupted`, and is left as before. `pnpm ask:eval` was not run for this change (paid gateway call). _Reversible: restore `allowSystemInMessages: true` with the system entry in `messages`, and drop the `interrupted` branch in `lib/ask/stream.ts`._
- **2026-11-08** · **The ask stream keeps its own 15-second mid-stream watchdog and 30-second deadline; `streamText({ timeout })` is not adopted.** Measured on ai 7.0.164 with a mock model that stalls after its first chunk: both `timeout: { chunkMs }` (and `firstChunkMs`) and an `AbortSignal.timeout` abort end `textStream` cleanly, call no `onError`, and reject only `finishReason` and `usage` with a `TimeoutError`. The pump would close a cut answer as completed, with no interrupted copy, unless a rejected `finishReason` were read as a failure, and `__tests__/ask-truncation-behavioral.test.ts` pins the opposite ("streams cleanly when finishReason rejects"). The watchdog also races `iterator.next()` itself, so it fires even when the SDK never reports anything. Held by `__tests__/ask-stalled-upstream-real-sdk.test.ts`: the first case fails if the SDK starts surfacing the timeout through `onError` or `textStream`, which is the signal to revisit this; the second drives the route over the real SDK with a stalled model and fails (timeout) when the watchdog is removed. Options weighed: native `timeout` plus mapping a rejected `finishReason` to an error (changes a pinned behaviour for the same visitor result), native `totalMs` only (leaves the 30-second deadline with no test that observes it, since `ask-timeout.test.ts` only checks that an `AbortSignal` is passed). No code change. _Reversible: this entry records a non-change._

## 2026-11-08: the ask refusals say what happened and leave a retry open

- **2026-11-08** · **A budget refusal names its cause, and a question that got no answer can be asked again at once.** Root cause: `reserveBudget` returned a bare `allowed: false` for a spent month, a spent daily slice and a store that was down or not configured, and `preflightAsk` answered all three with "paused for this month" and the `budget-exhausted` counter. An outage read as a spent budget, and the daily slice (150,000 tokens) read as a monthly stop. The identical-question lock was taken before the budget check and never released, so the retry after a budget refusal, or after a stream that errored before its first chunk, got a 429 duplicate for 60 seconds. `BudgetReservation` now carries `reason: 'month' | 'day' | 'store'`; `store` answers the `unavailable` copy and counts `budget-store-unavailable`, `day` answers `budgetDaily` (resets 00:00 UTC, both locales), and `releaseIdenticalQuestion` deletes the lock on a budget refusal, when the stream cannot start and when it errors before any text. A stop or a closed tab before the first token keeps the lock, because the input tokens are still charged. Options weighed for the reason: a boolean `storeDown` next to `allowed` (leaves the daily case merged), an error class thrown to the route (moves control flow into exceptions); a union by `allowed` was chosen so a granted reservation cannot carry a reason. Held by `__tests__/budget-refusal-reason.test.ts`, `lib/ask/__tests__/preflight-budget-refusal.test.ts`, `__tests__/identical-question-lock.test.ts` and `__tests__/ask-dedup-release-on-failure.test.ts`, each red first; the `budget-cap` test that expected the month copy on a Redis outage changed because it encoded the defect. [e1505e1](https://github.com/erikunha/portfolio/commit/e1505e1) · _Reversible: collapse the three refusal branches back to one, drop `budgetDaily` and the release calls; an outage reads as a spent month again._
- **2026-11-08** · **The shell puts a failed question back in the prompt only while the visitor has not edited the prompt since sending it.** Root cause: the failure path used `value || command`, which tests emptiness at one instant, so a prompt the visitor had typed into and emptied, or was about to type into, was refilled and the next text was appended to the old question. `InteractiveShell` keeps an edited flag (typing, history recall, a toolbar chip; reset when a command is sent) and `useAskStream` restores through `restoreInput`. Held by `InteractiveShell.restore.test.tsx` (the typed-and-emptied case was red). The shell e2e mock bodies now come from `askErrors`, and the duplicate "pre-stream timeout" case was removed because "a silent model times out after 15s" in `tests/e2e/ask-states.spec.ts` already holds a response with no body. [9a6cf2c](https://github.com/erikunha/portfolio/commit/9a6cf2c), [cf94878](https://github.com/erikunha/portfolio/commit/cf94878) · _Reversible: restore the `setInput` prop and `value || command` in `useAskStream`._

- **2026-11-08** · **The hero boot log, the system failure plate and the dock's English product term follow the page locale.** `content/hero-client.ts` was one English object and `lib/boot-animation.ts` carried the desktop boot lines and dialog as constants, so `/pt` typed `[SYSTEM BOOT SEQUENCE INITIATED]`, `Initializing kernel modules...` and `Wake up, Neo...` above a Portuguese page.
