## 2026-11-08: installed dependency features replace hand-written equivalents

- **2026-11-08** · **Resend sends are cancelled by `AbortSignal.timeout`, not raced against a timer.** `resend` 6.37 accepts `signal` in the request options and rethrows the abort reason, so the contact send (10s) and the PSI alert (5s) now abort the request itself instead of leaving the loser running after a `Promise.race` timeout. The contact `idempotencyKey` stays, which keeps the retry cron safe if an aborted request had reached Resend. An abort lands in the existing catch and counts as unavailable. _Reversible: restore the race in `lib/contact-delivery.ts` and `app/api/psi-refresh/route.ts`._
- **2026-11-08** · **The MCP tools declare `annotations`, and `get_profile` declares `outputSchema` and returns `structuredContent`.** `registerTool` in `@modelcontextprotocol/server` 2.4 accepts both, and neither tool set them. `get_profile` is `readOnlyHint` and `idempotentHint` with `openWorldHint: false`, so an agent can call it without confirmation; `ask_erik` is the opposite on all three (it writes an ask-log row and calls a model). The text block stays for clients that ignore structured output. _Reversible: drop the three fields in `lib/agent/mcp-tools.ts`._

- **2026-11-08** · **Both Lighthouse configs assert on the insight ids, not on the legacy ids LHCI 0.16 maps.** The explicit `*-insight: off` entries made LHCI drop the legacy `uses-text-compression`, `uses-responsive-images`, `font-display` and `render-blocking-resources` assertions, so those four gates were not running. `document-latency-insight` (covers compression, response time and redirects, replacing `server-response-time` and `uses-text-compression`), `image-delivery-insight`, `font-display-insight` and `cache-insight` use `minScore: 1`; `render-blocking-insight` keeps `maxLength: 3`; `dom-size-insight` keeps `maxNumericValue: 1500` (desktop warn). Measured on a production build, 24 URLs x 2 runs per profile: every `minScore` insight scores 1 on all runs, render-blocking reports 2 items, and the warnings equal the run on the old ids (desktop: 3 `dom-size-insight`, 24 `unused-javascript`; mobile: none). Category scores are all 1 except mobile performance, 0.94 to 1 against 0.93 to 1 before. `__tests__/lighthouse-budgets.test.ts` fails if a replaced id returns. _Reversible: restore the legacy ids; LHCI maps them until it removes the table._
## 2026-11-08: server paths stop doing work nobody asked for

- **2026-11-08** · **No API route handler runs during `next build`.** `GET /api/lighthouse` called `getClientIp(req)` inside a `try`, and `request.headers` throws the prerender interrupt at build time; the catch logged it as a rate-limit outage and went on to call PageSpeed Insights and Redis, so every build logged `lighthouse rate-limit unavailable` and `PSI fetch failed` and spent a PSI request. A catch around a request API now calls `unstable_rethrow(err)` first (`app/api/lighthouse/route.ts`, `lib/server/rate-limit-guard.ts`, which the `GET` of `/api/mcp` goes through). Handlers that are `POST`-only (`defineHandler`) are never prerendered and stay as they were; `/api/healthz` already opts out with `connection()`; the cron routes read `authorization` outside any `try`. Held by `__tests__/api-prerender-interrupt.test.ts`. _Reversible: drop the `unstable_rethrow` lines._
- **2026-11-08** · **`getScores` spends one Redis command per strategy on a hit, writes after the response and remembers a failure for 60 seconds.** The last-good copy was read on every view next to the cache key (4 GETs per `/` view for two strategies); it is now read on a miss that then fails, or hedged after 250 ms when the cache read is slow, so the 3 s render bound is unchanged. The cache and last-good writes go through `afterResponse` instead of a floating promise. A PSI failure is kept in memory per instance for `NEGATIVE_CACHE_MS`, so an outage costs one fetch a minute instead of one 8 s fetch per view; `refreshScores` clears it. `'use cache'` was rejected: `cacheLife` needs a mock in every test that touches `getScores`, a cached fallback sticks for the whole window, and `after()` cannot run inside a cached function. Held by `__tests__/lighthouse-scores-redis-commands.test.ts`. _Reversible: restore the parallel last-good read and the floating writes in `lib/lighthouse-scores.ts`._
- **2026-11-08** · **`last_updated` in `/api/erik.json` and the MCP profile is the build date, baked by `next.config.ts` (`env.BUILD_DATE`).** `new Date()` at module load stamped the date a server instance cold-started, so the field changed without a deploy. `lib/hiring-profile.ts` parses `process.env.BUILD_DATE` as an ISO date and throws when it is missing, so a build without it fails instead of publishing a wrong date; `vitest.config.ts` sets it for unit tests. _Reversible: restore the module-load date._
- **2026-11-08** · **The error bridge sends at most 200 reports per page session and backs off after a refused or failed one.** A page that throws in a loop posted without limit, and the server only drops records after the request. After a non-2xx answer or a network failure, reports are dropped for 5 s, doubling to 5 min, or the `Retry-After` value when longer; a 2xx clears it. `reportClientError` (error boundaries) goes through the same gate (`postLog`). Held by `__tests__/error-bridge-session-cap.test.ts` and `__tests__/error-bridge-backoff.test.ts`. _Reversible: call `fetch` directly from the two senders._
## 2026-11-08: the eval harnesses stop passing over what they did not measure

- **2026-11-08** · **Both eval runs strip Redis credentials for the whole run and restore them only around the aggregate publish; the ask gates fail on unmeasured items; calibration gates the true-negative rate apart from agreement; the judge reads the answer fenced; `agent-eval` splits its gate, samples at a temperature and caps cost on measured tokens.** Root causes: `EVAL_PUBLISH=1` made `isolateFromRedis` a no-op, so the scheduled run wrote `ask:log`, `ask:guard` and refusal counters to production; an errored jailbreak was dropped from the 100% rate and up to a quarter of the corpus could be missing; 0.85 agreement on 24 cases allowed three wrong passes, so a lenient judge cleared calibration; the answer reached the judge unfenced and the verdict regex was greedy; `agent-eval` printed `GATE PASSED` whatever the cases did, ran at temperature 0 (every run identical, pass@k equal to pass^k), compared arms without a standard error and capped cost from constants. Options weighed: a route flag or fake Redis client (rejected: touches product code), retrying errored jailbreak items inside the run (rejected: hides an outage), a single weighted calibration score (rejected: hides the failing side), escaping the answer (rejected: changes what the judge reads). The six new calibration gold cases carry `pendingRecalibration` because no judge has run against them; the first scheduled run confirms or corrects their labels. Reversible: restore the previous gate constants and the `EVAL_PUBLISH` early return.
## 2026-11-08: every rate limiter is keyed by the salted IP hash

- **2026-11-08** · **The unit setup turns React act warnings into failures, and `gates:runtime` picks its own server port.** `IS_REACT_ACT_ENVIRONMENT` was never set, so React printed no act warnings and 135 state updates outside `act` ran unseen in the ContactForm, ContactTimezone, InteractiveShell and DawMixer tests. `tests/mocks/rtl-setup.ts` now sets the flag, captures any `not wrapped in act` console error and throws after the test that caused it, naming the component; raw `dispatchEvent`, `focus()` and message-store resets in those tests go through `act` (the `fire` helper in `__tests__/helpers/render.ts`). `scripts/gates-runtime.ts` hardcoded port 3000, so a second dev server on the machine made the gates test the wrong app; `resolveServerPort` (`scripts/lib/free-port.ts`) takes `PORT` from the environment, falls back to a free port when it is taken, and passes the result to `pnpm start`, LHCI and Playwright (`E2E_BASE_URL`). The e2e absence checks in loading-states, shell-hydration and shell-early-input wait on a stability window (`expectStableFor`) or a frame count instead of a fixed sleep, and the contact error axe scan asserts the three invalid fields and the alert before scanning. Held by the setup itself (86 tests red when the flag was first turned on) and `scripts/__tests__/free-port.test.ts`. _Reversible: delete the setup guard and the flag, and restore the constant port._
- **2026-11-08** · **`tests/visual/visual.spec.ts` runs on `chromium` and `chromium-mobile` only; the webkit projects no longer match it and their twelve darwin baselines are deleted.** `CROSS_BROWSER_SPECS` gave `webkit-desktop` and `webkit-mobile` the visual spec, but `e2e-visual-chromium` runs the chromium pair on Linux and no job compares webkit, so those baselines were checked only by whoever ran the spec on a Mac, and the 2026-11-06 Playwright bump already found three of them failing with nobody told. CI could not run them cheaply: webkit needs its own Linux baselines, its own browser install in the visual job, and it renders text differently enough from Chromium to flake. `playwright.config.ts` now separates `VISUAL_SPEC` from the functional list and gives it to `chromium-mobile` alone besides `chromium`. Held by `__tests__/playwright-visual-projects.test.ts` (two cases red before the change). _Reversible: restore the visual spec to `CROSS_BROWSER_SPECS`, restore the twelve `*-webkit-*-darwin.png` files from git history and regenerate them on a Mac._
- **2026-11-08** · **Every client rate limiter takes the salted IP hash, never the client address: `clientRateLimitKey` (`lib/server/rate-limit-key.ts`) hashes the `getClientIp` bucket with `hashIp`, and the limiter getters in `lib/rate-limit.ts` return a `ClientRateLimiter` whose `limit` accepts only the branded `RateLimitIdentifier` that helper returns.** The contact form said the IP was hashed for rate limiting, but `defineHandler`, `preflightAsk`, `withRateLimit`, `/api/healthz`, `/api/lighthouse` and the `/api/log` per-IP daily cap passed the raw IPv4 address or IPv6 `/64` to `@upstash/ratelimit`, which stores `prefix:identifier`, so `rl:contact`, `rl:errlog`, `rl:errlog:ipday`, `rl:ask`, `rl:forget`, `rl:mcp`, `rl:healthz` and `rl:lighthouse` keys held the address; the hash was only computed later, for the stored record. The `/64` bucketing still runs before the hash, so one `/64` shares one bucket. `defineHandler` now hashes first and answers `503 storage_unavailable` before the limiter or the body read when the salt cannot be resolved (it already answered 503 after validation); `/api/ask` keeps its fail-open path and skips the per-IP limit when the hash fails, while its budget stays fail-closed. The handler context drops `ip`, so no handler can reach the address. The shared `/api/log` daily cap keeps its fixed key inside `getErrorLogDailyLimit`. The privacy line in both locales now says the message is stored 90 days in Redis, that the emailed copy stays in the owner's mailbox and Resend's logs without that limit, and that the IP is hashed before it becomes a key or a record. The longer line makes the resolved form 650px to 762px tall below 480px and 560px to 605px above it, so the `.contact-loading` reserve moves from 34.8rem and 31.5rem to 44rem and 36rem (`tests/e2e/loading-states.spec.ts` failed at `/pt` 320px before), the `#sec-contact` `--module-estimate` steps in `components-shared.css` move to the taller of the two locales' measured extents (2514px at 320px down to 1018px from 1024px; `tests/e2e/deferred-modules.spec.ts` failed at 11 of its 12 widths before), and the darwin `contact-section` baselines for chromium, chromium-mobile, webkit-desktop and webkit-mobile are regenerated. In production the salt is `DEPLOY_SALT` when set, else the `meta:deploy-salt` value created once with `SET NX`; the hash depends on it, so this change (and any salt change) starts every limiter window afresh, which is accepted. Rejected: hashing inside each limiter call site (seven copies of one rule) and a runtime check on the identifier format (the type makes the raw address unrepresentable at the call site). Held by `__tests__/rate-limit-identifier-privacy.test.ts` (a fake limiter and Redis behind all seven routes: no key or Redis argument holds the IPv4 address or the IPv6 `/64`, one `/64` shares a bucket, distinct clients differ; 14 cases red before the fix) and its `@ts-expect-error` on a raw address, which fails the typecheck when the brand is removed (82f6612). _Reversible: pass `getClientIp(req)` to the limiters again and widen `ClientRateLimiter.limit` to `string`; the raw address returns to the keys and the privacy line becomes false._
- **2026-11-08** · **The contact section's reserved heights are re-measured for the longer privacy line, the "all fields required" note and the visible timezone search label, in both locales.** Combined, the three additions made the resolved section 10 to 111px taller than its `#sec-contact` `--module-estimate` (`tests/e2e/deferred-modules.spec.ts` failed at 767, 768, 1024 and 1280) and the live timezone panel 22 to 38px taller than the `--ctz-host-min` its static host reserved (`tests/e2e/layout-stability.spec.ts` failed en and pt at 1280); the label takes two lines below 480px, and `/pt` is the taller locale at every band. `#sec-contact` now takes the taller locale's extent per band plus a 10px to 20px margin (2640px below 340px down to 1070px from 1024px), and `--ctz-host-min` the tallest live panel per band over five zones and three clock times, sampled every 20px plus the band edges (1.4rem to 2.4rem more than before). The timezone spec also covers 320 and 1120px, where the narrowest and the widest `/pt` panels peak. _Reversible: restore the previous steps in `components-shared.css` and `components-contact.css`; the page then moves 10 to 111px when the contact section paints._
## 2026-11-08: design-system class lists never share a property, and the field edge meets 3:1

- **2026-11-08** · **`lib/cn.ts` stays `clsx` without `tailwind-merge`; each design-system component keeps one utility per CSS property, and `Field` draws its resting edge in `primary-500`.** The review found `cn` could not resolve conflicting utilities, so three defects shipped: `Badge` `sm` carried both `px-[10px]` (base) and `px-2` (size), `Button` primary carried `border-primary-subtle` (base) and `border-primary-500` (variant), and `Field` drew its edge in `primary-border` (signal at 20%, 1.48:1 on black, below the 3:1 of WCAG 1.4.11), patched in `ContactForm` by a `FIELD_BORDER` class override. In each pair the stylesheet order, not the class order, picked the winner. Options weighed: add `tailwind-merge` (about 7 KB gzipped on every route that imports `cn`, out of a 32.8 KB app-owned allowance, plus a vetting entry and a new supply-chain surface) or give each property one owner. Chosen: one owner. `Badge` moves padding into its size map, `Button` moves the border color into each variant, `Field` takes `border-primary-500` and drops the focus-only border swap and the `ContactForm` override. A consumer `className` can still collide with a base utility; that is a consumer decision and not merged away. `Field` also takes its default id from `useId` (two fields named `email` shared `field-email`), and `Badge` and `KbdKey` accept and spread their element attributes like the other primitives. `.kbd-key` loses the declarations its utilities always overrode, plus the pointer cursor and hover glow on a non-interactive `<kbd>`; the dead `.hero-stats .stat-tile` padding is deleted. `scripts/lint-css-tokens.ts` now also flags `rgb()`, `rgba()`, `hsl()` and `hsla()` in `app/css` and raw color literals in `*-tokens.ts` files, against `scripts/lint-css-tokens.allowlist.json`: each entry names a file, a literal, a count and a reason, and an entry that no longer matches fails the gate so the list only shrinks (today: the 12 `crt.css` overlay colors and the 4 canvas colors in `rain-tokens.ts`). The docs now count eleven components, ten of them server components. Held by `__tests__/design-system-class-conflicts.test.tsx` (no two utilities of one property in any variant, red before), the `Field`, `Badge`, `Button` and `KbdKey` unit tests and `*.e2e.ts` computed-style checks, `__tests__/ds-dead-css.test.ts`, `__tests__/design-system-doc-counts.test.ts` and `__tests__/lint-css-tokens.test.ts`. The darwin chromium baselines of `Field states`, `Badge variants`, `Button variants` and `KbdKey variants` are regenerated. _Reversible: add `tailwind-merge` to `cn` and restore the per-component base utilities; revert the border token on `Field`; delete the allowlist to make the lint scan-only for hex._
## 2026-11-08: accessibility review fixes for the terminal redesign

- **2026-11-08** · **No CSS `order` reorders a home section: the visual order is the DOM order at every width.** The mobile `#sec-*` order map in `app/css/base.css` put Guitar and mixer above Opinions below 768px while the DOM, the section registry, the dock and the drawer all list Opinions first (WCAG 2.4.3 Focus Order: keyboard and screen reader order contradicted the screen). Every other entry in the map already equalled the DOM position, so the map is deleted rather than corrected. `scripts/check-section-order.mjs` now fails on any `order` declaration in a `#sec-*` rule, and `tests/e2e/section-visual-order.spec.ts` compares the rendered top-to-bottom order with the DOM order at 320, 390, 768 and 1280px. The old "every section needs an order rule" check and its nested-anchor exemption list are gone with the map (the 2026-10-28 `KNOWN_UNORDERED` bullet below is superseded). Mobile visual baselines that include the home page below Opinions change. _Reversible: restore the order map and the missing-entry check; the e2e spec would then need a declared exception._
- **2026-11-08** · **Knob, fader and VU meter declare `touch-action`, and the knob steps on a tap.** Without `touch-action` a touch drag on a tablet that receives the interactive mixer (the user-agent check serves the static mixer to phones only) was taken over as a page scroll, which fired `pointercancel` and reverted the value. The fader and the meter drag horizontally, so they use `pan-y` and a vertical swipe still scrolls the page; the knob drags vertically, so it uses `none`. The knob only dragged vertically (WCAG 2.5.7 Dragging Movements); a tap on the upper half of the dial now adds 15 degrees and a tap on the lower half subtracts 15, with a `title` (`DawMixerCopy.controls.knobHint`, both locales) that describes it. Rejected: `+` and `-` buttons per knob, because the 30px knob cells and the compact channel grid have no room for two more 24px targets; switching the mixer variant from the user agent to a pointer media query, because it moves a server-rendered branch to the client for a case `touch-action` already covers. _Reversible: delete the three declarations and the tap handler; the `knobHint` copy key goes with it._
- **2026-11-08** · **The section drawer scrolls focused items clear of its sticky head.** `.section-menu` is the scroll container and `.menu-head` is sticky, so Shift+Tab to an item scrolled it under the head (WCAG 2.4.11 Focus Not Obscured). `.section-menu` now carries `scroll-padding-top` from `--menu-head-height`, derived from the same `--menu-row` token the head's close button uses. Held by `tests/e2e/drawer-focus-visible.spec.ts`. _Reversible: delete the declaration and the token._
- **2026-11-08** · **The timezone search has a visible label, the contact form states that every field is required, and local shell replies and the language names carry `lang`.** The search input had only a placeholder and an `aria-label` whose words differed from it, so the label vanished while typing (3.3.2) and the name did not contain the visible text (2.5.3); the visible `<label>` is now the name and the placeholder is an example. The contact fields were `required` with no visible instruction (3.3.2), so the form opens with `All fields are required.` (`form.requiredNote`, both locales). The sr-only language names in the language switch and the English local command replies of the shell on the Portuguese page had no `lang` (3.1.2); they now carry the language they are written in. _Reversible: each is a one-line removal plus its copy key._
- **2026-11-08** · **The `HIRE_ON_LINKEDIN` accessible name stays "Hire on LinkedIn (opens in a new tab)".** A review reported that the visible `HIRE_ON_LINKEDIN` is not contained in the name. The underscore is a word joiner in the site's terminal vocabulary, not a spoken character: the name contains the visible words in order, the 2026-10-31 decision holds, axe's `label-content-name-mismatch` reports nothing on the home, `/pt`, `/cv`, `/summary` and `/links` pages, and the Lighthouse gate on that audit passes. `__tests__/hire-label-in-name.test.ts` now holds the rule for the Portuguese strings too, and `tests/a11y/axe.spec.ts` runs the axe rule on those routes. _Reversible: nothing changed._

## 2026-11-08: the header, every page and the footer share one safe zone

- **2026-11-08** · **`--measure-page` wide, centered, `--space-gutter` on each side is the one safe zone; `.safe-zone`, `.page-container` and `.footer-inner` are the same rule (`components-shared.css`), and `--measure-read` caps text blocks, never a page container.** Owner: `/cv` content did not start or end on the header content edges. Measured per route at 320, 390, 768, 1024, 1280, 1440 and 1920 (content edge minus header content edge, left / right): `/cv` and `/pt/cv` 52.1 / -52.1 at 768, 180.1 / -180.1 at 1024 and 273.6 / -273.6 from 1280 (`.reading-doc` was `--measure-read` wide and centered); the 404 and route error pages 32.9 at 768, 160.9 at 1024 and 254.4 from 1280 (`.err-main` was `--measure-read` wide); `/design-system/*` ended 104.2 (768), 94.3 (1024) and 60.4 (1280 and up) short of the right edge (`.ds-main` was `--measure-read` wide); every route at 320 and 390 sat 2 / -2 inside the header, because the phone status bar and title bar padded 14px while the gutter is 16px. After the change every delta is 0 (within 1px) on the 12 routes at the 7 widths. The desktop topbar's inner row, the page containers and the footer now read the same class; the phone bars use `px-[var(--space-gutter)]`. `.reading-doc` fills the zone: `/cv` takes the `.reading-split` golden-ratio pair that `/summary` already used (main: summary, experience, community; aside: skills, certifications, education, contact), one column below 1024px and in print (`.reading-split--flow`). Line length stays readable through `max-width: var(--measure-read)` on `p` and entry list items, left aligned. `.err-main` is a `.page-container`, so the 404 panel spans the zone and the text stays centered; `.ds-main` fills its grid column and its prose paragraphs and lists keep the 72ch cap. The `/summary`-only `reading-doc--wide` modifier is gone because both pages are wide. Rejected: a per-page width (it drifted six ways before), and a header-only fix that left the pages narrow. Held by `tests/e2e/safe-zone.spec.ts` (page container, rendered content and footer edges against the header controls on 12 routes at 7 widths, red before the change; on the reading pages every block starts at the left edge, the aside ends on the right edge, and main to aside is 1.618 from 1024px) and `tests/e2e/layout-tokens.spec.ts`. The footer's phone `padding-inline-end` for the to-top button is the one documented inset; the guard compares its box minus the gutter. The nine darwin chromium baselines of `tests/e2e/design-system-components.spec.ts` are regenerated because the previews now fill the column (606px to 887px wide). _Reversible: restore `max-width: var(--measure-read)` on `.reading-doc`, `.err-main` and `.ds-main`, the 14px phone bar padding, and the single-column `CvPage`; regenerate the nine darwin baselines._

## 2026-11-08: framework floor re-measured at 131.4 KB; route total tightens to 164.2 KB

- **2026-11-08** · **`FRAMEWORK_FLOOR_KB` moves from 131.9 to 131.4 and `MAX_ROUTE_TOTAL_KB` from 164.7 to 164.2, so the app-owned allowance stays 32.8 KB.** A bare hello-world app (`app/layout.tsx` + `app/page.tsx`, `turbopack.root` pointed at the checkout that holds `node_modules`) on Next 16.4.5, React/React DOM 19.3.1 and TypeScript 6.0.3 measures 131.417 KB on `/` and on `/_not-found` (five `.js` chunks each), built with Node 22.23.4 and the default Turbopack `next build`. The method is the one the 2026-10-23 entry uses: sum `zlib.gzipSync` sizes of each route's `.js` `firstLoadChunkPaths` in `.next/diagnostics/route-bundle-stats.json`, divided by 1024. Before: 131.9 KB on Next 16.4.3. The 0.5 KB drop is inside the gate's 2 KB stale-floor tolerance, so the old floor still passed; it is moved anyway because the reported app-owned figure was inflated by 0.5 KB and the total only keeps its meaning as floor plus 32.8 KB. The real app is unchanged by this: its lightest content route measures 150.2 KB and `/` 158.8 KB on the same build, both under 164.2 KB. `__tests__/check-route-js.test.mjs` holds the numbers through the gate output it asserts. _Reversible: re-measure after any Next or React bump and move floor and total together, preserving the 32.8 KB allowance._

## 2026-11-08: pnpm quarantines new releases at install time

- **2026-11-08** · **`pnpm-workspace.yaml` sets `minimumReleaseAge: 10080` (minutes, seven days) and `packageManager` moves from `pnpm@10.15.0` to `pnpm@10.34.9`; `scripts/check-pkg-age.mjs` is deleted.** The script fetched abbreviated npm metadata, which has no `time` field, so every package was skipped and the CI step (`--warn-only`) never flagged anything. pnpm resolves against the full metadata and refuses a version younger than the window with `ERR_PNPM_NO_MATURE_MATCHING_VERSION`, for direct and transitive dependencies, before anything lands in the lockfile. Measured in a scratch project: 10.15.0 ignores the setting and installs a one-day-old release; 10.16.0 is the first release with the setting but fails on registry metadata without `time` (`ERR_PNPM_MISSING_TIME`); 10.34.9 refuses `ai@7.0.165` (released 23 hours earlier) and installs `zod@4.6.5`. 10.34.9 is the newest 10.x and was published 2026-10-25, outside the window itself. The `.npmrc` spelling (`minimum-release-age`) behaves the same; the yaml file is used so the setting is separate from credentials. A security fix younger than seven days is installed by listing that package in `minimumReleaseAgeExclude`, not by lowering the window. `__tests__/release-age-quarantine.test.ts` holds the window and the pnpm floor. Supersedes the 2026-05-21 age gate. _Reversible: delete `pnpm-workspace.yaml` and the test; the pnpm bump alone can stay._

## 2026-11-08: /links cards become four grouped sections

- **2026-11-08** · **`/links` groups its cards into four sections (watch, playlists, profiles, contact) with a featured channel card, and `noLeakedRender` becomes an error.** The pill grid (`LinkPills`) is replaced by `ProfileLines` and `ContactLines`: full-width 48px rows with glyph, label, host at the right edge and arrow, so a new network is one more row and no layout change. `ChannelCard` becomes one featured card (cover and text split 1 to 1.618, playlists in two columns, both from a 600px container query on `.links-col`), with the video title as its link and the cover link out of the tab order. The grid DOM order is now identity, feed stack, rows stack, so on a phone the video comes before the profiles and the old `display: contents` plus `order` trick is gone; from 768px grid areas keep identity and rows left and the feed right. The four warnings the previous bullet left in `components/links` are fixed with `hidden={description === ''}` and the lookup tables `COPY_VIEW` and `SUB_VIEW`, so the rule is an error. Rejected: keeping the pills for the contact group (two designs for one kind of row) and a `hidden` pair of labels in `SubscribeCta` (two labels in the DOM for one). Held by `components/links/LinksPage.test.tsx`, `ChannelCard.test.tsx`, `ProfileLines.test.tsx`, `ContactLines.test.tsx`, `tests/e2e/links-layout.spec.ts` and the `links.aria.yml` snapshot. _Reversible: restore `LinkPills`, the `.links-pill` rules and the stack `display: contents` from git history, and set the rule back to `warn`; no stored data depends on the layout._

## 2026-11-08: Biome adds import cycles, nested ternaries and leaked renders

- **2026-11-08** · **`biome.json` turns on `suspicious/noImportCycles` (error), `style/noNestedTernary` (error, off in `*.test.ts(x)` through an override) and `suspicious/noLeakedRender` (warn).** Measured before the change with `biome lint --only=<rule> .`: import cycles 0, nested ternaries 13 (10 outside tests, now 0; the 3 test sites keep their readable inline form), leaked renders 21 (now 4). The 17 fixed leaked renders became `cond ? <X /> : null`, and the `MeasuredTile` label branch tests `reserve === undefined || reserve === ''`, so rendered output is unchanged for every string, object and boolean value involved. The 4 left are `ChannelCard`, `CopyLinkButton` (2) and `SubscribeCta` under `components/links`: the `/links` layout is waiting on an owner decision, so the rule warns there until that lands and the sites are then fixed with it. **Not enabled:** `style/noMagicNumbers` (1948 hits, 623 outside tests) and `complexity/noExcessiveCognitiveComplexity` (71 hits at the default limit of 15, 60 outside tests). Both need a burn-down in their own milestones; turning them on now would force a baseline ignore list, which hides the debt instead of paying it. Reversible: revert the `biome.json` hunk and the rule stops running; the code changes are behaviour-neutral refactors.
## 2026-11-08: partial prefetching is on beside cache components

- **2026-11-08** · **`partialPrefetching: true` sits next to `cacheComponents: true` in `next.config.ts`.** Next 16.4 warns at build when Cache Components is enabled without an explicit choice; `false` exists only for apps migrating from the first Cache Components release, and this app never shipped on it. Reversible: delete the line (the build warning returns) or set it to `false`, one config line, no data or content change.
## 2026-11-08: AI SDK 7 spans need @ai-sdk/otel

- **2026-11-08** · **`/api/ask` spans now reach Langfuse: `@ai-sdk/otel` is installed and its `LegacyOpenTelemetry` integration is registered through `registerTelemetry` next to the existing `registerOTel` call, still only when `LANGFUSE_ENABLED` is `true`.** `ai@7.0.164` contains no OpenTelemetry code, so `experimental_telemetry: { isEnabled: true }` produced nothing even with the flag on. Registering the legacy integration keeps the `ai.*` span attributes that `langfuse-vercel` reads; the modern `OpenTelemetry` integration would emit `gen_ai.*` attributes it does not map. `registerTelemetry` pushes onto a `globalThis` array without deduplication, so one guard on `globalThis` (not module state, which dev reloads reset) makes the registration run once, and a failed registration does not latch it. `@ai-sdk/otel` depends on an exact `ai` version, and the lockfile holds `1.0.164` beside `7.0.164`, so `pnpm why ai` shows one copy; bump both together, and `__tests__/telemetry-register.test.ts` fails when they drift. The call moved to `telemetry: { functionId: 'ask', recordInputs: false, recordOutputs: false }`, the non-deprecated option, and `__tests__/ask-telemetry-privacy.test.ts` fails if either record flag turns on. This annotates the 2026-05-21 observability deferral: the Langfuse path it never covered now works, but tracing stays opt-in. Turning OpenTelemetry on in production unconditionally is an owner decision (trace volume, sampling and Vercel trace cost), not made here. With the flag off the production default is unchanged. _Reversible: remove the `registerTelemetry` line, the `@ai-sdk/otel` dependency and its vetting entry; spans stop and nothing else changes._
## 2026-11-08: forced-colors support is one last stylesheet

- **2026-11-08** · **Windows high contrast is handled in `app/css/forced-colors.css`, imported last, every rule inside `@media (forced-colors: active)`.** Forced colors repaints backgrounds as `Canvas` and removes shadows and gradients, so the VU meter, fader thumb, current-nav states, the shell input and similar controls lost their boundary or state, and the Matrix rain and CRT overlays painted over the system palette. Those states now use system colors (`Highlight`, `CanvasText`, `GrayText`) on borders and outlines; no markup changed. **Owner to confirm: hiding the CRT overlays and the rain under forced colors is a policy choice, not a measured need.** The alternative is to keep them and rely on the browser repainting them; the cost of keeping them is a gray veil over the page and a rain canvas drawn in the forced palette. The rain canvas is hidden with `visibility` because it sets `display` inline, so its loop still runs while hidden; stopping it needs a code change. axe runs in the spec without `color-contrast`, which measures author colors the forced palette replaces. Held by `tests/e2e/forced-colors.spec.ts`. _Reversible: delete the stylesheet, its import and the spec; nothing else references it._

## 2026-11-08: forced colors keep state, hierarchy and focus distinct without recoloring

- **2026-11-08** · **Under forced colors every state is drawn with system colors and a border style, and no rule uses `forced-color-adjust: none`.** Primary actions get a `ButtonText` double border at `--forced-state-width` plus bold, not an `adjust: none` filled background: a fill would override the palette the user chose and need its own hover, focus and contrast proof in every high-contrast theme, while a border keeps the user's colors and still shows hierarchy. Current is a `Highlight` double border plus underline, pressed a `Highlight` border plus underline, and focus a `Highlight` outline from one global `:focus-visible` rule, so no two states share a treatment (a double border is current, an outline is focus; the underline separates current and pressed from a bare primary). SVG shapes use `currentColor` so an icon inside a link or button follows `LinkText` or `ButtonText`; `Canvas` fills the knob face and `GrayText` the dim map land. `GrayText` stays for disabled controls and unavailable timezone cells only; structure such as the fader track and empty meter segments uses `ButtonText` with a dotted outline. **Cost:** the focus ring, the primary and current `.btn`, the pressed toggles, the meter fills and the window dots need `!important`, in one `biome-ignore-start` range at the end of the layer, because Tailwind utilities sit in a later layer than `components` and win otherwise; moving those rules into a utilities layer would break the "forced rules live in `components`" rule instead. The global focus rule is `!important` too, which overrides per-component focus outlines (`.shell-input` re-declares its inset ring the same way). The design-system `Button` now emits `data-variant`, a semantic hook for the primary rule; consumer attributes still pass through. Indicators that were background-only (`StatusBar` bars and battery, `FaderBar`, strength dots, static meter segments, plugin LEDs) gained `meter-fill`, `meter-dim` or `meter-alert` classes, with no change to normal rendering. **Not covered:** `/links` (another change owns it; the global focus and pressed rules reach it, its own selectors were not touched). Held by the `forced colors state, hierarchy and focus` block in `tests/e2e/forced-colors.spec.ts` (computed-style reads, no screenshots). _Reversible: delete the added rules and hook classes; `Button` keeps working without `data-variant`._

## 2026-11-08: hero CSS has one owner

- **2026-11-08** · **Every `.hero-*` selector rule lives in `app/css/components-hero.css`; `animations.css` keeps only the hero `@keyframes`.** The hero layout and boot rules were spread over four partials (`animations.css`, `components-about.css`, `components-footer.css` and the hero partial), so a change to one hero state meant reading the cascade across files, and `.hero-boot-mobile` was declared in two of them. The rules moved in their original relative order: the `animations.css` block first (it is imported before the hero partial), then the hero partial, then the former `components-about.css` blocks, which were already imported after it. Computed styles and box rects of every `[class*="hero-"]` element at 320, 390, 768, 1024, 1280 and 1440px were identical before and after, and no visual baseline changed. Held by `__tests__/hero-css-owner.test.ts`. _Reversible: move the blocks back; no rule text changed._

## 2026-11-08: Matrix Rain values are a TS constants module

- **2026-11-08** · **The rain's colours, sizes, canvas font and the mobile and desktop profiles live in `components/responsive/MatrixRain/rain-tokens.ts`, not in `@theme` custom properties.** Tailwind v4 drops a `@theme` variable nothing references, and reading one with `getComputedStyle` when the rain starts at idle costs a style recalculation on the path the idle start exists to keep clear. The canvas black is not a second copy: `rain-tokens.test.ts` fails if it stops matching `--color-secondary-950`. The `"JetBrains Mono"` canvas family is pinned there too, because the `--font-mono` stack resolves through a `next/font` variable a canvas cannot read. Reversible: inline the constants back or declare them in `theme.css` and read them once at mount.
## 2026-11-08: SectionNavigation split by job

- **2026-11-08** · **`SectionNavigation.client.tsx` (483 lines) is five client modules and two plain ones under `components/responsive/SectionNavigation/`, with the same barrel exports.** `sectionScroll.ts` holds `jumpToSection`, `jumpTarget` and the pending-correction abort logic; `SectionNavigationProvider.client.tsx` holds the context, the hooks and the section spy; `SectionDrawer.client.tsx` holds the drawer (`SectionNavigation`); `LanguageSwitch.client.tsx` and `MenuTrigger.client.tsx` hold the two header controls; `navigationIds.ts` holds the drawer id and the menu trigger class so the provider can find a trigger without importing the component that imports it. The provider's context value stays fixed and the spy writes only through `store.set` (2026-11-06), and the drawer still registers no media query listener (2026-10-30); both are held by `SectionNavigationProvider.test.tsx` and `SectionDrawer.test.tsx`. The spy takes `observeSections` from `lib/section-spy.ts`, which hands back the intersecting sections in document order, and keeps this component's own band. Alternative rejected: one provider file with the drawer moved out, which leaves the scroll helper and the spy interleaved with the context. Reversible: concatenate the modules back into one file and point the barrel at it; no stored data or public contract moves.
## 2026-11-08: one set of in-view primitives

- **2026-11-08** · **Four components shared one IntersectionObserver recipe, now `lib/in-view.ts` (`observeInView`), `lib/use-in-view.client.ts` (`useInView`) and `lib/section-spy.ts` (`observeSections`). The footer, the contact timezone preload, the clock panel and `pauseWhileHidden` consume them; `TypeReveal` and `MatrixRain` do not.** Root cause of the duplication: each caller built its own observer, its own missing-API branch and its own section lookup, and the footer carried a private copy of the home id and the mobile hero selector. The missing-API answer is the caller's choice and matches what each did before: the preload and the clock panel count as in view, the pause helper never pauses, the section spy reports nothing. A section batch reports its intersecting sections in document order, so the section navigation can take the first and the footer can add all of them to its seen set; an empty set reports the empty id. Each caller passes its own reading band, because the footer counts a section only inside `-40% 0 -40% 0` and a shared band would change what "sections seen" means. `TypeReveal` stays on its own observer: it watches elements that a `MutationObserver` adds later, unobserves each one after its first reveal and decides motion and observer support together, which a fixed-target wrapper would have to re-implement. `MatrixRain` stays: it observes a canvas to gate a frame loop and its observer lives inside that loop's lifecycle. `SectionNavigationProvider` reads the home id, the mobile hero selector and the section lookup from `observeSections` and keeps its own band, `-45% 0px -54% 0px`. Alternative rejected: one hook for every caller, which would hide the three different missing-API answers behind flags. Reversible: inline the three modules back into their callers; no stored data or public contract moves.
## 2026-11-08: the contact form is split along its states

- **2026-11-08** · **`ContactForm.tsx` is a composer; the send state machine, the field checks and each visible state are separate modules.** The 521-line file held the status machine, validation, the mailto fallback, the receipt with its forget action and both result panels, so the machine could only be tested by mounting the whole form. `useContactSubmit` owns the status, field errors, request error, receipt and the `pendingRef` guard; `contact-form-checks.ts` holds `FIELD_CHECKS` and `errorMailto`; `ContactShell`, `ContactResult` and `ContactReceipt` render the chrome and the delivered, queued and receipt states. The pre-hydration adoption of typed text (`useLayoutEffect` over the server DOM, DECISIONS 2026-11-06) stays in `ContactForm`, because it reads the form element and seeds `messageStore` before the first paint. The new modules import `content/contact-rules.ts` only, never `content/contact.ts` as a value, so Zod stays out of the lazy chunk (2026-11-06, contact copy table). Held by `useContactSubmit.test.tsx` (202 queued, `ok:false` mapping, double-submit guard, locale header), `contact-form-checks.test.ts` and `ContactReceipt.test.tsx`; the existing form, hydration and e2e specs and the `contact-section` baseline are unchanged. Reversible: inline the modules back into one file; no data, route or markup changes.
## 2026-11-08: Panel is a design-system primitive

- **2026-11-08** · **`Panel` moves from `components/Panel` into `design-system/components/Panel`, exported from `@/design-system`, and its frame classes are `.panel`, `.panel-head`, `.panel-title` and `.panel-marker`.** The frame was shared by two sections and named after one (`ab-*`), and its CSS lived in the About partial, so a second consumer meant editing About's stylesheet. The frame rules now sit in `app/css/components-panel.css`, imported before `components-about.css`; section-specific title sizing and the repo-link header layout stay in the About partial, scoped under `.ab-code`, `.ab-resp` and `.panel-head`, so cascade order is unchanged. The `## Panel` docs section and a `Panel variants` darwin baseline pin the look. `reveal` stays a plain boolean that sets `data-type-reveal`: the typing comes from the `TypeReveal` island in the root layout, and the docs state that coupling. Alternative rejected: a `data-type-reveal` pass-through only, which hides the `suppressHydrationWarning` the rewritten heading needs. Reversible: move the directory and the four class names back.
## 2026-11-08: the shell island is split along its seams

- **2026-11-08** · **`InteractiveShell.tsx` composes four units: `ShellLines.client.tsx` (line renderer, thinking timer, countdown), `ShellToolbar.client.tsx` (roving focus), `useCommandHistory.ts` and `usePrivacyCommands.ts`, each with its own test, and no nested ternary is left in the folder.** The composer keeps what must stay in React and in one place: the layout effect that adopts text typed before hydration, the rAF-coalesced stream in `useAskStream`, and the `aria-live` log and status regions. `useCommandHistory` stores the unsent draft only when recall leaves the newest entry, because every other write was overwritten before it could be read. The shell no longer needs an entry in either meta allowlist for `SHELL_COMMAND_EVENT`, `MS_PER_SECOND` or `SECONDS_PER_MINUTE`. Undo by reverting the commit; the unit tests move with the files.

## 2026-11-08: pull requests that touch a high-risk surface are labelled

- **2026-11-08** · **A PR that changes a high-risk surface gets the `high-risk` label automatically, and loses it when no changed file is on the list; no PR size limit is added.** Review attention should follow blast radius, and size says little about it: a one-line change to `proxy.ts` or `lib/env.ts` outweighs a large content edit. The list is one module (`scripts/high-risk-paths.ts`), unit-tested, with a guard that every entry exists on disk so a rename cannot drop a surface unnoticed. `high-risk-label.yml` takes `pull-requests: write` on the labelling job only, passes event values through `env`, and skips fork and Dependabot runs (read-only token) with a notice instead of failing. Alternative rejected: a size cap, which the owner declined. Reversible: delete the workflow, the script and its two tests.
## 2026-11-08: the slow rollback path is rehearsed weekly

- **2026-11-08** · **`rollback-rehearsal.yml` reverts the latest commit on `main` in the runner every Monday and proves the result installs, typechecks, passes its tests and builds.** Root cause: the slow rollback in `AGENTS.md` (`git revert HEAD`) was only described, so a revert that no longer builds (a lockfile or generated-file dependency on the reverted commit, for example) would first show up during an incident. The workflow is read-only toward production: top-level `contents: read`, no push, no Vercel call, no Vercel token; only its `report` job holds `issues: write`, to open or update one "Rollback rehearsal failed" issue. It adds `-m 1` only when the latest commit is a merge, because `main` takes squash merges. Held by `__tests__/rollback-rehearsal-workflow.test.ts`. Not rehearsed: the fast path (`vercel promote`), which is a platform action. Reversible: delete the workflow and its test.

## 2026-11-08: the mobile breakpoint has one reader and one test fake

- **2026-11-08** · **Production reads the mobile breakpoint only through `isMobileViewport()` and `watchMobileViewport()` in `lib/media-queries.ts`, and tests fake it with `installMatchMedia` in `__tests__/helpers/match-media.ts`.** Four hand-rolled `matchMedia` calls and ten hand-rolled stubs had drifted apart: several stubs returned listeners that nothing could fire, so a viewport change was never exercised. The helper keeps a real listener set per query, so `emit(query, matches)` reaches only registered listeners and unsubscribing is observable. Held by `__tests__/match-media-helper.test.ts`, `__tests__/use-breakpoint.test.tsx` and `components/client/HeroBootAnimation/HeroBootAnimation.test.tsx`. _Reversible: inline the calls and stubs again._

## 2026-11-07: Section surfaces are opaque

- **2026-11-07** · **Every home section panel sits on an opaque background, so the app-wide Matrix Rain never shows through a panel or a card.** The `Module` shell painted `rgba(0,0,0,0.22)` below 768px and nothing at 768px and up, `.module-body-content` was `transparent`, the projects and live-metrics panels and the green variant tinted it with `--color-glow-05` (5% green, still see-through), and the project, guitar and mixer cards stacked 3% to 10% tints on top, so rain glyphs rendered behind the text and the panels read lighter than the Opinions section, which has no tint. Fix: the panel base is `--color-secondary-950` (the black) everywhere, the tinted panels (projects, live metrics, the green variant) drop their 5% green tint and use the same black, so every panel reads as dark as Opinions, which the owner named as the reference, the mobile `Module` shell is `bg-secondary-950`, `.about-section` below 768px is black, and the gradient panels (`.panel`, `.footer-card`, `.contact-success`, `.contact-queued`) end in a black layer instead of `transparent`. Inner card and tile tints stay translucent: they now sit on the opaque panel. Opinions is a `Module` too, so it became opaque with the rest; it was never opaque, it only looked dark. Held by `tests/e2e/section-surfaces.spec.ts` (every bordered or tinted surface of the ten home sections has an opaque background on itself or an ancestor inside the section, at 1280 and 390). Held on the other routes by `tests/e2e/route-surfaces.spec.ts` (every bordered or tinted surface inside `main` of `/`, `/pt`, the CV, summary, links, 404 and every design-system page has an opaque background on itself or an ancestor, at 1280 and 390; 1px dividers and links, buttons and inputs are not surfaces). Same black everywhere: `.reading-stats` and the skill chips, the `/links` identity box, channel card, history list, empty and show-all rows, skeleton blocks, QR panel and playlist cards, `TerminalPanel` (its `bg-transparent` default became `bg-secondary-950`), `Badge`, the design-system stat row, opinion block, tables, code blocks and inline code, and the colour swatches, which draw their token colour over black so a translucent token reads the same on every page. Not changed: the mobile `Dock` (92% black with a 12px blur, a frosted bar rather than a panel), the menu scrim and the CRT overlays, which are translucent by design. _Reversible: restore the `transparent` and `glow-05` values and `bg-[rgba(0,0,0,0.22)]` in `Module`, and the pre-change backgrounds on the route surfaces above (`git revert` the route-surfaces commit); delete both specs._

Inner card and tile tints stay translucent: they now sit on the opaque panel. Opinions is a `Module` too, so it became opaque with the rest; it was never opaque, it only looked dark. Held by `tests/e2e/section-surfaces.spec.ts` (every bordered or tinted surface of the ten home sections has an opaque background on itself or an ancestor inside the section, at 1280 and 390). Not changed: `/links`, `/summary` and `/design-system` surfaces use their own classes and still let the rain through. _Reversible: restore the `transparent` and `glow-05` values and `bg-[rgba(0,0,0,0.22)]` in `Module`; delete the spec._
## 2026-11-07: the repository's AI setup stays, personal workflow leaves

- **2026-11-07** · **The repository keeps its AI tooling layout and drops the content that was one engineer's workflow.** `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md` (importing it), `.claude/` (settings, rules, the CI review agent, skill links), `.agents/skills`, `.codex/config.toml` and `.mcp.json` stay and keep working together: they describe the repository to any tool. What leaves is workflow: the Claude Code hooks and their scripts (`guard-merge-main`, `guard-test-edits`, `stop-verify`), the local `claude-gate` approval check and the `pr:comments` inspector, the rule that agents never merge into `main`, the claude-review fix-loop rounds, the per-push baseline YES/NO ritual, the subagent dispatch prompt and the communication-style lines. `.claude/settings.json` keeps the deny rules that state repository rules and registers no hooks, and `__tests__/meta/ai-config.test.ts` fails if it registers one. CI pieces stay where they are: `claude-review.yml` with its orchestrator and `review-worker`, `ask:eval`, `eval:agents`, `pr:gate`; the verdict guard and the reviewer prompt no longer cite the removed gate. Rejected: removing the AI config folders and moving their project knowledge into the handbook, because the shared layout is how each tool finds the same rules, and the skills and path rules carry repository procedure, not personal preference. Undo by reverting the commits of this change. Supersedes the 2026-11-07 entries below for `guard-merge-main`, `guard-test-edits`, the `FIX_MODE` hook and the `gh pr merge` rule: those mechanisms no longer exist.

## 2026-11-07: Local CI prediction

- **2026-11-07** · **`pnpm ci:predict` runs the CI jobs a branch's commits trigger before the push, and it reads `.github/workflows/ci.yml` instead of copying it.** Root cause: every recent CI-only failure came from a local run that covered a subset of CI in a different environment: a new spec run on Chromium only failed on WebKit, an LCP spec that passed on macOS fonts failed on Linux metrics, flaky specs passed once locally while CI fails a run that passed only on a retry, and a spec that needed an external image host passed on a networked machine. Options weighed: a hand-written list of local commands (drifts the day a job is added), `act` (runs the whole matrix with GitHub-hosted artifacts and secrets and cannot narrow to changed specs), and an interpreter over the workflow with a drift test (chosen). The interpreter evaluates job and step conditions, env, the e2e and visual matrices and step outputs from `ci.yml`, runs change detection through `scripts/detect-changes.mjs`, and takes the projects for each spec from `playwright test --list`. `scripts/__tests__/ci-predict.test.ts` fails when `ci.yml` gains a job or an action the predictor does not map, and when a command in the `ci:local` chain is covered by no CI step. `--linux` runs Playwright in the official image for the installed `@playwright/test` version and builds and serves the app inside that same container, so the origin is `localhost` and the build is a Linux build. Options weighed: a host-built server reached through `host.docker.internal` (rejected: a different origin changed the `/_next/image` requests and failed `links.spec.ts` thumbnails on every project, which passes in CI), and mounting the macOS `node_modules` and `.next` (rejected: the native `sharp` binary is a macOS build). The bundle and route-JS gates still read the host build. Not reproduced, and reported as not-run or partial: the visual spec without `--linux` (CI gates only the `-linux.png` baselines, and the macOS run failed on sections nobody touched), Lighthouse (scores depend on the host), `dependency-review`, the PR comment gate, `semgrep` (report-only). New or changed specs run with `--repeat-each=3 --retries=0`; a change to app code with no matching spec runs no e2e unless `--full` is passed. The live `ask:eval` runs only with `--eval`, through the pinned Vercel CLI from the linked checkout, so secrets reach the process and are never printed. Reversible: delete `scripts/ci-predict.ts`, `scripts/lib/ci-*.ts`, their tests and the docs lines; nothing else depends on them.
- **2026-11-07** · **`pnpm ci:predict` never reports a pass for work it did not run or could not reproduce.** Root cause: the first version let several unreproduced paths read as success. A multi-line step ran under `sh -c` without `-e`, so only the last line decided the result; an expression root the evaluator did not know became an empty string and the step silently skipped; workflow and job `env:` were ignored; `hashFiles` took globs literally; `sudo` steps were skipped without a word; a job with no executed step, a narrowed e2e run and `--list` (which stubbed every command to exit 0) all printed pass and `no failure found`; the Linux session ignored env changes and the container, the server and every step child outlived Ctrl-C; the native server inherited `.env.local` and could write to real Redis and Resend; a spec that compared against `localhost:3000` asserted nothing on the predictor's random port; `--skip-build` trusted any `.next`; and the default base was `origin/main` even for a branch cut from an integration branch. Options weighed for the evaluator: keep returning empty and add a warning (rejected: a warning is read after the table says pass), or throw on anything it does not populate (chosen, with the parser allow-listing workflow, job, step and defaults keys so a new construct fails the drift guard). Options weighed for `--skip-build`: trust `.next` and warn, or stamp the build with the commit, tracked edits, untracked files and build id (chosen: a mismatch makes the build and every server on it `partial`). Options weighed for server readiness: any listener answering on the port (rejected: a stale server on a port the OS reused passes), or readiness only when the listener descends from the spawned process and that process is alive (chosen; the first version checked the process group, which fails for `pnpm start` because `next-server` runs in its own group, found by running the predictor end to end). Verdicts gained `would-run`, narrowed e2e is `partial`, and the exit code is 0 only for a fully verified clean run, 1 for a failure, 2 for a dry run, 3 for no failure found with unverified jobs. Guards: `scripts/__tests__/ci-predict.test.ts`, `ci-predict-process.test.ts`, `ci-predict-stamp.test.ts`, `ci-workflow.test.ts`, and `__tests__/meta/e2e-origin.test.ts` (bans `localhost:3000` in specs). Reversible: revert the commit; the earlier behaviour returns with its gaps.
- **2026-11-07** · **`pnpm ci:predict` closes the remaining ways a run reports less than it verified.** Root cause: five paths let an unverified or contaminated run look verified. (1) The build stamp hashed untracked file names, not contents, and used a text diff, so editing an untracked file after the build left the stamp valid; it now hashes each untracked file's bytes and diffs with `--binary`. (2) A branch with no commits ahead of its base exited 0, the code that means verified; it now exits 3. (3) A failed Linux setup left its container, and a second request while one was half-started tried to reuse its name; one session keeper now removes the container on failure and before any new one. (4) A cached server whose process had exited was reused for later jobs, and `killGroup` signalled the pid of a child that had already exited, which the system may have given to another process; both check that the child is still running. (5) The build, server and Playwright children inherited the shell's whole environment, so a secret exported there reached the app; they now get an allowlist plus the step's own `env:`. Smaller: readiness requests and the free-port probe have a timeout, a `ps` that cannot run yields no descendants, a live-eval step in an ordinary job is reported not reproducible, and every `${{ secrets.X }}` read as empty is named in the job's row. Options weighed for the environment: keep inheriting and blank known keys (rejected: it only covers keys named in `.env*.local`), or an allowlist (chosen; the blank of `.env*.local` keys stays because Next reloads a key the process lacks). Guards: `ci-predict-process.test.ts`, `ci-predict-process-stubbed.test.ts`, `ci-predict-stamp.test.ts`, `ci-predict-cli.test.ts`, `ci-predict.test.ts`, `ci-workflow.test.ts`. Reversible: revert the commit; the earlier behaviour returns with its gaps.

## 2026-11-07: the MOTION switch is the only motion control

- **2026-11-07** · **A first visit starts with motion on even when the operating system asks for reduced motion, and the MOTION switch is the single control: on is full motion, off is reduced motion everywhere.** Owner decision, made after being told the cost: "motion off turns reduce motion on; motion on turns it off; I only said it must start ON initially." Trade-off: the OS `prefers-reduced-motion` setting is no longer honoured by default, so a visitor who set it still sees animation until they switch MOTION off, and the choice then persists in `localStorage` (`erik.motion`). `lib/motion.ts` `readMotion()` returns false only for a stored `off`; `readSystemReducedMotion()`, the `MOTION: REDUCED` label and the `CRTOverlay` media-query listener are gone. The body bootstrap (`lib/motion-bootstrap.ts`) writes `data-motion` from storage alone. Every `@media (prefers-reduced-motion)` rule in `app/css/*.css` and the two Tailwind `motion-reduce:` utilities now key off `body[data-motion="reduce"]`; the `print` rule for the EQ bars stays. One exception, for WCAG 2.2.2 (level A): with JavaScript off no script can offer the MOTION switch, so a single `@media (scripting: none) and (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)` block at the end of `app/css/animations.css` stops every animation and transition, shows the links ticker as one static line and hides the blinking dots, the CRT layers and the guitar scan beam. A scripted page never matches it, so the switch stays the only control wherever it exists. Held by `__tests__/motion-single-switch.test.ts` (no stylesheet or source reads the OS preference, except that one block), `__tests__/motion.test.ts` and `__tests__/motion-bootstrap.test.ts` (first visit on, stored off respected), `tests/e2e/motion-switch.spec.ts` (first visit under an emulated reduce setting is `MOTION: ON` with the EQ bars and CRT beam animating; the switch reduces everything, persists and survives a reload) and `tests/e2e/no-js.spec.ts` (JavaScript off under an emulated reduce setting animates nothing; without the setting the animation runs). Specs that need a still page seed the stored choice through `tests/e2e/_helpers/motion.ts`. Supersedes the 2026-10-31 label decision below. Reversible: restore the `matchMedia` fallback in `readMotion()` and the bootstrap, and re-add the media-query rules beside the body-attribute ones.

## 2026-11-07: Shared header accessibility follow-ups (drawer motion, dock at 320, module commands, hero metadata)

- **2026-11-07** · **The drawer motion toggle has the fixed accessible name `MOTION` and `aria-pressed` carries the state; its visible text still reads `MOTION: ON`, `MOTION: OFF` or `MOTION: REDUCED`.** The button used its changing text as its name and also set `aria-pressed`, so a screen reader announced "MOTION: OFF, toggle button, not pressed", one state twice that could disagree after a toggle. The topbar control already used a fixed name, so both controls now match. Rejected: dropping `aria-pressed`, because the topbar twin keeps it and the reduced reading needs the visible word. Held by `components/responsive/SectionNavigation/SectionNavigation.test.tsx`. Superseded below: the name now comes from the visible label, with no `aria-label`.
- **2026-11-07** · **Below 360px the dock drops its letter-spacing, its cell padding and its gaps, and keeps its labels.** At 320px each cell was 57px wide and the labels `PROJECTS`, `PROJETOS` and `CONTRATE` measured 61px, so the text overflowed its cell and neighbours sat 3.5px apart. Spacing 0 alone still leaves 57.6px of text in a 57px cell, so the dock container also keeps a 4px side padding and no gap. Rejected: a shorter Portuguese label, because `PROJETOS` has the same length as `CONTRATE`, and a smaller type size, because the type scale holds 12px as its floor. Held by `components/responsive/Dock/Dock.e2e.ts` (every label inside its cell and 8px from its neighbour at 320px on `/` and `/pt`). _Reversible: restore the `max-width: 359.98px` block of `app/css/components-nav.css`._
- **2026-11-07** · **A module heading hides its shell command from assistive technology when a localized section name already names the heading, and tags the command `lang="en"` in every case.** On `/pt` the heading name read the Portuguese name followed by an English command such as `SUDO CONTACT --INIT`, English words in a Portuguese name with no language tag (WCAG 3.1.2). The About and Career headings already hid their command; `Module` now does the same when it has a `name` or `srLabel`. Held by `components/responsive/Module/Module.srlabel.test.tsx` and the `/pt` heading-name test in `tests/e2e/html-lang.spec.ts`. _Reversible: drop `aria-hidden` and `lang` from the command spans in `Module.tsx`._
- **2026-11-07** · **Each hero metadata pair is a nowrap item and the separator between pairs is drawn by CSS in front of the pair, clipped where a pair starts a line.** This supersedes the separator span and the `display: none` rule of the 2026-10-31 hero grouping. The markup separator could wrap to the end of a line (`LOC: Brazil ·`), the domain split at its dot, and from 768px up the facts had only a gap between them (`private markets LANG:`). `components/sections/Hero/Hero.tsx` now renders the location, domain and language as items (the domain is split at its separator), `app/css/components-hero.css` places a `·` at half the pair gap before every pair that is not first in its fact, and the container clips its left edge so a pair at the start of a line shows no dot. The gap is `3ch`, the width of the literal ` · `. The generated content has an empty alternative text so assistive technology does not read it. The English fact rows at 768px and 1280px and the one-fact-per-line column below 768px are unchanged. Held by `components/sections/Hero/Hero.test.tsx` and `components/sections/Hero/Hero.e2e.ts` (no dot in front of a pair at the left edge, no pair past the viewport, at 320, 390, 768, 1024 and 1280px on `/` and `/pt`). _Reversible: restore the separator span and the 2026-10-31 rules._

- **2026-11-07** · **The no-JavaScript header carries each page's own language pair and current-page link: every page hands `SiteShell` its static `route`.** Measured on the production HTML before the change: the `Suspense` fallback of the language switch used the two language homes, so `/cv` shipped `/` and `/pt`, `/links` shipped `/` and `/pt`, `/pt/summary` shipped `/` and `/pt`, and the topbar fallback rendered every link without `aria-current`. Root cause: the fallback could not know the route, because only `usePathname` knows it and that hook is what suspends. `SiteShell(locale, route?, drawerExtra?)` now passes the route to `AppShell` and `SectionNavigationProvider`, and both fallbacks derive from it (`headerLanguageSwitchPaths(route)`, `TopbarLinks pathname={route}`); `usePathname` still drives the hydrated switch and the section hash, so the design-system layout (one layout, many pages) keeps a correct switch after hydration while its server fallback uses the design-system root. The 404 and route error pages pass no route and keep the language homes. Rejected: reading the pathname in the layouts (it moves the switch out of the static HTML), and dropping `Suspense` for static routes (the design-system layout serves many pathnames from one shell). Held by `components/responsive/SectionNavigation/SectionNavigation.test.tsx` and `__tests__/desktop-topbar-nav.test.tsx` (server HTML with the pathname suspended), `__tests__/site-shell.test.tsx` (each page names its route) and `tests/e2e/shared-header.spec.ts` (the href pair of `/cv`, `/pt/summary`, `/links`, `/links/en`, `/design-system` and the current `/links` link without JavaScript). Known limit: on a design-system page below the root, the no-JavaScript English link points at `/design-system`. _Reversible: drop the `route` prop; the fallbacks return to the language homes._
- **2026-11-07** · **The drawer motion toggle takes its accessible name from its visible label `MOTION`, and the state word sits in an `aria-hidden` span.** The previous fix set `aria-label="MOTION"` over the visible text `MOTION: ON`, which fails WCAG 2.5.3 (Label in Name): voice control users who say what they see (`MOTION: ON`) match nothing. The name is now computed from the visible `MOTION` text alone and `aria-pressed` reports the state, so the state is announced once and the visible label is the name. `NavigationCopy.motionOn`, `motionOff` and `motionReduced` hold only the state word. Rejected: a name that spells the state, because it announces the state twice and goes stale after a toggle. Held by `components/responsive/SectionNavigation/SectionNavigation.test.tsx` (no `aria-label`, name equals the visible label). _Reversible: render the state inside the label again._
- **2026-11-07** · **The hero domain items come from a validated list, and the separator glyph is one CSS token.** `components/sections/Hero/Hero.tsx` split the employer context string on its separator, so a fact containing the separator would have split into two items. `currentEmployer.contextFacts` is now a `ContextFactsSchema` array (non-empty, no fact containing `FACT_SEPARATOR`), `profilePresentation` exposes `employerContextFacts` and the joined `employerContext`, and the Portuguese facts pass through the same schema. `content/fact-separator.ts` is the one text separator; `--separator-glyph` in `app/css/base.css` is the one CSS spelling and `components-hero.css` reads it instead of a literal `·`. Rejected: setting the token inline from the TypeScript constant, because inline style attributes would need a CSP allowance for one glyph; a test holds the two spellings equal instead. Held by `content/schemas.test.ts`, `__tests__/content-single-source.test.ts` and `__tests__/separator-token.test.ts`. _Reversible: restore the string field and the literal in `components-hero.css`._
- **2026-11-07** · **The error pages stop adding a second bottom padding under the dock clearance.** At 320x700 the 404 page scrolled 1px: below 1024px the body already reserves the dock clearance, and `.err-main` added a 32px bottom padding on top of a `min-height` that subtracts the same clearance, so content of 526.66px met 526px of room. `.err-main` takes `var(--spacing-4)` bottom padding below 1024px. Measured at 320x700 before the change: document 701px in a 700px viewport. Held by `tests/e2e/error-states.spec.ts` (no scroll at 320x700, 390x844, 768x1024 and 1280x720). _Reversible: remove the `max-width: 1023.98px` block from `components-error-pages.css`._
- **2026-11-07** · **Rendering the shell in the lazy route error page leaves every route's first-load JS unchanged, so the error page keeps `SiteShell`.** `RouteErrorPage` loads through `lazy()` from `error.tsx`, so it is outside `route-bundle-stats.json` and `pnpm route-js-check`. Measured on production builds before and after the shell fixes: the heaviest route stays at 158.3 KB gzipped (`/` and `/pt`), `/links` at 151.5 KB, the reading views at 149.8 KB, and the design-system routes move by 0.1 KB at most. The lazy page chunk is 3.4 KB gzipped; the shell code it imports sits in three shared chunks of 8.7 to 9.7 KB gzipped each that every page route already ships, so an error raised after hydration costs only the 3.4 KB chunk, and an error raised while the server renders costs those three chunks (about 27 KB gzipped) on demand. Passing the chrome from the server is not possible here, because an error boundary file is a client component and cannot receive server-rendered children from its layout. _Reversible: render the error body without `SiteShell`._
## 2026-11-07: dead code, shared constants and accessibility sweep

- **2026-11-07** · **Every class selector in `app/css` is referenced from `components`, `app`, `design-system`, `content` or `lib`, and `__tests__/css-referenced-classes.test.ts` fails on one that is not.** Thirteen dead rules (the halt and dmesg reveal, `.bar-pulse`, `.live-dot`, `.netstat-link`, `.sp-bar`, `.signal-glow-strong`, `.community-tags a`, `.hero-dialog-desktop`, `.not-found`) and their keyframes shipped to every visitor. The test matches whole class tokens, not substrings. Dynamic class strings need an entry in the test's `SCRIPT_SET_CLASSES` with a reason. A component focus-visible rule is deleted only when no class on that element sets `outline: none` in `@layer components`, because that layer beats the global rule in `@layer base`; the fader, VU meter and knob lost their rings that way until their `outline: none` was dropped, and `tests/e2e/focus-rings.spec.ts` focuses each by keyboard and asserts a computed outline. The global rule and the two inset and the green deviations use `--focus-ring-width`, `--focus-ring-offset` and `--focus-ring-offset-inset`. `CmdLine` merges `className`, forwards the rest of the `div` attributes and takes `user` as a required prop, so the design system imports nothing from `content/`; `__tests__/design-system-content-agnostic.test.ts` fails on any such import, and copy reaches it as props. _Reversible: restore the deleted rules from git, or drop the test._
- **2026-11-07** · **The offline notice receives its copy through `siteNavChrome(locale).offline`, so the client bundle carries one locale of it, and `useDragSlider` holds its config without `useCallback`.** `AppShell` is bundled once per root layout (four copies), and the notice imported both locales from `content/offline`, so every copy shipped the other language. Extracting shared constants and the drag hook had pushed the client chunk sum to 220.1KB against the 220KB gate (base 219.8KB); resolving the copy on the server brings it to 219.3KB. Held by `content/navigation-chrome.test.ts`, `OfflineNotice.test.tsx` and `pnpm bundle-check`. Reversible: import `offlineNotice` back into `OfflineNotice.client.tsx` and drop `offline` from `SiteNavChrome`.
- **2026-11-07** · **Section aliases, the two registry lookups, `stripLocalePrefix`, `careerCommand`, `profileCityLine`, four icons and the `cx` helper are deleted, and `__tests__/meta/dead-exports.test.ts` fails when `Icons`, the section registry, `site-locale` or `links.constants` export a symbol no production file reads.** Each had no caller, and `icons.test.tsx` listed all twelve icons by hand, which forced the dead four to stay. A future shell command defines its own matching. Landed in 42baf40; undo with `git revert 42baf40`.
- **2026-11-07** · **Window event names, time units and the two-digit clock pad each have one definition (`lib/events.ts`, `lib/time.ts`), and meta tests fail on a second one.** A bare event string falls back to the untyped `addEventListener` overload, so a rename on one side stops the other without any type error. `ErrorPages` stays listed as an unmigrated time-unit consumer until its own work lands. Landed in e999023 and 532cabb; undo by reverting them.
- **2026-11-07** · **`LinksLocale` is `SiteLocale`, and the links Open Graph and Intl tables come from the site tables.** Two nominally different unions with identical values only forced conversions. Landed in 0dd0b1b; undo by reverting it.
- **2026-11-07** · **The DAW mixer islands hold no copy: control names and the degree word live in `DawMixerCopy.controls` (en and pt) and arrive as props, and `no-inline-aria-copy.test.ts` fails when any component types a word into `aria-label`, `aria-valuetext`, `alt` or `title`.** The pt page read English control names to a screen reader, and a locale-parity test cannot see a string inside a `.tsx`. Landed in 4597e78; undo by reverting it.
- **2026-11-07** · **Sentence text uses `--color-tertiary-50`, and `signal-color-sentences.test.tsx` renders the sections and reading pages and fails when a rule colours a sentence class with `--color-primary-500`.** A sentence is four or more words with a lowercase letter outside headings; `ctz-loading` and `mixer-boot-current` are named terminal status lines and keep the signal colour as machine voice. `.career-body` is body text too (the 2026-11-08 entry reversed the signal-green description), held by `tests/e2e/type-scale.spec.ts`. Landed in 1b1ae01; undo by reverting it.
- **2026-11-07** · **A hidden to-top button is `inert`, not only transparent.** Opacity and pointer-events leave a control in the tab order, so focus landed on an invisible button. Landed in 716432b; undo by reverting it.
- **2026-11-07** · **The changelog generator rewrites em and en dashes to a colon and titles the page with the middle dot.** Hand edits would be undone by the next `pnpm changelog:sync`. Landed in e07908f; undo by reverting it.
- **2026-11-08** · **Every design-system page lists its sections under ON THIS PAGE, below 768px the list follows the intro, and the changelog groups entries under Added, Changed and Fixed.** The list was a block between the heading and the intro, so on a phone it pushed the intro off the first screen; `.ds-main` is now a column below 768px and `order` puts the first element after the list ahead of it (from 768px up the list keeps its row and the 1280px right rail). Components, enforcement and changelog had no list; the changelog list is generated from its dates. `scripts/lib/changelog-merge.ts` stores each entry as a kind (feat is Added, fix is Fixed, everything else Changed), drops the `(#123)`, `(T075 ...)` and `(PR-C)` references and capitalises the first word, so `pnpm changelog:sync` writes the grouped page and still reads the old `- **fix:**` lines. A swatch followed by prose now has a 12px gap under its rule (`.ds-swatch`). Held by `__tests__/scripts/changelog-merge.test.ts` and `tests/e2e/design-system-pages.spec.ts`. _Reversible: drop the `max-width: 767.98px` block of `app/css/components-design-system.css` and revert the generator; old entries re-sync from the file._
## 2026-11-07: /links mobile LCP budget

- **2026-11-07** · **`/links` ships only the two font faces its text uses: JetBrains Mono 400 and 700.** Root cause: Lighthouse's simulated LCP for an image element is the time to download everything that finished before the observed paint (document, CSS, fonts, scripts) at 1.47 Mbps, so the thumbnail's own load was not the long pole and the page sat 330 ms under the 3500 ms budget with CI runs at 3535 and 3556 ms. Two faces were dead weight there: `inter-900` (24 KB) because the `sr-only` h1 inherited the display face and laid out its text, and `jetbrains-mono-500` (21 KB, preloaded on every route) because one index chip in hottest takes was the only text at weight 500. `.sr-only` now sets the mono family, the 500 face is gone, and the chip renders at 400. The unreferenced `public/fonts/jetbrains-mono-500.woff2` file is deleted. Measured with `lhci` on a production build (mobile preset, simulated throttling): `/links` median 3172 ms over 6 runs before, 2875 ms over 8 after; `/links/en` 3168 ms before, 2881 ms after. Held by the `font payload` specs in `tests/e2e/links.spec.ts`. _Reversible: restore the 500 entry in `RootShell.tsx` and the chip weight, or drop the `.sr-only` family line; both revert in one commit._
- **2026-11-07** · **The latest-video cover on `/links` sets `fetchPriority="high"`.** It was fetched at the browser's default priority (Lighthouse `lcp-discovery`: `priorityHinted` false); the hint keeps it ahead of the Low priority script chunks in a real browser, with no effect on the simulated number since Lighthouse's model ignores priority. Held by `ChannelCard.test.tsx`. The cover is the LCP element only on taller phones: it starts 713 to 787 px down the page, so only the strip above the fold counts. Measured with a `PerformanceObserver` on a production build: 360x800 `/links` ends on the identity card's `dd` text (15K px²), 375x812 `/links` on the `dd` (17K px², 44 px of cover visible), 390x844 and 412x823 on the cover `IMG` (31K to 32K px²); Linux font metrics move the tie, so a spec pinning the cover as the LCP element failed in CI and was dropped rather than tuned to one viewport. Open owner decision: moving the channel card above the identity card on mobile would make the cover the LCP element at every phone height. _Reversible: delete the prop._
- **2026-11-07** · **Splitting `app/globals.css` by route is not done here.** The single stylesheet is 31 KB gzipped and `/links` needs about a third of it; Lighthouse's model prices each KB at about 5 ms of LCP, so a split would buy roughly 110 ms. It breaks the rule that the `@import` order is the cascade order and the home partials share classes with the design-system pages, so it needs its own change with a measured cascade check. _Reversible: nothing changed._
## 2026-11-07: solo-maintainer review model, blocking categories and the test-edit guard
