- **2026-11-07** · **`guard-merge-main` fails closed by intent: a command that carries a `gh` invocation together with a merge intent is refused unless every merge in it parses as a single `gh pr merge <selector>` or `gh api .../pulls/<n>/merge` whose base resolves to a branch other than `main` or `master`.** Root cause: the first version matched a command start against an allow list of spellings, and each review round found another spelling that exited 0: `\gh`, a path to the binary, a quoted `gh`, `-R` or `--repo` before the subcommand, a brace group, `if` and `for` bodies, `eval`, `env -i`, `sudo -u`, `xargs`, `gh api` to the `merges` endpoint or to an auto-merge mutation, and `GH_REPO` pointing at another repository (resolved against the working directory's repository). The hook now normalizes the command (backslashes, quotes and the path of the `gh` binary removed) and counts merge intents in the normalized text: `pr` with optional flags then `merge`, a `pulls/<n>/merge` path, a `merges` path, `mergePullRequest`, `enablePullRequestAutoMerge` and the merge-queue mutations. It then counts the merges that parse cleanly from the token stream: `gh` at the start of a command (only assignments and a bare `env`, `command`, `exec`, `time`, `nohup` or `sudo`, or a keyword such as `then`, `do` or `{`, may precede it), optional `-R` or `--repo`, then `pr merge` or `api` with a `pulls/<n>/merge` path. Each clean merge asks `gh pr view` for its base, in the directory a leading `cd` selects and in the repository named by `-R`, `--repo` or an earlier `GH_REPO` assignment; a selector, repository, `cd` target or `GH_REPO` only the shell can resolve is refused. Fewer clean merges than intents blocks, which covers every wrapper, the `merges` endpoint and the GraphQL mutations. A variable used as the command word before `pr merge` counts as `gh`. The GitHub MCP merge tool takes `owner`, `repo` and `pullNumber` from its input through the same lookup. Trade-off chosen: text that merely mentions a `gh` merge (a commit message, an `echo`, a `grep`) is refused too, so such text is reworded or kept out of a command that also holds `gh`; failing closed costs that inconvenience, and an allow list costs a bypass. Options weighed: keep extending the allow list (each round found a new bypass); deny every merge call (stops the sanctioned merge into `feat/*`); fail closed by intent (chosen). Limits: a merge assembled at run time or hidden in a script file has no intent text and is not seen, and the hook runs in Claude Code only. Held by `__tests__/scripts/guard-merge-main.test.ts` (every bypass above, red before the redesign) and the registration check in `__tests__/meta/ai-config.test.ts`. _Reversible: restore the previous script; the MCP hook entry can stay._
- **2026-11-07** · **`guard-test-edits` compares real paths: it resolves symlinks in both the project root and the edit target (the nearest existing parent for a new file).** Root cause: it compared the textual root with the textual path, so `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR=/tmp/x` against an edit at `/private/tmp/x/lib/a.test.ts` (the form macOS hands tools) read as outside the root and the edit passed, and an alias inside the project pointing at a test file passed too. Held by the symlink cases in `__tests__/scripts/guard-test-edits.test.ts` (red before the change). _Reversible: remove the `canonical` helper in `scripts/guard-test-edits.mjs`._
- **2026-11-07** · **The retired-name check also covers `ai-engineer`, `architect-reviewer`, `dx-optimizer`, `nextjs-developer`, `performance-engineer`, `seo-specialist` and `test-automator`; `STANDARDS.md` and the engineering-standards chapter name the review as a convention, not as an agent.** Root cause: the agents were pruned but two docs still told a reader to run them, and the guard listed only the earlier retired names. Held by `RETIRED_NAMES` in `__tests__/meta/ai-config.test.ts` (red on the old wording). _Reversible: drop the names from the list._
- **2026-11-07** · **The repository has one maintainer, so it does not meet OpenSSF Scorecard Code-Review or SLSA Source Level 4 (both need a second person who understands the change), and it does not chase either score.** `claude-review` is a model-judged sensor, not an approver: its output varies between runs on the same diff, it runs only when an owner comments `/claude-review`, it is not a required status check, and `pnpm claude-gate` is optional. The controls that stand in for a second reviewer, read from GitHub on 2026-11-07 (`gh api` on `branches/main/protection` and `rulesets`, read only, nothing changed): the active ruleset `Default` on the default branch has no bypass actors and blocks branch deletion and non-fast-forward pushes, requires a pull request (0 approving reviews, stale reviews dismissed on push), and requires `quality-fast`, `typecheck`, `test`, `build`, `detect-changes` and `ci-gate` to pass on a head that contains the base; classic protection on `main` also requires conversation resolution, with `enforce_admins` off and no required reviews or checks of its own. `quality-fast` runs `pnpm pr:gate` on every PR (every review thread resolved with a reply, `silent_resolve` flagged). `ci-gate` aggregates `detect-changes`, `quality-fast`, `typecheck`, `test`, `dependency-review`, `build`, `performance`, `e2e-functional`, `e2e-visual-chromium`, `ai-eval` and `gitleaks`; Semgrep and CodeQL report to code scanning and are not in it. `.github/CODEOWNERS` assigns the owner to the AI configuration paths, but the ruleset sets `require_code_owner_review` false, so it routes review requests and blocks nothing. "Agents never merge into `main`" is held by the `guard-merge-main` hook in Claude Code only, for any command that carries a `gh` merge it cannot read as a single merge into another base, and for the GitHub MCP merge tool; GitHub itself lets anyone with write access merge a PR whose checks pass, so Codex, the web UI and any merge the hook cannot see rely on a written rule. Not in force: a second human reviewer, a required approval, a required code-owner review, a model review acting as the approver. Options weighed: add a required model approval (non-deterministic blocker, and it would still not count for Scorecard, which excludes bot reviews); require one human approval (the only approver is the author, so it would be bypassed or self-approved); accept the gap and put the weight on deterministic gates, which Scorecard cannot see (chosen). _Reversible: this is a record; revert the entry to retract it. To close the gap, add a second maintainer, then set `required_approving_review_count` to 1 and `require_code_owner_review` to true in the ruleset (a settings change the owner makes on GitHub)._
- **2026-11-07** · **Only correctness, security, accessibility, performance budget, test validity and spec or plan compliance block a merge; style and naming never do, nits are capped at five, and the review loop stops after three rounds.** A reviewer told to find problems always finds something, and "loop until the bot approves" turns that into review theater (extra layers and tests for cases that cannot happen). Remaining non-blocking findings become follow-up issues named in the thread reply before the thread resolves, so `pnpm pr:gate` still passes; a blocking finding open after round three goes to the owner. Options weighed: approve-on-bot-verdict (makes the sensor an approver); an unbounded fix-everything loop (the status quo); severity-gated blocking with a round cap (chosen). Limit: nothing counts rounds mechanically; the categories and the cap are instructions in `REVIEW.md` (read by the CI review from the base branch) and `AGENTS.md`. _Reversible: delete the "What blocks" section of `REVIEW.md` and the matching lines in `AGENTS.md` and `docs/handbook/review-merge-release.md`._
- **2026-11-07** · **A pre-merge self-review by specialist reviewers is no longer a rule of this repository.** The repo's review gates are CI, `pnpm pr:gate` (every thread resolved with a reply) and claude-review on request; reading your own diff before opening the PR stays an author habit. It replaces the same-day entry that made a specialist self-review a process step before every merge. _Reversible: restore the removed self-review bullet in `AGENTS.md`, the "Self-review before merge" section of `REVIEW.md` and the handbook lines._
- **2026-11-07** · **A `PreToolUse` hook (`scripts/guard-test-edits.mjs`) refuses edits to existing test files while a fix task is declared; the declaration is `FIX_MODE=1` in the session environment or the gitignored marker `.claude/fix-mode`.** The failure it stops is an agent editing the test that fails instead of the code. It blocks `Edit`, `Write`, `MultiEdit` and `NotebookEdit` on existing `*.test|spec|e2e.*` files and anything under `tests/` or `__tests__/`, exits 2 and says to add a new test file and change the source; creating a new test file is allowed because that is how a fix adds its regression test. Options weighed: infer fix mode from the branch name (`fix/*` branches legitimately add tests and some `chore/` branches fix defects, so it would block the wrong work silently); a permission deny pattern (static, cannot depend on a mode); a CI check that fails when the assertion count drops (valid and tool-agnostic, but needs a base comparison and a label escape, so it is queued, not built here); an explicit trigger (chosen, because the person who declares a fix task is the one who can lift it). Limits: edits made through `Bash` (`sed -i`, redirects) are not seen, the agent can delete the marker, and Codex has no equivalent hook here, so `AGENTS.md` states the rule in prose for it. Held by `__tests__/scripts/guard-test-edits.test.ts` (stubbed hook input) and `__tests__/meta/ai-config.test.ts` (registration). _Reversible: delete the hook entry in `.claude/settings.json`, the script and its test, and the `.gitignore` line._
- **2026-11-07** · **Claims that a check enforces a rule were checked against the mechanism, and two were false.** `.claude/rules/api-boundary.md` said the hard gates on the API surface were enforced by hooks (no hook does) and named Semgrep and CodeQL as hard gates (Semgrep runs with `continue-on-error` and neither is in `ci-gate` or the ruleset); both now say the behavioral tests are the only blocking check there. `AGENTS.md` said INP under 200ms is checked in browser tests; the Lighthouse INP audit is off and the one guard is the shell-input keystroke check, so it now says that. The Lighthouse thresholds, which "never disable a gate" covered only in prose, are now pinned by `__tests__/lighthouse-budgets.test.ts` (red when a score floor is lowered or an audit is set to warn or off). _Reversible: restore the earlier wording; delete the test to drop the pin._

## 2026-11-07: AI configuration shared by Claude Code and Codex

- **2026-11-07** · **`AGENTS.md` is the single source of project instructions; `CLAUDE.md` is an `@AGENTS.md` import plus the Claude-only lines; skills live in `.agents/skills` with a relative per-skill symlink in `.claude/skills`; no AI file names another.** Options weighed: a `CLAUDE.md` symlink to `AGENTS.md` (Claude Code's Edit and Write refuse to write through a symlink, and Claude-only lines would have no home); two copies (they drift); a plugin (heavier than one repository needs); keeping skills only under `.claude/skills` (Codex reads `.agents/skills`, and neither tool documents reading the other's directory). Persona agents were removed because the documentation endorses only tool-restricted, repeated-use agents, so `review-worker` (Read, Grep, Glob, dispatched by the CI review) is the one agent; the repo-specific parts of seven personas became `plan-review`, `perf-budget` and `seo-discovery`, and the rest restated gates. The `/commit` command was dropped because commitlint and the commit-msg hook enforce the convention. Rules with no exception became permission deny rules and a Stop hook (`scripts/stop-verify.mjs`, about 0.5s warm, skipped in CI); the prose remains for Codex, which has no hook here. `pr-review-orchestrator` and `semgrep` set `disable-model-invocation`, a Claude Code key outside the Agent Skills specification, mirrored for Codex by `agents/openai.yaml`; the cost is that claude.ai uploads of those two skills may reject the key. The CI review looks for its skill under `.agents/skills` and then `.claude/skills` because `git archive` of a symlink path fails and the default branch holds the old layout until this lands. Held by `__tests__/meta/ai-config.test.ts`, `__tests__/claude-review-ci.test.ts`, `__tests__/scripts/stop-verify.test.ts` and `pnpm check:harness-size`. _Reversible: `git revert` the four commits; the CI step then needs only the `.claude/skills` candidate._
- **2026-11-07** · **`gh pr merge` is no longer a blanket permission deny; a `PreToolUse` hook blocks it only when the PR base is `main`, `master`, or cannot be read.** The owner's rule is that agents never merge into `main` but may merge sub-PRs into an integration branch (`feat/*`) once CI is green (Argos excepted) and review is done; a permission pattern matches command text and cannot see the PR base, so the deny rule blocked the sanctioned workflow too. Options weighed: keep the deny and have the owner merge every sub-PR (blocks the integration-branch workflow); allow-list `gh pr merge` with no guard (nothing stops a merge into `main`); a hook that asks `gh pr view <selector> --json baseRefName` and exits 2 on `main`. The hook fails closed: gh missing, offline, or an unknown PR blocks with the reason stated, because an unreadable base cannot be shown to be safe. Limit: it inspects `gh pr merge` command text only, so `gh api` merge calls and the GitHub UI are outside it; branch protection on `main` remains the backstop and its live state needs separate inspection. Held by `__tests__/scripts/guard-merge-main.test.ts` (stubbed `gh`) and `__tests__/meta/ai-config.test.ts`. _Reversible: restore the deny entry and delete the hook entry and `scripts/guard-merge-main.mjs`._
## 2026-11-07: Next's managed agent rules are committed in AGENTS.md

- **2026-11-07** · **`next dev` rewrote `AGENTS.md` whenever it detected a coding agent, so the managed `nextjs-agent-rules` block is committed verbatim and `experimental.agentFeedback` is pinned to `false`.** `syncAgentRulesForDev` appends the block unless `hasCurrentAgentRules()` is true, which dirtied the tree on every agent session; `agentRules: false` would have removed Next's version-matched docs pointer. The block was generated with Next's own `writeAgentFiles`, adds 10 lines (budget 200), and keeps Next's em dash because the match is byte-exact. `__tests__/meta/ai-config.test.ts` calls Next's `hasCurrentAgentRules` on the repo root and fails on a Next upgrade that changes the block (regenerate with `node -e "require('next/dist/server/lib/generate-agent-files').writeAgentFiles(process.cwd())"` after deleting the old block). Reversible: set `agentRules: false` and delete the block.
## 2026-11-07: Ask hardening (cache bound, MCP caller identity, log minimization, guard monitoring)

- **2026-11-07** · **The prompt-cache test measures a conservative token lower bound against Haiku 4.5's 4096-token minimum, not a 3500-character proxy.** The old assertion called 3500 characters a proxy for 1024 tokens, but the Anthropic prompt-caching docs set the minimum cacheable prefix for `claude-haiku-4-5` at 4096 tokens, so a prompt of 3600 characters (about 900 tokens) passed the test while the `cacheControl` marker did nothing, silently and without an error. `lib/ask/cache-eligibility.ts` takes `floor(chars / 5)` as the token count (5 characters per token is above any ordinary English tokenization, so the bound can only under-count) and `lib/ask/__tests__/cache-eligibility.test.ts` holds `SYSTEM_TEXT` to it. `SYSTEM_TEXT` is 21,589 characters, a lower bound of 4317 tokens, so the marker is live today; the earlier "probably under the minimum" note predates the prompt growing. Rejected: calling the Anthropic token-count endpoint from the test (network in a unit test, and a key in CI), and dropping the marker (it is free when ignored and pays off once above the minimum). Known cost: a prompt trimmed under 20,480 characters fails the test even if its real token count would still clear 4096; that is the conservative direction. Not held: whether the 5-minute TTL sees enough traffic to read the cache, which only `cacheReadTokens` in production logs shows. _Reversible: delete `cache-eligibility.ts` and restore the character assertion; the test then passes on a prompt that cannot cache._
- **2026-11-07** · **The ask log redacts email addresses and phone numbers before it stores a question or an answer.** The log kept up to 500 characters of the visitor's question and 1000 of the answer for 90 days with no scrub, and visitors paste their contact details into a question about hiring. `redactPii` (`lib/ask/redact-pii.ts`) replaces an email (`EMAIL_RE`, Unicode letters allowed) and a phone number (three to six digit groups split by one space, dot or hyphen with at least nine digits in all, or a bare run of 10 to 15 digits) with `[redacted-email]` and `[redacted-phone]`. It runs on the whole text, never on text already cut (see the 2026-11-07 note on the truncation boundary below). The model still receives the original question, and `requestId` and FORGET are untouched. Rejected: redacting names (no pattern separates a name from a project or employer name without a model), and scrubbing at read time (the raw text would still sit in Redis). Known cost: an IPv4 address matches the phone shape and is redacted too; a date, a year range and a version number do not. Held by `lib/ask/__tests__/redact-pii.test.ts` (en and pt-BR formats, plus strings that must pass unchanged) and `__tests__/ask-log-persistence.test.ts`, red before the fix. _Reversible: remove the two `redactPii` calls in `lib/ask-log.ts`; raw text is stored again._
- **2026-11-07** · **The ask log redacts the whole question and the whole answer, then cuts, and the settlement runs before the counters.** Root cause: the question was cut to 500 characters in `preflightAsk` and the collected answer to 1000 in `createAskStream`, and only then did `persistAskInteraction` call `redactPii`, so an email or phone number straddling either earlier cut reached the log as `jane.doe@` or `11 99999-9`, which no pattern matches. The note above claimed the redaction ran before the cut; it ran before the last of three cuts. Options weighed: (a) strip a trailing partial-address fragment after the cut, which needs a second heuristic for every shape `redactPii` knows and still misses a phone head; (b) redact what the model receives, which changes the dedup hash and the injection input; (c) hand the log the untruncated text. Chosen: (c). `AskContext.fullQuestion` carries the trimmed question (the body is already bounded to 8 KB) and the answer is collected whole (the stream guard aborts past 4000 characters), so `persistAskInteraction` redacts first and cuts to 500 and 1000 last. The model still receives the question cut to 500. Layer 2 now audits the whole collected answer instead of its first 1000 characters, which only widens what it can see. The same pass reordered settlement: the budget refund and the log write come first and `countAskGuardFinding` last, because the platform can end an `after()` task early and money should not wait behind a counter. A synchronous throw in `createAskStream` after `reserveBudget` now refunds the whole reservation and answers 500 with the `interrupted` line, where it used to leave the month charged for a stream that never existed. The counter helpers and `persistAskInteraction` log `describeError(err)`, not the raw error, because a client error can carry the command it sent. Held by `__tests__/ask-pii-truncation.test.ts`, `__tests__/ask-settle-after-response.test.ts`, `__tests__/ask-stream-start-failure.test.ts`, `lib/ask/guard-count.test.ts`, `lib/ask/refusal-count.test.ts` and `__tests__/ask-log-persistence.test.ts`, each red before the fix. _Reversible: restore the two `slice` calls in `preflightAsk` and `createAskStream` and drop `fullQuestion`; straddling contact details are stored again._
- **2026-11-07** · **`ask_erik` forwards the MCP caller's address to `/api/ask`, marks failures as tool errors and returns the request id.** Root cause of three faults in one place: the tool built a synthetic `Request` with no address header, so every MCP caller keyed to `'unknown'`, shared one ask bucket and one 60-second duplicate check (a second agent asking the same question was refused as a duplicate); a refusal, a 4xx or a stream error came back as ordinary text, so an agent could not tell an answer from a failure; and the request id was dropped, so a stored exchange could not be forgotten. The tool callback now reads `ctx.http.req`, copies `x-forwarded-for` and `x-real-ip` onto the synthetic request, sets `isError` for a non-2xx status or an error sentinel in the stream (the partial text stays), and returns the id from `X-Request-Id` in `_meta.requestId` plus a second text block naming `/api/log/forget`. This supersedes the 2026-05-21 "known, accepted limitation". The cost cap is unchanged: `/api/mcp` keeps its own per-IP limit and the monthly and daily token budgets still bound spend. Known cost: the ask limit now gives each MCP caller its own 8 an hour instead of sharing 8 worldwide, so one site-wide abuser is bounded by the budgets, not the bucket. Rejected: a fixed per-tool identity (keeps the shared bucket and the false duplicate), and an error code in the text (agents read `isError`). Held by `__tests__/mcp-ask-tool.test.ts`, red before the fix. _Reversible: drop the header copy in `runAsk`; callers share one bucket again._
- **2026-11-07** · **The output guard is monitoring, and a Layer-1 abort is now recorded as a finding and counted.** Reproduced: when the stream guard aborted an answer for a leak marker, the aborted chunk was never added to the collected text, so the Layer-2 `validateAnswer` ran on a clean prefix and the persisted verdict said `clean: true` for the one answer the guard had just cut. The review's other claim, that Layer 2 sees only the first 1000 characters, changes nothing: Layer 1 inspects every chunk against the same markers and aborts past 4000 characters, so Layer 2 cannot find a verbatim marker that Layer 1 missed. The real limit is that markers are verbatim English plus pt-BR translations, so a paraphrased or translated leak passes both layers. The route now keeps the abort reason and merges it into the persisted verdict (`withStreamAbort`, kind `leak` or `length`), and every finding adds to `ask:guard:{date}:{kind}` (`lib/ask/guard-count.ts`, 32-day TTL, same shape as `ask:refused:*`). Docs state that the guards are monitoring. Rejected: extending Layer 2 to the full answer (no new coverage, since the marker set is the same), and a semantic leak classifier (a model call per answer on the cheapest layer). Held by `__tests__/ask-output-guard-behavioral.test.ts` (red before the fix) and `lib/ask/guard-count.test.ts`. _Reversible: remove `withStreamAbort` and the counter call in `lib/ask/stream.ts`; the persisted verdict reads clean after an abort again._
- **2026-11-07** · **`/api/ask` is split into `preflightAsk` and `createAskStream`; `route.ts` only composes them.** The handler was one 300-line function holding about twenty mutable closure variables, so each rejection path (kill switch, origin, limit, parse, injection, duplicate, budget) could be tested only by driving a full stream, and settlement shared mutable state with the pump loop. `lib/ask/preflight.ts` returns the rejection `Response` or an `AskContext`; `lib/ask/stream.ts` owns the model call, the pump, the watchdog and settlement, with its state in one `RunState` record. Behaviour is unchanged: every route test passed untouched; the two tests that read source files for `ASK_MODEL` were replaced by behaviour checks (a stand-in `ASK_MODEL` must reach `streamText`, and the eval result must carry the model every call used). The `streamText` options live in `createAskStream`, so a change to a model option is one edit there. Rejected: moving the order into `defineHandler` (the stream response does not fit its JSON envelope), and a class per request (indirection with no gain). Held by `lib/ask/__tests__/preflight.test.ts` (each rejection path with no model call) and the existing route tests. _Reversible: inline both modules back into `handleAsk`; no behaviour changes._
## 2026-11-07: eval publishing is opt-in

- **2026-11-07** · **Eval aggregates publish to Redis only when `EVAL_PUBLISH=1` is set, and runs without it drop every Redis credential from `process.env` at start.** Root cause: `lib/rate-limit.ts` accepts the Vercel-injected `KV_REST_API_*` pair as Redis credentials and `publishAggregate` published whenever any pair existed, so a local `vercel env run -- pnpm ask:eval` overwrote the production `ask:eval:latest`; the in-process route also wrote ask logs and rate-limit counters there. Options weighed: skip only the publish (rejected: route writes still hit production), name the production env pair in a denylist (rejected: breaks when the injected names change). The flag is set only in `ask-eval.yml` and `agent-eval.yml`, held by `lib/eval/__tests__/publish-gate.test.ts`, which also asserts `ci.yml` never sets it. Reversible: remove the flag check.

## 2026-11-07: Least-privilege workflow tokens

- **2026-11-07** · **Every workflow declares a top-level `permissions` block no broader than `contents: read` (`{}` for `smoke.yml`, which uses no token); extra scopes live on the one job that needs them.** `codeql.yml` and `smoke.yml` had no top-level block, and `claude.yml` and `claude-review.yml` granted write scopes (`contents`, `pull-requests`, `issues`, `id-token`) to the whole workflow, which OpenSSF Scorecard Token-Permissions flags and which would widen silently when a job is added. Held by `__tests__/scripts/workflow-permissions.test.ts`. Reversible: revert the YAML move and the test; no scope was added or removed, only relocated (`smoke.yml` drops an unused `deployments: read`).
## 2026-11-07: Playwright flakes fixed at the root, then `failOnFlakyTests` on

- **2026-11-07** · **A CI run fails when a test passes only on a retry (`failOnFlakyTests: !!process.env.CI`); retries stay at two so a failure still leaves a trace.** Recent green runs reported 1 to 6 flaky tests each because a retry turned a real race into a pass. Locally nothing retries, so a flake fails on first sight. Held by `__tests__/scripts/playwright-flaky-policy.test.ts`. Lands in c8ceb14. _Reversible: delete the config line and the test._
- **2026-11-07** · **Specs that read streamed content navigate with `gotoSettled` (`tests/e2e/_helpers/streamed.ts`), which waits until no Suspense boundary is pending.** Root cause: `page.goto` resolves at `load`, and the swap of a streamed boundary is a separate step after it, so the DOM can still hold the fallback plus a hidden staged copy, and the swap removes the fallback. Reproduced by delaying every swap by up to 500ms: the unpatched specs failed with the CI errors (`NaN`, `Element is not attached to the DOM`), the patched ones passed. A spec that measured in that window got `NaN` from `getComputedStyle` on a detached node (`type-scale.spec.ts:28` and `:98`), a link that moved under `scrollIntoViewIfNeeded` (`touch-targets.spec.ts:16`), a `::before` content of `''` read as a pseudo element (`type-reveal.spec.ts:71` and `:89`), a `null` bounding box (`links-layout.spec.ts:50`) and a thumbnail count of zero (`links-thumbs.spec.ts:32`). Options weighed: waiting on `aria-busy` alone (rejected: hydration clears it while the staged copy is still in the DOM), raising the retries (hides it), and a CPU-throttled run (does not reproduce on macOS). Pending means `template[id^="B:"]`, `[hidden][id^="S:"]` or a fallback marked `aria-busy`. Held by `tests/e2e/streamed-settle.spec.ts`, which holds every swap and asserts the helper stays pending. Lands in c8ceb14. _Reversible: replace `gotoSettled` with `page.goto` in the six specs._
- **2026-11-07** · **The INP guard counts only long tasks that start after its observer is installed, once the main thread is idle.** Root cause: the observer used `buffered: true`, so a 115ms task from page load (`cross-cutting.spec.ts` test 4, `chromium` and `chromium-mobile`) was charged to a keystroke that came later. Held by test 4b, which blocks the main thread for 150ms before the keystroke. Lands in c8ceb14. _Reversible: restore `buffered: true`._
- **2026-11-07** · **`ContactTimezone` reads the message field id through `useSyncExternalStore`, and `messageStore.bindField` notifies its subscribers.** Root cause: the id was read during render from a module variable, so a visitor who proposed a slot before the contact form island bound its field saw the confirmation but never the "edit message" link (`contact-timezone.spec.ts:176`). The two islands hydrate separately, so the order is not fixed. Held by `ContactTimezone.test.tsx` ("offers the link as soon as the form field mounts") and `messageStore.test.ts`. Lands in c8ceb14. _Reversible: read `getFieldId()` directly again._
- **2026-11-07** · **Focusing a control in the phone's sticky bars no longer scrolls the page.** Root cause: `html` carries `scroll-padding-top: calc(var(--mobile-bars-height) + var(--spacing-3))` (106px) so anchors land below the bars, and the bars are `position: sticky`. A scroll into view (keyboard Tab or `focus()`, `scrollIntoView`, Playwright's `locator.click()`) therefore sees a control at y=38 as inside the reserved band and centres it in the padded scrollport: 439 - 60 = 379px, constant at 390px wide whatever the scroll offset (logged: `scrollY` 5104 to 4725 at pointerdown, 3000 to 2621 on `focus()`, all four bar controls; desktop is unaffected because its top bar is `position: fixed`, which Chrome never scrolls into view). A pointer press never calls scroll-into-view, so mouse users were not affected; keyboard users were, and the locale spec followed a link whose section hash had changed mid-click. The earlier workaround (`page.mouse.click` at the box) skipped Playwright's actionability checks and hid the defect. Fix: `.mobile-title-bar` and `.status-bar` controls take `scroll-margin-top: calc(-1 * var(--mobile-bars-height))`, which moves their snap area out of the reserved band without touching anchor landing (anchors still land at 106px). Options weighed: lowering the html scroll-padding (breaks anchor landing), making the bars `fixed` (changes layout and stacking for every phone page), keeping the mouse workaround (leaves the keyboard defect). Held by `tests/e2e/sticky-bar-focus.spec.ts` (red before at 3000 to 2621, green after) and `locale-switch-position.spec.ts`, which now presses with `locator.click()`. _Reversible: remove the rule._
- **2026-11-07** · **Four specs wait on the state they assert instead of reading once.** `mobile-reflow.spec.ts:111` polls the button gap after `setViewportSize` (CI read a gap of 0 at 1280, the phone layout), `links.spec.ts:144` polls the tagline while advancing the fake clock (the typer starts after hydration, so a fixed six samples can all precede it), `Footer.e2e.ts` repeats the command inside `toPass` (lesson 31) and compares the reboot total with the total it read, and `locale-switch-position.spec.ts` waits for the section and the switch href to stop changing, then presses the switch with `locator.click()` (cause and product fix in the next bullet). Only the first, the third and the fourth reproduced in a local run: the others are fixed from the CI error and the code path, not from a local failure. Lands in c8ceb14. _Reversible: restore the single reads._
- **2026-11-07** · **`redis-missing-env.test.ts` asserts that the dead client rejects without a timer, not that it rejects within 50ms.** The wall-clock budget failed at 107ms under parallel load and passed alone. Under fake timers a retry delay never fires, so the old behaviour (a backoff before the rejection) hangs the test and leaves a pending timer; a negative control that delays the rejection by 100ms times the new test out. The scan of `__tests__` and the co-located unit tests found no other assertion on a real elapsed time: `contact-retry-deadline.test.ts` and `frame-governor.test.ts` run on fake clocks. Lands in c8ceb14. _Reversible: restore the `performance.now()` budget._
- **2026-11-07** · **Two unit tests no longer depend on the clock.** `ContactTimezoneLazy.test.tsx` preloads the lazy module (a 1s `findBy` raced a cold transform; with the import delayed 1.5s the old test failed and the new one passes), `InteractiveShell.test.tsx` fills the log with `whoami` instead of `help` (1.5s instead of 5.2s of work, plus a named 30s ceiling). Lands in c8ceb14. _Reversible: restore the earlier tests._
- **2026-11-07** · **One flake is not fixed: a `webkit-mobile` `page.goto` that waited 30s for `load` on `design-system-pages.spec.ts:283`.** Seen once in 40 runs and not reproduced in 108 local runs on macOS WebKit; the same route loaded in about a second in the four tests before it. It is an engine stall, not a race in a spec, so `failOnFlakyTests` can surface it again. If it recurs, read the trace of the retry before changing the spec. Lands in c8ceb14. _Reversible: not applicable, nothing changed._
- **2026-11-08** · **`gotoSettled` navigates to `domcontentloaded`, and waits for the declared self-hosted font faces itself.** Root cause of the recurring 30s `page.goto` timeout (CI run 42477033404, `webkit-mobile`, `/`; the retry passed in 1.5s and the other worker kept passing against the same server): WebKit holds `load` for as long as any one subresource has not answered, and a probe that stalled `site.webmanifest`, a font or a script chunk reproduced it on WebKit while DOMContentLoaded fired in about 270ms (a stalled stylesheet blocks DOMContentLoaded too, and stays a real failure). `document.fonts.ready` is no substitute, because WebKit leaves it pending until `load`, so the helper awaits `FontFace.load()` of every declared face instead. Options weighed: raising the 30s timeout (waits longer for a page that was ready at 270ms), more retries (hides it; `failOnFlakyTests` exists to prevent that), changing the wait (chosen). Held by `tests/e2e/streamed-settle.spec.ts`, which stalls the manifest and a low-priority script chunk and asserts the helper returns within five seconds with a laid-out page and loaded fonts (red on the old helper at 30s). The font wait covers only faces whose `src` is a `url()`: next/font also declares `src: local(Arial)` size-adjust fallback faces, which end in `error` on a runner without that font (the Ubuntu runner has no Arial) and are not a defect, so neither the wait nor the stall cases' `status === 'loaded'` check count them. A stalled font is not guarded: it is a real dependency of the measurement, and the helper fails it after the settle timeout. A spec that needs decoded images passes `{ waitUntil: 'load' }`. The earlier `load` also implied hydration in practice; `domcontentloaded` does not, because the Next chunks are async, so a click on a server-rendered button can land before React attaches its handlers and is lost. Options weighed: waiting for hydration inside `gotoSettled` (hangs again on the stalled chunk this entry guards), a wait per interaction (chosen: `gotoHydrated` waits for the one control the spec uses; held by the delayed-chunk case in `tests/e2e/streamed-settle.spec.ts`, red with plain `gotoSettled`). _Reversible: restore `page.goto(path, options)` in `gotoSettled`._
- **2026-11-08** · **The Playwright trace stays `on-first-retry`; `retain-on-failure` was tried for the first failing attempt and reverted.** A trace of every attempt records a DOM snapshot per action, and the action-heavy `Footer.e2e.ts` site-map test went from 2.0s to 8.0s locally (`--trace=off` against `--trace=retain-on-failure`, `webkit-mobile-components`, three runs each) and from 11 to 13s to 28 to 30s on the CI runner, past the 30s test timeout (CI run 42484691770, three attempts: 30.4s, 30.7s, then 27.9s on the last retry). The timeout fired on whatever line was running, `expect(tree.locator('.sm-groups')).toBeHidden()`, which is not a hydration race: that test uses a plain `page.goto` and none of the helpers changed. The request log that would name a stalled request cannot be had cheaper: with `snapshots: false` the trace's `.network` file is empty, and with `snapshots` on the cost stays (`screenshots: false, sources: false` left the Footer test at 7.0s). Options weighed: raising the test timeout (hides the cost on every spec), splitting the Footer test by viewport (treats one spec, leaves every other action-heavy spec near its budget), tracing every attempt (chosen against), keeping `on-first-retry` (chosen). Held by `__tests__/scripts/playwright-flaky-policy.test.ts`. _Reversible: set `use.trace` to `'retain-on-failure'` and split or time out the specs that exceed 30s._
## 2026-11-07: weekly delivery metrics

- **2026-11-07** · **One weekly workflow measures delivery and opens an issue when a metric leaves its band; its history is a workflow artifact, not a file in the repository.** Six numbers are recorded per complete week: successful `Production` deployments, the share of production deployments that failed, median lead time (first commit to merge), median PR cycle time (opened to merged), the share of `ci.yml` runs on `main` that failed, and flaky tests per `e2e-*` job read from Playwright's `N flaky` summary in the job logs. Each is scored against the mean and sample standard deviation of its previous 12 weeks (6 needed first); 1, 2 and 3 sigma on the bad side are the log, diagnose and propose tiers, and the Western Electric run rules (2 of 3 beyond 2 sigma, 4 of 5 beyond 1 sigma, 8 on one side) catch a sustained drift. An issue opens from `diagnose` up, one per metric, found again through a hidden marker, and later breaches comment on it. Detection is arithmetic over API data and calls no model. Options weighed for storage: (a) commit `metrics/weekly.json` through a pull request (an Actions-created PR does not trigger CI, so it could never pass the required checks, and it needs `contents: write` and `pull-requests: write`), (b) push to an unprotected data branch (needs `contents: write` and a ruleset check we cannot read without admin), (c) `actions/cache` (evicted after 7 days unused, immutable keys), (d) the previous run's artifact (chosen: 90 days of retention renewed weekly, only `actions: read`, and a missing artifact self-heals because the API returns 90 days of history for a 13-week backfill). Options weighed for the signal: a dashboard (rejected, the DORA guide warns against measuring at the expense of improving and nothing here is read daily), blocking merges on a band (rejected, it turns a metric into a target), and counting flaky tests raw (rejected, the count follows how many runs happened, so the band uses the rate per job). Lighthouse LCP per route is not collected: LHCI uploads to `temporary-public-storage` and CI keeps no result file, so each week records the reason; storing it needs a `ci.yml` upload step. Lead time and cycle time cover every merged PR by a person, including those into the integration branch, because `main` receives few merges. Held by `scripts/__tests__/metrics-bands.test.ts` (rules and tiers on fixed series), `scripts/__tests__/metrics-collect.test.ts` (parsing over recorded API payloads), `scripts/__tests__/metrics-issues.test.ts`, `scripts/__tests__/metrics-github.test.ts` and `__tests__/scripts/weekly-metrics-workflow.test.ts` (token scope, pinned actions, no secret but `GITHUB_TOKEN`). _Reversible: delete `weekly-metrics.yml`, `scripts/metrics/` and `metrics/`; nothing else reads them, and the open `metrics-breach` issues can be closed by hand._

- **2026-11-07** · **The metrics job fails or warns on missing data instead of recording a quiet week.** Options weighed: tolerate gaps and rely on the bands (rejected: a week with no data scores as no-data, which reads as healthy), fail on every gap (rejected: one unreadable log or an empty `Production` range is information, not a reason to lose the other five metrics), fail on gaps that make a number wrong and warn on the rest (chosen). Fail: a search or run list at the 1000-result cap or reporting more than it returned (each week is queried alone so the cap is reachable only in a window that is itself too large), a missing history artifact when an earlier run succeeded (only the first run may start empty), and a week whose e2e logs all failed to download. Warn: no value for the latest week, unreadable or summary-less logs (excluded from the flaky rate), expired logs, and no finished `Production` deployment in the whole range. A week that scored diagnose or worse is excluded from later baselines so a second spike is not hidden by the first, which also means a lasting shift keeps reporting until the cause is fixed. Eight points on the bad side is diagnose, the tier that opens an issue. 2-of-3 and 4-of-5 need the latest point among the counted ones. Only reads are retried after a network error or 5xx, and secondary rate limits are waited out up to 60 seconds. Held by `scripts/__tests__/metrics-collect.test.ts`, `metrics-bands.test.ts`, `metrics-github.test.ts`, `metrics-issues.test.ts` and `__tests__/scripts/weekly-metrics-workflow.test.ts` (download step has no `continue-on-error`, history file verified, step order). _Reversible: restore the earlier collector and workflow; recorded history files stay readable because the added `logsExpired` count is optional to readers._

## 2026-11-07: API abuse bounds (budget slice, refusal rows, log caps, body order)

- **2026-11-07** · **`/api/ask` reserves against a daily slice of one twentieth of the monthly token budget, as well as the month.** One IP at the 8-an-hour limit spends about 860,000 tokens a day, so the 3,000,000 monthly budget lasted about 3.5 days and every visitor then got 503 until the key expired. A slice of 150,000 tokens (`ask:tokens:day:{date}`, 2-day TTL) caps one day at about a twentieth of the month, so an attacker costs one day of answers at a time. Options weighed: a global hourly limiter (still lets the month go in a few days of sustained load), a per-IP daily token cap (one IP bucket is `unknown` for MCP callers, so it cannot separate them), a daily slice (chosen, no new identity needed). Twenty rather than thirty days leaves headroom for a quiet-week traffic spike. The daily reservation is returned with the monthly one when either denies, and settlement refunds both keys. Held by `__tests__/rate-limit-budget-store.test.ts` and `__tests__/budget-cap.test.ts`. _Reversible: drop the daily reservation in `reserveBudget`; the monthly key is unchanged._
- **2026-11-07** · **A refused `/api/ask` request (rate-limited, duplicate, budget-exhausted) writes no `ask:log:*` row; it logs `ask refused` and adds to a per-day counter `ask:refused:{date}:{status}`.** The 2026-11-06 decision removed rows for injection and invalid-body rejections because any caller can mint them in bulk, and the same reason applies to these three: after the 8th request in an hour every further POST wrote a 90-day row. The counter keeps the abuse signal in two Redis commands and one key per status per day. `AskInteractionStatus` now lists only statuses that reach the model, and `X-Request-Id` on a refusal names no stored row, which `privacy forget` already treats as harmless. Held by `__tests__/ask-rejected-not-persisted.test.ts`. _Reversible: call `persistAskInteraction` again in the three refusal branches._
- **2026-11-07** · **`/api/log` caps each client at 50 records a day before the shared 500, stores at most 4096 characters of `stack`, and keeps records 7 days.** One IP filled the shared cap in about 50 minutes and muted every real report for the day, and a record could hold about 20 KB for 30 days. The per-IP check runs first so a capped client does not spend the shared budget. The schema still accepts a 16,000-character stack so a long report is shortened rather than refused. Held by `__tests__/api-log-shape.test.ts`. _Reversible: change `ERROR_LOG_IP_DAILY_CAP`, `MAX_STORED_STACK_CHARS` and `ERR_KV_TTL_S`._
- **2026-11-07** · **Budget settlement and the ask-log write run from `afterResponse`, a wrapper over `after()` from `next/server`, not after `controller.close()`.** Work started after the stream closes can be cut off when the platform ends the invocation with the response, which leaves the worst-case reservation on the budget and lets a quick `privacy forget` run before the row exists. The wrapper registers one callback that waits for the stream to end, then settles and persists. It runs the task directly when `after()` has no request scope (unit tests), which is the behaviour before this change. A stream that ends before its first token, by client abort or timeout, now keeps the input tokens charged and refunds the output share; the AI SDK reports an abort through `onAbort`, not `onError`, so the old failed-before-output check never matched it. Held by `__tests__/ask-settle-after-response.test.ts` and `__tests__/ask-client-disconnect.test.ts`. _Reversible: call `settleAndPersist` directly in the stream `finally`._
- **2026-11-07** · **Body-reading routes read their body after the cross-site check and the rate limiter, not before.** `withBodyLimit` buffered up to the cap before `defineHandler` ran the limiter, so a slow-drip sender held a function instance before any limit applied. `defineHandler` now takes a required `maxBodyBytes` and reads through `readBoundedBody`; `/api/ask` reads in its handler after its limiter; `/api/mcp` keeps `withRateLimit(withBodyLimit(...))`. The declared `Content-Length` check also moved behind the limiter, so a 413 consumes a token. Options weighed: wrapping `withRateLimit` around each route (the limiter would run twice with `defineHandler`'s own), or a lazy body stream (the 413 would become a parse error). Held by `__tests__/api-body-limits.test.ts`. _Reversible: wrap the three handlers in `withBodyLimit` again._
- **2026-11-07** · **A contact email can duplicate when a send that reached Resend is retried more than 24 hours later; accepted.** Resend idempotency keys expire after 24 hours, the retry cron runs daily, and the 10-second client timeout can fire after Resend accepted the message. The cost is one duplicate email to the owner; recording the Resend id on timeout would need a read the provider does not give for a timed-out call. _Reversible: store the Resend email id when a response arrives and check it before a retry._
## 2026-11-07: /api/ask injection gate and eval harness

- **2026-11-07** · **Every page starts one section gap below the header, and the 404 and route error content is centered.** Measured at 1280x720: the 404 started 3px under the fixed topbar (`.err-main` had no topbar offset) and `/summary`, `/links`, `/design-system` 23-24px; at 390 they sat 24px under the title bar. `--space-page-top` now equals `--space-section` (32px below 768, 48px from 768) and `.page-container.page-main` and `.err-main` add `--topbar-height` from 768 up. `.err-main` centers its text and column and, with `min-height` of the viewport minus the bars and dock, its block vertically; the terminal panel keeps left-aligned command lines because centered shell output reads as broken. Rejected: a per-page padding, which drifts. Held by `tests/e2e/shared-header.spec.ts` (first content block at least 48px desktop and 32px phone below the header on ten routes; 404 heading and button within 2px of the page center) and `tests/e2e/layout-tokens.spec.ts`. Reversible: restore `--space-page-top` to `--spacing-6` and drop the `.err-main` rules.
- **2026-11-07** · **The `/api/ask` injection gate stays a gate, but it no longer refuses a question that only mentions an override phrase.** Root cause: `INJECTION_RE` matched the phrase anywhere, so "How do you defend against 'ignore previous instructions' attacks?", "Can I ignorar as regras de lint?", "Desenvolvedor: senior?" and "Sistema: Angular?" returned HTTP 400, and the first of these is the question this site's positioning most invites. Options weighed: (a) log-only, because the sentinel wrapper and the output guard already contain injection; (b) a narrower pattern plus mention handling; (c) a model classifier. Chosen: (b). Log-only would turn every blocked attempt into a paid model call, and a classifier adds a call and a failure mode to the cheapest layer. `preflightAsk` now calls `isInjectionAttempt`, which removes quoted spans (straight, curly and guillemet double quotes, backticks, single quotes that are not apostrophes) and the unquoted form "against/about/sobre/contra + phrase + attacks/ataques" before matching. The pt "regras" and "diretrizes" alternates need "suas/seus" or a trailing "anteriores/acima/do sistema", because "ignorar as regras de lint" is ordinary. A role word and colon followed by a fragment of up to four words ending in "?" is a label question, not a role line. `normalizeInjectionInput` also folds Cyrillic and Greek lookalike letters into Latin, so "ign\u043Ere all previous instructions" is caught. Known cost: wrapping a real override in quotes, or prefixing it with "against" and ending it with "attacks", now passes layer 0, so the gate stays a heuristic and the sentinel wrapper and output guard remain the boundary. Held by `lib/ask/__tests__/injection.test.ts` (benign en and pt-BR questions must pass, override phrasings and lookalike spellings must be caught) and `__tests__/ask-eval-corpus.test.ts` (corpus items that mention a phrase must not trip the gate). _Reversible: restore the single `INJECTION_RE.test(normalizeInjectionInput(question))` call in `lib/ask/preflight.ts`; benign questions that mention an override phrase then return 400 again._
- **2026-11-07** · **Every fact about Erik that the ask system prompt states lives in `content/ask-facts.ts`, and the judge fact sheet reads the same module.** Root cause: the stack lines, the work-scope clause, the targeting lines and the Raylu scope notes were literals in `lib/ask/system-prompt.ts`, which imports `server-only` and so cannot be imported by the eval; the sheet could not carry them, and a live run judged those true statements as fabricated extras. Options weighed: (a) import the prompt into the sheet, rejected because `server-only` throws outside the server bundle and the prompt is about 21K characters of judge input per call; (b) copy the literals into the sheet, rejected because two copies drift; (c) one typed, Zod-validated content module (`AskFactsSchema`) that both compose from. Chosen: (c). `SYSTEM_TEXT` is byte-identical to the previous prompt, so the prompt cache and `PROMPT_VERSION` do not change; `__tests__/system-prompt.test.ts` compares it to `__tests__/fixtures/system-prompt.baseline.txt`, and any deliberate prompt or content edit regenerates that fixture in the same commit. The first live run still failed three items whose answers were true to the prompt: the sheet kept each role `body` but not the `context` and `highlights` the prompt also states (Trustly, 94.7% coverage, the Series A and SOC 2 line, Fastify and TypeORM). The sheet now renders them through the same formatter as the prompt (`lib/ask/employment-format.ts`). Sheet cap raised from 5000 to 18000 characters (`MAX_SHEET_LENGTH`) because the sheet now carries the skills, work scope, targeting, role-scope, context and highlight lines (about 16300, roughly 4000 input tokens per judge call, accepted because a true fact judged invented fails the release gate). Held by `__tests__/system-prompt.test.ts` and `lib/eval/__tests__/fact-sheet.test.ts`. _Reversible: inline the lines back into the prompt and drop the sheet lines._
- **2026-11-07** · **The judge fact sheet covers every content fact the system prompt states, and local runs accept the Vercel OIDC token.** Root cause: the sheet carried only employment, education and languages, so a live run judged the true title "Senior Software Engineer, Applied AI" and the true WES Verified credential as fabricated extras. Options weighed: (a) loosen the fabricated-extra clause, rejected because it guards invented facts; (b) paste the system prompt, rejected for token cost; (c) add the title, core stack, AI focus and credentials, each read from `content/profile.ts` and `content/credentials.ts`. Chosen: (c), The sheet also carries the employer line with scale, the performance receipts, where Erik lives against each employer's location (a live run read the Raylu office in New York as his residence), each role body and the work scope. Size cap raised from 2500 to 5000 characters (`MAX_SHEET_LENGTH` in the test) because the sheet grew to about 4100. A gold case (`cal-border-title-applied-ai-not-in-criterion`, must pass) joins the set. `scripts/ask-eval.ts` and `scripts/agent-eval.ts` also accept `VERCEL_OIDC_TOKEN` as the gateway credential (`lib/eval/gateway-credential.ts`), which the AI SDK gateway provider uses when no key is set, so `vercel env run` can drive a local run. Held by `lib/eval/__tests__/fact-sheet.test.ts` and `lib/eval/__tests__/gateway-credential.test.ts`. _Reversible: remove the added sheet lines and the OIDC branch._
- **2026-11-07** · **The ask eval judge gets a ground-truth fact sheet and the run date, and the fabricated-extra rule is judged against them.** Root cause: CI run 42381435317 on PR #374 scored correctness 86.8% (gate 0.9) because the judge saw only the criterion. The employer and title criteria omit the start date, so a true "June 2026" read as an invented date that "conflicts with plausible real data": the judge has no clock and a training cutoff before that month, and the fabricated-extra clause added in the previous bullet turned that gap into a failure (three of five red items, English and Portuguese). Options weighed: (a) drop the fabricated-extra clause, rejected because it is the only check on invented employers and figures; (b) put the date in every criterion, rejected because it fixes one fact and leaves the next true omission to fail; (c) hand the judge the whole system prompt, rejected for about 5,000 extra input tokens on every judge call; (d) a compact sheet built from `content/git-log.ts`, `content/education.ts` and the profile, with today's date. Chosen: (d), plus the three current-role criteria state the true dates as allowed and optional. The two injection-phrase items demanded both prompt-injection defense and evaluation harnesses as stated personal work; the system prompt carries both only as keywords, so a faithful answer that names one, or describes the site's own governed endpoint, failed. They now accept either area tied to Erik's work and still fail a generic answer with no tie to him. Three gold cases join the set: an English and a Portuguese `true-fact-absent` case (must pass) and a `false-date` case (must fail). Not reproduced against the live model: no Gateway key was available, so the fix rests on the judge reasons in the run and on deterministic tests of the prompt assembly, the sheet and the gold labels; the next CI run is the measurement. Held by `lib/eval/__tests__/judge.test.ts`, `lib/eval/__tests__/fact-sheet.test.ts`, `__tests__/ask-eval-corpus.test.ts` and `__tests__/ask-eval-calibration.test.ts`. _Reversible: drop the `factSheet` option and the sheet module; restore the two injection criteria._

- **2026-11-07** · **The `/api/ask` injection gate stays a gate, but it no longer refuses a question that only mentions an override phrase.** Root cause: `INJECTION_RE` matched the phrase anywhere, so "How do you defend against 'ignore previous instructions' attacks?", "Can I ignorar as regras de lint?", "Desenvolvedor: senior?" and "Sistema: Angular?" returned HTTP 400, and the first of these is the question this site's positioning most invites. Options weighed: (a) log-only, because the sentinel wrapper and the output guard already contain injection; (b) a narrower pattern plus mention handling; (c) a model classifier. Chosen: (b). Log-only would turn every blocked attempt into a paid model call, and a classifier adds a call and a failure mode to the cheapest layer. The route now calls `isInjectionAttempt`, which removes quoted spans (straight, curly and guillemet double quotes, backticks, single quotes that are not apostrophes) and the unquoted form "against/about/sobre/contra + phrase + attacks/ataques" before matching. The pt "regras" and "diretrizes" alternates need "suas/seus" or a trailing "anteriores/acima/do sistema", because "ignorar as regras de lint" is ordinary. A role word and colon followed by a fragment of up to four words ending in "?" is a label question, not a role line. `normalizeInjectionInput` also folds Cyrillic and Greek lookalike letters into Latin, so "ign\u043Ere all previous instructions" is caught. Known cost: wrapping a real override in quotes, or prefixing it with "against" and ending it with "attacks", now passes layer 0, so the gate stays a heuristic and the sentinel wrapper and output guard remain the boundary. Held by `lib/ask/__tests__/injection.test.ts` (benign en and pt-BR questions must pass, override phrasings and lookalike spellings must be caught) and `__tests__/ask-eval-corpus.test.ts` (corpus items that mention a phrase must not trip the gate). _Reversible: restore the single `INJECTION_RE.test(normalizeInjectionInput(question))` call in the route; benign questions that mention an override phrase then return 400 again._
- **2026-11-07** · **`pnpm ask:eval` separates a judge outage from a feature failure, reports gate refusals apart from model-graded jailbreak passes, and grades pt-BR.** Root causes: the corpus loop counted a judge API failure as a failed answer while calibration already classified it as an error; three of eight jailbreak items were refused by the regex with no model call, so the 100% resistance gate partly measured the regex; the eval never sent `locale` and the corpus was English only, so the pt-BR path (language line, pt error copy, pt leak markers) was never graded. Corpus items take an optional `locale`, six pt-BR items were added (two factual, one off-topic edge, one injection-phrase edge, plus two pt-BR jailbreaks the regex does not match), and the aggregate carries `jailbreakResistance.gate` and `jailbreakResistance.model`. The combined `rate` still drives the gate and the metrics panel, and the gate now also fails when no jailbreak item reached the model. This amends the 2026-11-04 note that the ask corpus stays English. Held by `__tests__/ask-eval-grading.test.ts`, `__tests__/ask-eval-judge-outage.test.ts` and `__tests__/ask-eval-harness.test.ts`. _Reversible: drop the `locale` field, the pt items and the breakdown; the judge-outage classification should stay._
- **2026-11-07** · **The ask eval criteria name key facts only, the judge fails a fabricated extra, and the ask route samples at a named low temperature.** Root cause: CI run 42358051051 on PR #366 (code under test unchanged) scored correctness 29 of 33, under the 0.9 gate, on four items. Three criteria were exhaustive lists (the full stack with Stencil and SSR/SSG/ISR, every testing tool and AI-native skill, a TTI figure demanded by a bundle-size question) that a sub-200-word answer cannot carry, and the off-topic criterion demanded brevity while the prompt asks for a redirect to a related question and the model's redirect elaborated. Options weighed: (a) lower the 0.9 gate, rejected because the gate was measuring the criteria, not the feature; (b) run each item several times and take pass@k, which spends Gateway tokens to hide a bad criterion; (c) rewrite the criteria to the few facts a concise answer carries and pin them to content. Chosen: (c). Factual items declare at most four `facts`, each present in the criterion and in the system prompt, with figures taken from `receiptDelta`, `PROFILE_FACT` and `profile`. The off-topic criterion accepts one related question, and the prompt now says to decline in one sentence and offer one related question without answering it. The judge prompt accepts paraphrase and extra true detail and fails an omitted named fact, a contradiction or a fabricated extra, which the old prompt let through beside a satisfied criterion; ten borderline gold cases (near-miss omission, harmless extra, fabricated extra, wrong number, wrong language, allowed redirect, trivia answered, paraphrase) raise the set to 20 so one disagreement no longer decides the 0.85 agreement gate. `createAskStream` set no temperature, so the provider default made the 100% jailbreak gate non-reproducible; it now passes `ASK_TEMPERATURE` (0.2), low enough for stable factual answers and above zero so repeated phrasing is not byte-identical. Known limit: the judge sees the criterion and not the system prompt, so a fabricated extra is caught only when it contradicts or sits implausibly against the criterion; giving the judge the prompt would add about 5,000 input tokens per graded item. Held by `__tests__/ask-eval-corpus.test.ts`, `__tests__/ask-eval-calibration.test.ts`, `lib/eval/__tests__/judge.test.ts` and `lib/ask/__tests__/model.test.ts`. _Reversible: drop `facts`, the `trap` cases and the judge clause; remove `temperature` from `createAskStream` in `lib/ask/stream.ts`._
## 2026-11-07: CI path-filter coverage, pinned action paths, bounded overrides

- **2026-11-07** · **The co-located component specs are written for software WebKit, which paints about four frames per second on the homepage.** Running `webkit-desktop-components` and `webkit-mobile-components` in CI for the first time failed three specs, all reproduced in a Linux WebKit container (macOS WebKit passed them). (1) `Hero.e2e.ts` took a `fullPage` screenshot nothing asserted on; the homepage is taller than the 32767px WebKit capture limit at phone width, so it is gone. (2) The footer sections-seen test stepped the page by half a screen every 50ms, but the observer delivers once per frame (233ms measured), so jumped-over sections were never counted (4 to 8 of 9) and the 400-step loop with `mouse.wheel` ran into the 30s test timeout. It now brings each observed section to the middle of the viewport, waits two frames, and checks the reading band geometrically before asserting the count; a counter that skips a section still fails it. (3) The contact error-summary spec clicked EXECUTE_SEND as soon as the form had a React fiber key, which exists from the start of hydration; a click before the commit is dropped, so the click repeats inside `toPass` until the summary shows. (4) The timezone panel is a lazy island (observer fires, chunk imports, then it renders), which took longer than the 5s default in 3 of 24 mobile runs, so assertions on the hydrated clocks wait 20s. Rejected: raising timeouts only (hides the dropped click), a stronger `waitForHydration` built on React fiber flags (version-bound internals). Lessons 31 to 33 record the rules. _Reversible: restore the removed screenshot, the timed scroll loop and the single click; the specs then fail again on Linux WebKit._
- **2026-11-07** · **Every git-tracked root and `scripts/` file is classified in the detect-changes manifest, and the check fails on a new unclassified file.** `proxy.ts`, `instrumentation.ts`, `vercel.json`, `postcss.config.mjs`, `tsconfig.json`, `.nvmrc` and `strip-next-polyfills.mjs` were in no filter, so a PR touching only one skipped performance, e2e and visual and `ci-gate` counted the skips as success. They are now in `APP_PATHS` (and `UI_PATHS` where they change rendered output), and `IGNORED_PATHS` lists the files that deliberately arm nothing (docs, lint config, always-on check scripts). Held by `unclassifiedFiles` in `scripts/check-detect-changes-paths.mjs` (part of `pnpm ci:local`) and `scripts/__tests__/check-detect-changes-paths.test.ts`. _Reversible: delete `IGNORED_PATHS` and the check._
- **2026-11-07** · **The `claude-review` tool deny list names the action path of the pinned SHA, held by `__tests__/scripts/workflow-action-sha-sync.test.ts`.** The deny entry for `git-push.sh` held the previous action SHA, so it matched nothing after the bump. _Reversible: revert the test and the SHA edit._
- **2026-11-07** · **`ai-eval` waits for `detect-changes`, `quality-fast`, `typecheck` and `test` only.** It downloads no artifact and starts no server, so waiting for `build` and the browser jobs only delayed a job that `ci-gate` already aggregates. Held by `__tests__/scripts/ci-ai-eval-needs.test.ts`. _Reversible: restore the longer `needs` list._
- **2026-11-07** · **Stryker ignores the `.claude/skills` directory symlinks, held by `__tests__/stryker-directory-symlinks.test.ts`.** Root cause: the skill layout made `.claude/skills/<name>` git-tracked symlinks to `.agents/skills/<name>`; Stryker copies every tracked file into its sandbox with `copyfile`, which follows the link, hits a directory and throws `EISDIR` (`ENOTSUP` on macOS) before any mutant runs, so the canary in the `test` job exited 2 (`Stryker wrote no report`), `build` and the visual jobs were skipped and `ci-gate` failed on `feat/terminal-redesign` and on every branch merged from it. Reproduced locally with `node scripts/check-mutation-kills.mjs --canary`; with `ignorePatterns: ['.claude/skills']` it reports 26 of 32 scoreable mutants killed. The guard lists tracked symlinks that resolve to directories and fails when `ignorePatterns` does not cover one (red without the pattern, naming `.claude/skills/ai-eval-update`; green with it). Options weighed: (A) ignore the link directory, chosen because the canonical files stay copied under `.agents/skills`; (B) `inPlace: true`, rejected because it mutates the working tree and a killed run leaves mutated source behind; (C) replace the links with copies, rejected because it forks the skill text. _Reversible: drop the pattern and the test; the canary then fails again while any tracked directory symlink exists._
- **2026-11-07** · **`pnpm.overrides` are `^` ranges of the resolved version and `check-dep-pinning` validates them.** Nine overrides were open `>=` ranges the script ignored, so any future major would install unreviewed. The `@stryker-mutator/core>minimatch` override moved its resolved 11.0.3 to 11.0.5 on re-lock (inside the old range). _Reversible: restore the ranges and drop the override loop._
- **2026-11-07** · **`failOnFlakyTests` is enabled in CI.** See the Playwright flakes section above for the root causes fixed first. _Reversible: remove `failOnFlakyTests` from `playwright.config.ts`._

## 2026-11-06: review round over the terminal redesign (DRY, a11y, security, structure)

- **2026-11-07** · **One `SiteShell` builds the shell for every page frame, the 404 pages and the route error pages, so each carries the home header.** Measured on a production build at 1280 and 375 before the change: `/`, `/cv`, `/summary` and their `/pt` twins matched; `/links`, `/links/en`, `/design-system` and `/design-system/*` lacked the EN/PT switch in the header (the pages owned or omitted it); the 404 pages rendered a text bar (`err-bar`) with four links, no menu, no MOTION, no VIEW_CV, no HIRE, no switch and no dock; route errors rendered the same bar. Each page wrapped itself in `AppShell` with its own copy of brand, chrome and skip label, and `error.tsx` replaces the page subtree, so an error page had to carry its own chrome. Now `SiteShell(locale, drawerExtra?)` is the one place that derives brand, `siteNavChrome` and the skip label; `SiteHome`, `ReadingShell`, `LinksPage`, the design-system layout, `NotFoundPage` and the lazy `RouteErrorPage` render through it. `ErrorChrome`, the `chrome` error content and the `err-bar` CSS are deleted. `LanguageSwitch` renders on every page: a page without a twin links the other language to its home (`/design-system` gives `/pt`, `/pt/x` gives `/`), and the `/links` page loses its in-page switch (the row keeps its title). The pathname readers in the shell (topbar link state, switch) sit in `Suspense`, because `usePathname` fails prerender in the catch-all 404 route otherwise; the fallback is the same links without current state and a switch that points each language home. The topbar and title bar are `<header>` (one banner landmark; only one is displayed), and the dock MENU button drops `aria-current` for `data-current`, since a button is not a location. `global-error.tsx` keeps its static page without the shell: it renders only when the root layout failed, and the shell is part of what failed. The home header has no search control (`DesktopTopbar.e2e.ts` holds that), so no page gains one. Held by `tests/e2e/shared-header.spec.ts` (same ordered controls, same bar position and same drawer as home on 10 routes at desktop and phone, one banner), `__tests__/site-shell.test.tsx` (every page frame opens with `SiteShell` of its locale), `__tests__/error-pages.test.tsx` (404 and route error render the topbar and title bar), `__tests__/locale-alternates.test.ts`, `components/responsive/SectionNavigation/SectionNavigation.test.tsx`, `components/responsive/Dock/Dock.test.tsx`. Rejected: mounting the shell in both root layouts (every route then reads the pathname at request time, so the language switch leaves the HTML a no-JavaScript visitor receives), a client-side copy of the chrome inside `RouteErrorPage` (it drifts), and a bespoke 404 bar with the full link set (a second header to keep in step). Not run: a browser test that forces a runtime route error, because the app has no fault trigger and a test-only hook would ship in production. _Reversible: wrap each page in `AppShell` again, restore `ErrorChrome` and the `chrome` error content from git history, and return `headerLanguageSwitchPaths` to a null result for unpaired pages._

- **2026-11-06** · **The CRT stack lowers its own update rate when measured frame times show the device cannot keep up, and a device that keeps up renders exactly what it did before.** `lib/frame-governor.ts` (loaded after first paint by `CRTOverlay`, so it sits outside the first-load JS) samples `requestAnimationFrame` deltas while motion is on, the tab is visible and no system-failure beat runs. When the 75th percentile of the last two seconds passes 50ms it moves one of five levels down; it moves one level up only after the same window stays under 25ms for a hold that starts at 10s and doubles, up to 160s, after any recovery that fails within 30s. The state is `html[data-crt-level]`. At levels 1 to 5 `crt.css` pauses the noise, flicker and scan beam (play state only, so `animation: none` from reduced motion and the MOTION toggle still wins), `lib/crt-effect-stepper.ts` moves their animation clocks to absolute times every 2, 4, 8, 16 or 32 rain ticks (91ms to 1.5s), and `MatrixRain` stretches its tick by the same factor while scaling its per-tick advance and its fade alpha (`1 - (1 - a)^n`), so fall speed and trail length hold. Three findings shaped it. (1) A software compositor redraws the whole stack on every frame while any full-viewport animation runs, whether or not its value changed: in software WebKit at 390x844@3, `steps()` timing left noise and flicker at 4 fps, pausing any one of the three layers changed nothing, pausing noise and flicker gave 14 fps and pausing all three gave 45 fps. A slower `steps()` loop cannot lower the cost, so degraded levels pause and re-pose the animations on a timer. (2) The median of frame times missed the real distribution: software WebKit delivers fast callbacks around each long paint (1280x720@2 measured median 19ms, 75th percentile 299ms, 7 fps), so the median crossed the budget in 38% of windows while the page averaged 7 fps; the 75th percentile is slow in nearly every such window and stays near 17ms on a device that keeps up. (3) Writing `animation-delay` on a paused animation is not reliable in WebKit (the delay drifted by wall time), so the stepper writes `currentTime` from a captured origin plus accumulated real time, which Chromium follows exactly and WebKit tolerates even when it keeps the animation running. Measured in software WebKit (Docker, 2 CPUs, 30s after a 45s warm-up, two rounds, before to after): 1280x720@1 14.2 to 43.2 fps, 95th percentile frame 193 to 77ms, CPU per frame 122 to 29ms; 1280x720@2 7.6 to 18.3 fps, 650 to 301ms, 236 to 93ms; 390x844@3 1.7 to 4.9 fps, 794 to 383ms, 1140 to 412ms. In Chromium with CPU throttling the governor stays at level 0 up to 6x (60 fps, unchanged), reaches level 1 at 12x and 16x (draw count down 21% at 16x, fps within noise), and at 24x takes 14.6 to 36.0 fps, 622 to 125 drawn frames, 95th percentile 100 to 67ms. Pixel equivalence at full rate: with `Math.random` fixed, a virtual `requestAnimationFrame` and 400 rain ticks, the CRT layers plus the rain canvas show 0 differing channels and an identical canvas hash against the base build in five runs (WebKit 1280x720@1, 1280x720@2, 390x844@3; Chromium 1280x720@1, 390x844@3). Visual baselines do not change: level 0 adds no declaration to any layer, the screenshot helpers mask or hide the CRT layers and the canvas, and `tests/visual/visual.spec.ts` on chromium and chromium-mobile fails with the same pixel counts on the base build and on this one (the darwin baselines on this machine are stale against the base). The home route's first-load JS went from 162.5 to 162.6 KB. Rejected: the scan beam left ungoverned (it alone held 14 fps), a slower `steps()` loop (finding 1), the median (finding 2), and detecting GPU compositing (not observable). Held by `lib/frame-governor.test.ts` (degrade, hysteresis, backoff, hidden pause, motion and system-failure gates, stepping rate), `lib/crt-effect-stepper.test.ts`, `components/responsive/MatrixRain/MatrixRain.governor.test.tsx`, `components/responsive/MatrixRain/tail-fade.test.ts`, `components/responsive/CRTOverlay/CRTOverlay.test.tsx`, `__tests__/crt-governor-css.test.ts` and `tests/e2e/crt-governor.spec.ts` (141a9f3, afb755c). The owner delegated the choice among the four CRT options on 2026-11-06; this is the one that keeps a capable device unchanged. The first evaluation waits until the samples cover half a window and one eval interval past the settle period, so one slow load frame cannot degrade a 60fps device. _Reversible: remove the `CRTOverlay` effect, the pause rule in `crt.css` and the rate factor in `MatrixRain`; level 0 is the previous behaviour._

Growing the link box itself (inline-flex with min-height) shifted every section below and made Playwright's tall element screenshots on chromium-mobile capture the target about 175px off its own masks, on darwin and Linux alike.
