The design-system side rail shows from 1024px and is hidden below it, because it repeated at the end of every phone page and left a tablet's prose column 443px wide; a `<noscript>` copy of the page tree keeps phones without JavaScript able to reach every page. `/links` loses its bordered frame and window bar: the language switch and the prompt line stay as an in-page row, the ticker moves under the topbar, and the grid becomes two independent stacks split 1.618 : 1 from 1024px so no row leaves dead space under a shorter column. Rhythm (heading, paragraph, list and table margins in the design-system prose, the `/links` gaps, the mobile table rows split 0.382 : 0.618) reads `--phi-space-1` to `--phi-space-4`; `--guitar-pair-columns` is renamed `--phi-pair-columns` since three surfaces use it, and the duplicate `--phi` in `base.css` is removed so there is one set. The home footer netstat table keeps its table semantics through explicit roles while its rows stack below 23rem of card width (protocol and state, then the endpoint on its own line), endpoints never wrap, and rows are 28px for a fine pointer and 44px for touch. The design system documents the EQ spectrum strip, the phi spacing tokens, the icon-only menu trigger with the position line, and the external company link. `/cv` and `/summary` stayed on their print-first reading layout, with their own reading bar. Superseded 2026-11-05: both now run inside `AppShell` and print without it (see the reading-views shell bullet). _Reversible: render the three pages without `AppShell`, restore `SiteBar` and the `.links-shell` frame from git history; the token rename is a find and replace._

- **2026-11-03** · **The footer `LS -R ~/` site map stays a folder grid, set on the type scale and the phi spacing tokens, in three columns at desktop and tablet and one centered column below.** The three footer card titles (session report, NETSTAT and the site map) now share one size, `--text-section-title`, raised from `--text-xs` in the one `.footer-card-title` rule so a title is never smaller than the directory headings under it, the directory headings use `--text-base` at weight 700, and the links use `--text-body` in `--color-text` (the signal green stays on headings only, since running text in it fails WCAG AA). Gaps are `--phi-space-3` (title to grid and between columns), `--phi-space-1` (heading to list) and `--phi-space-4` (between rows from 26rem of container width up). Six columns do not fit: the longest row, `Interactive shell` plus the 4ch tree indent, needs about 176px and a column at the 75rem footer width is about 169px, so the six-column step is removed and links never break mid word (`overflow-wrap: normal`). Container steps: one column below 26rem, two from 26rem, three from 38rem; targets stay 44px under a coarse pointer and 28px under a fine one. The tree card keeps the same padding as the NETSTAT and session cards so the title and column edges align. Reversible: CSS, `Footer.e2e.ts` and the regenerated footer baselines.

- **2026-11-03** · **The header brand is the glasses mark as an inline SVG; the wordmark text and the window dots are not rendered.** Owner request: the supplied glasses mark (one SVG path) replaces the `ERIK_CUNHA.SH` text, and the three macOS-style dots leave the header. The icon is an inline SVG Server Component (`BrandIcon`, `design-system/components/BrandIcon`) filled with `currentColor`, so `--color-primary-500` paints it; a static `drop-shadow(0 0 2px var(--color-glow-30))` matches the heading text-shadow and nothing animates, so no reduced-motion override exists; print drops the glow. `BrandLink` wraps it in a link home named `erikunha.dev, home` (`content/brand.ts`, parsed by `content/_validate-client-content.ts`) with a 44px minimum target. Wordmark decision: the text disappears entirely instead of staying visually hidden. The link already has an accessible name, a second hidden copy would be read twice by assistive technology, and the site name is stated in the title, the Open Graph tags and the JSON-LD for search; a screen-reader-only duplicate adds nothing a gate or a crawler uses. The viewBox is cropped to the glasses (`0 1433 4335 1300`): the supplied `0 0 4335 4335` leaves about two thirds of the square empty, so the mark rendered 10px tall at a 28px box. The raster icons keep the square source viewBox with the path centred. The client shells receive the link as a `brand` slot from the server (`AppShell`, `ErrorChrome`, the design-system `SiteBar`), so the path never enters the client bundle; `RouteErrorPage`, which must be a client module, shows the same mark through `<img src="/icon.svg">`, and `BrandIcon` is not exported from the design-system barrel (the barrel export left the path in seven client chunks and put `/` at 168.1 KB against the 164.7 KB gate; imported by path, no route changed size). `WindowChrome` stays in the shell and contact panel headers: those are panels, not the site header. The favicon, `app/icon.svg`, the 180/192/512 PNGs, `favicon.ico`, the web manifest and the Open Graph image come from the same path (`pnpm generate:icons`, `pnpm generate:og`) on black. `topbarChrome` and its schema are replaced by `brand`. Owner supplied the icon and still has to confirm its licence. _Reversible: restore `topbarChrome` and the dots in the two topbars._

## 2026-11-02: career links, guitar rig layout, menu trigger and contact section

- **2026-11-02** · **Every career entry carries a required https `companyUrl`, and the company name is a link wherever it renders.** The link renders through one component (`CompanyLink`) so rel, target, the new-tab disclosure, the underline and the hit area live in one place; the hit area is a 28px `::before` pseudo-element (`::after` is taken by the `/cv` print rule that appends the URL). It is the compact target, not 44px, because every consumer sets the link in lines of 20 to 22px, and a 44px zone would cover the lines above and below and take their taps; the e2e spec probes that the zone does not reach past the line. The `/cv` headings are typed text (`data-type-reveal` sizes its clip from the heading's text length), so they carry no sr-only disclosure inside the heading: the link points at one sr-only note per page through `aria-describedby`. Only the brand is linked in the career log (`GRUPO_SBF`, not `GRUPO_SBF (NIKE / CENTAURO)`), since a long wrapped link has no stable target. Encora links its LinkedIn company page because `encora.com` now redirects to its acquirer's site. `public/erik-cunha-cv.pdf` is not regenerated: it is a distributed binary and carries no company links. The ask prompt now lists each company URL so answers cite the real site; the shell prints answers as plain text, so those URLs are not clickable there. _Reversible per surface; the schema requirement is the one that keeps a new entry from shipping unlinked._

- **2026-11-02** · **Below 768px the mixer channel row is one column, and coarse pointers get 44px R/M/S buttons at every width.** At 320px the PAN knob overlapped the R button by 21px and the master LIMIT knob overlapped FX by 36px (16px at 360px), because the first grid column was sized for the id badge while the two knobs inside it overflowed into the buttons column. Stacking only the knobs would have fixed the overlap but kept a three-column first row (id, track, dB), so every level of the row now takes its own grid row: id, track, dB, knobs, R/M/S, plugin chips, meter, fader. Controls that belong together (the two knobs, the three R/M/S buttons, the chip wrap) stay a row inside their level. The desktop grid already budgets three 44px buttons, but they rendered at 24px, so a `pointer: coarse` rule lifts them to the touch target without changing the mouse layout. _Reversible: one revert of this commit; `tests/e2e/mixer-mobile.spec.ts` and `tests/e2e/guitar-mobile.spec.ts` fail at 320px without it._

- **2026-11-02** · **The guitar module opens with one EQ spectrum strip, drawn with DOM bars and CSS, not SVG animation or a client island.** The strip shows the lead chain under a fixed EQ curve on an 87 BPM groove. Bars are `span`s animated with a `scaleY` keyframe because SMIL `<animate>` ignores `prefers-reduced-motion`, a canvas or client island would add route JavaScript for a decoration, and per-bar gradients on SVG rects need document-unique ids that break a second render. The curve is the only SVG layer, stretched with `preserveAspectRatio="none"` and a non-scaling stroke so the line stays 2.6px at every width, and its glow is a CSS `drop-shadow` because an SVG blur filter distorts under non-uniform scaling. The EQ values (high-pass 140 Hz, 3 dB cut at 400 Hz, 5 dB boost at 2.8 kHz, low-pass 9 kHz) are illustrative until the real settings land in `content/guitar-rig.ts`. _Reversible: one revert of this commit removes the strip; `content/schemas.test.ts` covers the schema change._

- **2026-11-02** · **The menu trigger is the menu icon alone, and the `NN/9` position readout is removed, not relocated.** The trigger read `☰ INDEX 01/9`: a bordered control about 133px wide at 1280 and 127px at 390 (measured on screenshots), with a status readout inside an action. It is now a 44px square button with an SVG menu icon, named `Open menu` (`aria-expanded`, `aria-controls` and `aria-haspopup="dialog"` kept), left of the brand at every width. Scroll progress is carried by the position line alone: horizontal across the top from 1024px, and a thin vertical line on the right edge below 1024px, where the line was `display: none` and the native scrollbar overlays and fades on touch. It is the same `role="progressbar"` element (`Page position`) restyled by breakpoint, so one named progress control exists at every width. The drawer badge keeps the bare section count, and the dock MENU control is named by its visible label with no position suffix, so no readout of the index remains in any name. The line does not print. The component and class are `MenuTrigger` and `.menu-trigger`. The icon box is `--spacing-target` divided by `--phi` twice; the line thickness is `--position-line-thickness`. `indexGlyph`, `indexLabel`, `menuLabelPrefix` and `formatSectionPosition` leave `content/navigation.ts`; `openMenu` replaces the first two. Reversible: restore the copy keys, the position span and the pill styles in `app/css/components-nav.css`, the dock `aria-label`, and `display: none` on `.pos-line` below 1024px.

- **2026-11-02** · **The contact section is rebalanced: the form column carries the channels and the facts, and the timezone panel stands alone on the right, split at the golden ratio.** With the panel above the facts footer, the right column was 1071px tall against a 518px form, which left 553px empty under the form at 1280. The left column now stacks the form, a `LS -L ~/CHANNELS` block (email with `[COPY EMAIL]`, LinkedIn, GitHub) and six facts, and the right column is the panel under its own `DATE --TZ ~/OVERLAP` heading; the `tz` fact is gone because the two clocks already state it. The row splits `minmax(0, 1.618fr) minmax(0, 1fr)` from 1024px (owner rule: harmony by the golden ratio). The inverse split was measured and rejected: the form column is then 345px at 1024 and its left column grows to 1066px against a 740px panel, while the form-first split leaves 886px and 835px at 1024 and 880px and 866px at 1280. Spacing between the contact blocks and inside the panel is `--phi-space-2` and `--phi-space-3`, a scale in `app/css/theme.css` that multiplies the 8px `--spacing-2` by `--phi` (1.618) per step (`--phi-space-1` to `--phi-space-4`, about 8, 13, 21 and 34px). Layout is flex and grid only. The block under the form is `.contact-reach` (channels, then `.contact-facts`); neither is a timezone-panel class any more. Variants checked: phone, tablet and desktop widths, the placeholder estimates, JavaScript off, reduced motion (no new motion), print and the design-system pages (no contact demo exists); the dead `channels.replyLabel`, `channels.replyValue` and `channels.timezoneLabel` copy is removed. Below 768px every horizontal row stacks into a column: channel label above value, fact label above value (`.contact-fact` stays a `6ch minmax(0, 1fr)` grid from 768px so a label never wraps away from its value at 1024), the slot above its button, the confirmation above its link, and the channel actions fill the width centred; below 900px the clocks stack centred (side by side from 900px), and below 24rem the two map labels stack. The 24-cell hour rows stay horizontal: they are a chart. Below 1024px the source order is form, channels, facts, panel, so the stack reads as it is announced; below 768px the channel links and the copy button are 44px targets. After `PROPOSE_IN_MESSAGE` the confirmation gains an `edit message` link that moves focus to the message field below 1024px, where the form is out of sight; the form registers its field id with `messageStore` while the field is on screen. The static panel's placeholder height (51rem, 50rem, 49rem and 50rem in four width bands) and `#sec-contact`'s `--module-estimate` are re-measured in eight bands (977px from 1024px, 1840px from 768px, 2136px from 600px, 2322px below 340px). Held by `components/sections/ContactSection/ContactSection.test.tsx` (source order), `components/sections/ContactSection/ContactSection.css.test.ts` (golden ratio, phi gaps, phone columns), `components/sections/ContactSection/ContactSection.e2e.ts`, `components/client/ContactTimezone/ContactTimezone.test.tsx` (focus link), `tests/e2e/contact-timezone.spec.ts` and `tests/e2e/deferred-modules.spec.ts`. Reversible: restore the single `.contact-channels` card around the panel and the `ctz-footer` (git revert of this change), the 7fr to 5fr split and the previous `#sec-contact` estimates; the phi tokens can stay.

- **2026-11-02** · **The contact section's right column is a timezone overlap panel above the facts footer, and it no longer sticks.** The channels card listed contacts but said nothing about when Erik can talk, so a visitor in another zone had to work out the overlap alone. The panel shows Erik's and the visitor's clocks, a dot map, a zone search, three 24-hour rows and a best slot, and `PROPOSE_IN_MESSAGE` appends that slot to the form. It is taller than the viewport, so the 2026-10-31 sticky rule is dropped and the card scrolls with the page. The panel also makes the section about twice as tall as its old deferred placeholder (1.1 to 2.1 thousand px against 0.7 to 1.1 thousand), so `#sec-contact`'s `--module-estimate` in `app/css/components-shared.css` is raised in eight width bands, each at least the tallest measured height of the static or live panel in its band. The server renders only the dim map and the footer; one `React.lazy` island mounts within 400px of the viewport, so its chunk, the land grid and the `Intl` zone logic stay out of first load, and the static panel is the Suspense and error fallback. The form message moved to `messageStore`, a string store read through `useSyncExternalStore`, so the panel can append a line without prop drilling and typing does not re-render the island (#327). The panel subscribes to a boolean (is the proposed line still in the message), so its confirmation ends when the form clears after a send or the visitor deletes the line, and typing elsewhere changes no snapshot. The panel uses `Intl` only (no dependency) and its clocks tick only while it is on screen and the tab is visible. Held by `components/client/ContactTimezone/ContactTimezone.test.tsx`, `lib/timezone/overlap.test.ts`, `components/client/ContactForm/messageStore.test.ts`, `components/sections/ContactSection/ContactSection.e2e.ts`, `tests/e2e/contact-timezone.spec.ts` and `tests/e2e/deferred-modules.spec.ts` for the estimates. Reversible: restore the channels list in `ContactSection.tsx`, keep the message in local `ContactForm` state, restore the 1120px, 910px and 710px `#sec-contact` estimates, and delete `components/client/ContactTimezone/`, `lib/timezone/` and `content/world-map.ts`.

## 2026-11-01 — the /links relative label is anchored to the feed's fetchedAt

- **2026-11-01** · **`formatRelative` requires `now`, and the feed stamps `fetchedAt` inside the cached function after its network reads.** Main broke with no new commit: the YouTube channel published its first video, so `getChannelFeed` stopped returning the empty feed and `ChannelCard` began calling `formatRelative`, whose `now = new Date()` default Next 16 prerender rejects (`next-prerender-current-time`) because it runs before any uncached read. The label is now deterministic per cache entry. _Reversible: restore the `now` default and drop `fetchedAt`._

## 2026-11-01 — the secret scan reads only history reachable from the checked-out commit

- **2026-11-01** · **`gitleaks git` runs with `--log-opts="--full-history HEAD"`.** `actions/checkout` with `fetch-depth: 0` fetches every remote branch and `gitleaks git` defaults to all refs, so a PR into main scanned `feat/terminal-redesign` commits whose `p95LatencyMs` metric keys match generic-api-key. Scoping to HEAD scans exactly what the PR would merge; each branch is still scanned on its own PR run. No allowlist entry was added. _Reversible: drop `--log-opts`._

## 2026-10-31: layout fixes, one hire control name and the links feed fallback

- **2026-10-31** · **The topbar hides VIEW_CV and HIRE_ON_LINKEDIN between 768 and 1023px, where the dock already carries the hire path.** At 768px the fixed desktop topbar (INDEX, the brand, VIEW_CV, HIRE_ON_LINKEDIN) and the bottom dock (HOME, WORK, SHELL, HIRE, MENU) rendered together, so one viewport showed three hire controls and two controls that open the same section drawer (topbar INDEX, dock MENU); Material 3 and Apple HIG both pair a compact top bar with a bottom bar by giving each one distinct jobs. The two links now carry the row's hide-below-tier pattern, a base `hidden` plus an `lg:inline-flex` override, so from 768 to 1023px the topbar keeps INDEX and the brand while the dock HIRE and the hero action stay the only hire paths, and from 1024px the row is unchanged; `display: none` keeps the hidden links out of the tab order and the accessibility tree. Rejected: hiding the dock MENU control instead, because the dock is the only navigation on phones and the drawer must stay one tap away there. Held by `tests/e2e/section-navigation.spec.ts` (VIEW_CV and HIRE_ON_LINKEDIN hidden at 768 and 1023, visible at 1024 and 1280, INDEX and the brand on the row at every width) and `__tests__/desktop-topbar-nav.test.tsx`. Reversible: drop the two `hidden lg:inline-flex` classes and the links return at every topbar width.

- **2026-10-31** · **The `/cv` job titles keep one line at 320px because their size follows the viewport.** The typing clip is one line box, so a wrapped `data-type-reveal` heading splits the clip. `Zup Innovation (Itaú Unibanco)` measured 288px at `--text-base` in a 273px reading column at 320px, so it wrapped and the reveal read as two lines. `app/css/components-reading.css` gives `.reading-entry h3[data-type-reveal]` the size `clamp(var(--text-xs), 4.6vw, var(--text-base))`: 14.72px at 320px, where the title measures 265px in 273px, and the unchanged `--text-base` from 348px up. Every `/cv` job title reads the rule, so the ten titles share one size at every width. Rejected: an ellipsis, because the employer name is a fact the reader needs, and `white-space: nowrap`, because a title that outgrew the size would then overflow the clip box and lose its tail instead of wrapping. The About panel title `LS -LA ~/RESPONSIBILITIES` follows the same pattern in `app/css/components-about.css`, where `.ab-resp .panel-title` takes a viewport relative size between `--text-xs` and `--text-base`, so it holds one line at 320px and keeps the base size from 375px up. Held by `tests/e2e/type-reveal.spec.ts`, which now runs the marked elements of `/`, `/cv`, `/summary` and `/links` at 320px and 375px, and by `tests/e2e/type-scale.spec.ts` for the size of the About title. Reversible: drop the `.reading-entry h3[data-type-reveal]` rule and the fixed `--text-base` size returns, and drop the `.ab-resp .panel-title` rule for the About title.

- **2026-10-31** · **Every terminal module header shows its section name as visible text beside the icon.** The label string and its place in the heading did not change: `content/section-labels.ts` already supplied it and `Module` already rendered it as the first child of the `<h2>`, so the accessible name of the heading is the same before and after. What changed is that the span is visible. Two sections had tried to make it visible with a CSS override that reset the old `clip` property, but Tailwind v4's `sr-only` utility clips with `clip-path: inset(50%)`, a property the override never reset, so the title stayed clipped while `position: static` and `width: auto` gave its box the width of the label and pushed the icon and the command away from the edge; the other modules kept the label screen-reader only, so two header anatomies coexisted and neither matched the section label. Shell, Measured, Projects, Guitar and Contact now pass `name` instead of `srLabel`, and Opinions already did, so every heading reads name, icon, command and the name is the section label from `content/section-labels.ts`. The per-section overrides are gone and `app/css/components-utilities.css` styles the command for every titled module with one rule. Rejected: keeping the CSS override and the two anatomies, since the override never reset the clip the utility applies. Held by `components/responsive/Module/Module.e2e.ts` (the name is visible, matches the section label and keeps the icon beside it at 320, 390, 768 and 1280) and `components/sections/GuitarSection/GuitarSection.test.tsx`. Reversible: pass `srLabel` again in the five sections and drop the `name` branch from `Module`.

- **2026-10-31** · **The contact submit button spans its row below 768px and keeps its natural width from 768 up.** On a phone the button sat beside the status text at its natural width, a small target on the one form that matters. The row already wraps, so `app/css/components-contact.css` gives `.contact-send` `flex: 1 1 100%` below 768px and the button takes the full row; at 1280 it keeps its size. Held by `tests/e2e/mobile-reflow.spec.ts` (no free space beside the button at 390px, free space beside it at 1280px). Reversible: drop the `max-width: 767.98px` block that sets `flex: 1 1 100%` on `.contact-send`.

- **2026-10-31** · **The dock labels wrap under text zoom instead of being forced onto one line, and the tightest breakpoint tightens the letter spacing.** The dock is a grid of `minmax(0, 1fr)` tracks, so `white-space: nowrap` on a label let it spill into the next cell when the text was zoomed or a label grew; clipping the spill would drop the label, so the rule is gone and the label wraps instead, which is the correct fallback for a zoomed visitor. Below 360px the dock items take `letter-spacing: 0.04em` and `padding-inline: 2px`, which fits the longest label in its cell at the default size, so no label wraps and no item grows taller than its neighbours at 320. Rejected: clipping the overflow, because it drops the label, and keeping `nowrap`, because it spills into the next cell. Held by `tests/e2e/mobile-reflow.spec.ts` (every dock item measures the same height at 320px). Reversible: restore `white-space: nowrap` on `.dock > a, .dock > button` in `app/css/components-nav.css`.

- **2026-10-31** · **The two new gates now hold the properties the 2026-10-30 bullet attributes to them, and the dead mobile boot specs are deleted (PR #316).** The RoleTyper ARIA contract selected `span[aria-hidden="true"]`, which after the r106 slot change matched the outer `.role-typer` wrapper rather than the `[data-role-pill]` span the typing effect mutates, so both assertions in that block read the wrapper and the pill could lose its `aria-hidden` while the suite stayed green. The selector is `[data-role-pill]` now, and the About COPY test asserts the payload handed to `navigator.clipboard.writeText` equals the joined retry code tokens instead of only asserting the button exists. `MOBILE_LINE_SPECS` is deleted from `lib/boot-animation.ts`: the 2026-10-30 mobile boot rewrite left it with no reader, since the mobile boot lines now come from `content/hero-client.ts` and are typed in place by `runMobileBoot`, so the reversal path the 2026-10-30 bullet names needs those specs re-added. Held by `components/client/RoleTyper/RoleTyper.test.tsx` (the pill, not the wrapper) and `components/sections/About/About.test.tsx` (the copied payload). Reversible: restore the `span[aria-hidden="true"]` selector and the existence-only COPY assertion, and re-add the mobile specs to `lib/boot-animation.ts`.

- **2026-10-31** · **A deferred module's placeholder is at least as tall as its content, per width band.** `.module-deferred` kept a 520px placeholder while its rows laid out at 1.0 to 3.9 thousand px, so before it painted each module lay its rows over the next section. The painted page was unaffected, but axe measures boxes, not paint: the git log's last link landed on the contact form's send button at 412px and Lighthouse read `target-size` 0 and accessibility 0.97, with no code in that area changed. Each deferred module now declares `--module-estimate` for phone (up to 767px, sized from the 320px worst case), tablet and desktop, about 3% above the measured extent, and `tests/e2e/deferred-modules.spec.ts` fails with the number to use when a module outgrows its estimate at any of eight widths. An overestimate is safe because `contain-intrinsic-size: auto` replaces it with the real height after the first paint, and a module below the fold shrinking does not move what the visitor sees. Rejected: `overflow: clip` on the module (axe ignores it, measured), and revealing the modules inside Lighthouse (no hook carries state into its page load without a flag in product code). Reversible: restore the single 520px estimate and drop the spec.

- **2026-10-31** · **Below 768px every hero metadata fact starts its own line, and from 768px up the facts keep the rows they had.** The metadata block was a flex row of five spans, so at 390px the availability and the employer landed on one line (`UTC-3 · available now NOW: Raylu`) and the domain wrapped with `SaaS` alone on its last line. `components/sections/Hero/Hero.tsx` now groups the spans into four facts (location, employer, domain, language), and the location fact holds the country and the timezone facts as two items. `app/css/components-hero.css` keeps the container a flex row from 768px up, where the location fact is a nested flex row with the same 12px column gap the row gave the two items before, so every fact box lands on the coordinates it had (the country item at left 49px and the timezone item at left 178px at 768px, left 82px and left 210px at 1280px, the employer at left 422px at 1280px); below 768px the container becomes a column, every fact takes a row, the two location items keep `white-space: nowrap` so `UTC-3` never splits at its hyphen, and the domain takes `text-wrap: balance` so its last line never holds a single word. The separator between the two location items is a `display: none` span from 768px up and an inline `·` below it, so the wide row keeps the bare gap it had and the mobile row reads the two facts as one line. Rejected: shrinking the metadata type below 768px, because that line is 345px against the 309px the column has at 390px, and a type change is a separate decision from the grouping one. The mobile hero grows one line, from 572px to 594px at 390px, which stays inside the 600px budget `tests/e2e/hero-layout.spec.ts` holds. Held by `components/sections/Hero/Hero.e2e.ts` (the NOW fact starts below the availability fact at 320px and 390px, no fact ends on a one-word line, and the wide fact rows keep their shape at 768px and 1280px). Reversible: drop the fact classes and the `max-width: 767.98px` block of `app/css/components-hero.css` and the five spans come back.

- **2026-10-31** · **The reading views and the design system stack above the matrix rain, and the reading view drops its opaque background.** The rain canvas is `position: fixed` with `z-index: 0`, so it painted over the `/cv` and `/summary` text and over the design-system content, while the home page kept the rain behind its content because `PageMain` carried `relative z-10`. `.reading-view` and `.ds-layout` now carry the same stacking, `position: relative` with `z-index: var(--z-content)`, and `.reading-view` no longer paints its own background, because the page background is already `--color-secondary-950` and the opaque fill was hiding the rain behind the reading text. Rejected: raising the canvas above the content, because the rain is decoration and the text is the page. Held by `tests/e2e/reading-views.spec.ts` and `tests/e2e/design-system-pages.spec.ts`, which make the canvas hit-testable and assert the h1 is the topmost element at its centre. Reversible: drop the stacking from `.reading-view` and `.ds-layout` and restore the background on `.reading-view`.

- **2026-10-31** · **Anchor jumps land clear of the fixed bars: `scroll-padding-top` is the bars' height plus `--spacing-3`.** The phone bars measure 94px, the status bar at 29px and the mobile title bar at 65px, and the desktop topbar measures 45px, but `scroll-padding-top` was 76px on phones and 52px on the desktop, so a heading chosen in the section drawer tucked under the bars. `app/css/base.css` declares `--mobile-bars-height: 94px` next to `--topbar-height: 45px` and sets `scroll-padding-top: calc(var(--mobile-bars-height) + var(--spacing-3))` below 768px and `calc(var(--topbar-height) + var(--spacing-3))` from 768 up. Held by `components/responsive/SectionNavigation/SectionNavigation.e2e.ts`, which opens the drawer at 390 and 1280, chooses About and asserts the section and its heading land below every fixed bar. Reversible: restore the two literal `scroll-padding-top` values.

- **2026-10-31** · **The home page clears the fixed topbar by `--spacing-6` from 768px up, and the hero-to-section gap is the section spacing.** The desktop hero started 3px below the fixed topbar, and its 40px bottom margin was shorter than the 48px gap the other sections use at that width. `PageMain` now carries `page-main`, and `app/css/components-shared.css` gives `.page-container.page-main` a `padding-block-start` of `calc(var(--topbar-height) + var(--spacing-6))` from 768px up, while `.hero-frame` takes `margin-bottom: var(--space-section)` at every width. The phone bars are in the flow, so the phone keeps the `--space-page-top` padding. Rejected: a taller hero, because the clearance belongs to the container. Held by `tests/e2e/hero-layout.spec.ts` (the hero clears the topbar by the section spacing, and the hero gap equals the gap between the other sections at 768 and 1280) and `tests/e2e/layout-tokens.spec.ts`. Reversible: drop the `.page-main` padding rule and restore the `.hero-frame` margin.

- **2026-10-31** · **The footer and the About panel lose their COPY controls; the netstat endpoint cells become links and the About panel header carries the repo link.** The footer COPY copied four fake `tcp ESTABLISHED` rows that no visitor asked for, and the About COPY copied a decorative excerpt with no visible object; the destinations the reader can act on replace both. The footer's endpoint cells keep the terminal look and carry a `↗` glyph, and the About panel header carries the `github ↗` link on one row, with the panel title's size budget reserving the link's width. The contact form keeps its copy control, because there the copied value is the address the visitor wants; it is restyled as a compact control next to the address, `--spacing-target-compact` tall under a fine pointer and `--spacing-target` tall under a coarse one, with the same 2px radius as the other controls. Held by `components/sections/Footer/Footer.e2e.ts`, `components/sections/About/About.e2e.ts`, `components/sections/Footer/Footer.test.tsx` and `components/sections/ContactSection/ContactSection.e2e.ts`. Reversible: restore the two `CopyButton` instances and drop the endpoint links.

- **2026-10-31** · **The contact section's right column is a channels card, `▌ LS -L ~/CHANNELS`, that sticks under the topbar.** The aside held a languages line, the address and a COPY button, and the rest of the column was empty, while "Prefer LinkedIn?" and "Replies within 48h" sat orphaned under the form. The card lists the address with its copy control, a `MESSAGE_ON_LINKEDIN ↗` button, GitHub, the reply window, the languages and the timezone, in the About-card style, and the grid splits `minmax(0, 7fr) minmax(0, 5fr)`. The copy lives in `content/contact.ts`; the address, the GitHub URL and the timezone are read from `content/social.ts` and `content/profile.ts`, so each fact has one source. The card is `align-self: start` and sticky below the topbar from 1024px up, and stacks under the form on phones. Held by `components/sections/ContactSection/ContactSection.e2e.ts` and `components/sections/ContactSection/ContactSection.test.tsx`. Reversible: restore `.contact-side` and the two lines under the form.

- **2026-10-31** · **The contact error summary reads on one line and draws no focus ring of its own.** The summary outlined a container it only focuses to announce the error, and its list rows sat 44px apart, so the summary took more space than the field it pointed at. The summary now reads `3 fields need attention: name · email · message` on one line, the field links keep the compact hit height, and `.contact-alert:focus` draws no outline, because the container is not interactive. Held by `components/client/ContactForm/ContactForm.test.tsx` (the labelled headline and one link per field) and `components/sections/ContactSection/ContactSection.e2e.ts` (one paragraph, three links, no outline). Reversible: restore the list markup and the focus rule.

- **2026-10-31** · **Form placeholders use `--color-tertiary-500` instead of the signal colour.** The fields rendered their placeholder in the lime signal at 60% opacity, which reads as a value and competes with the salmon error border; the tertiary token measures 4.72:1 against the base surface and `scripts/contrast-check.mjs` audits the pair. Held by `pnpm lint:contrast`. Reversible: restore the signal colour and the opacity on the `Field` primitive.

- **2026-10-31** · **The root declares a dark colour scheme and a themed thin scrollbar.** The page renders its own dark form controls, but the scrollbar kept the browser default, which reads as a light band against the black surface. `:root` sets `color-scheme: dark`, `scrollbar-color` from the theme's primary and secondary tokens and `scrollbar-width: thin`. Held by `tests/e2e/layout-tokens.spec.ts`. Reversible: drop the three declarations from `:root`.

- **2026-10-31** · **Superseded 2026-11-07 (the MOTION switch is the only motion control): the `MOTION: REDUCED` label and `readSystemReducedMotion()` no longer exist.** **The motion control reports the operating system's reduction.** The drawer read `MOTION: ON` while the OS asked for reduced motion, so the control misreported the state it owns. `lib/motion.ts` exposes `readSystemReducedMotion()` next to `readMotion()`, and the drawer renders `MOTION: REDUCED` when the system asks for reduction and motion is off, `MOTION: ON` when motion runs and `MOTION: OFF` when the visitor turned it off. Every animation stays as it was; only the label follows the system. Held by `components/responsive/SectionNavigation/SectionNavigation.e2e.ts` (the reduced and the no-preference readings). Reversible: drop the third label and the system read.

- **2026-10-31** · **The typed command's caret reserves its own width inside the command.** The caret is an absolutely positioned block drawn one caret width past the typed text, so when the command landed exactly on the module heading's right edge the caret spilled past the heading and the heading's scroll width exceeded its box. The theme's thin scrollbar gave the heading four more pixels, which moved the command onto that edge and turned the spill into a measured overflow. The caret pseudo-element is now exactly as wide as the command (`width: 100%`) and paints its block inside that box, in the last `--module-caret-width` of the gradient, so it never leaves the element and needs no padding reserve; the slide-in animation starts from the same place. Held by `components/responsive/Module/Module.e2e.ts`, which fails when a module heading spills past its box. Reversible: restore the caret width to `calc(100% + var(--module-caret-width))`.

- **2026-10-31** · **The netstat table sizes its columns from the content, and the footer takes the page gutter.** The table ran on `table-layout: fixed` with column widths of 4ch and 12ch, so the `Proto` header, which needs five characters, overflowed its column into `State` at every width, and the endpoint cell took only the width the fixed layout left over, which at 390px was less than the longest label and its `↗` glyph need, so the link clipped on a phone with a classic scrollbar. The columns are now sized from their content, the endpoint cell wraps instead of clipping when the card is narrower than the longest label, and `.footer-inner` takes the page's `--space-gutter` token, 16px on phones and 24px from 600px up, instead of a fixed 24px, which is the gutter the sections above it use and the room the table needs to keep one row per line at 390px. Held by `components/sections/Footer/Footer.e2e.ts`, which asserts that every netstat cell stays on one line and inside its column at 390px and 1280px and that the endpoints stay inside their cells at 320px. Reversible: restore `table-layout: fixed` with the two column widths and the fixed footer padding.

- **2026-10-31** · **Design-system shell: the MENU drawer replaces the inline topbar links below 768px only, and the sidebar stays in the page at every width.** The prototype stacks the sidebar under the content below 1024px; the port stacks it below 768px, the same cut as the drawer, so tablets keep the side column. Hiding the sidebar instead (the first port) dropped the skip target and broke the stacked-nav gates. The open drawer is a modal dialog: `lib/dom/isolate.ts`, shared with the home section drawer, makes the rest of the page `inert` until it closes. The drawer is `inert` while closed and traps Tab while open. The HOME arrow is CSS content with empty alt text, so the link is named HOME. DIALOG left the nav because no Dialog primitive or page exists. Reversible: CSS breakpoints and one nav entry.

- **2026-10-31** · **Every hire control answers to one accessible name, "Hire on LinkedIn (opens in a new tab)", and its visible label is a word sequence inside that name: the hero, summary and /cv controls read `HIRE_ON_LINKEDIN ↗`, the topbar and the dock read `HIRE ↗`, the rail reads `LinkedIn`.** The six surfaces spelled one LinkedIn URL with four labels and four names, so a visitor could not tell they were one action. `content/nav.ts` holds the strings (`NAV_VOCABULARY.hire`, `HIRE_SHORT_LABEL`, `HIRE_ACCESSIBLE_NAME`) and every surface reads them. The topbar takes the short label because the long one pushed its row to 1228px against a 1200px column at 1280 on Linux (measured in the Playwright noble image; macOS fitted). The name drops the word "me" so each visible label stays inside it in order (WCAG 2.5.3, Label in Name); `__tests__/hire-action-labels.test.tsx` fails when any surface's visible words leave the name. Reversible: revert the commit.

- **2026-10-31** · **Between 768 and 1023px the hero boot log moves below the hero text and takes the full content width, so every boot line renders on one line.** The log shared the hero row with the hero text in a 40% column and the overlay insets 24px on each side, so the log box measured 233px at 768 and 335px at 1023, while the widest boot line ("Welcome to DEV_OS v2.0.4-stable [user: erik]") measures 369.61px: 7 of the 11 lines wrapped at 768 and 2 wrapped at 1023. In that band `app/css/components-hero.css` turns `.hero-desktop` into a column, gives `.hero-terminal` the full frame width, takes `.hero-boot-desktop` out of its absolute box (static position, the same 24px inline inset, 16px block inset) and moves the hero text separator from a right border to a bottom border. The log box is 703px wide at 768 and 958px at 1023, and every line is one line tall (23.8px). The overlay reserves its height with `--hero-boot-lines` (the 11 lines the desktop boot renders: 8 specs, the command, the blank and the dialog) and the `lh` unit, so the reservation follows the log's own line height instead of repeating it, the hero does not grow while the log types, and the cumulative layout shift of the band falls from 0.0665 to 0.0433 at 768 and from 0.0505 to 0.0394 at 1023, measured with the layout shift observer over the animated boot. The hero text spans the frame in that band, so the metadata facts take the two rows they have at 1280; the 2026-10-31 metadata bullet recorded four rows at 768 and this bullet supersedes that part of it. Below 768 and from 1024 up nothing moves: the mobile hero is 594.16px tall at 390 and 529.09px at 767, and the desktop hero is 526.81px at 1024 and 451.23px at 1280, before and after. In the band the hero grows from 576.19px to 793.89px at 768 and from 526.81px to 772.20px at 1023. Rejected: widening the terminal column, because the hero text would fall to 295px at 768, the display name would wrap and the 30S_SUMMARY action would overflow its 77px cell; and shrinking the boot type, because the log would read 12px in the band and 14px from 1024 up. Held by `components/sections/Hero/Hero.e2e.ts` (every boot line is one line tall at 768 and 1023, and the metadata rows at 768 are the two rows of 1280); the mobile hero budget stays held by `tests/e2e/hero-layout.spec.ts`. Reversible: drop the `@media (min-width: 768px) and (max-width: 1023.98px)` block from `app/css/components-hero.css` and the log returns to the 40% column.

- **2026-10-31** · **The links feed falls back to the channel's uploads playlist through the Data API when the RSS feed returns nothing.** The RSS endpoint answered 404 for every channel, Google's own included, so `/links` showed no videos in production. With `YOUTUBE_API_KEY` set, `getChannelFeed` reads `playlistItems` for the `UU` twin of the channel id (1 quota unit per refresh, cap 2,000 a day) and keeps only public items; RSS stays first because it costs no quota. Without a key the page keeps its empty state. _Reversible: drop `fetchUploads`; RSS alone returns._

## 2026-10-30: navigation vocabulary, type tokens, component partials, gates and advisory overrides

- **2026-10-30** · **The retry-code panel lines are hard-wrapped to the narrowest panel instead of relying on CSS reflow.** The `$ cat src/lib/with-retry.ts` sample ran to 70 chars on its widest line and leaned on `white-space: pre-wrap` to fit, so the owner asked for lines that fit on their own at every width from 320px up. `content/about.ts` now wraps the sample to the 320px budget: the pre text area inside the panel is 232px (16px page gutter, 14px module padding, the About section border, the panel border and 12px pre padding on each side), and 12px JetBrains Mono at its 0.6em advance fits 32 chars, which the widest line uses exactly. 390px, 768px and 1280px only add width (one column to 1024px, then 1.5fr/1fr to 1279px and 1fr/1fr from 1280px), so the panel cannot scroll sideways at any of them; `pre-wrap` stays only as a safety net. Held by `components/sections/About/About.test.tsx` (per-line char budget) and `tests/e2e/type-scale.spec.ts` (scrollWidth equals clientWidth at 320, 390, 768 and 1280). Reversible: re-tokenize `content/about.ts` with longer lines; no CSS was touched.

- **2026-10-30** · **About panel headings track tighter below 768px so the 16px heading holds one line.** The type scale raised `.panel-title` to `--text-base` at every width, and at 0.14em tracking `LS -LA ~/RESPONSIBILITIES` needs 296px against the 287px the panel header has at 375, so the heading wrapped to two lines and the `data-type-reveal` clip, which types one line at a time, split it. Below 768px the tracking drops to 0.04em (256px), which fits at 375 and 390 and keeps the marker inline with the title. At 320 the panel content is 232px and no 16px mono heading of 25 characters fits (240px of glyphs alone), so that width still wraps. Held by `tests/e2e/type-reveal.spec.ts` (one line at 375) and `tests/e2e/type-scale.spec.ts` (16px at every width). Reversible: restore `letter-spacing: 0.14em` on `.panel-title` and drop the About panel headings below 16px.

- **2026-10-30** · **The mobile boot log is a three-line server-rendered skeleton, the wide hero row drops the CV action, and the About role badge is sized by its own text.** r106 shows three boot lines on mobile; the app printed eight and reserved 165px of empty block for them. The lines now exist in the server HTML and the animation types into them, so the block reserves no dead space and nothing is appended after paint. That buys the same zero-CLS property the fixed height bought (the 2026-05-27 bullet) and supersedes it. `content/hero.ts` carries `hiddenOnWide` for the CV action and `components/sections/Hero/Hero.tsx` renders it `lg:hidden`, so the wide hero row is GITHUB and 30S_SUMMARY beside the hire CTA while the mobile row keeps all three. The About badge reserves its width with a hidden sizer instead of a fixed `min-w-[9em]`, which removed the gap between the badge and the rest of the status line; `RoleTyper` types Senior once and holds it, because the owner decision forbids Staff and Principal wording and a one-word cycle would only empty the badge. Held by `components/client/HeroBootAnimation/HeroBootAnimation.test.tsx`, `components/client/RoleTyper/RoleTyper.test.tsx`, `components/sections/Hero/Hero.test.tsx`, `components/sections/About/About.test.tsx` and `tests/e2e/hero-layout.spec.ts`. Reversible: restore the appended-line boot in `lib/boot-animation.ts`, drop `hiddenOnWide` from `content/hero.ts` and put the `min-w-[9em]` wrapper back in `components/sections/About/About.tsx`.

- **2026-10-30** · **Heading and reading type follow two tokens.** `--text-body` (14px) is the reading size (git log, project copy); `--text-xs` stays for labels. `--text-section-title` (16px, 18px from 768) sets the Module, About and Career h2, all uppercase, and card titles (`.project-name`, `.measured-heading`, `.panel-title`) use `--text-base` at every width so no heading sits below its copy, except the retry code panel title (`.ab-code .panel-title`), which scales with the viewport between `--text-xs` and `--text-base` because its 27 characters have no slack at 375px and do not fit at 320px at full size; it reaches the full size from about 380px. About and Career margins read `--space-section`. The README code panel wraps (`pre-wrap`) and never scrolls sideways; it stays a labelled tab stop because `scroll-regions.test.tsx` and `Hero.e2e.ts` assert that. Held by `tests/e2e/type-scale.spec.ts`. Reversible: revert the commit; baselines regenerate either way.

- **2026-10-30** · **Hero follows r106: no availability chip, signal-green h1, stats 2x2 on mobile, three secondary CTAs in one row; About drops the permissions legend and gains a COPY control on the retry code.** Owner decision removed the chip. Reversible: restore the Badge in `components/sections/Hero/Hero.tsx`.

- **2026-10-30** · **The stylesheet test helper resolves the partial order from `app/globals.css`, not from the filesystem.** `__tests__/helpers/components-css.ts` sorted the `components-*.css` partials alphabetically, while the browser resolves them in the `@import` order of `app/globals.css`, so a "last match wins" assertion over the concatenated stylesheet disagreed with the real cascade: `components-hottest-takes.css` is imported first and `components-career.css` fourth, but alphabetically career comes first. The helper now parses the `@import` list, and it fails closed when a partial exists on disk but is not imported, because an unimported partial is never shipped and the assertion would pass against CSS the browser never loads. Held by `__tests__/helpers/components-css.test.ts`. Reversible: sort the filenames again.

- **2026-10-30** · **`app/css/components.css` is split into 20 flat `app/css/components-<section>.css` partials, imported from `app/globals.css` in the original rule order.** One 4,900-line file was unreviewable and every section edit conflicted with every other. Rules are byte-identical and keep their relative order and `@layer` (one partial wraps `@layer utilities`), so the cascade is unchanged. Flat files, not a subdirectory, because `scripts/lint-css-tokens.ts` scans only `app/css/*.css`. Tests read every partial through `__tests__/helpers/components-css.ts`. Reversible: concatenate the partials' inner rules in `globals.css` import order back into one `@layer components` file and restore the single import.

- **2026-10-30** · **The drawer returns focus to the control that opened it when that control is still rendered, and to a rendered INDEX trigger when there is none or it is not rendered.** The drawer survives a viewport change, so the control that opened it can stop being rendered before the drawer closes: the dock, which carries the `MENU` control, is `display: none` from 1024px, and `.focus()` on an element inside a `display: none` subtree is a no-op, so focus was left on the closing dialog and the browser moved it to the body. `settleDrawer` now walks the original trigger first and then the INDEX triggers in document order, and focuses the first one that is rendered, so the keyboard path survives the change. Held by `components/responsive/SectionNavigation/SectionNavigation.test.tsx` (the control that opened the drawer is not rendered, so focus lands on the INDEX trigger) and `tests/e2e/section-navigation.spec.ts` (the drawer opens at 390px, the viewport changes to 1280px, the drawer closes and focus lands on the desktop INDEX trigger). Reversible: restore the single `triggerRef.current?.focus()` call in `settleDrawer`.

- **2026-10-30** · **The unit test that holds the brand badge on the topbar row resolves the Tailwind display cascade at every desktop width, it does not match a class substring.** The old assertion searched the ancestor class strings for `xl:hidden`, a string the codebase never writes: the hide-below-a-tier pattern is a base `hidden` plus a tier override (`hidden xl:inline`, `hidden xl:inline-flex`), so the search found nothing and the assertion held whatever the classes were. A brand badge wrapped in `hidden xl:inline-flex`, the exact regression the motion button had, would have left the test green while the badge left the row. The test now resolves the display utilities of every ancestor at 768, 1024, 1280 and 1440px and fails when one of them hides the badge. Held by `__tests__/desktop-topbar-nav.test.tsx` and `tests/e2e/section-navigation.spec.ts`. Reversible: restore the substring assertion.

- **2026-10-30** · **The drawer staying open across a viewport change is held by a browser guard, not by a unit test.** The unit test that claimed the property fired `matchMedia(...).addEventListener('change')` on a stub the component had never registered with, so the listener set stayed empty and the assertion passed without anything observing a viewport change. The component registers no media query listener, and a unit test cannot observe one that does not exist, so the property now lives in `tests/e2e/section-navigation.spec.ts`: the test opens the drawer at 390px, calls `page.setViewportSize` for a desktop width and asserts that the drawer, the trigger state and the scroll lock all survive the change. A drawer that closed on a breakpoint would fail that test. Held by `tests/e2e/section-navigation.spec.ts`. Reversible: restore the deleted unit test in `components/responsive/SectionNavigation/SectionNavigation.test.tsx`.

- **2026-10-30** · **The page root reserves the scrollbar gutter in every scroll state, so the drawer scroll lock moves nothing.** The lock replaces the page scrollbar with `overflow: hidden`, which removes the scrollbar and widens the centred column on platforms with classic scrollbars. A gutter reserved only while the lock is on is the mirror image of that fault: on platforms with overlay scrollbars the gutter appears only while the drawer is open, so the column shifts the other way. `app/css/base.css` therefore sets `scrollbar-gutter: stable` on `html`, in the unlocked state too, and the rule in `app/css/components-nav.css` sets `overflow: hidden` alone. Held by `__tests__/nav-chrome-offsets.test.ts`. Reversible: drop `scrollbar-gutter: stable` from the `html` rule in `app/css/base.css`.

- **2026-10-30** · **A schema a client component reads is parsed in `content/_validate-client-content.ts`, never in the module that holds the content.** `content/terminal-chrome.ts` is imported by `DesktopTopbar`, `MobileTitleBar`, `ContactForm` and `InteractiveShell`, so a `.parse()` in that module would ship zod to the browser; `TopbarChromeSchema` had no parse site at all, so an empty `brandMark` or `brandLabel` would have reached the topbar as a blank brand badge while the build gate stayed green. The build-time aggregator now parses `topbarChrome` with `TopbarChromeSchema`, beside the `shellChrome` and `contactChrome` parses. Held by `content/_validate-client-content.test.ts`. Reversible: drop that parse line.

- **2026-10-30** · **The desktop topbar carries the motion control from the `xl` tier (1280px); below it, the drawer carries the motion control at every width.** The topbar motion button and the section shortcut links share the same `xl` gate (`hidden xl:inline-flex` and `hidden xl:inline`), so below 1280px the row carries the brand badge, the `INDEX` trigger, `VIEW_CV` and `HIRE_ON_LINKEDIN`. The row neither overflows its column nor clips a control at 768, 1024, 1280 and 1440. Motion stays reachable below the tier because the drawer foot carries the `MOTION: ON` row at every width, so no width loses the control. Held by `tests/e2e/section-navigation.spec.ts`. Reversible: drop the `hidden xl:inline-flex` class from the topbar motion button.

- **2026-10-30** · **The desktop topbar and the section drawer carry the r106 navigation vocabulary.** `content/nav.ts` holds the one label set (`ABOUT`, `WORK`, `CAREER`, `CONTACT`, `DESIGN_SYSTEM`, `30S_SUMMARY`, `HIRE_ON_LINKEDIN`, `LinkedIn`) that the topbar, the drawer rows and the dock read, so no surface spells a label itself; the topbar drops the old `01_WORK` / `02_IMPACT` / `03_STACK` / `04_CONTACT` / `SSH_CONNECT` set, and the drawer foot drops its old two rows for `VIEW_CV`, `30S_SUMMARY`, `DESIGN_SYSTEM`, `LinkedIn` and a `MOTION` toggle (the topbar motion control spells its compact word `MOTION` from `TOPBAR_MOTION_LABEL` in the same module, the drawer row keeps the verbose `MOTION: ON` copy). The drawer opens with the nine destinations in their groups with the `01` to `09` index, a count badge in the head, and no dead gap: `.menu-foot` loses `margin-top: auto`, which pushed the foot to the bottom of the panel and left an empty band under the sections. The topbar row now fits the 1200px column at every width instead of running 1330px into it and clipping the last control: the brand badge stays on the row at every desktop width, beside the `INDEX` trigger on its left, and the row makes room for it by tightening instead of dropping items (the row gaps and the nav gaps read the `--spacing-3` and `--spacing-2` tokens, the nav labels take the `tracking-wider` token, and the motion control shows the dot plus `MOTION`, with the state on `aria-pressed` and the dot). The state word does not fit beside the badge at 1440 either: the row leaves 40px free in the 1200px column and `MOTION: ON` needs 39.4px, so the topbar spells the control `MOTION`, without the state word, and the drawer keeps the full `MOTION: ON` copy. The `HIRE_ON_LINKEDIN` CTA carries no `↗` glyph and no `aria-label`, so the visible label stays part of the accessible name (WCAG 2.5.3). The one motion state moved to `SectionNavigationProvider`, so the topbar and drawer toggles cannot disagree. Held by `tests/e2e/section-navigation.spec.ts` (no topbar overflow and no clipped control at 768, 1024, 1280 and 1440, the brand badge beside the `INDEX` trigger at every desktop width, the motion tiers, the drawer rows, the foot gap) and `__tests__/desktop-topbar-nav.test.tsx` (the vocabulary, the hrefs, the hire CTA, the brand badge, the compact motion label). Reversible: restore the old label set and `margin-top: auto`; the drawer row order follows `NAVIGATION.siteLinks`.

- **2026-10-30** · **A changed section regenerates all four darwin baselines in one pass.** `tests/visual/visual.spec.ts` runs on the `chromium`, `chromium-mobile`, `webkit-desktop` and `webkit-mobile` projects locally, because `playwright.config.ts` gives the two webkit projects the same `CROSS_BROWSER_SPECS` match, so a captured section has four darwin baselines. The regen that added the opinions closing link refreshed only the chromium pair: the webkit pair kept its pre-change pixels, and the linux pair was never refreshed at all, which is why the visual gate was red in CI on both chromium projects. The procedure now names the four projects in one command, and the linux pair comes from the CI dispatch in the same change, so a section that moved ships every baseline it owns. Rejected: a per-section baseline allowlist (the project set comes from `playwright.config.ts`) and a darwin runner in CI (the darwin pixels cannot be produced on the ubuntu runner). Held by the local `tests/visual/visual.spec.ts` run over the webkit projects, which fails on the stale pair, and by the `e2e-visual-chromium` CI job, which was red on the stale linux pair. Reversible: restore the previous `*-darwin.png` and `*-linux.png` files and the previous procedure text.

- **2026-10-30** · **The manifest and the site publish one degree wording, held by the identity gate.** `content/education.ts` spells the UNISAL degree `B.S. - **2026-10-30** · **A prototype rule travels with its precondition, not on its own.** The LIVE_PERF score grid forced four columns from a 1280 breakpoint while the panel holding them is half a column wide, so `PERFORMANCE`, `ACCESSIBILITY` and `BEST PRACTICES` broke mid-word at 1280 and `ACCESSIBILITY` broke at 1024; the prototype ships the same four-column grid behind a container query, because four columns only fit a wide column. Opinions takes the prototype's closing link to the design-system opinion (`/design-system#opinion-07`), keeps the header command label inline with the section name instead of pushed to the right edge, keeps the category tag neutral and inline with the take title at every width, and sets the parenthetical in the body colour the prototype inherits. - **2026-10-30** · **The shell input is a terminal line: focus is a 2px lime rule drawn inside the field, not a ring around it.** The old indicator was a 1px outline offset 4px on a borderless full width input, so focusing it painted an empty rectangle of 969 by 44 px on desktop and 324 by 44 px at 390, out of proportion with the panel hairlines and reading as a form box inside a terminal. - **2026-10-30** · **Headings and command prompts type in as they scroll into view, on every route.** Owner request: the self-typing that RoleTyper and the module headers have spreads across the app. One client island (`TypeReveal`, mounted with Matrix Rain by `GlobalMotion` in the root layout, so both run on every route and not only on the home, which is the only page that renders `AppShell`) runs a single IntersectionObserver over `[data-type-reveal]` and flips a class; CSS does the typing with a stepped `clip-path` and a caret, so the text never leaves the DOM and the box never resizes (no CLS, nothing hidden from screen readers or search). Only single-line elements are marked, and the attribute sits on a span around the typed text alone so icons and hidden siblings neither inflate `--type-chars` nor share the clip box (wrap-capable containers such as `CmdLine` are not marked); the h1 and the first hero viewport are never marked, so LCP is untouched. Elements revealed while motion is off, and elements already typed when motion turns off, get `is-static`, which cancels the clip and the animation, so turning motion on later never replays text already on screen. Rejected: per-element React typing (state per keystroke, one island per heading) and JS text mutation (hides content from assistive tech and crawlers until it finishes). Reversible: remove the `data-type-reveal` attributes, or drop `GlobalMotion` from `app/layout.tsx` and the text renders static.

The 900px gutter and the 769-900px body font overrides are gone; the module `h2` is `text-base` below 768 and `text-lg` from 768, bold. `/links` desktop text is `text-xs`, with no per-page micro size tokens. ## 2026-10-29: scroll-typed headings, lazy loading and e2e coverage

- **2026-10-29** · Reading views, error states, no-JavaScript recovery and mobile reflow run in desktop and mobile Chromium and WebKit. Recruiter reading and recovery must work on phones and constrained browsers; the route-coverage plan requires deliberate mobile/WebKit filters. Reversible: remove these four suites from `CROSS_BROWSER_SPECS`.

- **2026-10-29** · About owns the Responsibilities and retry-code scroll regions. Both are keyboard focusable with distinct accessible names; scroll-label validation maps both to the About parent. Responsibilities retains responsive row wrapping and horizontal overflow access. The Profile manual retains its own named keyboard scroll region. Reversible: remove the region attributes and parent mapping together.

- **2026-10-29** · **Module headers self-type via CSS scroll-driven animation; `html, body` switch from `overflow-x: hidden` to `overflow-x: clip`.** `components/responsive/Module/Module.tsx` wraps each header span in `.module-scroll-type` with an inline `--module-type-chars` count; `app/css/components-scroll-type.css` reveals the text with `animation-timeline: view()`, `animation-range: entry 0% entry 80%` and `steps(var(--module-type-chars), end)` on a clip-path, plus a `::after` caret that is as wide as the text box plus the caret, with the block painted by a gradient at its right edge and slid by `transform: translateX` in the same steps (percentages resolve against the caret's own box (text plus caret width), so it tracks the box including the header's letter-spacing, where a `chars * 1ch` offset parked it short; `left` was rejected because animating it forces layout on every scroll tick for ~18 headers), and blinks four times from page load on the document timeline, then holds solid (an endless blink would breach WCAG 2.2.2). All of it sits inside `@supports (animation-timeline: view())` plus `prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference` plus `body:not([data-motion="reduce"])`, so unsupported engines, reduced motion and the MOTION toggle each get the fully visible static header; text stays in the DOM and accessibility tree (paint-only animation). Two non-obvious causes are load-bearing: `hidden` on `overflow-x` makes `body` a scroll container (its `overflow-y` computes to `auto`) that never scrolls, so `view()` binds to the wrong scroller and the timeline freezes; `clip` clips identically without creating one. And a `content-visibility: auto` section far offscreen leaves the timeline unresolved, computed clip-path `none`, which is the fully-visible fallback and resolves well before the header enters the viewport. Rejected: per-keystroke JS typing (adds client JS to an RSC-only header) and `overflow-x: hidden` on a wrapper (same wrong-scroller trap one level down). _Reversible: delete the keyframes and rules in `app/css/components-scroll-type.css`, the wrapper spans in `Module.tsx`, and restore `overflow-x: hidden` in `base.css`._

- **2026-10-29** · A barrel next to a `*Lazy` wrapper exports only the wrapper. `components/client/InteractiveShell/index.ts` and `components/client/ContactForm/index.ts` also re-exported the real `'use client'` components; the RSC sections import through those barrels, so Turbopack registered the real modules as client references of `/` and put their chunks in the first-load set despite `ssr: false`. Exporting only the Lazy wrappers took home from 162.3 KB to 158.6 KB total, and dropping the same re-export from `components/responsive/MatrixRain/index.ts` took it to 157.7 KB; tests import the real component from its own file, and `__tests__/lazy-barrels.test.ts` fails when a barrel exports anything but its wrapper. _Reversible: re-add the exports; the gate will show the cost._

- **2026-10-29** · MatrixRain loads with `next/dynamic` (`ssr: false`) from `AppShell` instead of a static import. The canvas is fixed, behind content and `pointer-events: none`, so deferring it cannot shift layout; the animation still runs once the chunk arrives. Measured: home first-load 164.3 KB to 162.3 KB, which is the headroom the redesign islands (RoleTyper, contact, shell states, rail, palette trigger) need under the 164.7 KB gate. _Reversible: restore the static import; the component is unchanged._

- **2026-10-29** · **`parseFeed` normalizes YouTube feed thumbnail shard hosts (any single-digit `iN.ytimg.com`) to the canonical `i.ytimg.com` origin.** The RSS feed serves `<media:thumbnail>` from per-video CDN shards, while `images.remotePatterns` allows exactly `i.ytimg.com`; unnormalized URLs 400 in `/_next/image`, failing Lighthouse errors-in-console on `/links` and `/links/en` and pushing the LCP candidate off the priority thumbnail. Normalizing in the parser keeps the allowlist at one exact host instead of widening `remotePatterns` to a wildcard, and all shards serve identical `/vi/**` bytes. _Reversible: widen `remotePatterns` to `**.ytimg.com` and drop `canonicalThumbnailUrl`._

- **2026-10-29** · **`tests/e2e/` is in `UI_PATHS`, next to `tests/visual/`.** `APP_PATHS` already covers every spec through `tests/`, so a PR that changed only an e2e spec armed `e2e-functional` but set `ui=false` and skipped the visual jobs. Each pathspec now appears once per list, and the manifest test keeps every literal `UI_PATHS` entry under an `APP_PATHS` entry. Reversibility: drop `tests/e2e/` from `scripts/detect-changes-paths.mjs`; the path tests in `scripts/__tests__/detect-changes-paths.test.ts` then need the e2e case removed.

## 2026-10-29 — the project no longer pins a Claude Code permission mode

- **2026-10-29** · **The project baseline no longer pins `defaultMode` and inherits the user's global permission mode.** The owner runs one global mode across repositories; a project pin overrode it. Keeping `acceptEdits` pinned per project was rejected. _Reversible: restore `"defaultMode": "acceptEdits"` in `.claude/settings.json`._

## 2026-10-28: section anchors, contact limits and guitar presentation

- **2026-10-28** · `sec-readme` is the About section id: the section registry, Dock and Footer target it as a top-level section, so it keeps its mobile order rule. `HeroStats` takes `stats` and its accessible `label` as required props: the hero tiles (r106 claims) and `heroStats` in perf-receipts (CV and summary claims) are two authored sets, so the caller supplies both the data and its accessible name. Reversible: restore the rule and the content defaults.

- **2026-10-28** · **Community tags belong in Career (`sec-git-log`), alongside `cert/*` and `edu/*`.** About contains Whoami, Now, Responsibilities, Stack and retry-code panels. `content/community.ts` supplies the community content. _Reversible: move the community tag block back into About and update the Career and About tests._

- **2026-10-28** · **RoleTyper stays: animations are core.** It mounts in the About `Open to [X] roles` line, where cycling Senior, Staff and Principal names target roles. The Hero title stays static because cycling it would misstate the real title. The server HTML carries `[Senior]` plus an sr-only `Senior` and the full line; a `min-w-[9em]` slot prevents layout shift; typing is mount-time and `motionchange` or reduced motion stops it. The h1 stays the name only. About and `RoleTyper` tests hold the markup and the loop. _Reversible: replace the island in `About.tsx` with the static `whoami.role` text and delete `components/client/RoleTyper`._

- **2026-10-28** · The topbar link formerly labelled `03_DEPS` points at `/#sec-readme` as `03_STACK` (label and href in `content/nav.ts`), because the About Stack panel replaced the removed npm-stack section. `content/npm-stack.ts` and `content/now.ts` are deleted with their schemas: nothing rendered them, and the live Now rows are `aboutContent.now.rows`. A home-page test fails any topbar `/#` link whose id no section renders. _Reversible: restore the files and the section from git._

- **2026-10-28** · **Contact uses shared limits with Zod-free client checks and a server-only schema.** `content/contact-copy.ts` owns the limits because client copy interpolates them. `lib/contact-validation.ts` re-exports the limits and applies them in `ContactSchema`; the route consumes that schema rather than maintaining another shape. Client copy reads email and LinkedIn constants from `content/social-urls.ts`, with external Zod validation in `content/_validate-client-content.ts`. This preserves the client-content boundary without spending the route's JavaScript headroom on Zod. _Reversible: restore schema-based client checks only after recovering enough route JavaScript headroom; server validation remains mandatory._

- **2026-10-28** · `#sec-ai-metrics` and `#sec-live-perf` are nested anchors inside Measured; `#sec-perf-receipts` is nested inside Projects. These anchors are outside the page column's flex children, so mobile `order` rules have no effect. All three belong in `KNOWN_UNORDERED` of `scripts/check-section-order.mjs`. _Reversible: if an anchor becomes a top-level section, give it an `order` rule and drop it from the set._

- **2026-10-28** · **GuitarSection uses one responsive presentation, superseding the 2026-06-13 C2 fallback decision.** A single RSC tree renders INPUT → AMP → OUT across viewports, with CSS controlling layout. Removing the UA variant swap removes the differing-height replacement that caused the guitar layout shift. GuitarSection needs neither a UA boundary nor a Suspense fallback. _Reversible: restore the desktop/mobile components, UA boundary and fallback together; remeasure mobile CLS before restoring the variant swap._

- **2026-10-28** · `scripts/check-client-naming.mjs` accepts `app/**/error.tsx` and `app/global-error.tsx` without the `.client` suffix: Next requires those exact names and a `'use client'` directive, so the naming rule cannot apply there. The error-page UI lives in `components/ErrorPages/ErrorPages.client.tsx`, apart from `ErrorBoundary`, because every home section mounts the boundary and it must not pull the design-system barrel or error copy into the home chunk; `content/errors.ts` is a typed, zod-free module validated in `content/_validate-client-content.ts`. Reversible: drop the regex and rename once Next accepts `error.client.tsx`.

- **2026-10-28** · **`pnpm ask:eval` meters the real `/api/ask` handler against a bounded in-memory budget store.** Since the fail-closed reservation (2026-10-24), a run with no Redis credentials, as in the `ai-eval` CI job, got HTTP 503 on every corpus item. `lib/rate-limit.ts` now routes `reserveBudget` and `settleBudget` through a `BudgetStore`; the default is the Redis store, unchanged and fail-closed. Only `scripts/ask-eval.ts` calls `setBudgetStore(createEvalBudgetStore(ASK_EVAL_CORPUS.length))`, which allows exactly one reservation per corpus item. Rate limiting and dedup already fail open, so they need no seam. Rejected: an env flag, header or query parameter that skips the budget (a bypass reachable in production), and a Redis stub in CI (it hides the real reservation path). Cost: the eval no longer spends the production monthly counter, even when Redis credentials are present. `ASK_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS` in `lib/rate-limit.ts` duplicates the route's `MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS`; `__tests__/ask-eval-budget.test.ts` fails when the route reserves more than the store allows. _Reversible: revert `setBudgetStore`, `createEvalBudgetStore` and the one call in `main()`; the reservation then reads Redis directly as before._

## 2026-10-28 — relative video ages use the cached feed timestamp

- **2026-10-28** · `/links` relative video ages use the timestamp captured with the cached channel feed. The existing explicit `cacheLife` refreshes data and reference time together, keeping both locale routes and their priority thumbnail prerendered under Cache Components. Labels describe the cached snapshot, with hourly background revalidation and one-day expiry. Request-time `connection()` would delay this content beyond the static shell. _Reversible: move only relative labels behind Suspense with `connection()` if request-time freshness becomes necessary._

## 2026-10-24: contact delivery and budget reservation

- **2026-10-24** · Contact submission returns 200 only when Resend accepts delivery. Provider errors and 10s timeouts return `202 queued` after durable outbox persistence, with a LinkedIn alternative. A daily 04:00 UTC cron retries at most 3 times within 7 days, preserving the 90-day KV audit record after success, expiry or exhaustion. Hobby cron frequency requires a daily schedule. Both routes share send configuration and a message-id idempotency key; logs retain only request and message ids. _Reversible: revert the delivery routes, form states and cron entry together; existing KV audit messages remain available._

- **2026-10-24** · **Budget reservation fails closed on Redis errors, and MCP POST bodies are bounded before JSON parsing.** `/api/ask` uses its existing HTTP 503 budget-rejection JSON and never starts a model call without a reservation. Rate limiting and dedup remain fail-open. `withBodyLimit` on `/api/mcp` delegates accepted bodies to the MCP transport, preserving JSON-RPC and SDK Zod validation outside `defineHandler`. Its 4096-byte limit fits a 500-character question with six-byte JSON escapes and the envelope. Both declared length and streamed bytes are checked; oversized streams are cancelled and receive HTTP 413. _Reversible: revert the reservation fallback and unwrap `withBodyLimit` from the route; this restores uncapped outage spend and unbounded MCP parsing. The body limit can be adjusted independently._

## 2026-10-24: claude-review gathers evidence with the review orchestrator, and holds no tool that can write, run commands or read credentials

- **2026-10-24** · **The CI reviewer follows the `pr-review-orchestrator` skill's CI profile, loaded from the default branch, and reads only snapshots.** The skill is extracted from the default branch to `/tmp/review-skill` because a sub-PR's base can be an integration branch that edited it. PR metadata, the diff and check results are written by a workflow step before the review (inline: the root checkout is the base branch, which may not have a script yet, and the workflow file is already the trusted unit). The reviewer gets `Read`, `Grep`, `Glob`, `Task` and the inline-comment tool, and `--disallowedTools` denies the edit, git and push tools the action's tag mode adds, every `Bash` command, web tools and reads of credential paths. Rejected: installing PR dependencies in the job (pnpm loads a PR `.pnpmfile.cjs` even with `--ignore-scripts`) and granting `gh` commands (`--jq` reads the environment, `--repo` reaches the network). Subagents cannot post: the built-in `Task` types that inherit every tool are denied, and workers run as `review-worker` (`Read`, `Grep`, `Glob`), so the main thread's refute-and-challenge step stays the only path to an inline comment. A failed `gh pr view` or `gh run list` in the snapshot step writes an `UNAVAILABLE` marker; a failed `gh pr diff` fails the job, since a review with no diff could only approve blind. Only `Task(review-worker)` is allowed. Cost: dependency changes are reviewed from the manifest and lockfile only, with installed source left to the local orchestrator. Reversible: revert the workflow, prompt and `review-worker` agent together.

## 2026-10-24: MCP subscriptions are disabled

- **2026-10-24** · **`/api/mcp` sets `maxSubscriptions` to the named constant `MCP_MAX_SUBSCRIPTIONS = 0`.** The tools never change, so subscriptions provide no useful notifications. The default permits 1024 long-lived SSE streams outside the shared rate limiter and token budget, consuming function time and delaying shutdown. mcp-handler 2.3.0 forwards zero unchanged to `@modelcontextprotocol/server` 2.4.0, which rejects `subscriptions/listen` before allocating an SSE stream with JSON-RPC error `-32603`, `Subscription limit reached`. _Reversible: remove the option to restore the SDK default; enabling subscriptions requires a bounded lifetime and abuse controls._

- **2026-10-24** · **Retain `chromium-bidi>zod: ^4.6.7` with its incompatible invalid-input path confined to unused tooling.** Commit `d696c1b` introduces the override to clear the audit gate; removing it restores chromium-bidi 14.0.0's declared `zod: ^3.24.1` branch. Its protocol parser reads `parseResult.error.errors`, which Zod 4 removes, so invalid input throws a `TypeError` instead of `InvalidArgumentException`. This repository reaches chromium-bidi only through the development dependency chain `@lhci/cli > lighthouse > puppeteer-core`; neither Lighthouse config specifies a `puppeteerScript`, LHCI launches Chrome through chrome-launcher, and Playwright runs its own browser automation. No production route imports BiDi, and no configured test or audit run invokes its protocol parser. _Reversible: remove the override after the audit findings permit Zod 3, or upgrade chromium-bidi to a Zod 4-compatible parser before enabling Puppeteer BiDi._

- **2026-10-24** · **Leave `proxy-agent` at 6.5.0 despite `@puppeteer/browsers` 3.2.6's optional `>=8.0.1` peer.** The `@puppeteer/browsers: ^3.2.6` override removes extract-zip from Lighthouse's development dependency chain. Its HTTP helper optionally imports `ProxyAgent` for browser downloads and falls back to Node agents if the import or construction fails. LHCI uses chrome-launcher without a Puppeteer script, and Playwright manages its own browsers, so this repository does not use that download path. A global proxy-agent override would also change LHCI's direct dependency for an unused optional integration. _Reversible: add a scoped compatible peer override and verify proxied downloads if Puppeteer-managed browser downloads become necessary._

## 2026-10-23: the Node floor rises to 22.22.2

- **2026-10-23** · **`engines.node` is `^22.22.2 || ^24.15.0 || >=26.0.0` and `.nvmrc` pins 22.23.4.** jsdom 30 declares exactly that range, and vitest 5 runs on it; the old `>=22.0.0` let a contributor on the previous `.nvmrc` (22.19) install a test stack that refuses their runtime. CI reads `.nvmrc`, so CI moves with it. _Reversible: pin jsdom 29 and restore `>=22.0.0`._

## 2026-10-23: framework floor re-measured at 131.9 KB; route total tightens to 164.7 KB

- **2026-10-23** · A bare hello-world app (`app/layout.tsx` + `app/page.tsx`) on Next 16.4.3, React/React DOM 19.3.1 and TypeScript 6.0.3 measures 131.9 KB on `/` and `/_not-found`.
