Built with Node 22.23.4 and `next build` (default Turbopack), it uses the same method as `scripts/check-route-js.mjs`: sum `zlib.gzipSync` sizes of each route's `.js` `firstLoadChunkPaths` in `.next/diagnostics/route-bundle-stats.json`, divided by 1024. The measured floor falls from 142.2 KB, so the total becomes 164.7 KB to preserve the 32.8 KB app-owned allowance. Keeping 175 KB would widen that allowance by 10.3 KB without reducing app code. _Reversible: re-measure after a framework change and adjust the floor and total together, preserving the app-owned allowance._

## 2026-10-23: the audit gate ignores nothing again

- **2026-10-23** · **`pnpm.auditConfig.ignoreGhsas` is deleted: extract-zip is gone from the graph, so the 2026-08-30 ignore of `GHSA-jmr9-qjv8-65gv` has nothing left to cover.** `@puppeteer/browsers` 3 replaced extract-zip with modern-tar, and a `"@puppeteer/browsers": "^3.2.6"` override moves lighthouse's puppeteer-core onto it. The repository's Lighthouse config uses no puppeteer script; lhci runs Chrome through chrome-launcher, and lighthouse loads with the override in place. This also clears a second unpatched extract-zip advisory (`GHSA-7pqw-9j4j-h8q3`) that the 2026-08-30 entry would have had to re-argue. `__tests__/audit-ignore-scope.test.ts` now asserts an empty ignore list and no extract-zip snapshot in the lockfile. The same change floors `basic-ftp` (>=6.2.1) and `browserslist` (>=4.28.7), pins `vite` ^8.3.5 as a root dev dependency (vitest's peer was resolving 8.0.16), and points `chromium-bidi>zod` at ^4.6.7 (chromium-bidi is loaded only by puppeteer scripts, which this repository does not run). _Reversible: removing the overrides brings back the advisories and the red audit gate._

## 2026-10-23: MCP moves to mcp-handler 2 at a fixed /api/mcp route

- **2026-10-23** · **The MCP server is served only at `/api/mcp`, on stateless Streamable HTTP, through mcp-handler 2 and `@modelcontextprotocol/server` 2.** mcp-handler 2 no longer filters the pathname, so the old `app/api/[transport]` dynamic segment would have answered MCP at any `/api/<anything>` path that no other route claims. A fixed route makes the served surface exactly the one `public/.well-known/agent.json` advertises. The legacy HTTP+SSE transport at `/api/sse` and Redis-backed MCP sessions are gone with v1; the route still accepts 2025 Streamable HTTP clients through the handler's stateless compatibility path, and `__tests__/agent-surfaces.test.ts` holds both the 2025 initialize handshake and the 2026 `server/discover` path. _Reversible: pinning mcp-handler 1 and the v1 SDK restores the dynamic route; any client that adopted the 2026 protocol in the meantime would lose `server/discover`._

## 2026-08-30 — site-wide pt-BR/EN is in scope, and the shape it has to take

- **2026-08-30** · **Site-wide bilingual pt-BR/EN is now in scope, as the exception `CLAUDE.md`'s "Out of scope (unless asked)" list provides for.** The list reads "unless asked" and this was asked for directly. The scope is a translation of one site into two languages, not an i18n framework: no translation library, no message catalogue, no request-time language negotiation, no request-intercepting middleware. A page's language is determined by its address alone, so two visitors opening the same address see the same language regardless of browser or history. This extends the 2026-08-30 `/links` decision below from one page to the site, and inherits every refusal that decision made. _Reversible: the second language is additive; deleting the second locale's routes and content restores a single-language site. The address scheme is the part that is not cheaply reversible once external links accrue against it, which is why the bare address is pinned in the next bullet._
- **2026-08-30** · **The bare `https://www.erikunha.dev/` must keep returning a page, never a redirect, whatever address scheme the site adopts.** `public/erik-cunha-cv.pdf` embeds that exact address and has already been distributed to recruiters; it cannot be reissued, and neither can a screenshotted QR from the bio page. This eliminates the otherwise-cleanest scheme, which prefixes both languages and demotes the bare address to a 308: that would insert a redirect round trip in front of the route the mobile LCP budget is tightest on, on the traffic that matters most, and would make the site's own advertised canonical a URL that redirects, which is the mistake the 2026-07-13 canonical decision exists to fix. Checked rather than assumed: the address is present in the PDF's decompressed content streams. _Reversible in principle, but only for as long as no new external artifact accrues against a new address; treat it as fixed._
- **2026-08-30** · **Each language variant names itself as its own preferred version. The pair does not share one.** Translations are not duplicates, and canonicalising pt and en onto one URL removes the other language from search results, which is the inverse of the goal. The reciprocal relationship is carried by the alternates plus one default for unmatched languages, which is what `app/links/_lib/metadata.ts` already does correctly. This is recorded because the natural English phrasing of the requirement ("name a single preferred version so the two are not treated as duplicates") describes a canonical tag's job and reads as an instruction to do exactly the harmful thing. A related trap is already in this log at 2026-07-13: the root layout sets an **absolute** canonical, which every child inherits unless it overrides, and that is what canonicalised all five design-system pages onto the homepage. Any new locale route that forgets to override inherits it too. _Reversible: it is metadata; the risk is the indexing lag after a wrong value ships, not the edit._
- **2026-08-30** · **The delivery headroom for this feature is ~3KB, not the 33KB the budget's headline suggests, and recovering more is a prerequisite rather than an optimisation.** `/` measures 172.2KB against the 175KB per-route limit; 32.8KB is what the 142.2KB framework floor leaves, and 30.0KB of it is already spent. Three client islands import from `content/` directly (`InteractiveShell`, `ContactForm`, `Footer.client`), so any shape holding both languages in one module ships both languages into those chunks. Having those islands receive the active language's strings as props from their server parent moves those bytes into the RSC payload, which the per-route JS gate does not measure, and buys margin back before the feature spends any. _Reversible: the prop threading is a refactor with no user-visible behaviour; it stands on its own merits either way._
- **2026-08-30** · **Two language-dependent security filters and one content gate go vacuous when Portuguese content arrives, and are fixed in the same change that adds it.** `lib/ask/injection.ts` matches English override phrasings only. `lib/ask/output-guard.ts` `LEAK_MARKERS` are six English substrings lifted verbatim from the system prompt, so instructing the model to answer in Portuguese makes a leaked prompt come back translated and match none of them, turning the layer-1 stream guard into a no-op for exactly the case this feature creates. `__tests__/metric-consistency.test.ts` admits new content by directory scan but matches on English keywords (`/latency/i`, `/page[\s-]*load/i`, `/onboarding/i`), so a Portuguese content subtree joins the corpus and matches nothing: the contradiction sweep would pass over the whole of it while appearing to run. All three fail open and all three read as healthy while doing so, which is why they are written down before the work rather than found after it. _Reversible: each is a widened pattern list; reverting restores English-only coverage, which is only correct while the content is English-only._
- **2026-08-30** · **The question-answering endpoint keeps one cached instruction prefix, byte-identical across languages, with the answer language carried in the per-question turn.** A per-language system prompt splits the ephemeral cache into two entries, each paying full creation cost on a short expiry, against a fixed `MONTHLY_TOKEN_BUDGET` of 3,000,000; concatenating both languages into one prefix roughly doubles the cached prefix, billed above input rate on creation. Keeping one prefix also keeps one prompt-version hash in the logs and one evaluation baseline. For the same budget reason the factual evaluation corpus stays English and language coverage is added as a small separate set asserted deterministically, rather than by doubling a corpus whose calibration is the accuracy baseline the change is measured against. _Reversible: the language instruction is one line in the user-turn wrapper._

## 2026-08-30 — the /links bio page, and the three choices inside it that needed recording

- **2026-08-30** · **`/links` and `/links/en` ship as two prerendered routes, not one route with a client language toggle.** `CLAUDE.md` lists i18n under "Out of scope (unless asked)"; this was asked for, and the scope is deliberately one page rather than an i18n framework: there is no message catalogue, no locale negotiation and no middleware. Each locale is a route with its own canonical, its own OG metadata, `alternates.languages` plus `x-default`, and a `lang` attribute on the page subtree so a screen reader switches voice. The language control is therefore a link carrying `aria-current="page"`, not a button carrying `aria-pressed`: `aria-pressed` on a link is invalid ARIA and would fail the axe gate that holds Lighthouse accessibility at 100. Adding a second locale is a third route and a third column in the content object; adding a *language framework* is a separate decision this one does not make. _Reversible: delete `app/links/`, `components/links/`, `lib/links/`, `content/links*.ts`; nothing else imports them._
- **2026-08-30** · **YouTube thumbnails route through `next/image` with `images.remotePatterns` for `i.ytimg.com`, rather than widening the CSP.** The alternative was adding `https://i.ytimg.com` to `img-src` in `proxy.ts`, which loosens the policy for **every** page on the site to serve images on one. `next/image` fetches the origin server-side and serves from `/_next/image`, which is same-origin, so the existing `img-src 'self' data: blob:` is untouched. The cost is the `next/image` client runtime on this route; measured at 14.6 KB app-owned of the 32.8 KB budget, so it fits with room. _Reversible: swap to a plain `<img>` and add the host to the CSP, accepting the wider policy._
- **2026-08-30** · **Channel identity lives in `content/links.ts`, not in env; only the Data API key is env.** The channel id and handle are public facts and belong in the Zod-validated content layer per Ch.6, which means the page renders fully with zero configuration. `YOUTUBE_API_KEY` stays in env because it is the only secret. The split has a failure mode worth naming: a wrong channel id returns a **valid but empty** RSS feed, which is indistinguishable from a channel with no videos, so the page would render its empty state forever instead of failing. That trap is written into the schema's regex error message rather than a comment. View count and duration come only from the Data API and are omitted when the key is absent, because the RSS feed does not carry them and inventing them is worse than a shorter card. _Reversible: move the two fields back to env; `getChannelFeed` reads one object either way._
- **2026-08-30** · **`ask:eval` gets its own weekly workflow (`.github/workflows/ask-eval.yml`) instead of a schedule on `ci.yml`.** The AI_METRICS section reads `ask:eval:latest` from Upstash and prints `LAST_RUN` with no staleness guard, while the job that writes that key ran only inside `ci.yml`'s `ai-eval`, path-filtered by `detect-changes` to AI-relevant diffs. The numbers therefore aged whenever AI files went untouched, and a quality section stamped months old reads as an abandoned project on the page a recruiter opens first. A schedule on `ci.yml` would fire the entire pipeline weekly, so this follows the precedent set by `agent-eval.yml` on 2026-06-19: separate file, `workflow_dispatch` plus `schedule`, non-blocking, one hour ahead of the agent eval. _Reversible: delete the workflow; `ci.yml`'s `ai-eval` job is untouched and still gates PRs._

## 2026-08-30 — the audit gate ignores exactly one advisory, and the reason is itself gated

- **2026-08-30** · **`pnpm.auditConfig.ignoreGhsas` gains `GHSA-jmr9-qjv8-65gv` (extract-zip, unvalidated symlink path traversal, high), and nothing else.** `CLAUDE.md` names "add the rule to an ignore list" as an unacceptable response to a failing gate, so this entry exists to argue why this is the documented exception rather than the thing that rule forbids. **The advisory has no fix.** npm reports `Patched versions: <0.0.0`, meaning no released version resolves it; there is no upgrade to take, so there is no measured property left to move. The rule's target is suppressing a finding you could fix, and its stated alternative is followed here: escalate with root cause, options, and the minimum change. **Reachability is dev-only.** extract-zip arrives at `@lhci/cli > lighthouse > puppeteer-core > @puppeteer/browsers > extract-zip`, where it unpacks browser archives fetched from Google's CDN over HTTPS at test time. Exploiting it requires controlling that archive; no request from the site reaches it and it is never in the production bundle. **Why the alternatives lose.** Dropping `@lhci/cli` trades a security gate for the Lighthouse perf gate. Waiting for `@puppeteer/browsers` to migrate leaves every pull request in the repository red for an unbounded period, because `pnpm audit --audit-level=moderate` fails on a high. Raising the level to `high` would hide the eleven moderate advisories as well, which is strictly worse than ignoring one id. **The ignore is scoped by id, never by severity or package**, so a second extract-zip advisory would still fail the gate. **The justification is held by a gate rather than by this paragraph.** `__tests__/audit-ignore-scope.test.ts` asserts the list contains exactly this one entry, that every entry is a GHSA id rather than a wildcard, and — the load-bearing one — that extract-zip is absent from the production dependency closure computed from `pnpm-lock.yaml`. The dev-only half of the argument is therefore mechanically true or the suite is red. Both properties were mutation-tested: seeding extract-zip into the lockfile's root `dependencies` reds the closure test, and appending a second GHSA reds the scope test. The remaining half, "no patched version exists", is the half a test cannot hold offline; Dependabot is the re-check, and the entry should be deleted the moment a patched release or a migrated `@puppeteer/browsers` appears. _Reversible: delete the `auditConfig` block from `package.json` and the test file. Reverting restores a red audit gate until extract-zip is fixed upstream._

## 2026-07-27 — the agent-eval corpus takes over the behaviours the deleted hooks used to hold

- **2026-07-27** · **Three cases added to `evals/agents/`: `pnpm-only`, `no-agent-merge`, `api-edit-security-dispatch`.** Removing the hooks earlier today deleted two eval cases whose subject no longer existed (`claude-review-request`, `architect-gate-respect`) and left the corpus at four. **What went unnoticed in that pass is the opposite move: three behaviours simultaneously became convention-only and got no case at all.** `bash-guard` had blocked npm/yarn and a direct `gh pr merge`; `api-edit-marker` + `api-security-push-guard` had forced a `security-auditor` dispatch before a push. Each of those is now a sentence in `CLAUDE.md` and nothing else. **The corpus changed role rather than shrinking.** While the gates existed, `eval:agents` measured adherence to rules a gate already enforced — useful, but belt-and-braces: `git-add-scoping` could fail and `bash-guard` would still stop the command. With the gates gone, these cases are the only instrument that can tell whether a rule written as prose actually changes behaviour, which is the question the whole harness was built to answer and which now has no backstop underneath it. **One of the two cases deleted with the hooks should not have been.** `architect-gate-respect` was dropped because its subject was the hook, but the RULE it measured survives in `CLAUDE.md` as convention — only the enforcement died. That is precisely the situation this entry argues makes a case MORE valuable, so deleting it inverted the reasoning. It returns as `architect-review-before-plan`, reworded so its `systemText` and `expect` no longer assert a hook blocks anything. `claude-review-request` stays deleted, correctly: that rule disappeared outright rather than becoming convention. **The same drift had reached the judge calibration and nothing caught it:** `cal-architect-gate-respected`'s canonical answer asserted "the architect-gate hook blocks writing-plans without it" as fact, so the gold set used to validate the judge encoded a mechanism that no longer exists — and named the pre-rename `writing-plans` skill while doing it. Both calibration items are corrected. Ratio for the record: 6 cases of which 2 were redundant with gates, then 4, now 8 of which 0 are redundant. **The `@claude` review of this change found the guard against that class was itself too narrow, twice over.** The tier/grader test named the property but hardcoded two case ids, so it could not see `ab-consumer-scan-ci-config` — judgment-tier, code-graded, and passing `"I would NOT check .github/workflows here"` on a bare keyword match. And the corpus test iterated a hand-maintained array that silently omitted `ab-rule-loadbearing`, so a case on disk could escape coverage entirely. **The recommended fix was to convert that case to a judge grader; it was not taken, and the reason is the A/B design.** An A/B case measures a delta between a control and a treatment prompt, so a nondeterministic grader adds variance to the exact quantity being measured and costs runs to recover. The real invariant was never "judgment implies judge" — that is a proxy — it is **"a code grader must never score a decline as a pass"**, and the test now iterates `loadCases()` and requires every code-graded case ON DISK to declare a compliant/declining pair. **Three review rounds went into a shared `declinesWithin` helper before the design was admitted wrong:** a 60-character window let an unrelated decline word excuse a violation ("To avoid any delay, I will run `gh pr merge`"), a non-global match examined only the first occurrence, and once boundaries were added a coordinating conjunction still shielded what followed it ("I never expected this **but** I will run…"). Each fix was the same class one level less punctuated. The root cause was upstream of the helper: an earlier round had widened `no-agent-merge`'s prompt to "or say what you would do instead" so its positive branch was reachable, and grading arbitrary prose with a regex is what made decline-detection necessary at all. **At HEAD the two mechanical cases accept no prose** — both prompts demand the command and nothing else, `no-agent-merge` with an explicit `NONE` — so a banned string IS the violation and both asserts are bare bans with a positive check, importing no helper. `declinesWithin` backs exactly ONE case, `ab-consumer-scan-ci-config`, which is judgment-tier, genuinely needs prose, and is A/B so it needs a deterministic grader; there conjunctions are clause boundaries. Reintroducing a prose-accepting prompt to either mechanical case reopens the conjunction-shield hole, because nothing there is negation-aware any more. Mutation-proven: dropping a probe case into `evals/agents/` reds until it declares one. The judge-grader rule survives with an explicit A/B exemption carrying its reason. **Deliberately NOT made a blocking gate.** `eval:agents` calls a real model through the Gateway, so it is nondeterministic, costed per run, and Monte-Carlo by design (pass@k / pass^k over N runs). Wiring that into PR CI would buy a flaky merge gate and train exactly the `--no-verify` reflex this repo's false-positive budget forbids. It stays weekly `schedule` + `workflow_dispatch`, non-blocking, and the honest statement is that these three behaviours are **measured, not enforced**. **`api-edit-security-dispatch` is marked `knownHard`** because it is the only judgment-tier case of the three: the other two reject a literal command spelling, while this one asks whether the agent volunteers a security review nobody is requiring any more. _Reversible: delete the three directories; the loader is a directory scan, so nothing else needs editing._

## 2026-07-27 — claude-review is restored; the local battery and the hooks are not

- **2026-07-27** · **Owner reversal, same day: `.github/workflows/claude-review.yml`, `.github/claude-review-prompt.md`, `scripts/check-claude-approval.ts` (`pnpm claude-gate`) and the four test files that hold their properties are restored from `186cce3^`.** The reviewer is back to what it was: automatic on every PR open and push, **inline** findings via `create_inline_comment` anchored to changed lines, one call per finding, a complete pass rather than only the most salient issue, closing with a `Verdict:` line the gate parses. **The restore is deliberately partial, and the boundary is the point.** Back: the reviewer and its merge gate. Still gone and staying gone: the local 4-agent battery, `battery-synthesis`, the findings ledger, `review:stamp`, the pre-push stamp check, every `.claude/hooks/*`, `check-gate-health` and the transcript library. Those constrained the AGENT mid-turn or gated the push; claude-review reviews the DIFF after it lands on a PR. Conflating them is what made the earlier removal look like one decision when it was two. **The four test files came back with the workflow, not after it, and that was the load-bearing call.** `claude-review.yml` carries security properties no reader can verify by inspection: the `author_association` fence checked on the auto disjunct in ISOLATION (the issue_comment disjunct has always carried the same three role literals, so scanning the whole `if:` went green while the fence was deleted), the root checkout pinned to the base rather than the PR head, the instruction-shaped-file strip matched by NAME across the subtree, and the verdict guard's `gh api --paginate --slurp` piped to a real `jq` rather than `--jq`, which `gh` refuses outright. Restoring the workflow without the 25 assertions that pin those would have restored a security-sensitive file with every one of its protections unverified — the exact "delegating a claim to a gate that does not exist" failure this log keeps recording. All 67 tests across the four files pass against the restored workflow. **`check-claude-approval.ts` is not optional decoration:** the verdict-guard and prompt-shape tests import `CLAUDE_LOGIN` and `parseClaudeVerdict` from it precisely so the workflow's jq filter and the gate's parser cannot drift apart, and the gate itself fails closed on an Approve that names no head SHA or names a stale one. ****Nine documents written earlier today are falsified by this restore and all nine are corrected** rather than left as the rotted-doc failure mode. **The sweep that found the first seven was itself too narrow, and the review caught it.** It grepped the phrase the removal had introduced (“there is no AI reviewer”), which finds a page that SAYS the reviewer is gone and misses a page whose entire SUBJECT is the removal: `docs/handbook/agents-skills-hooks-mcp.md` carried a heading `## The review toolchain — removed` naming `claude-review.yml` and `check-claude-approval.ts` in a list of casualties, and a mechanical-gates table with `claude-gate` absent. A phrase-shaped predicate cannot see a structure-shaped claim. That is the same lesson as the earlier prune in this log, where a filename predicate found references and missed descriptions, arriving one level up: derive the sweep from what the change makes TRUE, not from the wording the previous change happened to use. The ninth was `docs/handbook/engineering-standards.md` still asserting the deleted battery runs before every PR. **The reviewer alone was not the flow, and restoring it alone would have missed the point.** Reading today's merged PRs rather than the config shows what the flow actually was: #227 ran 8 review rounds and carried 9 inline threads, #229 carried 6, every thread resolved, and the mean comments-per-thread was 2.0-2.1 — which is the measurable signature of “never resolve silently” being followed, since 2 means every finding got exactly one reply. #233 carried 0 threads, the documented carve-out for a PR editing the workflow itself. That loop was not encoded in the workflow; it lived in `.claude/skills/review-convergence`, an 11-step procedure deleted with everything else in #235. **It is NOT restored as a skill.** Owner decision: the flow belongs in the CI/CD harness, not in a load-on-demand procedure. Its one checkable rule is promoted from prose to a gate — `pnpm pr:gate` now fails `silent_resolve` on any resolved thread carrying a single comment, which is exactly the property the measurement above detects after the fact (mean 2.0 comments per thread = finding plus one reply; a 1 means the reply never happened). The rules a gate cannot hold — rebase before every push, verify the pushed SHA, poll the run rather than trusting a green job, do not re-request after a push — live in one `CLAUDE.md` bullet with the rationale in `docs/handbook/review-merge-release.md`, which is the cheapest slot that still fires: gate first, then prose, never a skill nothing dispatches. **Two mutations were needed to prove the new gate, and the second exposed a blind spot the first would have hidden.** Disabling the filter reds a row; reverting the query to `comments(first:1)` did NOT, because every assertion mocks `ghExec` and none could see the query string — at `first:1` each thread returns one comment and every thread reads as a silent resolve. The page size is now pinned by its own assertion. The same pass found the mock emitting `comments` as a bare array while production reads `comments.nodes`: a mock more permissive than the API it doubles, which is the identical trap the `gh` stub hit earlier this month. _Reversible: `git revert`. Reverting removes the reviewer again and re-falsifies those nine documents in the other direction._

## 2026-07-27 — the AI-review gate chain and every agent-runtime hook are removed

- **2026-07-27** · **Owner decision: remove all Claude/Codex gates. Deleted: `.github/workflows/claude-review.yml`, `check-claude-approval.ts` (`pnpm claude-gate`), `review-stamp.ts`, `review-findings.ts`, `transcript-doctor.ts`, `check-gate-health.ts`, `scripts/lib/transcript.mjs`, the `review-battery` / `battery-synthesis` / `review-convergence` skills, all six `.claude/hooks/*` with their `settings.json` wiring and fixture suite, and the pre-push stamp + API-marker blocks.** **What this trades away is stated plainly rather than softened, because the whole point of the layer was that it could not be talked around.** Three properties now have no mechanical holder at all: an agent can run a broad `git add`, a force-push, or `npm` mid-turn (`bash-guard`); an API-surface edit can reach a push with no `security-auditor` dispatch (`api-edit-marker` + `api-security-push-guard`); and `speckit-plan` can run with no architect PASS (`architect-gate`). All three are conventions in `CLAUDE.md` now, and every row that previously claimed enforcement was rewritten to say "convention, not enforced" in those words. **The distinction that survives is git-time versus turn-time.** `.husky/pre-commit` (Biome, gitleaks, commitlint) and `.husky/pre-push` (main-push guard, branch-name, full `pnpm verify`) still constrain *the repository*, and CI still runs the whole chain — lint, types, content schemas, bundle size, route JS, Lighthouse, axe, Playwright, semgrep, gitleaks, CodeQL. What is gone is the layer that constrained *the agent* while it worked. A repo-time gate catches a bad change before it lands; a turn-time gate caught a bad *action* before it happened, and nothing replaces that. **Pre-PR review becomes unverifiable by construction.** The stamp proved four roles were dispatched and every Critical/Important finding was resolved; with the stamp, the ledger and the battery gone, nothing records what a review found, so nothing can check it was addressed. `pnpm pr:gate` survives and still holds thread resolution plus `suspicious_self_resolve` on the PR. **Two orphans went with the chain rather than being left to rot:** `scripts/lib/transcript.mjs` (every consumer — the stamp, the doctor, `architect-gate`, `api-security-push-guard` — was deleted in the same commit) and two agent-eval cases whose subject no longer exists (`claude-review-request`, `architect-gate-respect`). **The prose sweep is the expensive half and was run to zero across 25 files** — `CLAUDE.md`, `STANDARDS.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `.claude/rules/api-boundary.md`, `.claude/agents/architect-reviewer.md`, two skills and seventeen docs, including four mermaid diagrams and the two handbook pages whose entire subject was the deleted machinery. Dated `DECISIONS.md` entries were NOT edited: they are append-only history and describe what was true when written. _Reversible: `git revert`. Reverting restores the hooks and the review chain together; restoring only the hooks would leave `check-gate-health` gone and nothing verifying their wiring, which is the state that produced the dead `css-token-guard`._

## 2026-07-27 — the bespoke PR-gate wrappers are deleted; the underlying gates stay

- **2026-07-27** · **`ready-for-pr`, `validate-pr-body`, `ready-to-merge`, `review:converge` and `check:mermaid` are deleted, along with the scripts only they called: `pr-metrics.ts`, `check-branch-protection.ts`, and the `mermaid` devDependency.** Owner decision, and the distinction that makes it safe is that **every one of these was a wrapper, not a gate.** `ready-for-pr` ran `ci:local` + `bundle-check` + `route-js-check` + `gates:runtime`; all four survive and are still invoked directly. `ready-to-merge` ran `ci:local` + branch protection + `claude-gate` + thread resolution; `pnpm claude-gate` survives as the merge gate, and branch protection was always enforced by GitHub itself — the local check merely re-read it, and re-reading a server-side invariant locally is not enforcement. `review:converge` computed a next-action from `gh` state that `gh pr view --json reviewThreads` and `pnpm claude-gate` answer directly. **No measured property lost a gate; what was lost is the single command that bundled them**, which is a real ergonomic cost on a solo repo and was accepted as such. **`check:mermaid` is the one deletion that removes a check rather than a wrapper, and it earned it by failing the job it existed for.** Hours earlier in this same branch, deleting two edges from a flowchart left `auto --> human` pointing at a node no longer defined; `check:mermaid` passed, because `mermaid.parse` auto-creates an undefined node id rather than erroring. It validated syntax, and the defect class in a documentation diagram is semantic — an orphan node, a dangling edge, a box naming deleted machinery. A gate that is structurally blind to its own failure class is the `css-token-guard` pattern with a green checkmark, and the diagram was caught by a reviewer reading it, which is what actually holds this property. **`pr-metrics.ts` and `check-branch-protection.ts` are deleted as orphans, disclosed rather than quietly swept:** each had exactly one caller (`ready-to-merge`), so both became unreachable the moment it went. `check-branch-protection` had a test, which under the earlier prune's criterion would have spared it — a script whose only remaining caller is its own test is dead code with a fixture, and the criterion is about dependents, not about test count. **The `review-convergence` SKILL is KEPT.** The command was a state readout; the skill is the procedure (rebase before every push, reply-before-resolve, thread-not-timeline, poll the run rather than trusting a green job), and every rule in it was learned from a real failure. Deleting the readout does not make the procedure wrong, it makes it manual. _Reversible: `git revert`. The merge path after this is `pnpm ci:local`, `pnpm claude-gate`, and every review thread resolved with a reply; if that proves too easy to half-run, the fix is one new wrapper over those three, not the five that were here._

## 2026-07-27 — ten pieces of harness machinery deleted on an evidence criterion, not a taste one

- **2026-07-27** · **Three slash commands (`merge`, `pr-metrics`, `ready-for-pr`), five hooks (`css-token-guard`, `learning-loop`, `mandated-skill-gate`, `review-converge-reminder`, `section-order-guard`), two skills (`fallow-audit`, `pr-merge-gate`) and `scripts/review-learn.ts` are removed.** The criterion applied was **no fixture AND no recorded catch AND nothing depends on it** — all three, so a hook with a live test fixture survives even if it has never fired, and a hook that has demonstrably caught something survives even with no fixture. Applying that criterion is what kept `bash-guard`, `api-edit-marker`/`api-security-push-guard`, `architect-gate`, `biome-format` and `session-context`, and it is the reason the list is ten rather than everything unwired. **The measurement that motivated it:** `~/.claude/scripts/capability-usage.mjs` over 4,653 transcripts found 88 of 97 skills never invoked once, including four that `CLAUDE.md` mandated. A mandated capability with zero invocations is not a standard, it is a claim about a standard, and it costs a line of every session's context to make. **The honest cost, stated rather than papered over:** two of these hooks were the only mechanical enforcement of a documented ordering — `mandated-skill-gate` blocked `speckit-plan` on `thinking-risk-premortem` and gated the whole Playwright `browser_*` namespace on `web-design-guidelines`. Both orderings are now convention held by `CLAUDE.md` prose alone, and both rows say so in those words rather than continuing to claim enforcement. That is a real downgrade and it was chosen deliberately: a gate whose predicate had never been shown to fire is not evidence of enforcement either, it is an untested claim wearing a gate's costume, and this repo's own rule is that delegating a claim to a gate is only honest if the gate holds it. **`css-token-guard` is the precedent that justifies the whole sweep.** It was found pointing at two scripts deleted in the Tailwind-v4 migration — a dead hook that never fired and never false-fired, indistinguishable from a working one from the outside, which is exactly why `check-gate-health` exists. The CSS property it aimed at is held by `pnpm lint:css-tokens` in `verify` and CI, so removing the hook loses edit-time feedback and no coverage. **The consumer sweep is where this class of change goes wrong, and the first version of it was not sufficient — an earlier draft of this entry claimed it was re-run "to zero", and the battery falsified that.** It was a predicate over a fixed file set — `.claude/settings.json`, `.claude/hooks/__tests__/run.sh`, `package.json`, `.husky/**`, `CLAUDE.md`, `STANDARDS.md`, `docs/**`, the skills, and the `.agents`/`.codex` mirrors — and it did catch what reading did not: `STANDARDS.md` Chapter 7 asserting the Playwright namespace gate, `docs/06-ai-development.md` claiming `css-token-guard` "now points at `scripts/lint-css-tokens.ts`", and `docs/handbook/knowledge-architecture.md` describing a `SessionEnd` hook the repo no longer has. **But its patterns matched deleted FILENAMES, and prose does not have to name a file to describe it.** Five residues inside that same file set survived: two rows documenting `/merge` and `/ready-for-pr` after their command files were deleted, a note asserting four hook events where `settings.json` now wires three, an entire "The learning loop" section describing a `SessionEnd` auto-trigger that cannot run, and four sentences advertising the loop as live self-healing across `docs/README.md`, `docs/handbook/README.md` and `engineering-audit.md`. The mermaid case is the sharpest: deleting two edges left `auto --> human` referencing an undefined node, and `check:mermaid` passed because `mermaid.parse` auto-creates a bare node id — a gate structurally blind to the defect, in the same shape as everything else in this entry. **A filename predicate finds references; only reading finds descriptions, and the lesson is that a deletion sweep needs both.** Dated `DECISIONS.md` entries were deliberately NOT edited: they are append-only history and describe what was true when written. `.review-learnings.md` is untracked and was left on disk. **The Codex mirror subsystem is deleted outright: `scripts/sync-codex.mjs`, its declarations and tests, the `sync:codex` and `check:codex-sync` scripts, the CI gate, and the generated `.codex/`, `.agents/` and `AGENTS.md` trees.** Owner decision: the Codex harness is a local, per-machine concern and never was repo content. Mirroring it made every `.claude/` edit a two-copy edit gated in CI, so a spine change could red `verify` for a reason that had nothing to do with the change. **The sweep is what forced the question.** `pnpm check:codex-sync` reported `OK` while `.codex/hooks/` still held all five deleted guards and `.agents/skills/` held both deleted skills, because every check it ran was source-to-mirror: drift, dangling refs, hook siblings, undisclaimed claims. Nothing walked the mirror back the other way, so a target whose `.claude/` source no longer existed was structurally invisible — and Codex wires hooks by listing that directory, so a guard this repo deleted kept executing there. An `orphanedMirrorFiles` walk was written, tested and mutation-verified against exactly that, and is deleted here with the rest: closing a fail-open in a subsystem that should not exist is a more expensive way of keeping it. **What is retained rather than mirrored:** the `claude-review.yml` strip still removes `AGENTS.md`, `.agents` and `.codex` by NAME from the PR-head checkout, because a pull request can still introduce one and the strip is about the instruction channel, not about this repo's own files. _Reversible: `git revert` restores the script and the generated trees; nothing else depends on them._

**`check-gate-health` did not hold the claim this entry first delegated to it, so the gate was extended rather than the claim weakened.** `auditGates` emitted only `hook->script` and `settings->hook`: both walk a reference to its target, so a hook file present on disk that nothing wires produced no `DeadRef` and the gate printed "all references resolve" — the identical fail-open as the mirror's, in the very gate cited as the defence against it. A third kind, `hook->unwired`, flags any `.claude/hooks/*.sh` unreachable from `settings.json`. Reachability is a **transitive closure starting at `settings.json`**, and both cheaper spellings were tried and rejected under review: matching hook paths in raw file content let a shell comment (`# replaces .claude/hooks/foo.sh`) count as an invocation, and unioning every hook's outbound refs let an unwired hook vouch for the hook it calls, so a dead `a.sh` naming `b.sh` certified `b.sh` as live. Both are the same fail-open this kind exists to close, displaced by one level — prose standing in for a call, then a dead caller standing in for a live one. The sibling exemption survives because a helper invoked only by another hook is legitimately absent from `settings.json`, and a false positive there would train bypass. All six committed hooks pass. Mutation-tested in four directions: disabling the check reds two rows, dropping the sibling union reds one, reverting the closure to a flat union reds one, and removing the comment strip reds one. _Reversible: `git revert`. Restoring any single hook additionally requires re-adding its `settings.json` wiring and its `run.sh` fixture — the hook file alone is inert, and `check-gate-health` now fails on exactly that state._

## 2026-07-27 — the trigger reverts to `pull_request`: Anthropic's token exchange rejects `pull_request_target`

- **2026-07-27** · **`claude-review.yml` triggers on `pull_request` again. Every other change from the `pull_request_target` attempt is KEPT.** `pull_request_target` fires reliably, but Anthropic's token-exchange endpoint refuses the OIDC token minted under it: `App token exchange failed: 401 Unauthorized - Invalid OIDC token`, deterministic across four runs spanning two different checkout layouts, posting zero comments each time. **The OIDC token is obtained successfully** — `getIDToken()` returns, and the failure is at `POST /api/github/github-app-token-exchange` (`src/github/token.ts`) — so this is a server-side policy about the event, not a permissions or configuration error on our side, and not something reachable from this repo. **A hypothesis was falsified in the process, and cheaply, which is the argument for the live test.** The intermediate fix adopted the action's documented base-at-root layout on the theory that checking the PR head out at the workspace root caused the 401, since `docs/security.md` warns against exactly that. It did not: with both checkouts, the materialisation and the strip all passing, the action failed identically. That layout is a real security improvement and it fixed a bug it never had. **`github_token:` would bypass the exchange and was rejected on cost.** `setupGitHubToken()` returns `OVERRIDE_GITHUB_TOKEN` unconditionally when set, skipping the exchange — but the supplied token posts as `github-actions[bot]` rather than `claude[bot]`, and 14 surfaces key on that identity including `scripts/check-claude-approval.ts`, which IS the merge gate. Swapping the reviewer's identity to make a trigger work inverts the cost/benefit. **What the `pull_request` revert costs, precisely:** a PR editing `claude-review.yml` runs a workflow loaded from its own head, the action refuses, and no verdict can exist — so those PRs need one `/claude-review` comment, which runs the default-branch copy. The verdict guard's carve-out is reinstated for exactly that case, and deliberately does NOT skip the `issue_comment` path, which can always post. That is the pre-2026-07-27 status quo, and it is bounded. **What is kept, because none of it depended on the trigger:** base at the workspace root, the PR head confined to `pr-head/` behind `--add-dir`, the strip of instruction-shaped files from the mounted copy, and the default-branch prompt materialisation. Together those close the PR-authored-prompt hole and the instruction-channel hole that existed under `pull_request` before any of this. **One trigger-sensitive trap is now pinned by a test.** Under `pull_request_target` the DEFAULT checkout ref is the base, so omitting `ref:` was correct; under `pull_request` the default is `refs/pull/N/merge`, which is PR-authored content. Leaving the root checkout unpinned across the revert would have silently inverted the entire trust model with no other symptom. The root is pinned to `github.event.pull_request.base.sha`, and mutation-tested: removing the ref reds, pointing it at the head reds. _Reversible: restoring `pull_request_target` reinstates the 401 and reviews nothing. Do not retry it without first confirming upstream accepts that event — the symptom is silent (a failing check and no comment), not loud._

## 2026-07-27 — claude-review adopts the action's documented two-checkout pattern; the root checkout broke it outright

- **2026-07-27** · **The workspace root is checked out at the BASE with no `ref:`, the PR head goes into `pr-head/`, and the reviewer reaches it through `--add-dir pr-head`.** The previous shape checked `refs/pull/N/head` out at the root, and the action failed on **every** run with `App token exchange failed: 401 Unauthorized - Invalid OIDC token` — reproduced on re-run, so deterministic, and it posted zero comments. Net effect: after the `pull_request_target` switch landed, NO pull request could be reviewed at all, which is worse than the manual-comment state it replaced. **The action documents this exactly and I did not read it.** `docs/security.md` at the pinned SHA says "Do not check out an untrusted ref into the workspace root before this action", and prescribes base-at-root plus head-in-a-subdirectory via `--add-dir`; the action expects a git repo at the root and restores project configuration from the base ref. I designed the trigger change from first principles and never checked the vendor's own guidance for the pattern I was adopting, which is the same class as hand-rolling a push predicate next to an AST detector that already existed. **The layout and the prompt's provenance are ORTHOGONAL, and a revision of this entry wrongly treated them as alternatives.** It claimed root-at-base made `.github/claude-review-prompt.md` "the default-branch copy by construction" and deleted the `git show FETCH_HEAD` materialisation on that basis. False: under `pull_request_target` the root is the PR's BASE, which is not the default branch on a sub-PR into an integration branch — a flow `CLAUDE.md` documents. Once one sub-PR landed a prompt edit in `feat/x`, every later `feat/x-part` review would have loaded the prompt that branch authored. The battery caught it. Both mechanisms are now present: the layout keeps untrusted content out of the workspace root, and the materialisation makes the prompt the DEFAULT branch's copy. **A second finding closed a hole the layout opened:** `--add-dir pr-head` mounts PR-authored content as project scope, so `pr-head/CLAUDE.md`, `pr-head/AGENTS.md` or `pr-head/.claude/**` would arrive on the INSTRUCTION channel rather than the data channel, with prompt prose as the only defence. A step now deletes them and VERIFIES the deletion with the SAME predicate that did the removing — an independently enumerated verify drifts from the `rm` the first time either is edited, and then passes while removing nothing. Matched by NAME across the subtree rather than by fixed root paths: the first version listed seven root paths, so `pr-head/lib/CLAUDE.md` survived AND its verify loop reported success, because it checked the same seven. The reviewer still sees every one of these files in `gh pr diff`, which is where a change to them belongs under review. **Consequence for the prompt, and it is not cosmetic:** the root now holds PRE-change content for every changed file, so the prompt states the split — read conventions from the root, read `pr-head/<path>` for the full content of anything the PR changed, and treat a "convention" present under `pr-head/` but absent at the root as one this PR is proposing rather than one that binds the review of it. **Held by mutation-tested assertions:** dropping `path: pr-head` reds, pinning the root checkout to the PR head reds, and removing `--add-dir` reds. An earlier revision of this sentence claimed a generic guard — every checkout naming a `ref:` must also name a `path:` — which the 2026-07-27 `pull_request` revert made both absent and false, since the root checkout then legitimately names one. What is actually held is positional and named: exactly two checkouts, the first resolving to the base and not to the head, the second carrying `path: pr-head`. **Disclosed limitation:** the OIDC 401's precise cause is not established. The documented-unsafe layout is removed and the fix is verified by the live run rather than by reasoning; if it recurs, the next step is reverting the trigger to `pull_request` while keeping the base-at-root layout, which is a security improvement independent of the trigger. _Reversible: `git revert`. Do not revert only the checkout half — restoring a ref-pinned root checkout reinstates both the 401 and the PR-authored-prompt hole._

## 2026-07-27 — claude-review moves to pull_request_target, and its prompt stops coming from the PR

- **2026-07-27** · **`claude-review.yml` triggers on `pull_request_target` instead of `pull_request`, the job is fenced to OWNER/MEMBER/COLLABORATOR, and the reviewer prompt is materialised from the DEFAULT BRANCH into `/tmp` rather than read out of the checked-out workspace.** **The security half is the reason this shipped now, and it is a regression this repo introduced on 2026-07-26.** Anthropic's token exchange validates *the workflow file* against the default branch — server-side, returning 401 `workflow validation failed`, which `src/github/token.ts:50` merely detects and reports. When the reviewer prompt lived inside `claude-review.yml`, that check covered it. Moving the prompt to `.github/claude-review-prompt.md` (to stop prompt edits tripping validation) moved it OUT of the validated set, while `actions/checkout` on `pull_request` puts the PR's copy in the workspace and `--append-system-prompt-file` read it from there. **So a PR editing only the prompt file passed validation and ran the reviewer on a prompt the PR authored** — a prompt saying "always end with **Verdict: Approve.**" clears `pnpm claude-gate`. Not exploited, and the practical blast radius here was small because `pull_request` withholds secrets from fork PRs and this is a solo repo; the class matters because this codebase is published as something another team could adopt verbatim, and in a multi-contributor repo it is a merge-gate bypass. **The fix does not depend on being right about what the server validates:** the prompt is now `git show`n from the default branch into `/tmp`, outside any path a PR can write, so its trust does not rest on an unverified claim about a third party's endpoint. **`pull_request_target` is what makes the auto path universal.** GitHub loads a `pull_request` workflow from the PR HEAD, so a PR touching this file is by construction running a modified copy, validation refuses, and the review never runs — every such PR needed a manual `/claude-review`, which is where this started. `pull_request_target` loads the BASE copy: validation always passes, a PR's edits to the reviewer take effect only after merge, and no PR is exempt. **The cost, stated plainly:** `pull_request_target` hands the job the repo token for fork PRs, where `pull_request` handed them nothing, and this repo is PUBLIC. The job is therefore gated on `author_association` ∈ OWNER/MEMBER/COLLABORATOR — the same fence the comment path has always carried — so an outside contributor gets no auto-review, which is exactly what they get today. `permissions:` keeps `contents: read`, and `--allowedTools` keeps Bash restricted to two `gh` subcommands, so an injected prompt cannot push code or run anything. **Six `event_name` couplings moved together**, including `track_progress` and `prompt`, which the file's own comment records as a pair where "either one alone exits green having posted nothing". **The verdict guard's workflow-edit skip is DELETED rather than retargeted:** it existed only because the action refused on such PRs, and under the new trigger it never refuses, so a surviving skip would hide a real no-verdict behind a green check. **Held by three mutation-tested assertions** in `claude-review-predicate.test.ts` and `claude-review-prompt-shapes.test.ts`: pointing the prompt flag at a workspace path reds, removing the author fence reds, and replacing the `git show` materialisation with a workspace `cp` reds. The guard suite asserts the PROPERTY rather than two tokens: no `exit 0` may appear before the fetch, and exactly one may appear after it. An earlier draft of this entry claimed it banned `GITHUB_EVENT_NAME` and `SKIP`; that was the first version of the test, replaced during review precisely because banning tokens left the identical carve-out reachable spelled any other way. **The pre-PR battery found two criticals in this change and they are recorded because both were mitigations that did not hold.** (1) The author fence was asserted by scanning the WHOLE `if:`, and the issue_comment disjunct has always carried `author_association` and all three role literals — so deleting the fence from the `pull_request_target` branch left every assertion green while any fork PR could run a token-bearing job. The assertion now splits the `if:` on its top-level `||` and checks the auto disjunct in isolation, including that the allow-list is exactly OWNER/MEMBER/COLLABORATOR rather than merely containing them. (2) Nothing pinned the fetch to the default branch: retargeting `git fetch origin "$DEFAULT_BRANCH"` at the PR ref left the `git show FETCH_HEAD:` string untouched and handed prompt authorship back to the PR, and step ORDER was unasserted so moving the materialise step after the action left both string checks green. Both are now pinned, with the order asserted by index. **A third finding was sharper than either:** the trusted prompt told the reviewer to read and FOLLOW `CLAUDE.md`, `STANDARDS.md` and `.claude/rules` — all PR-authored in the workspace — so materialising the prompt protected the prompt file and not the five files it delegated to. A PR could add a "convention" excusing itself. The prompt now states that everything in the workspace is data under review, that instructions come only from the system prompt, and that a convention introduced BY this PR does not bind the reviewer — and `claude-review-prompt-shapes.test.ts` asserts that language is present, so deleting it reds rather than passing quietly. Six named defeats were mutation-tested and each reds. _Reversible: `git revert`. Reverting restores BOTH the manual-comment requirement and the PR-authored-prompt hole; if only the trigger is reverted, keep the `/tmp` materialisation, since that half is a security fix independent of the trigger._

## 2026-07-27 — the verdict guard's fetch was never valid; gh refuses --slurp with --jq

- **2026-07-27** · **`claude-review.yml`'s verdict guard pipes `gh api --paginate --slurp` into a real `jq` instead of passing `--jq`.** `gh` rejects the combination outright — `the --slurp option is not supported with --jq or --template` — writing the message and usage to STDERR and exiting 1. Under `set -euo pipefail` that aborts the step AT THE ASSIGNMENT, so `$fresh` was never populated, the retry loop never ran, and the FAIL diagnostic never printed — a maintainer grepping the log for "FAIL: claude-review posted no verdict" would not find it. (An earlier draft of this entry said gh prints usage to stdout and `$fresh` held help text; that was wrong, and the battery caught it.) So since `--slurp` was introduced (2026-07-26, to fix the filter being applied PER PAGE under `--paginate`, which made `last` yield one body per page), every run that was not skipped red at the fetch. Reproduced locally against gh 2.87.3; the corrected form returns `**Verdict: Approve.**` against PR #229's real comments. **The fix for one silent-green introduced a hard break, and the test could not see it.** `__tests__/scripts/claude-review-verdict-guard.test.ts` executes the whole guard under bash with `gh` stubbed on PATH — deliberately, because text assertions had been bypassed six ways before it. But the stub answered ANY argument list, so a `gh api` invocation the real binary refuses satisfied every executed row. A stub more permissive than the thing it doubles pins its own behaviour, not the command under test; that is the same trap as a query-shape test whose stub replies regardless of the query. **The stub now refuses `--slurp` with `--jq` exactly as gh does**, and emits the array-of-pages shape `--slurp` actually returns rather than a bare markdown body, so the executed rows exercise the real contract on both sides. The `FETCH` regex is re-aimed at the piped form; it failing is now the signal that someone reintroduced the invalid one. Mutation-tested: restoring `--jq` reds three rows, where previously it reded none. **What this says about the guard's history:** every green it ever reported on a non-skipped run was the skip carve-out, not a verified verdict. #229 merged with a red guard and a genuine Approve, which is the false-positive budget being spent on the gate that guards merges. **The same invalid invocation shipped TWICE and only one copy was found by reading.** The cancelled-run supersede step added 2026-07-26 carries it too, with `|| exit 0` converting gh's refusal into a silent success — so that step has never marked a comment and its ADR entry's claim that it "rewrites this run's claude[bot] progress comment" was false from the day it landed. All four battery roles found it independently; nothing found it by inspection when it was written, because `guardStep()` selects only the step it executes by name. The structural assertion added here therefore scans the WHOLE workflow, joining backslash-continuations first — without that join it sees only each fetch's first physical line and a `--jq` on the continuation is invisible, which is how the first version of this very assertion missed the supersede copy. The stub also now refuses `--slurp` with gh's real predicate (`--jq`, its shorthand `-q`, and `--template`), not just the literal `--jq`. Both mutation-tested: `-q` on the guard reds, reverting the supersede step reds. _Reversible: `git revert`. Do not revert only the workflow half — restoring `--jq` without restoring the permissive stub reds the suite, which is the gate working._

## 2026-07-26 — a cancelled claude-review marks its own progress comment superseded

- **2026-07-26** · **`claude-review.yml` gains an `if: cancelled()` step that rewrites this run's claude[bot] progress comment to say it was superseded.** Cancellation is NORMAL here, not exceptional: the concurrency group is per-PR with `cancel-in-progress: true` precisely so a review of a replaced HEAD cannot outlive the push that replaced it. But a cancelled run cannot finish the checklist comment it posted at job start, so every supersession orphaned one. Three accumulated on #229 and read as three live reviews. **The trigger was a second-order effect of a fix two PRs earlier.** #228 moved the reviewer prompt out of the workflow so prompt edits stop tripping the action's workflow-validation guard; that un-broke the `pull_request` auto-trigger, which had been dead. `.claude/skills/review-convergence` step 2 still said "re-request claude-review after every successful push" — correct while auto was broken, actively harmful once it worked, because the `/claude-review` comment lands seconds after the push and cancels the auto-run already reviewing that SHA. Two runs cancelled, three skipped, and a finding posted quoting a comment the push had just deleted, because the cancelled run had read the pre-fix SHA. The instruction is now inverted (re-request ONLY when no run will otherwise fire) and `review:converge`'s messages say the same. **Marks rather than deletes**, because the run did happen: a reader tracing why a finding cites an older SHA needs to see it was superseded, not find a gap. **Disclosed limitations:** `if: cancelled()` does not run on a hard/forced cancellation, so a force-cancelled run can still orphan a comment; the step has no test, since exercising it needs a real cancelled run in CI; and it uses the same `SINCE`-filtered latest-claude-comment selection as the verdict guard, so it inherits that selection's assumptions. The three pre-existing orphans on #229 were marked by hand with the same text. _Reversible: delete the step. The regression is cosmetic-looking and is not — orphaned checklists read as in-flight reviews and were what made #229's timeline unreadable._

## 2026-07-26 — review:stamp anchors to the branch base, so the battery runs ONCE per PR

- **2026-07-26** · **`scripts/review-stamp.ts` compares battery dispatches against the merge-base with `origin/main` instead of against the HEAD commit's timestamp.** Root cause: `CLAUDE.md` says run the 4-agent battery **ONCE** before opening a PR, while the stamp refused unless all four dispatches POSTDATED HEAD. Fixing a battery finding creates a commit, that commit moves HEAD past the dispatches, and the stamp then demanded a fresh battery — so the only states satisfying both rules were "found nothing" and "fixed nothing". Observed on the branch that produced this entry: three full rounds, twelve Opus dispatches, where the rule budgeted four. **The stamp was asking the wrong question.** "Did the battery run after the last commit" is not the property anyone wants; "did the battery review this branch's work, and were its findings resolved" is. A fix commit is the RESOLUTION of a finding, so treating it as evidence the review is stale inverts the intent. The first half of the real property is now the merge-base anchor; the second half was already there and unchanged — the ledger check still refuses while any Critical/Important finding is `open`, and `invalidResolutions` still refuses a resolution with no SHA. **What is deliberately NOT weakened:** the transcript still fails closed when unresolvable, all four roles are still required, the accept-list still matches on `subagent_type` alone, and a battery predating the branch entirely is still refused (pinned by a test). **What this does give up, stated plainly:** a battery dispatched at the first commit of a branch now stamps a branch whose later commits it never saw. The ledger is what carries that weight, and the ledger is only as good as the findings recorded into it — a boundary `review:stamp` already prints on success ("the stamp cannot know about a finding you never recorded"). The alternative, re-running four Opus agents per fix round, was measured against that risk and lost. `REVIEW_STAMP_BASE` overrides the base ref for a sub-PR into an integration branch. _Reversible: restore the `headCommitIso` anchor in `decideStamp` and `main`. The regression if reverted is loud and immediate — the next branch with a fixable finding cannot stamp without a second battery._

## 2026-07-26 — the review-convergence loop gets a state command and a per-push heartbeat

- **2026-07-26** · **`pnpm review:converge` is added, and `.claude/hooks/review-converge-reminder.sh` runs it after any `git push` on a branch with an open PR.** Owner requirement: the claude-review → fix → reply-in-thread → resolve → re-request cycle must run on every PR until it approves. **The loop was already fully specified and still got skipped, which is the whole point of this entry.** `.claude/skills/review-convergence` step 9 already said "Repeat 4-8 until CI is green AND 0 unresolved threads AND `pnpm ready-to-merge` exits OK", and CLAUDE.md already said to invoke it. On PR #228 the three claude-review findings were fixed and pushed, and the threads were left unanswered and unresolved until the owner asked directly. So the gap was never content — a third prose copy of the loop would have been the third copy that failed. It was **dispatch**: nothing fired at the moment the loop had to re-enter. Per this repo's own slotting rule (mechanical gate > on-demand skill > contextual memory > prose), the fix belongs one rung up from where the rule already lived. **`review:converge` returns the single next action rather than a checklist**, ordered because the steps are not commutative: unresolved threads outrank everything (re-requesting before answering spends a review cycle on a state the author has not responded to), then silent resolves (a resolved thread carrying one comment destroyed the only record linking a finding to its fix), then verdict, then staleness. It reuses `parseClaudeVerdict` / `extractReviewedSha` from `check-claude-approval.ts` rather than re-deriving them, so the loop and the merge gate cannot disagree about how an Approve is PARSED. They could still disagree about WHICH comment to parse, which is why `fetchState` sorts the claude[bot] comments by `created_at` the same way `check-claude-approval.ts` does rather than trusting array order; `evaluateGate` itself is deliberately not shared, since the loop needs a next-action, not a merge verdict. **The hook is advisory on purpose and always exits 0.** PostToolUse runs after the command, so it cannot un-push, and the merge is already gated by `pnpm ready-to-merge`; a non-zero exit would render as a failed hook that stops nothing, which trains the operator to ignore exactly the line that matters. It is also deliberately fail-quiet rather than fail-closed — no branch, no PR, `gh` missing, or a non-push command all exit silently — because a false nudge on every unrelated Bash call spends the budget that makes the real one worth reading. **Validated against real data, not only fixtures:** `fetchState` + `nextStep` against merged PR #228 returns 3 threads, all resolved, minimum 2 comments, approve on `0ae2f0d` → `converged`. The suite covers both sides of the silent-resolve boundary, the `**Verdict:** Approve` near-miss that parses as none, a stale approve, an approve stating no SHA, an abbreviated SHA prefixing HEAD, a SHA sharing a prefix then diverging, a PR with no threads, and `fetchState`'s truncation refusal and `created_at` ordering. No count is written here on purpose: a count goes stale the moment a case is added, and this entry already had that happen once inside its own branch. **The hook's push predicate is pinned by 13 fixtures in `pnpm test:hooks`.** Pinning it needed a small affordance: a real push and an undetected one both exit 0 silently, so the predicate was unobservable from outside and any fixture would have had to re-implement the awk and drift from it. `REVIEW_CONVERGE_DETECT_ONLY=1` makes the hook report the live predicate's answer and stop, so the fixtures exercise the shipped code rather than a copy. They cover five real push forms (`git -C <dir> push`, `/usr/bin/git push`, a compound `ls && git push`) and five non-pushes, plus the advisory contract (every path exits 0, including unparsable input). Mutation-tested against the exact regression they exist for: reverting to the original two independent greps over the whole emitted record set reds both `git commit -m "push the fix"` and `grep -r "git push" .`. _Reversible: drop the `review:converge` script entry, the hook file, and its `settings.json` wiring. The regression is a return to the prior state, where the loop is documented and unenforced._

## 2026-07-26 — the claude-review system prompt moves out of the workflow so prompt changes stay auto-reviewable

- **2026-07-26** · **The ~1,870-word reviewer system prompt moves from an inline `--append-system-prompt` string in `.github/workflows/claude-review.yml` to `.github/claude-review-prompt.md`, loaded with `--append-system-prompt-file`.** Root cause: `anthropics/claude-code-action` refuses to run when the workflow file differs from the copy on the default branch — "Skipping action due to workflow validation" — which is a supply-chain guard, since otherwise any PR could rewrite the reviewer's own prompt and permissions and have the action execute that rewrite with repo credentials. Correct, but with the prompt living **inside** the workflow it meant every prompt edit was unreviewable by the reviewer it edited. Observed on PR #228 run 30221557514: action skipped, zero comments, job green. Splitting the prompt out makes a prompt-only change leave `claude-review.yml` byte-identical to main, so validation passes and the auto path reviews it; only genuine workflow-structure edits still skip, which is the case the guard actually exists for. **`--append-system-prompt-file` was verified behaviourally before adoption, not read off `--help`:** `claude -p "What is 2+2?" --append-system-prompt-file <file>` where the file says to answer only "BANANA" returns `BANANA`, and the same prompt without the flag returns `4`. The action's `claude_args` input passes straight through to the CLI, so no action-side support was needed. **The move is content-identical** — the prompt text is byte-for-byte what the inline string held, so this diff is a relocation and nothing else; restructuring that text into sections is now trivially possible (a `.md` file has real newlines where a folded YAML scalar did not) and is deliberately NOT done here, because that change is unmeasured and would hide inside a mechanical move. **The gate moved with it and gained a second assertion.** `__tests__/scripts/claude-review-prompt-shapes.test.ts` now reads the prompt from the file, but first asserts the workflow actually passes `--append-system-prompt-file .github/claude-review-prompt.md`; without that, repointing the workflow at a different path leaves the suite asserting against a file nothing loads — green while the live reviewer runs with no prompt at all. Both directions mutation-tested: repointing the workflow reds it, drifting the prompt file reds it. **Boundary:** this PR itself edits `claude-review.yml` (it is the move), so the auto path still skips here; the benefit starts with the next prompt-only change. _Reversible: inline the file's contents back into `claude_args` as a folded scalar and restore the test's `claudeArgs()`-based reader. The regression if reverted is silent in CI and loud only later, when the next prompt edit gets no review._

## 2026-07-26 — `pnpm pr-size` is deleted, and PR size stops being a gate

- **2026-07-26** · **`scripts/pr-size.ts`, its test, its `package.json` entry, and its step inside `pnpm ready-for-pr` are deleted, along with every doc and rule that described it.** Owner decision, stated plainly ("drop pr-size honestly, no need for that. remove completely now and forever"). What it measured was a proxy: files, lines, and "subsystems touched" against fixed thresholds, none of which is the property anyone actually cares about, which is whether a change tells one reviewable story. **The trigger was a false positive of exactly that shape.** On this branch it went red on subsystems (6, red ≥5) while files (8) and lines (894) were green and yellow — and two of the six were measurement artifacts rather than concerns: `.agents/skills` is the generated mirror of `.claude/skills`, so every skill edit inflates the count by one automatically, and `scripts` + `__tests__/scripts` were one parser plus the test holding it. A gate that reds on a cohesive change and can only be cleared by writing a justification paragraph is training the bypass it exists to prevent, which is the false-positive budget rule in `CLAUDE.md` applied to itself. **The milestone rule survives the tool.** "Commit in scope blocks, merge by milestone" and the integration-branch-plus-sub-PRs pattern for large features both remain; what is gone is the numeric trigger, so the judgment is now stated as judgment ("does this tell one reviewable story") rather than delegated to a threshold that could not see the difference between a real subsystem and a generated mirror. **Consumer scan, since a stale reference to a deleted script fails silently rather than loudly:** 12 surfaces referenced `pr-size` or `PR_BASE` — the script and its test (deleted), `package.json`, `scripts/ready-for-pr.ts` (the gate block and a now-stale "Gate 3" label, since gate 2 no longer exists), `CLAUDE.md` ×5, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `.claude/hooks/bash-guard.sh`'s pre-merge checklist text, `docs/07-workflows.md` ×3, `docs/handbook/development-lifecycle.md` ×3 including a mermaid node, `docs/handbook/review-merge-release.md` ×2 including the thresholds paragraph, and `.claude/skills/review-battery/SKILL.md`, where the dispatch envelope defined `<BASE>` by delegating to `pr-size`'s resolution order. That last one is the one worth naming: the envelope shipped in this same PR, so deleting `pr-size` without it would have left the battery's diff-range definition pointing at a script that no longer exists — the same fail-open the envelope was added to close. `PR_BASE` is gone with it; the envelope now states the base directly. _Reversible: `git revert`. Nothing depends on the script, so the regression if reverted is only that the threshold returns._

## 2026-07-26 — the battery dispatch becomes a self-contained task contract, and claude-review emits shapes the merge gate can parse

- **2026-07-26** · **The battery dispatch grows a fifth block (the DISPATCH ENVELOPE) carrying the fields a subagent task contract needs and this one lacked, and `claude-review.yml` is corrected to emit a verdict and head SHA in the exact shapes `scripts/check-claude-approval.ts` parses.** **Two live defects drove the claude-review half, both verified by running the real regexes rather than by reading them.** `parseClaudeVerdict` requires the verdict word INSIDE a `**…**` span (`/\*\*[^*]*\bapprove\b[^*]*\*\*/i`) and the prompt never asked for bold anywhere near the verdict line, so `**Verdict:** Approve` — one of the most natural renderings of "end with one verdict line" — renders correctly to a human and reads as `verdict=none` at the merge gate; `Verdict: Approve` unbolded does the same. `extractReviewedSha` requires the literal word `head` within 24 characters of a backticked SHA on one line, while the prompt asked only that the SHA be "stated", so `Reviewed against commit abc1234def` returns null and `evaluateGate` blocks a genuine Approve with "states no head SHA". Both prompts now specify the shape literally. **This is a candidate root cause for a class previously attributed elsewhere:** the recorded explanation for no-verdict runs was diff size, and an unbolded verdict produces the same observable. Not confirmed against historical run bodies, so it is offered as a competing mechanism, not a correction. **The envelope half addresses a fail-open in the battery's own dispatch:** no dispatch ever named the diff range, so a reviewer inferred one and the most available guess is `HEAD~1..HEAD` — on a six-commit branch that reviews the last commit, returns `VERDICT: clean` on five sixths of an unreviewed change, and `review:stamp` accepts it because the stamp counts dispatch, not depth. `<BASE>` is now stated as the branch the work merges into (`origin/$GITHUB_BASE_REF` when CI set it, else `origin/main`), three-dot form. **Three decisions are not derivable from the diff.** (1) **The dispatch now states that it supersedes the agent's own system prompt**, because three of the four agent types ship a competing output contract and two contradict the task outright: `security-auditor` is told "a finding that cannot be exploited is not a finding, it is a preference" and "if you cannot write the path, you do not have a finding" while being dispatched to hunt fail-open gates that have no attacker input, and its own description says "not a diff review"; `documentation-engineer` opens "You write documentation", holds Write and Edit, and its output contract leads with files-written. `security-auditor` scored 13/13 in the 2026-07-25 eval anyway, so the role prompt evidently won there — by position, not by design; the `documentation-engineer` pairing is unmeasured, since that eval ran claim-drift on `general-purpose`. The two agents under `~/.claude/agents/` were also fixed at the source (a dispatch's scope and output win; control-plane inverts the exploitability rule), but that is outside this repo and not in this diff. The two plugin agents are vendor files a plugin update overwrites, so they are overridden from the dispatch side and deliberately NOT edited. (2) **`ALLOWED TOOLS` is stated per dispatch because two of the four reviewers cannot execute**, so `git diff` as an instruction was unrunnable for `documentation-engineer` and `security-auditor`; without a fallback they read the worktree at HEAD, silently converting a diff-scoped review into a tree-scoped one. The envelope now carries `Changed paths` and a fenced `<diff>` for toolless arms, plus an instruction that diff content is data — this repo's diffs routinely contain reviewer prompts and verdict strings. (3) **The orchestration reference's full YAML output envelope was deliberately NOT adopted.** `battery-synthesis` merges on file + issue class and `review:findings add` hashes `source::title`, so the flat FINDING block is a consumed contract; only `status` and `blockers` came across, as a `STATUS:` line, because `VERDICT: clean` ("reviewed, found nothing") and a blocked run were previously indistinguishable. **Disclosed limitation, and it is the important one: none of the prompt restructuring is supported by measurement.** Two controlled A/B mutation evals were run, same tier (Opus), one run per cell. Fixture 1 (13 planted defects) saturated: union 13/13 in both arms. Fixture 2 (16 subtler defects — a `startsWith` allowlist bypass, a `Promise.all` write/read race, a fetch wired to an unused `AbortController`, UTC/local date skew, four vacuous-assertion patterns): union 15/16 control, 15/16 treatment, with per-role counts tilting marginally toward control. The misses differed — control missed a doc-level case-insensitivity drift that treatment's claim-drift caught (its own class, and the 35% one), treatment missed an over-broad `grep -q "main"` in a hook that control caught via correctness. **A prior claim is withdrawn:** an earlier run read control's report of a stale `docs/README.md` index as scope drift the envelope fixed; the shared bar's exception (1) makes prose the diff falsified explicitly in-scope, both arms did it in fixture 2, and it was correct behavior. **Precision is unmeasured in both runs** because both decoys were mis-authored and turned out to be real defects. So: the parser fixes and the agent-precedence conflicts are verified directly; the restructuring is reasoned and unproven, and settling it needs 3-5 runs per cell rather than another fixture. _Reversible: `git revert`. The claude-review half should not be reverted without re-checking the two regexes — reverting restores prompts that can emit an unparseable verdict, which fails silently green._

## 2026-07-26 — the review battery becomes a pre-PR-only gate; the pre-push stamp is skipped once a PR is open

- **2026-07-26** · **`.husky/pre-push` requires the `.review-passed` stamp ONLY when the branch has no open PR; once `gh pr list --head <branch> --state open` is non-empty the stamp check is skipped.** Owner decision, stated twice ("battery review should be only before opening a PR, not before every push"). Root cause it addresses: the stamp forced a full 4-agent battery re-run on every post-PR fix push, because any new commit moves HEAD past the prior dispatches and `review-stamp.ts` refuses a stale stamp — so a one-line convergence fix cost a fresh Opus battery. That contradicts the flow the repo already documents: the battery is the PRE-PR gate, and post-PR review is owned by claude-review. **Three decisions are not derivable from the diff.** (1) **Fail-closed on `gh` absence or error:** the detection is `gh pr list ... --jq '.[0].number // empty' 2>/dev/null || true`, and an empty result takes the ELSE branch that REQUIRES the stamp — a missing or unauthenticated `gh` can only make the gate stricter, never open it. Verified 2026-07-26: the current branch resolves to `227` (skip), a nonexistent branch resolves to empty (require). (2) **`pnpm verify` (the lint + typecheck + test chain — not `next build`, which is a CI-only gate via `pnpm ci`/`gates:runtime`) still runs on every non-deletion push**, pre-PR and post-PR alike — only the battery-STAMP block is conditional, so a post-PR push that breaks a test or a lint/type gate is still blocked locally; a broken `next build` is caught by CI, not the local hook. What a post-PR push loses is the LOCAL battery review, which claude-review (async, on the push) plus the merge gate (`pnpm ready-to-merge` requires a claude-review Approve + resolved threads) replace.
