(3) **The ~10 doc/skill surfaces that described the battery as a per-push / "two triggers" gate were aligned in the same commit** (`CLAUDE.md`, the `review-battery`/`pr-merge-gate`/`visual-baseline-regen` skills, and the handbook), because leaving them would be exactly the claim-drift a reviewer flags. **Disclosed limitation:** `.husky/pre-push` has no unit-test harness in this repo, so the open-PR skip is verified by the runtime check above and by the push itself, not by an automated test; adding a pre-push test harness is out of scope here. _Reversible: restore the unconditional stamp block in `.husky/pre-push` (the ELSE body) and revert the doc edits. The regression if reverted is loud — every post-PR push is blocked until a fresh battery stamps HEAD — which is the friction this entry removes, not a silent failure._

## 2026-07-26 — claude-review gains a blocking guard that a review posting nothing reds the job

- **2026-07-26** · **`.github/workflows/claude-review.yml` gains a step that fails the job unless THIS run posted a verdict `check-claude-approval.ts` can parse.** Root cause it addresses: the `pull_request` auto-trigger has fired since #225 and never once produced a review. It runs in TAG mode, where the action's own task framing outweighs the review system prompt, so it posted a `track_progress` checklist and made zero `create_inline_comment` calls — evidenced on run 30182860369 (#226): no workflow-validation skip, exit success, 3 comments / 0 reviews / 0 inline. Because the gate parses a verdict out of comments, a checklist yields `verdict=none` while the check renders green, so the merge gate stalled with no visible cause. Same silent-green class as #221-#223, but permanent. **Three decisions here are not derivable from the diff.** (1) **The guard deliberately skips PRs that edit this workflow.** The action refuses when the file differs from the default branch, so no verdict can exist on such a PR no matter what it does; asserting one would be a red nothing could clear, which trains override and spends the false-positive budget. The cost is that a workflow-editing PR is never guarded by the guard it changes. (2) **The verdict pattern is grep's, applied per line, while the gate's is a whole-string JS regex** — so a bolded verdict wrapped across a newline reds the guard while the gate reads approve. Knowingly fail-CLOSED: a false red is noisy, a false green is the failure being fixed. (3) **The filter uses `--paginate --slurp` with an explicit `sort_by(.created_at) | last`.** `gh` applies `--jq` per page, so `last` alone yields one body per page and the guard greps their union — reintroducing the exact defect only once a PR passes 30 comments. `scripts/inspect-pr-comments.mjs` already carried this workaround. **Held by `__tests__/scripts/claude-review-verdict-guard.test.ts`, which EXECUTES the whole `run:` script under bash with `gh` stubbed on PATH** rather than asserting its text. That shape was reached the expensive way — run `git log --oneline -- __tests__/scripts/claude-review-verdict-guard.test.ts` for the list, which is the point: a count written here drifts the next time the file is touched, and this entry has proved it more than once. Every structural version before the last was bypassed at a coordinate its predecessor did not pin — an echo standing in for the predicate it represented, an unfiltered fetch, `and` rewritten to `or`, a per-page `last`, a single `!` inverting the grep, an `exit 0` hoisted above the FAIL branch, and finally an `exit 0` placed before the executed slice began. Each fix moved the class up one level instead of retiring it. Executing the script answers the only question all of them approximated: given this input, does it exit non-zero? Text assertions still stand alongside the executed rows, and deliberately, in three groups rather than a count, since a count is the thing that drifts. **Unreachable by execution:** the jq filter's composition and its `--paginate --slurp`, because the stub replaces `gh` so jq never runs; the step wrapper's `if:` and `continue-on-error`, which live in YAML rather than in the script; the guard-vs-`parseClaudeVerdict` pattern equivalence, which is a claim about two regexes rather than about any run; and the `issues` permission the guard's own Issues API call needs, which no row can reach because the stub replaces `gh` and therefore exercises no real scope. **Redundant with execution, kept as belt and braces:** the empty-`SINCE` refusal, the skip block, and the absence of `|| true`, each asserted textually and also exercised by a row. **Unreachable, and easy to miss:** the assertion binding the verdict grep to the variable the filtered fetch assigns — the stub ignores every `gh` argument, so substituting an unfiltered fetch leaves every executed row green and only that text assertion catches it. **Neither:** nothing — every branch of the script is reached by at least one row. _Reversible: delete the guard step and its test. The regression is silent by construction, which is why the test executes rather than reads._

## 2026-07-26 — The three frozen audit reports are deleted rather than banner-maintained

- **2026-07-26** · **The three frozen audit reports are deleted, and every reference to them with them (764 lines removed).** Owner decision. `docs/audit/2026-05-19-principal-audit.md`, `docs/audit-2025-05.md` and `docs/harness-audit.md` were point-in-time reports retained "for the record", and each had drifted: the harness audit asserted "Codex has **zero live surface on `main`** (no `AGENTS.md`, `.codex/`, `.agents/`)" while all three exist and `pnpm check:codex-sync` gates them. A frozen report is not free to keep — it is a standing input to every claim-drift reviewer, and the containment mechanism (a HISTORICAL banner plus a predicate that reads it) is machinery whose only job is to stop a reviewer flagging text nobody will act on. Deleting removes the class instead of managing it. **Nothing load-bearing is lost:** each audit's conclusions were already codified where they are enforced — the 2026-05-19 audit's twelve themes became `CLAUDE.md` standards and the dated ADR entries below that record them. The reports were the derivation, not the conclusion, and git history holds them regardless; a tracked file was never the only copy. **References were removed rather than left dangling, including inside dated entries.** That is a deliberate exception to the append-only rule, taken on the owner's explicit instruction after the alternative was raised: an entry now reads "from the 2026-05-19 principal audit, Debate 1 / Theme 2" instead of citing a path that resolves to nothing. The decisions those entries record are unchanged; only the dead pointers are gone. `STANDARDS.md` Ch.10 also loses its rule that superseded documents carry a header banner — no tracked document carries one now, and a standard with no subject is the drift this chapter exists to prevent. `ARCHITECTURE.md` §7 loses a "History: see …" pointer and the `docs/audit/` node leaves the `knowledge-architecture` diagram. **No gate would have caught any of this:** `check-doc-drift` reads only the fenced region of `ARCHITECTURE.md`, which contains no `docs/` path; `check:mermaid` asserts a diagram parses, never its node set; no test or CI path-filter references a `docs/` path. The consumer scan here was held by review, not machinery, which Ch.10 already states plainly. _Reversible: `git revert` restores the files and every reference edit._

## 2026-07-25 — Review battery re-aimed at the measured defect distribution, and run on Opus

- **2026-07-25** · **`BATTERY_ROLES` changed from code-review/security/performance/dependencies to correctness/claim-drift/gate-robustness/test-strength, and every standing reviewer now runs on Opus.** Two measurements drove this. First, the findings archive: 226 recorded cycles, 293 unique findings, 236 non-minor. Yield per mandatory role was code-review 51.7%, security 19.1%, **performance 4.7%, dependencies 1.3%** — and the roles did not predict the findings, since `performance-engineer` produced zero performance findings across its whole life (its ten findings are doc-provenance and gate defects) and `dependency-auditor` produced one unique finding in 226 cycles, a doc-provenance nit. Classified by defect rather than by agent, the real distribution is claim-drift 35%, correctness 27%, gate/CI robustness 22%, weak tests 12%, security 3%, and performance + a11y + dependencies ~1% combined. That last figure is not a coverage gap: LHCI, axe-core, `check-bundle-size.mjs`, `check-route-js.mjs`, `check-dep-pinning.mjs`, and `pnpm audit` already gate those properties, so a standing agent restating them is the redundancy CLAUDE.md forbids everywhere else. Perf, dependency, and a11y reviewers become conditional. **Second, a controlled mutation eval** (13 real defects planted across all four classes in a detached worktree at HEAD, plus 4 decoys, one run per cell): gate-robustness on Opus found 13/13, correctness on Opus 11/13, test-strength on Sonnet 7/13, claim-drift on Sonnet 6/13, with **zero false positives**. **Tier explains nearly all of the coverage variance and role explains almost none** — both Opus reviewers found most of the full set regardless of which role prompt they got. Role still pays at the margin and that is what justifies keeping four: gate-robustness alone caught a `CLAUDE.md` edit that reds `check:codex-sync`, and test-strength alone caught a test titled "rejects every origin" that asserted one case. Claim-drift scoring lowest is a tier artifact, not grounds to cut the largest defect class — the fix was Opus, not deletion. **No accept-list entry is verified on CI, and that is a disclosed limitation rather than an oversight.** `__tests__/battery-roles-resolve.test.ts` splits its entries: `resolvesWith` runs host-independently and pins the PREDICATE — only `agents/<name>.md` counts, never a skill or a command, which is what let `pr-review-toolkit:review-pr` sit dead in the list. Membership is a different question and `canObserve` routes every entry to `it.skip` on a runner: plugin-qualified names need `~/.claude/plugins/marketplaces`, bare names need `~/.claude/agents`, and a GitHub runner has neither. So all five accepts — `pr-review-toolkit:code-reviewer`, `feature-dev:code-reviewer`, `documentation-engineer`, `security-auditor`, `pr-review-toolkit:pr-test-analyzer` — are checked only on a developer machine. Verified there 2026-07-26: each resolves to an `agents/*.md` definition, and `pr-review-toolkit:pr-test-analyzer` in particular is an agent and not a command, which is the distinction that killed `review-pr`. Closing this properly means installing the marketplace in CI, which is out of scope here; the failure mode if an entry rots is loud — `review:stamp` refuses the role and `.husky/pre-push` blocks — rather than silent. **`claim-drift` accepts `documentation-engineer` and deliberately NOT `general-purpose`:** the stamp matches on `subagent_type` alone and cannot see what an agent was asked to do, so accepting `general-purpose` would let any unrelated dispatch satisfy the role — a fail-open gate. The eval ran claim-drift on `general-purpose`, so that specific pairing is unverified and is the weakest link here. `scripts/transcript-doctor.ts` derives its role list from `BATTERY_ROLES` and moved automatically; its test's hardcoded list did not, and the suite caught it. The prompts, the assembly order, the finding contract, and the eval evidence live in `.claude/skills/review-battery`, which replaces ~400 words of prose in CLAUDE.md. **Limits:** one diff, planted rather than organic defects, a single run per cell, so per-run variance is unmeasured; re-run before citing the numbers. _Reversible: restore the previous `BATTERY_ROLES` array and the matching literals in `__tests__/scripts/review-stamp.test.ts` and `__tests__/scripts/transcript-doctor.test.ts`. The doctor and the stamp must move together — a doctor reporting a role the stamp does not check is worse than either._

## 2026-07-24 — Dependency audit: overrides clearing every advisory at moderate and low

- **2026-07-24** · **pnpm.overrides added, forcing patched transitive versions to take `pnpm audit` from exit 1 to exit 0 at both moderate and low.** Same class and mechanism as the 2026-06-19 entry below, which is why this is an ADR and not a silent bump. Root cause: advisories published after main's last green run (6311bd6, 2026-07-19) turned the blocking `--audit-level=moderate` CI gate red; measured on the pre-fix base fba9150 (main tip, 2026-07-24 22:19) the gate reports **33 vulnerabilities (13 high, 18 moderate, 2 low), exit 1**. The exact count drifts upward as advisories publish (it read 31/11-high six hours earlier the same day) — the invariant this PR holds is the gate OUTCOME (exit 1 → exit 0), not a fixed count. Every vulnerable package is transitive and its parent is already at its latest release, so no dependency bump reaches them — overrides are the only mechanism that moves the property. **Added:** `@hono/node-server ^2.0.5` + `hono ^4.12.27` (path-traversal + per-request context-isolation XSS, via @modelcontextprotocol/sdk); `axios ^1.18.0` (SSRF/credential-leak, via wait-on); `body-parser@1 ^1.20.6` + `body-parser@>=2 ^2.3.0` (DoS: an invalid `limit` silently disables the size cap; two majors coexist — @lhci/cli>express pins 1.x dev, @modelcontextprotocol/sdk>express pins 2.x production); `brace-expansion ^5.0.8` (ReDoS + an unbounded-expansion OOM DoS). This is a single GLOBAL override, not the split `@1`/`@>=3` pair an earlier draft used: the newer OOM advisory flags the 1.x line (`brace-expansion@1.1.16`, dev-only via `@lhci/cli>chrome-launcher>rimraf>glob>minimatch@3`) and the 1.x line has NO patched release — only `5.0.8` fixes it. brace-expansion 5.x kept the CJS `expand()` API minimatch 3.x calls, verified end-to-end (lhci loads, `pnpm build`, and 1385 tests all pass with 5.0.8 forced on the minimatch-3 chain), so forcing every consumer to `5.0.8` is safe and closes both the 5.x-line and 1.x-line advisories with one entry; `fast-uri@3 ^3.1.4` (host-confusion, scoped so ajv 8.x is not dragged onto a newer major); `sharp ^0.35.0` (inherited libvips CVEs). **js-yaml raised ^4.2.0 → ^4.3.0** (new quadratic-CPU advisory) on the pre-existing unscoped key. **next/@next/mdx bumped ~16.2.10 → 16.2.11** in the manifest for the Next.js middleware-bypass / SSRF / DoS advisories. **The one override that crosses a declared range is `sharp`:** next 16.2.11 declares `optionalDependencies.sharp ^0.34.5` and the patched libvips is only in >=0.35.0, so it is forced to 0.35.3. Verified at RUNTIME, not by a green build — this app has no build-time blur path, so sharp only runs in next's on-demand image optimizer; `next start` + a direct `/_next/image` request confirmed both the JPEG and WebP re-encode paths work under 0.35.3. @argos-ci/core already resolved 0.35.3, so this collapses two copies to one. Every override was traced to a real consumer via `pnpm why`; each `body-parser` floor sits inside its parent's declared range (no forced cross). **Audit post-override:** `--audit-level=moderate` and `--audit-level=low` both exit 0, zero known vulnerabilities at any level. _Reversible: remove the added entries from the pnpm.overrides block (and revert the next/@next/mdx pins); a revert reopens the exact advisories, so the audit gate fails loudly rather than silently._

## 2026-07-24 - git's exported repo-local env is cleared once per hook boundary, not per suite

- **2026-07-24** · **`.husky/pre-push` and `.claude/hooks/__tests__/run.sh` each clear git's exported repo-local variables with `unset $(git rev-parse --local-env-vars)` before the work below them runs.** git exports `GIT_DIR`, `GIT_INDEX_FILE`, `GIT_WORK_TREE` and their siblings into every hook environment and **honours them over the cwd**, so a child process that `cd`s into a temp directory is still operating on THIS repo. Two distinct failures, one cause. In `pre-push`, the whole verify chain inherits the leak: `scripts/__tests__/detect-changes.test.ts` builds a temp repo and commits in it, which writes the **real** index with the temp dir as the work tree and stages every tracked file as deleted (791 entries, measured in 5cb8317, 2026-07-19; not re-measured here), and that commit re-enters husky through the global `core.hooksPath` and fails `pnpm check` in a directory with no `package.json`. In the hooks suite, a fixture asserting "outside a repo the hook emits nothing" was never outside the repo — it **passed by accident** in the main worktree, whose `GIT_DIR` is the relative `.git` and so is unresolvable from a temp dir, and failed in a linked worktree, whose `GIT_DIR` is absolute. That asymmetry is why it read as an environment flake rather than a bug. **`--local-env-vars` is git's own list for exactly this case and `git help githooks` prescribes this line**; a hand-written subset would be a fourth copy of that list to keep in sync with git. **Cleared once at the boundary rather than per suite or per invocation** because the environment is process-wide and everything downstream inherits one copy — a per-suite fix has to be re-added by the next suite that forgets. The reason is correctness, not speed: the per-invocation variant was rejected for that maintenance foot-gun, and no runtime claim is made either way. Placement is load-bearing in both files: every gate above the `unset` in `pre-push` resolves its own git state first, and `REPO_ROOT` in `run.sh` resolves on line 3, so clearing cannot change what either of them sees. _Reversible: `git revert` restores the leak. The regression is not loud — the hooks fixture goes back to passing by accident in the main worktree and only reds in a linked one, and the `pre-push` failure surfaces as `pnpm check` failing far from its cause._

- **2026-07-24** · **The same strip is applied in `__tests__/helpers/hermetic-git.ts`, because the hook boundary alone cannot hold it and CI structurally cannot catch it.** Two facts found while pushing this branch. First, `core.hooksPath` is an **absolute path into the main worktree**, so a push from any linked worktree runs `main`'s `.husky/pre-push`, whichever version main is currently on — a hook-level fix therefore cannot protect its own branch's push, and every agent worktree inherits main's staleness. Second, **CI never runs the suite under a git hook**, so no `GIT_DIR` is exported there and this entire failure class is invisible to it: this branch was green in CI while the same suite corrupted a local checkout (791 files staged as deleted, plus `core.bare` flipped to `true` on the real repo). `inheritedEnvWithIsolatedGit()` spread `...process.env` and isolated only `GIT_CONFIG_*`, so all 15 repo-identity variables rode through. The helper is the last point the leak can be stopped and the only one that does not assume a caller cleaned up first. **Order is load-bearing**: strip before applying `GIT_CONFIG_ISOLATION`, never after, since `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT` belongs to both sets and the reverse order deletes the isolation. _Reversible: `git revert` restores the spread. `__tests__/helpers/hermetic-git.test.ts` reds on that mutation (15 of 31, verified 2026-07-24), and it points `GIT_DIR` at a throwaway repo so a red run cannot corrupt the checkout it runs in._

## 2026-07-19 - spec-kit replaces superpowers as the dev flow; the plan gate moves with it

- **2026-07-19** · **Owner decision: adopt github/spec-kit as the spec-driven development flow and remove the superpowers plugin entirely, in this repo and in both global harnesses.** `specify init --here --integration claude` installs ten skills under `.claude/skills/speckit-*/` plus the `.specify/` engine (scripts, templates, `feature.json`, workflow registry); `specify extension add bug` adds the `speckit-bug-{assess,fix,test}` track. **The gate moved first, deliberately:** `.claude/hooks/architect-gate.sh` and its `.codex/` mirror keyed on `superpowers:writing-plans`, a skill that no longer exists, so the hard gate on planning was silently protecting nothing the moment the plugin left. Both now key on `speckit-plan`, verified by execution (exit 2 with no architect PASS, exit 0 for any other skill) rather than by reading. The 8 fixtures in `.claude/hooks/__tests__/run.sh` were repointed with it; the negative case now feeds `speckit-specify`, a real sibling skill, which is a stronger fixture than a skill that no longer exists. **479 hook tests pass** (`bash .claude/hooks/__tests__/run.sh | grep -c '^ok:'`, 2026-07-19). Seven dead `Skill(superpowers:*)` entries were removed from `.claude/settings.json`'s allowlist, and `evals/agents/architect-gate-respect/CASE.ts` + `evals/agents/calibration.ts` were repointed, since an eval asserting a trigger that cannot fire measures nothing. **Dropped with no replacement, knowingly:** `test-driven-development`, `systematic-debugging`, `verification-before-completion` and the review skills. Spec-kit covers the specify→plan→tasks→implement spine and nothing else. The two rules that named them now state the behavior instead of pointing at a skill — TDD reads "write the failing test, watch it fail, then make it pass", and verification reads "run the command and read its output", because pointing a verification rule at a skill let it be satisfied by invoking something rather than by checking something. _Reversible: `git revert <sha>` restores the superpowers triggers, the allowlist entries and the eval fixtures; re-enabling the plugin is a separate edit in `~/.claude/settings.json`. The `.specify/` tree and the `speckit-*` skills are additive and can be deleted without touching anything else._

- **2026-07-19** · **The decision was informed by a failed prior adoption in another repo, and the failure mode is recorded here so it is not repeated.** That repo adopted a hand-written imitation of spec-kit (7 command files totalling 64 lines, no `.specify/` engine) **alongside** superpowers in 2026-05, wiring `/speckit.*` as thin wrappers over superpowers skills. The `docs/superpowers/` directory that adoption committed to emptying permanently **refilled to 29 files starting two days later** and kept growing for three weeks; the repo now carries three parallel spec systems and no record of which is authoritative. The owner flagged it on 2026-05-30 ("dual planning loop, reconcile") and never did. Three lessons applied here: **(1)** never run two spec frameworks at once, which is why superpowers was removed rather than left installed; **(2)** a workflow entered only by typing a slash command loses to the one the model already invokes by habit, which is why the gate is a PreToolUse hook and not a prose rule; **(3)** the promised retrospective ADR for that adoption was never written, so this entry exists before the migration is judged rather than after it is forgotten. _Reversible: n/a, this is a record._

- **2026-07-19** · **Owner decision: the entire spec-kit surface is untracked. `specs/`, `.specify/`, and the generated `speckit-*` skills in `.claude/`, `.agents/` and `.codex/` are all gitignored.** Spec-driven development is personal dev flow here, not part of the reference system's adoptable surface, and this is the same call as the 2026-07-17 entry below that removed `docs/superpowers/`. It is also the same category superpowers itself always occupied: an installed tool that was never a repo artifact. Reinstall with `specify init --here --integration claude` and `specify extension add bug`; nothing in the tracked tree depends on it existing. The durable why continues to live in `DECISIONS.md` / `ARCHITECTURE.md`, which is where this repo's own "decisions in the cheapest durable slot" rule puts it. **Two costs, recorded rather than discarded.** First, `memory/constitution.md` is now untracked, so it is one machine failure from gone and a fresh clone starts without it; the mitigation is that governance which must survive belongs in `DECISIONS.md`/`STANDARDS.md` anyway, and a constitution that duplicates them would be the second copy that drifts. Second, in the prior repo `tasks.md` was the **only** artifact that survived as evidence a feature was actually implemented, and 9 of 10 features there have no completion record at all; this repo now has no in-tree implementation ledger either, and if that gap bites the fix is a completion note in `DECISIONS.md` at merge time, not re-tracking the spec tree. **The architect gate is unaffected**: `.claude/hooks/architect-gate.sh` keys on the `speckit-plan` skill name in the tool payload, not on any tracked file, so it fires whether or not the skill file is in git. _Reversible: delete the five lines from `.gitignore` and `git add` the paths; the files are untouched on disk, only untracked._

## 2026-07-19 - The push-guard predicate is a tokenizer walk, and the property is execution, not spelling

- **2026-07-19** · **`.claude/hooks/api-security-push-guard.sh` no longer matches tokens; it consumes `bash-guard-detect.py --emit-commands`, the same `bashlex` walk `bash-guard.sh` already used to judge.** This is the follow-up the 2026-07-18 entry below names as not done — that entry's "Follow-up, not done here" is superseded here, and its reversibility note ("do not revert without first replacing the regex with the vendored `bashlex` tokenizer") is now satisfied. The raw-payload fallback and its deliberate over-strip survive unchanged, because the walk still reports undecidable (exit 3) for constructs it cannot resolve, and the fallback is where those land. **The governing design rule, which cost the most to learn: a construct the walk cannot resolve is undecidable, never clean.** Two mid-branch fail-opens were regressions where the precise detector was strictly *narrower* than the regex it replaced — precision is not safety.
- **2026-07-19** · **The predicate stopped enumerating dangerous spellings and now states one property: while an unaudited API edit pends, a git invocation must not gain configuration or execution it would not otherwise have.** Enumeration lost thirteen consecutive rounds — each named key (`alias.*`, `core.hooksPath`, `include.path`, `core.sshCommand`) was followed by another. The class that justifies an over-block is specifically the **no-artifact** one: a single Bash call that writes no file, so nothing else in the transcript records it. That covers config injection (`-c`, `GIT_*` env, `git config` writes including `--unset`, which removes `core.hooksPath` and disables husky exactly as redirection does) and **program injection after the subcommand** (`--upload-pack`, `--receive-pack`, `--exec`, `--extcmd`, `--template`, `--separate-git-dir`) — verified against git 2.53.0 to exec a named program against a local remote. An arg loop that stops at the first non-flag word cannot see any of the second group, which is why the scan is now whole-record. Artifact-requiring routes (`bash ./script.sh`, direct `.git/config` writes) are deliberately left to the artifact being the visible event; `-C` was relaxed back to skip-the-path for the same reason, since redirecting at a repo is useless without a config someone already wrote, and `git -C <dir> status` is ordinary worktree work. _Reversible: `git revert` restores token matching and reopens every under-match and over-match pinned in `.claude/hooks/__tests__/run.sh`; the suite fails loudly rather than silently._

## 2026-07-18 - push-guard fallback: the metadata strip errs toward over-blocking, deliberately

- **2026-07-18** · **`.claude/hooks/api-security-push-guard.sh`'s raw-payload fallback strips `transcript_path`/`cwd`/`description` with `"[^"]*"`, which stops at the first bare quote and therefore under-strips when a value contains an escaped quote — this is chosen, not overlooked.** The fallback only runs when the JSON extraction already failed, so every bound in it is an approximation over text that did not parse; the only question is which way it errs. `"[^"]*"` can strip **less** than intended, leaving residue in the haystack, which **over-blocks**. The escape-aware alternative `"([^"\\]|\\.)*"` can strip **more**: an odd trailing backslash makes the closing quote read as escaped, the match runs past the field, and a real `git push` is deleted — verified fail-open, `{"cwd":"x\", command git push, "y":"end"}` exits 0 with a marker pending. Over-blocking is recoverable (this is a PreToolUse hook on the agent's Bash tool; the owner pushes from a terminal it does not intercept); a silent bypass is not — once an unaudited API-surface push ships, no later action makes the audit have happened first. **This was implemented the wrong way first and reverted**: `ec635ae` shipped the escape-aware bound to close a reviewer-reported hard-lock, `16e409d` reverted it once the fail-open was demonstrated. The hard-lock (an escaped quote in prose leaves a token tail and blocks the marker-clearing escape) is now an accepted, tested behaviour rather than a latent bug. Both directions are pinned in `.claude/hooks/__tests__/run.sh`; re-introducing the escape-aware bound fails both. _Reversible: `git revert` restores the escape-aware bound and reopens the verified fail-open — do not, without first replacing the regex with the vendored `bashlex` tokenizer (see the follow-up below)._
- **Follow-up, not done here:** seven review rounds on this one predicate each widened a boundary or normalization and were broken by the next adjacency. The structural fix is to tokenize with the vendored `bashlex` that `.claude/hooks/bash-guard-detect.py` already uses (basename matching, `-c` re-parse, substitution/compound traversal), which closes both the under-match (`/usr/bin/git push`, `bash -c "git push"`, `g\it push` — all verified to exit 0 today, all backstopped by `.husky/pre-push` except under `--no-verify`) and the over-match (a commit message mentioning `git push` false-blocking).

## 2026-07-17 - Correction: the monthly LLM token cap is 3,000,000, not the 400K the 2026-05-15 entry records

- **2026-07-17** · **`lib/rate-limit.ts` sets `MONTHLY_TOKEN_BUDGET = 3_000_000`, but the 2026-05-15 entry below records the cap as a "400K token hard cap (≈$0.40 at Haiku pricing)".** The 400K figure is stale: the cap is 3,000,000 tokens per rolling ~32-day window (`BUDGET_WINDOW_S`), counting input **and** output (settled via `settleBudget(reserved, totalBilledInput, outputTokens, …)`), consistent with the `$5 nominal / $50 hard cap` cost ceiling in `ARCHITECTURE.md`. The raise predates the current (squashed) git history — the first tracked commit already reads `3_000_000` — so the raising commit is not individually recoverable. The dated 2026-05-15 entry is **left exactly as written** per this log's append-only convention; this entry supersedes its number. `ARCHITECTURE.md` (§ observability, the `/api/ask` cost model) already documents `MONTHLY_TOKEN_BUDGET = 3_000_000` with a $3–$5.30 monthly-cost analysis, so the only stale figure is the dated DECISIONS entry — no current-state doc is wrong. The cap remains a fail-open Redis reserve/settle on key `ask:tokens:YYYY-MM`. _Reversible: n/a — a pointer to the authoritative `MONTHLY_TOKEN_BUDGET` constant, not a decision._

## 2026-07-17 - Dependabot zod ignore was inverted; corrected to ignore major+minor, allow patch

- **2026-07-17** · **`.github/dependabot.yml` ignored zod `semver-minor` + `semver-patch` updates, which suppressed the safest/security-carrying bumps while auto-opening the most breaking one.** The intent (CLAUDE.md package policy) is that zod is exact-pinned because its **minor** bumps break type inference, so those are upgraded deliberately — not that zod should never receive a dependabot PR. The old list ignored **patch** (4.4.x bugfix/security, semver-safe, and the one an exact pin most wants surfaced) and, by omission, **allowed major** (4→5, which breaks both the API and inference — exactly the deliberate-only upgrade). Corrected to ignore **major + minor** and allow **patch**: security/bugfix patches now surface as CI-gated, reviewed PRs (a patch that regressed inference fails CI and is caught), while the inference/API-breaking bumps stay manual. The adjacent `typescript` ignore (majors only, holding TS at 6.x for Stryker) was already correct and is unchanged. No gate tests `dependabot.yml`; verified by reading the corrected `update-types`. _Reversible: `git revert <sha>` restores the prior list; the only effect is which zod update-types dependabot opens PRs for._

## 2026-07-17 - Codex harness mirror is content-only, NOT activated

- **2026-07-17** · **`scripts/sync-codex.mjs` mirrors the `.claude/` harness into `.agents/` + `.codex/` + `AGENTS.md` so Codex reads the same skills, rules, agents, hooks, and instructions as Claude Code — but it deliberately does NOT wire Codex hook *activation*, and the mirror says so.** Path-ONLY rewrites (skills→`.agents`, hooks/rules/agents→`.codex`, `CLAUDE.md`→`AGENTS.md`); every repo fact (the model string, `/claude-review`, `claude[bot]`, `pnpm claude-gate`) passes verbatim, guarded by a `FORBIDDEN` fiction check. The gate `pnpm check:codex-sync` fails CLOSED on drift AND on incompleteness (a referenced `.agents/`/`.codex/` path or a `$HOOK_DIR` sibling that was never generated) — the predecessor's `--check` diffed only the files it wrote and reported OK while `bash-guard.sh`'s Python detector went unmirrored, which (python exit 2 = BLOCK) would have hard-blocked every Codex command. **The load-bearing boundary:** `.claude/settings.json`'s `hooks` block, which *registers* the hooks with Claude Code's PreToolUse/PostToolUse events, has no Codex equivalent in this repo and is NOT mirrored. So for Codex the mirrored guards are **read-only guidance, not active gates**. Rather than let `AGENTS.md` inherit CLAUDE.md's "WIRED"/"Confirmed enforced" prose as if it were true for Codex (a false claim of active security controls — caught by the review battery), `sync-codex.mjs` prepends a banner to `AGENTS.md` stating hook activation is not configured, so the hooks/gates are guidance to self-enforce, not automated gates — and, because skills and rules are loaded contextually without that banner in view, injects the same per-file note (after any YAML frontmatter, so a skill stays discoverable) into every mirrored skill/rule/agent. The banner and note are **Codex-native**: the mirror files must not cross-reference the other agent (and the `.claude/` originals carry no Codex references either). The only unavoidable non-Codex strings that remain are repo-infrastructure facts — the review bot and the review command — which renaming would turn into fiction (the naive-replace trap the path-only design exists to avoid). Wiring real Codex activation (its own hook-config format) is a deliberate, uncertain follow-up, not shipped here — chosen over both plunging into that build and shipping a dishonest mirror. _Reversible: `git revert <sha>` removes the generator, the gate wiring (`verify` + `quality-fast`), and the generated mirror; nothing else depends on `.agents/`/`.codex/`._

## 2026-07-17 - `docs/superpowers/` (specs + plans) removed from version control

- **2026-07-17** · **Owner decision: stop tracking `docs/superpowers/` — the spec and plan files the brainstorming/writing-plans workflow produces — and gitignore the directory.** 136 files (73 plans, 62 specs, the discovery-ledger) were `git rm`'d and `docs/superpowers` added to `.gitignore` (no trailing slash, same symlink reason as `.superpowers`). **Rationale:** these are *pre-decision* workflow artifacts, not the reference system's adoptable surface; the durable "why" already lives in the tracked layer — `DECISIONS.md` for decisions, `ARCHITECTURE.md`/`STANDARDS.md` for design — which is where this repo's own "decisions in the cheapest durable slot" rule says it belongs. The workflow still writes specs/plans to those paths locally (like `.superpowers` scratch); they are just no longer version-controlled. **Gate change:** `scripts/check-gitleaks-fixture.mjs` dropped `MAY_NAME_THE_COMMENT_DIRS = ['docs/superpowers/plans/']` and its `startsWith` branch — that prefix was the only consumer of the directory, and its sole tracked member naming the gitleaks suppression comment (the semgrep plan) is gone; `mayNameTheComment` is now exact-file-only, which is strictly stricter (the code's own comment flagged prefix-matching as the risky kind). This ADR deliberately does not spell that suppression phrase verbatim: the gate greps the tracked tree for the literal, and `DECISIONS.md` is not on its exact-file allowlist, so naming it here would red the gate on this very entry. Test updated to match; gate + 42 fixture tests pass. **Doc refs updated:** the two current-state citations (`ARCHITECTURE.md` §9, `STANDARDS.md` preamble) dropped their dead spec-file paths while keeping substance; the handbook (`knowledge-architecture.md`, `README.md`, `development-lifecycle.md`) reframes specs/plans as local-only/gitignored and drops the drifting counts (~48 specs, ~73 plans, ~2090 tools — numbers no gate held). **Dated ADRs below are left exactly as written** per this log's convention, including the 2026-05-30 fallow entry's `see docs/superpowers/discovery-ledger.md` pointer — that file is now local-only; this entry is the standing note for any such dead reference. _Reversible: `git revert <sha>` restores the 136 files, re-adds the `.gitignore` line for removal, and the gate/test/doc edits; the specs/plans themselves were never lost (they remain on disk locally, now untracked)._

## 2026-07-16 - The review battery is 4 agents, not 5: two of the required five no longer exist

- **2026-07-16** · **`scripts/review-stamp.ts` required a dispatch from `accessibility-tester` and `dependency-manager`. Neither agent exists any more** — no definition in `.claude/agents/`, none in `~/.claude/agents/`, and neither appears in the session's agent registry (`.claude/agents/` is untracked local/plugin-provided, so this was not a repo change). The stamp was therefore **unsatisfiable**: it could never write `.review-passed`, and `.husky/pre-push` blocks unless the stamp matches HEAD, so no branch could be pushed at all. This is the sanctioned gate-config exception — an assertion firing on an impossible state, not a property being weakened. **`dependencies` now accepts `dependency-auditor`** (same role: `pnpm audit`, outdated packages, bloat; `dependency-manager` is kept in the accepts-list so the gate does not break if it returns). **The `accessibility` role is removed**, and this is the part worth arguing with: no equivalent agent exists to replace it. The property is not unheld — axe-core (`tests/a11y/axe.spec.ts`) and Lighthouse accessibility = 100 both gate it in CI, mechanically, on every PR, which is strictly stronger than an agent's opinion. What is lost is the judgement an agent brings to things axe cannot see: focus order, whether a live region announces usefully, whether a custom widget is operable by keyboard. Those now rest on PR review and the pre-merge Playwright check. _Reversible: write an `accessibility-tester` agent definition and add the role back to `BATTERY_ROLES`. If the agent ever returns to the registry, do that — the 4-agent battery is a response to its absence, not a judgement that a11y review is unnecessary._

## 2026-07-14 - The per-route JS budget was unreachable; corrected to a measured 175KB total, gated

- **2026-07-14** · **`CLAUDE.md` documented "JS gzipped per route < 120KB" as a hard, non-negotiable budget. That number was never achievable and had never been measured.** Nothing measured it because `scripts/check-bundle-size.mjs` sums *every* chunk in `.next/static/chunks` **globally** against one cap — it is structurally blind to a per-route figure, so a route reusing chunks that already exist for `/` adds ~0 new bytes and that gate reads green regardless of what ships. The first per-route measurement (`scripts/check-route-js.mjs`, added here) found `/` at 173.0KB and `/_not-found` — **a route with zero application code** — at **148.9KB**. A bare hello-world Next 16.2.10 + React 19.2.7 app on this toolchain, built for comparison, measures **142.2KB gzipped shared before a single line of app code exists**. Deleting 100% of this site's client JavaScript would land at ~142KB, still 22KB over the documented budget. **A 120KB per-route budget fires on an empty Next app**, which is exactly CLAUDE.md's stated exception for a legitimate gate-config change ("an assertion that fires on the ideal state"); every other reading of the standing rule would require deleting Next to satisfy it. Hypothesis tested and **refuted**: there is no dependency leak — `zod`, the `ai` SDK, `@upstash`, `resend`, and `@anthropic-ai/sdk` are absent from every client chunk (the `content/_validate-client-content.ts` pattern is holding), and `cacheComponents: true` costs only 0.7KB. **Replaced with ONE gate that measures a real property:** per-route **total ≤ 175KB** — the 142.2KB measured floor plus 32.8KB of app headroom. `/` is 173.0KB today, so the margin is ~2KB; note that a routine `react`/`react-dom` bump (caret-pinned, and the stack policy runs `pnpm up --latest`) can trip it with ZERO app-code change, since the framework runtime chunk alone is ~70KB — a dependency-bump PR should budget for re-measuring the floor or trimming app JS. **App-owned is REPORTED, never asserted, and that is deliberate:** it is `total − FRAMEWORK_FLOOR_KB`, an affine transform of total by a constant, so an app-owned threshold is a second threshold on the SAME axis. The first version of this ADR claimed a gated 43KB app-owned budget; it could only have fired above 142.2 + 43 = 185.2, which the 175 total already caught, so it was unreachable — a budget that printed a number no input could ever trip while the real ceiling was 32.8. Caught in review. **The absence of the second threshold is NOT held by a test, and cannot be:** re-adding `|| appOwned > 43` to the gate leaves exit code and stderr byte-identical for every possible input (mutation-tested in review — the mutant survives), precisely because the threshold is unreachable dead code. What `__tests__/check-route-js.test.mjs` does pin is that a route in the disagreement band (175 < total < 185.2) fails at all, which would catch someone reintroducing the two-gate design by relaxing the total to 185. That is worth having; it is not the same claim, and this entry previously conflated them. The app-owned figure is the one engineering moves; the framework floor is not ours to shrink. Enforced by `pnpm route-js-check`, wired into `ci.yml` (after the build, which emits the manifest it reads), the `ci` script, and `ready-for-pr`. **App-owned is deliberately NOT "the chunks a route does not share with its siblings."** That definition — the first one written here — fails open twice: with a single route the intersection is the whole bundle, so app-owned computes to 0 and the gate prints OK over an unmeasured budget (proven in review); and a client island moved into `app/layout.tsx` lands on every route, joins the "shared" set, and silently leaves the budget it exists to constrain. Both are pinned by `__tests__/check-route-js.test.mjs`. The floor constant is itself gated: if the lightest route drops below it, the gate fails and says to re-measure rather than let every app-owned figure inflate silently. **Also fixed here:** `scripts/strip-next-polyfills.mjs` stripped `polyfill-module.js` but not `polyfill-nomodule.js`, a 112,594-byte file the build shipped as a real chunk (**38.6KB gzipped**). No supported browser ever downloaded it — it is served with `noModule`, and every browser in this repo's browserslist (`chrome >= 93`, `edge >= 93`, `firefox >= 92`, `safari >= 15.4`) has had ES modules since ~2017 — but `bundle-check` summed it blindly, inflating that gate's reading to **214.5/220KB**. Stripping it drops the real figure to **175.9/220KB**: the gate was never nearly-blown, it was reading ~38.6KB of bytes nobody fetches. **Measured in isolation on 2026-07-16, not inferred.** Restoring only `polyfill-nomodule.js` into `node_modules` (pristine from the npm tarball for next@16.2.10, MD5 `846118c33b2c0e922d7b3a7676f81f6f`, 112,594B raw) and running `pnpm build && node scripts/check-bundle-size.mjs --max-client-kb=220` gives **214.5KB**; re-stripping (`node scripts/strip-next-polyfills.mjs`) and rebuilding gives **175.9KB**. Delta: **38.6KB**. Reproduced independently twice, including a from-scratch `npm install next@16.2.10`. For scale, the pristine file alone gzips to 39,627B = 38.698 KiB under `zlib.gzipSync(buf)` with no options — the exact call `check-bundle-size.mjs` makes. **The file figure and the build delta differ by ~0.1KB purely because both reported figures are already rounded**: 214.5 and 175.9 each carry ±0.05, so their difference carries ±0.1 and spans 38.50–38.70, which contains 38.698. No mechanism is needed to explain the gap, and two were proposed and discarded — "bundler wrapping makes the chunk differ" (wrong direction: wrapping adds bytes) and "other chunks' rounding drifts between builds" (unverified, and unnecessary once the arithmetic is done). **Cite the build delta, not the file size** — the delta is what the gate measures.

**An earlier revision of this entry said 219.2 → 175.9, a 43.3KB delta, attributed to a "39.5KB" polyfill. All three figures were wrong and none reproduce.** 219.2 does not reproduce under any measurement tried here; 39.5 has no recoverable provenance, and guessing at its origin would repeat the error that produced it.

The lesson worth keeping: **gzip is not one number, and the `gzip` CLI's number is not even one number.** A revision of this entry cited 39,478B from `gzip -c`. A reviewer could not reproduce it and got 39,494B. Both are correct: **the gzip CLI embeds the source filename in its header**, so the same 112,594 bytes compress to 39,478B as `u.js` (a 4-character scratch name) and 39,494B as `polyfill-nomodule.js` — exactly 16 more, the filename-length difference. `gzip -nc` strips the header name and both collapse to 39,473B; Node's zlib never writes a name and reports 39,627B. That 39,478 was reproducible only on the machine that happened to name its temp file `u.js`: **it encoded an accident of the measurement, not a property of the artifact.** Measure with the tool the gate runs (`zlib.gzipSync`), or the number is about your filesystem rather than your bundle. **Rejected:** removing `<Analytics/>` + `<SpeedInsights/>` from the shared graph (2.3KB) — it does not bring `/` under any budget it fails, and it would cost the real-user Core Web Vitals data this site publishes as a feature. **Rejected:** switching to `--webpack` (~19KB) — the build currently fails under webpack (MDX design-system pages export `metadata` from `'use client'`) and it abandons the supported Next 16 bundler. _Reversible: restore the numbers in `scripts/check-route-js.mjs`. Do not re-tighten below the floor without re-running the hello-world measurement — the number is evidence, not preference._

## 2026-07-13 - Canonical host moved apex → www to match the live redirect

- **2026-07-13** · **Migrated the canonical host from `erikunha.dev` to `www.erikunha.dev` across all self-referential URLs.** After #198 shipped, the apex was found to 308-redirect to www (Vercel primary-domain setting) while every canonical/og:url/sitemap/JSON-LD still pointed at the apex — a canonical URL that itself redirects, which partly undoes #198's dedup fix (search engines see the preferred host disagree with the serving host). Changed `metadataBase`, root `openGraph.url`/`alternates.canonical`/authors url, `content/seo.ts` (`personSchema.url` + the then-present `SITE_ORIGIN`), `content/social.ts`, `lib/hiring-profile.ts`, `lib/agent/mcp-tools.ts`, `public/{robots.txt,llms.txt,.well-known/agent.json}`, the two Footer self-link `href`s, the README "Live" badge, and the gate tests that assert those URLs — every scheme-prefixed (`https://…`) self-referential URL on a served or crawled surface. The operational `curl https://erikunha.dev/api/healthz` runbook lines intentionally keep the apex: a one-shot health check follows the 308 transparently and is not a canonical surface. **Brand/identity strings stay bare `erikunha.dev`** — OG `siteName`, the page-`<title>` suffix, the MCP server `name`, and the `@erikunha.dev` emails are display labels, not hosts. Owner chose www-canonical over the zero-code alternative (flip the Vercel primary domain to apex so www→apex, keeping the existing apex refs). Reversible: revert the host strings AND flip the Vercel primary domain back to apex — both must agree or the redirect/canonical mismatch returns.

## 2026-07-13 - SEO + accessibility pass (canonical fix, single h1, sr-only headings, page nav)

- **2026-07-13** · **Fixed the `/design-system/*` canonical-to-homepage bug and added a11y/SEO heading semantics + per-page nav discoverability (one PR).** External SEO audit findings, each verified against code before acting.
  - **Canonical:** the root layout sets an ABSOLUTE `alternates.canonical = https://erikunha.dev`; Next.js inherits it into every child that does not override it, so all 5 design-system subpages self-reported the homepage as their canonical (a real dedup bug). Fix: `dsPageMetadata({slug,title,description})` (`app/design-system/_lib/page-metadata.ts`) sets a RELATIVE per-route `alternates.canonical` + `openGraph.url` (resolves against `metadataBase`). Next merges metadata SHALLOWLY — setting `openGraph`/`twitter` REPLACES (not deep-merges) the root's — so the helper re-specifies the full `openGraph` (type/locale/url/title/description/siteName + the branded `/og.png`) and a per-route `twitter` card. (An earlier draft set only `openGraph:{title,description,url,type}`, which silently dropped `siteName`/`locale`/`og:image` AND left the 5 subpages inheriting the HOMEPAGE's `twitter` title/description — caught in claude-review; the "drop og:image, it's a text doc" simplification was reversed because it cascaded into wrong twitter cards, and a branded default image beats a blank one.) Dropping `page.mdx`'s explicit `robots: 'index, follow'` is inert — the root layout sets robots and metadata inheritance carries it per-unspecified-field.
  - **Single h1:** the hero rendered TWO `<h1>Erik Cunha</h1>` (desktop + mobile variants, both in the DOM, CSS-toggled) — crawlers saw two, and each viewport's screen-reader saw one-per-viewport. Fix: one viewport-independent `<h1 class="sr-only">` + both visible names as `aria-hidden` `<p>` with identical classes (pixel-identical; verified against CI e2e-visual + the a11y agent's live ariaSnapshot showing exactly one level-1 heading). Making one visible variant the h1 was rejected: at the opposite viewport that section is `display:none`, dropping its h1 from the a11y tree.
  - **sr-only heading labels:** the ~20 terminal-styled `<h2>` (e.g. `LS -LA ./PROJECTS`) carried no plain-English topic for crawlers/AT. Fix: an `srLabel` sr-only span (first child of the `<h2>`) fed by Zod-validated `content/section-labels.ts` (20 labels), with a fail-closed guard test that enumerates rendered `Module` ids from source and reds if any lacks a label. Accessible name reads label-first ("Projects LS -LA ./PROJECTS").
  - **Per-page nav trail:** SERVER component + its JSON-LD schema (`content/seo.ts`), per-page `trail` prop (NO `usePathname`/client hook — a client island would breach the app-owned JS budget). JSON-LD rendered as a plain `<script type="application/ld+json">{JSON.stringify(...)}</script>` text child, matching the existing `personSchema` pattern — empirically confirmed (security review, against `.next/server/app/index.html`) that React 19/Next 16 SSR emits the quotes unescaped, so `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` is unnecessary. **Caveat retained because `personSchema` still uses this form:** SSR does not entity-escape `<script>` text content, so it gives no injection defense if that pattern is ever reused with DYNAMIC/user-derived data; today all values are compile-time literals. A future dynamic consumer must escape `<` characters (e.g. `JSON.stringify(...).replace(/</g, '\\u003c')`) before injecting. (Removed 2026-07-16 — see the entry above.)
  - **Dropped from the audit (out of scope / constraint conflict):** sitemap `lastmod` (already per-group; distinct per-page dates would be fabricated), a separate contextual body link (no natural anchor; footer + topbar + the page nav trail cover internal linking), images/hreflang/PWA/blog (perf budget + explicit out-of-scope). www→apex 301 is a Vercel dashboard toggle, not code.
  - **Verified:** LHCI passed on all 6 URLs (SEO/A11y/BP/Perf gates hold); axe 7/7 on every route; the nav trail's `mb-6` shifted the darwin-only DS-component snapshots by 1px (sub-pixel re-phase per the known baseline-impact rule) — regenerated + inspected. Reversible: each unit (metadata helper, hero, srLabel, footer link, nav trail) is independently revertible; no dependency added.

## 2026-07-13 - gitleaks config-shape gate: parse the TOML, validate an allow-schema

- **2026-07-13** · **Rewrote `assertConfigShape` (`scripts/check-gitleaks-fixture.mjs`) from a regex denylist over the raw `.gitleaks.toml` text to a parse of the TOML validated against an ALLOW-schema.** Added `smol-toml` (devDependency, EXACT-pinned `1.7.0` — its parse semantics are load-bearing for the gate, same rationale as zod's exact pin; a minor bump that changed table/dotted-key handling could silently blind the check. Zero transitive deps, build-gate only — no client-bundle impact, confirmed by `bundle-check`). **Why (task #25):** a denylist can only reject keys it already enumerated, and TOML's equivalent spellings (inline tables, dotted/quoted keys, multi-line arrays) slipped several past a line-anchored regex — an inline-table `paths` entry was a demonstrated working exploit. The old checks covered `paths`/`disabledRules`/`.gitleaksignore` files and inline gitleaks suppression comments, but MISSED `stopwords` (drops findings by surrounding text), `commits` (exempts commit SHAs), `regexTarget` (widens what the allowlist regex matches), `[[rules]]` overrides, and `[extend].path`/`url` (layer in an external disabling config this gate never reads) — each neuters a credential class the github-pat probe cannot see. **The parse collapses every spelling to one object and rejects any key outside three small sets** (top-level ⊆ {title, extend, allowlists, allowlist}; extend ⊆ {useDefault}, required `=== true`; each allowlist ⊆ {description, regexes, condition, matchCondition}), so a neutering key nobody has enumerated yet is rejected BY DEFAULT — the allow-schema doing what a denylist structurally cannot. This is the "measure the structure, don't regex the text" lesson (PR #190 getComputedStyle) applied to config. Also fails closed on unparseable TOML, which the old regex silently passed as clean. **The container shapes are type-guarded, not coerced, and each allowed key's VALUE type is checked** — the review battery CAUGHT a confirmed bypass in the first draft: `Array.isArray(allowlists) ? allowlists : []` silently SKIPPED a present-but-non-array `allowlists`, i.e. the `[allowlists]` single-table spelling (plural name, one table) — and gitleaks 8.30 was measured to HONOR that spelling, its `paths` suppressing a real `ghp_` token at exit 0 while the shape check returned ok. The fix rejects any allowlist container that is not `[[allowlists]]`/`[allowlist]` and validates value types, so a dangerous key cannot hide as `condition = { paths }`. This is the same fail-open class the rewrite was built to close; it reached "solid" only after the adversarial review, which is the point of the battery. **Verified:** the end-to-end self-test (`node scripts/check-gitleaks-fixture.mjs`) accepts the real config and still fires the fixture + probe (exit 0); the confirmed single-table `[allowlists]` bypass is now rejected (gitleaks suppresses via it at exit 0; the gate rejects it); a live mutation adding a `stopwords` allowlist to the real `.gitleaks.toml` reds it (exit 1) with a key-named reason. Unit cases across the residual vectors (stopwords/commits/regexTarget/[[rules]]/extend.path/url/single-table-allowlists/non-table-entry/scalar-allowlist/nested-condition/unknown-key/malformed) hold the property. **Known limitation (NOT closed here):** a `regexes` allowlist scoped to a credential class the step-2 probe never tests (e.g. an AWS-key pattern `AKIA[0-9A-Z]{16}`) is a permitted shape AND invisible to the github-pat-only probe, so it passes both mechanisms while suppressing that class globally. This is inherent — the repo legitimately needs `regexes` allowlisting for fake fixture tokens, and no structural line separates "exempt one fake token" from "exempt a whole class" (only regex breadth, which is undecidable). Closing it means widening the probe to a second credential class (task #24), not tightening the shape schema. The source comment at the top of `assertConfigShape` states this gap rather than claiming coverage the gate does not hold. Reversible: revert the function + drop the dep; the behavioral self-test and unit suite hold the property either way.

## 2026-07-13 - Scanner pins bumped to current; the freshness check verifies against the binary

- **2026-07-13** · **Bumped the pinned security scanners to the latest upstream that the freshness check (#194) had flagged stale: `gitleaks 8.30.0 -> 8.30.1` and `semgrep 1.97.0 -> 1.169.0`.** The 2026-06-19 entries below remain the record of the ORIGINAL pins; this is the bump, not a rewrite of that history. **Method (do this on the next bump):** each version was verified against the ACTUAL binary, not assumed. gitleaks — the CI install step derives the sha256 from the release's own `checksums.txt` at install time, so bumping is a one-line env change with NO hardcoded hash to update; verified by pointing the fixture self-test at the real 8.30.1 (`GITLEAKS_BIN=…`) and confirming it still fires `github-pat`. semgrep — a 72-minor jump, so installed 1.169.0 in a `uv` py3.11 venv and ran BOTH halves of the CI job against it: the fixture self-test still loads the vendored `.semgrep` rules and fires them, and the real scan produces valid SARIF (0 findings, clean tree). **The `setuptools<81` constraint (2026-06-19, added because semgrep 1.97.0 imported `pkg_resources`, which setuptools 81 removed) is kept but is now likely VESTIGIAL:** 1.169.0 ran `--version` with no setuptools present at all. It is retained because it is verified-compatible and fail-safe — `pip install "setuptools<81"` resolves to a PATCHED setuptools 80.10.2 (measured on PyPI 2026-07-13; above the CVE-2025-47273 fix at 78.1.1 and the CVE-2024-6345 fix at 70.0.0), so it pins nothing vulnerable — but the next bumper can test dropping it. (An earlier draft of this bullet said 80.9.0, which two review agents asserted and I wrote down without checking; the measured resolution is 80.10.2, matching the 2026-06-19 entry below. Verify, do not trust, a version number.) Reversible: revert the two version strings; the checksum/self-test gates make a bad bump fail closed in CI regardless.
- **2026-07-13** · **Dropped the `setuptools<81` constraint entirely from the CI semgrep install (`pip install "setuptools<81" semgrep==1.169.0` -> `pip install semgrep==1.169.0`).** The bullet above kept it as "likely vestigial, the next bumper can test dropping it" — this is that test, run and acted on. **Measured:** semgrep 1.169.0 in a fresh `uv` py3.11 venv with NO setuptools installed (`import pkg_resources` raises `ModuleNotFoundError`) runs a full scan — vendored-rule config load, rule engine, valid SARIF out — at exit 0. So 1.169.0 no longer imports `pkg_resources`; the constraint protected against a dependency the tool dropped. Removing it is strictly better than keeping or floor-bounding it: the constraint FORCED a downgrade of the runner's pre-installed setuptools to the 80.10.2 line, which only accrues its own CVE exposure over time while buying nothing for semgrep, and it fights the ecosystem direction (setuptools removed `pkg_resources`; semgrep stopped using it). **Why not a `>=78.1.1,<81` floor (the originally-logged #28 fix):** a floor keeps the pointless downgrade and freezes setuptools permanently below the security-patch frontier; the low-probability failure it guards (a future semgrep bump re-adding `pkg_resources` while the runner ships setuptools>=81) is already caught LOUDLY by two existing CI gates — the fixture self-test (`check-semgrep-fixture.mjs`) and the "assert Semgrep produced a report" step — so the floor is redundant protection at a permanent cost. Install-side is safe: semgrep ships wheels (no build-time setuptools), and dropping the explicit constraint only stops downgrading the runner's setuptools, never removes it. Reversible: re-add the constraint string; a bad drop fails closed via the self-test + SARIF-produced assertion.

## 2026-07-12 - The metric gate holds SIGN, not prose magnitude

- **2026-07-12** · **`__tests__/metric-consistency.test.ts` sweeps every published `content/` module (globbed `.ts`/`.tsx`, not hand-listed) plus six non-content surfaces (`app/layout.tsx` metadata, `lib/hiring-profile.ts`, `lib/ask/system-prompt.ts`, `public/llms.txt`, `public/.well-known/agent.json`, `scripts/generate-og-image.ts`) for a metric stated with the OPPOSITE sign of its receipt in `content/perf-receipts.ts`. It deliberately does NOT gate magnitude in prose.** The glob is load-bearing: the pre-glob version hand-listed 9 of the 26 published content modules, so a new module quoting a metric would have shipped unswept and green — the identical fail-open that let `man-page.ts` carry a retired job title past the identity check. It is an EXCLUSION pattern, not an inclusion list, so the polarity stays fail-closed: a new content module is swept by default. Excluded are the modules that publish nothing — `*.test.ts` (CLAUDE.md exempts test files from the literal rules, so a fixture quoting a flipped metric is legitimate) and the content infrastructure (`schemas.ts`, `_*.ts`). `schemas.ts` matters specifically: its Zod error message quotes example deltas, so sweeping it made the gate police its own documentation — harmonising that message's wording would have red the suite with a fake contradiction. Those six are hand-named and shared with the identity gate, so a new non-content surface is ungated until someone adds it to both. Reversible (widen the assertion), but do not re-attempt the magnitude version — it was built and measured twice and false-fired on correct content both times. The cause is structural, not a tuning miss: a dense receipt list (`-33% JS, -98% CSS, -52% TTI`) puts every metric's keyword inside the proximity window of every *other* metric's number, and proximity cannot disambiguate two metrics sharing a sentence. Per the false-positive budget, a gate that routinely reds true copy is miscalibrated, not strict — it trains bypass. **The root cause the gate does not fix:** `lib/ask/system-prompt.ts` hand-writes the consulting-role numbers in its narrative while `LIVE_DATA` in the same file already *generates* them from `perfReceipts`. That hand-copy of a derived value is what let `+25% initial load` ship — a duration stated as if it grew, in a sentence listing three wins. Deriving the narrative line from `perfReceipts` would delete the bug class outright; the sign sweep only catches its worst symptom. **Where magnitude IS held:** `PROJECT_STAT_METRICS` compares rendered stat tiles to the receipt exactly (`!==`), and `HIRING_PROFILE_RECEIPT_FIELDS` compares `/api/erik.json` by PREFIX (`startsWith`) — three served values carry a legitimate trailing gloss (`-97.5% (40s→<1s, Venturus)`), so an exact match would red three true fields. Do not "tighten" that to `===`. **The precondition is held by a schema:** `PerfReceiptSchema.delta` must be a bare signed percentage, because the sweep searches surfaces for the sign-flipped delta *verbatim* — a glossed `-40% build time` or an unsigned `10%` flips to a string no surface contains, degrading that metric's sweep to a permanent silent no-op. That regex is deliberately narrower than the precondition: it allows percentages only, so a future duration receipt (`-1.2s`) hard-fails `validate-content`. When that fires, widen the UNIT group (`(%|s|ms|x)`) — `-1.2s` flips cleanly to `+1.2s` and is a perfectly good needle. Do NOT reintroduce a gloss, and do NOT drop either anchor. Dropping `^` admits a leading gloss (`build time -40%`), which flips to garbage; dropping `$` admits a TRAILING gloss (`-40% build time`), and only two cases in the bite list guard `$` at all. Both reopen the permanent no-op.

- **2026-07-12** · **`__tests__/identity-consistency.test.ts` hunts ONE retired title string across every published `content/` module (globbed `.ts`/`.tsx`, not hand-listed) plus the layout metadata, hiring profile, system prompt, `llms.txt`, `agent.json` and `scripts/generate-og-image.ts`. It is not a proof that the surfaces AGREE.** Both gates go through ONE reader (`readContentSurfaces` in `__tests__/helpers/content-surfaces.ts`), which applies ONE `isPublishedSurface` predicate, so the same exclusions apply — `*.test.ts`, `schemas.ts`, `_*.ts` — and the two sweeps cannot diverge over `content/`. They now also read the SAME six non-content surfaces (`app/layout.tsx` metadata, `lib/hiring-profile.ts`, `lib/ask/system-prompt.ts`, `public/llms.txt`, `public/.well-known/agent.json`, `scripts/generate-og-image.ts`). Until this entry the metric gate read only three of those: identity swept a strict SUPERSET, so a sign-flipped delta on the OG card, the agent card, or the page metadata shipped green. Those two lists are still hand-maintained and nothing reconciles them — the next non-content surface is ungated until someone adds it to both. The predicate was briefly duplicated per file; each copy had its own table test, so adding an exclusion to one copy would have narrowed one sweep with both tables still green. One predicate, one table. The content exclusion is still not free: A retired title reaching a `z.default('…')` in `schemas.ts`, or a rendered enum literal there, would be invisible to this sweep. Nothing publishes a title from `schemas.ts` today, which is why the exclusion is safe today. `content/shell-commands.ts` answers `whois` with a third variant (`full-stack engineer`, no "Senior") and nothing objects. Two shipped artifacts are ungated because they carry the title as pixels and subset-font glyphs, which no string assertion can read: `public/og.png` and `public/erik-cunha-cv.pdf` — a title change can green the suite while every LinkedIn unfurl still shows the old one. Gating them needs a PDF/image text parser, which is a dependency decision, not a test tweak.

## 2026-07-12 - Collapsible sections removed; Module renders a plain <section>

- **2026-07-12** · **`<details>`, `<summary>`, and every open/hide affordance are GONE from the codebase.** `Module` — the wrapper all 20 page sections sit in — renders `<section tabIndex={-1} aria-labelledby>` + a non-interactive `<header>` + `<h2>`; the design-system `Preview` renders its source unconditionally. Nothing collapses. **Held by a gate, not by a one-off grep — and the gate bans the tokens, not the behaviour:** `__tests__/module-section-visibility.test.ts` sweeps `.ts`/`.tsx`/`.css`/`.mdx` under `components/`, `app/`, `lib/`, `design-system/` and fails if `<details>`, `<summary>`, `::details-content`, `HTMLDetailsElement`, or (in CSS) an `[open]` selector reappears. `.mdx` is in that set because the five shipped `app/design-system/*/page.mdx` pages compile raw HTML to JSX — a `<details>` there is a real disclosure widget, and the sweep was blind to it until a reviewer demonstrated the bypass. Owner decision: the affordance bought nothing (sections rendered expanded by default, so no visitor lost anything) while costing a disclosure widget plus 20 focusable `<summary>` tab stops. The `module:open` event path (`Dock` → `lib/events.ts` → an `AppShell` delegated listener) went with it; the `Dock` still scrolls.

  **The mechanics below are held by GATES, not by this prose. Do not restate them here, and do not "simplify" the code toward a shorter version of them — each of these has a test whose failure message explains the consequence.**
  - `__tests__/module-section-visibility.test.ts` — **the CSS trap.** The pre-refactor base state was *collapsed* (`grid-template-rows: 0fr`, `opacity: 0`), with the expanded values living only under `.module-root[open]`. A `<section>` never matches `[open]`, so *deleting* rather than *merging* those rules leaves every section body at zero height and zero opacity — blanking the entire site with no error and no build failure. Unit tests render without CSS and structurally cannot catch it.
  - `tests/e2e/design-system-pages.spec.ts` — the DS `<pre>` must keep `tabIndex={0}` + `role="group"` + a **per-component** `aria-label`. Drop the role and the `pre[role="group"]` selector matches nothing and the test fails. (A bare `<pre>`'s implicit `generic` role is *name-from: prohibited*, so an `aria-label` on it is **inert** — it never reaches assistive tech.)
  - `__tests__/dock.test.tsx` — the `Dock` must call `el.focus({ preventScroll: true })`. It `preventDefault`s, which suppresses the native fragment navigation that would otherwise move focus to the `tabIndex={-1}` target; without it the *mobile* nav scrolls without ever moving focus.
  - `components/responsive/Module/Module.test.tsx` and `__tests__/content-visibility.test.ts` — `tabIndex={-1}`, the `aria-labelledby` ↔ `<h2>` contract, unique ids across two instances, and the `content-visibility` deferral.

  **The judgments no test holds:** (1) **Re-collapsing via JavaScript.** The sweep is a token ban, so a `useState(false)` + `<button>` toggle + conditional render would restore the whole affordance with every gate green. Sections staying uncollapsed is therefore a standing owner decision, not a mechanically enforced invariant — do not read the gate as broader than it is. (2) The DS `<pre>` uses `role="group"`, not `role="region"`. Both expose the label, and their Biome profiles are identical (measured). The sole discriminator is that `region` is a **landmark**: it would add 9 code-sample landmarks to the rotor alongside the page's primary ones. That is a **UX judgment, not a WCAG requirement** — `region` with the per-component labels would be perfectly compliant, and a reader wanting to jump straight to a component's source might prefer it. Reversing it is a one-word change.

  _Reversible: restore `<details open>` + `<summary>`, re-add the `[open]`-qualified CSS, reinstate the `module:open` path — but regenerate both baseline sets. Nothing consumed the collapse affordance._

## 2026-07-12 - classic `typescript` restored as a devDependency for JS-API consumers (Stryker); `tsgo` keeps the typecheck

- **2026-07-12** · **`typescript@^6.0.3` is back as a devDependency — NOT as the typechecker, but because tooling that consumes the TypeScript *JS API* needs a compiler that still has one.** The 2026-07-11 TS7 ADR (PR #179) deleted `typescript` outright and moved `pnpm typecheck` to `tsgo --noEmit` (`@typescript/native-preview`). That silently killed mutation testing: `@stryker-mutator/core` 9.6.1 reads `tsconfig.json` through the TS JS API in `sandbox/ts-config-preprocessor.ts` (`const { default: ts } = await import('typescript')`, driven from the instrumenter/sandbox path), and with classic TS gone it resolved the hoisted transitive `typescript@7.0.2` peer (pulled in via `@commitlint/cli` → `cosmiconfig`), whose API surface no longer exports `parseConfigFileTextToJson` → `TypeError`, Stryker crashed before running a single mutant, no `mutation-report.json`. Reproduced locally byte-for-byte with CI (instruments 45 files / 2041 mutants, then dies). Nothing in the fast path noticed, because mutation testing is a weekly `schedule` job — the breakage shipped in #179 (2026-07-11) and surfaced 5 days later on the 2026-07-12 cron. The `Guard — fail loudly if the runner did not actually run` step in `mutation.yml` is what surfaced it rather than a silently-green `continue-on-error` run — the anti-masking guard earned its keep. **Fix:** add `typescript@^6.0.3` (last line with the full JS API; TS7 removed it) as a devDependency. `pnpm typecheck` still runs `tsgo --noEmit` on `@typescript/native-preview` — the TS7 adoption is untouched; only the JS-API consumers get a compatible compiler. Verified: Stryker completes and produces a scoreable report (11 scoreable mutants on a scoped run, score 81.82 ≥ break 65), `pnpm typecheck` exit 0, and `CI=true pnpm build` exit 0 (no segfault). **Second-order effect, accepted:** with `typescript` resolvable again Next 16.2 no longer skips its in-build type-check — it detects the package and runs it ("Finished TypeScript in 3.7s"). This does NOT hit the #179 dead-end, whose segfault (exit 139 under `CI=true`) was specific to `typescript@7.0.2` being loaded by Next's `run-typescript` verify worker; TS6 is the compiler Next built against pre-#179 and it completes cleanly. Cost is ~4s of build time plus a type-check that duplicates `pnpm typecheck` — accepted as cheap defense-in-depth rather than suppressing it via `typescript.ignoreBuildErrors`. **Regression guard:** `__tests__/stryker-typescript-api.test.ts` asserts `parseConfigFileTextToJson` is present on the `typescript` resolved **from `@stryker-mutator/core`'s own module context** (`createRequire(require.resolve('@stryker-mutator/core'))`), not from the root importer — the two are different resolution paths and that asymmetry IS this bug: a bare specifier inside the pnpm store walks `node_modules/.pnpm/node_modules/` (the hoisted store) before falling through to the project root, which is how Stryker loaded a TS the root did not have. Asserting from the root would only cover the direct-dep subset (battery code-review finding): a future hard `dependencies: { typescript: "^7" }` in any transitive gets hoisted into the virtual store and shadows for Stryker while the root devDep stays 6.0.3 — root-context guard green, Stryker broken, cron catches it a week later. The guard therefore fails on all three: classic TS removed (unresolvable), bumped to a TS7 line (API absent), and store-shadowed by a hoisted TS7 (API absent). It runs in the required `test` job (`pnpm test:coverage` = `vitest run --coverage`; `**/__tests__/**` is in `coverage.exclude` but NOT `test.exclude`), so this class is caught on the PR instead of a week later on the cron. **Do not `pnpm up --latest` typescript** until `@stryker-mutator/core` targets the TS7 API — CLAUDE.md's stated bump path rewrites the range, not just the lock, so `--latest` will pull `^7` and re-break Stryker (the guard reds in CI rather than failing silently, which is the point, but it is a guaranteed red). _Reversible: drop the `typescript` devDependency and delete the guard test once Stryker ships a release that targets the TS7 API (then mutation testing runs against native-preview alone). Revisit when `@stryker-mutator/core` supports TypeScript 7._

## 2026-07-11 - Architect-gate async trust model: task-file corroboration, not transcript-record shape

- **2026-07-11** · **`agentResultContains` (sole consumer: `.claude/hooks/architect-gate.sh`) corroborates async architect verdicts against the subagent's own task output file instead of trusting transcript record shapes.** Two harness-drift incidents proved record-shape anchors fragile: CLI 2.1.2xx dropped `origin.kind: task-notification` (gate failed closed against every async PASS), and the replacement `queue-operation` anchor was paste-forgeable because typed mid-turn user messages ALSO land as queue-operation records with raw text in top-level `content` — no transcript record class distinguishes harness notifications from queued typed text (attachment records are equally contaminated). New model: any notification-shaped string in any record is only a POINTER; the gate extracts `task-id` + `output-file`, requires the path to end with `tasks/<task-id>.output` (task-id charset `[a-z0-9]{6,}` blocks traversal into the suffix), reads the file, and requires `GATE_RESULT: PASS` in the subagent transcript itself. Typed chat cannot create files. The pointer path is additionally session-pinned (claude-review finding, PR #183): the output file must end with `/<sessionId>/tasks/<taskId>.output` where sessionId derives from the transcript filename (harness-provided input, charset-validated, fail-closed when absent) — this rejects planted files at arbitrary roots matching the generic suffix AND cross-session replay of a genuine old PASS output file. The sync tool_result path is unchanged. The pointer is also ordering-bound (second claude-review finding, PR #183): `task-id` and `tool-use-id` arrive in the same attacker-suppliable string with nothing binding them to one dispatch, so a forged notification pairing the CURRENT dispatch id with an EARLIER same-session genuine PASS file was a zero-privilege intra-session replay. The gate now records the governing (latest) architect dispatch record's timestamp and requires the output file's mtime to be strictly after it (`TaskOutputReader` returns `{content, mtimeMs}`; missing dispatch timestamp or mtime fails closed) — a stale file always predates the dispatch it is being replayed against. The pointer is finally provenance-bound (battery security finding, same PR): mtime proves FRESHNESS, not IDENTITY — a pasted pointer could still name a fresher SIBLING subagent's output file that merely quotes the sentinel, and that is not hypothetical (measured: two unrelated battery outputs in this session each contained `GATE_RESULT: PASS` verbatim, and the pre-fix gate ACCEPTED the substitution against real harness files; post-fix it rejects it while the genuine file still passes). The gate now requires the named output file to carry the architect dispatch's OWN prompt (first 200 chars of `input.prompt`, read from the dispatching `tool_use` record — structured blocks in an assistant message, which typed chat text cannot produce), fail-closed when the dispatch has no bindable prompt. Known cost: this couples the gate to the harness writing the dispatch prompt into the task output file; if that stops, the gate fails CLOSED (blocks writing-plans) rather than open. The verdict scan is scoped to the assistant's OWN text blocks (battery code-review finding from #183's own provenance review — the finding arrived after #183 had already MERGED, and is fixed forward in PR #186): the needle was matched against the whole file, but the harness echoes the dispatch prompt into the file's first record and architect prompts literally instruct "return GATE_RESULT: PASS or GATE_RESULT: FAIL" — so every accepted file contained the sentinel in its prompt echo and the gate accepted a real `GATE_RESULT: FAIL` as PASS (reproduced against real harness data; no attacker needed). The gate now requires `GATE_RESULT: PASS` in an `assistant`-role text block (thinking blocks carry `.thinking`, not `.text`, so deliberation is excluded), where the verdict actually lives; provenance (prompt anchor) and freshness (mtime) still gate the same file. That first cut still matched the sentinel as a SUBSTRING of the assistant block, leaving a narrower residual of the same class (battery security finding): the architect's verdict template is `GATE_RESULT: PASS | BLOCK` and its negative verdict is `GATE_RESULT: BLOCK`, so a genuine BLOCK whose prose quotes the PASS token or echoes the template line still read as PASS (no attacker; the architect's own phrasing triggers it).
