The match is now the LAST standalone verdict line: a trimmed line equal to the needle, taking the last line that begins with the verdict key (`GATE_RESULT: `) as the decision so a later `GATE_RESULT: BLOCK` overrides an earlier stray PASS line (last-verdict-wins). The same line-exact check replaced the sync `tool_result` path's whole-blob `JSON.stringify(item).includes(needle)`, which shared the substring fail-open. Reproduced against realistic architect-shaped data: a PASS with the verdict on its own line corroborates; a BLOCK that both quotes the PASS token in prose and echoes the `GATE_RESULT: PASS | BLOCK` template is rejected. **Inherent limitation (honor-system boundary):** any actor with file-write capability (Bash/Write) can still author a prompt-carrying, PASS-bearing file at the real session task root; that capability is inside the gate's own TCB (equivalent to editing the hook itself), same class as review-stamp's "cannot know about a finding you never recorded". Text-only injection — the class this ADR exists to close — cannot manufacture a corroborating artifact (it cannot create a file, forge a `tool_use` dispatch record, or cause a sibling agent to emit both the architect's exact prompt prefix and a PASS verdict in its own assistant output). _Reversible: revert `scripts/lib/transcript.mjs` to a record-shape anchor — but expect it to break on the next CLI transcript-shape change; the file model is shape-independent._

## 2026-07-11 - Zod-free client-content split; employers.ts deleted as a dead parallel career source

- **2026-07-11** · **Client-island-consumed content strings live in zod-free plain modules; Zod validation stays server/build-side.** `content/shell-commands.ts` is bundled into the INTERACTIVE_SHELL client island; importing the now-line from `content/now.ts` dragged `zod` into the client chunk (280.1KB, over the 220KB gate). The shared string moved to `content/now-currently.ts` (no imports), consumed by both `now.ts` (Zod-parsed server-side) and `shell-commands.ts`; measured back to 214.6KB (PR #185). Precedent already existed for the pattern (`content/_validate-client-content.ts` externally validates the zod-free client modules). Rule of thumb: anything imported by a `*.client.tsx` chain must not import `zod`. _Reversible: inline the string back — but the bundle gate re-fails; the split is the mechanism holding the budget._

- **2026-07-11** · **`content/employers.ts` deleted — the site has ONE career-history source, `content/git-log.ts`.** claude-review found (PR #185) that `employers` (`BlameEntry[]`) had zero runtime importers: the rendered career section is `GitLogSection` ← `git-log.ts`; `employers.ts` was a stale parallel copy that every career sync had to double-maintain (this PR updated both before the finding). Deleted with its `validate-content.ts` entry and the now-orphaned `BlameEntrySchema`/`BlameEntry` in `content/schemas.ts`; the `docs/02-domain.md` blame-view row and the ai-eval skill's ground-truth example (→ `lib/hiring-profile.ts`) updated in the same PR (#185). _Reversible: restore the file from git history and re-add the schema + validation entry — but re-creating a second career source needs a consumer to justify it._

## 2026-07-11 - Signal-emphasis exception to the two-token palette (all viewports)

- **2026-07-11** · **Owner-directed emphasis exception: git-log commit-body lines, projects stat rows, and all four ask-eval captions render in `--signal`/`primary-500` on every viewport.** This annotates — does not repeal — the 2026-05-13 two-token palette ADR (`--signal` for headings/accents/large text; `--fg` for body): the rule stays the default for body prose; these specific terminal-verbatim surfaces (stat labels/values in both `ProjectsDesktop` and `ProjectsMobile`, short metric captions, 1-2-line commit-body pull-quotes in `GitLogSection.tsx` `renderCommit` isRoot/regular and `renderCommitMobile`) are promoted for visual hierarchy at the owner's explicit direction (PR #180 + follow-up). Shipped first as desktop-only (`md:`, `090a871` + `6acbdd0`), then extended to all viewports on owner clarification — colors are viewport-invariant; only the community font-size tweaks remain desktop-only. The 2026-05-13 ADR's stated mechanism is WCAG AA; measured contrast of `#00FF41` on `#000000` is **15.38:1** (and `primary-400` is 12.05:1), so no AA property is violated at any size — the deviation is aesthetic-policy only. _Reversible: restore `text-primary-400`/`text-secondary-200` on the promoted spans in the four section components — pure class edit, no persisted state._

## 2026-07-11 - TypeScript native compiler via @typescript/native-preview; typescript@7-under-CI is a documented dead-end

- **2026-07-11** · **Replaced `typescript` with `@typescript/native-preview` (tsgo); `pnpm typecheck` now runs `tsgo --noEmit`.** TS 7 removes the compiler JS API (`ts.createProgram` undefined; the package is a Go-binary wrapper + `unstable/*` surfaces) that Next 16.2's build-time type-check consumes. **Dead-end (attempted first): `typescript@7.0.2` + `typescript.ignoreBuildErrors: true`.** It passed locally but `next build` segfaults (exit 139) whenever `CI=true` is set — reproduced in clean Linux containers and bisected to the `CI` env var alone; the crash is in Next's `run-typescript` verify worker (which still loads the TS package for tsconfig validation even when skipping type checking) and its error is swallowed by a bare `.catch(() => process.exit(1))` in `next/dist/build/type-check.js`. Since GH Actions and Vercel both set `CI`, that path can never deploy. **Chosen path:** `@typescript/native-preview` is Next's supported native-compiler detection — with it installed and `typescript` absent, Next skips in-build type-checking by design (info log) and the build completes under `CI=true` (verified in-container: tsgo `--noEmit` exit 0, full PPR build green). The type-safety property stays mechanically gated by `pnpm typecheck` (`tsgo --noEmit`) in `ci:local`, the pre-push chain, and the required CI `typecheck` job (ci.yml now calls `pnpm typecheck` so the compiler binary is named in one place). Editor note: VS Code needs the "TypeScript Native Preview" extension for editor-side tsgo. The ci.yml `tsconfig.tsbuildinfo` cache step may be inert under tsgo (harmless). Landed with the dependabot #176 group (13 bumps) after dependabot closed that PR on rebase. Known cost: the exact-pinned `-dev` snapshot is a rolling prerelease Microsoft may eventually prune from npm; if unpublished, `--frozen-lockfile` installs hard-fail (availability, not security) — bump the snapshot deliberately when that happens. _Reversible: swap `@typescript/native-preview` back to `typescript@^6` and restore `"typecheck": "tsc --noEmit"` — one commit, no persisted state. Revisit when Next supports the TS 7 package natively (tracking: Next 16.2 × typescript@7 CI=true segfault)._

## 2026-07-10 - Bare-code comment policy: no prose comments in source

- **2026-07-10** · **All prose comments stripped repo-wide; machine directives only.** Owner decision on PR #177: decision narration, history, and redundant restatements had accumulated across source comments (spec/PR/workstream refs, dated incident recounts, `See DECISIONS.md` pointers, JSX section labels) — with `// WHY:` constraint notes also removed by explicit choice. The only comments permitted are machine directives: `biome-ignore` (with reason, per Biome syntax), `behavioral-test-allow` (consumed by the no-source-grep gate), `@ts-*`, shebangs, and triple-slash references. Rationale/constraints/history live in DECISIONS.md, commit messages, and docs. Applied via a TypeScript-AST-backed stripper (parser comment ranges, not regex) + CSS state machine + full-line shell/YAML removal: 346 files, ~7.7k lines. Two functional-comment classes were re-expressed without prose: lint-suppressing comments inside intentional empty blocks (noop arrows became `() => undefined`; empty `catch`/method bodies carry an explicit `biome-ignore` pragma). CLAUDE.md working-agreement + fix-conditions rules updated to match. _Reversible: comments are recoverable from git history; the policy itself is one CLAUDE.md line._

## 2026-07-02 - detect-changes: an `ai`-major bump re-arms ai-eval

- **2026-07-02** · **`detect-changes` re-arms `ai=true` when the `ai` package MAJOR changes.** The `ai` detect-changes category (which gates the real-Gateway `ai-eval` job) matched only ask CODE paths, deliberately excluding `package.json`/`pnpm-lock.yaml` to avoid burning Gateway credits on routine dep churn. That let dependabot #169 bump `ai` 6→7 as a **deps-only** change and merge green while shipping v7 pkg + v6 code — breaking prod `/api/ask` unvalidated (fixed by hotfix #170). Fix: the runner (`scripts/detect-changes.mjs`) now reads the `ai` **major** at BASE vs HEAD (`aiMajor` slice, mirroring the existing `ui` render-slice) and ORs `aiMajorChanged` into the `ai` output. Precedence is `pnpm.overrides.ai` → `dependencies.ai` → `devDependencies.ai` (an override forces the resolved version, so it must win — else a PR forcing `ai` v7 via `pnpm.overrides` while leaving the declared range at `^6` would bypass the re-arm; the repo uses overrides elsewhere). A **major** bump runs ai-eval; minor/patch bumps stay credit-cheap. Fail-safe: an unparseable/absent specifier or git-read error yields `null`, which over-arms rather than skips. Proven by a real-git end-to-end test (`re-arms ai=true when a deps-only commit bumps the ai MAJOR (6->7)`). _Reversible: drop the `aiMajorChanged` term from `computeCategories`; pure config/CI change, no persisted state._

## 2026-07-02 - AI SDK v6 → v7 migration (/api/ask)

- **2026-07-02** · **Migrated `ai` to v7 (`^7.0.14`).** dependabot #169 bumped the `ai` package to v7 as a deps-only change (which skips the `ai-eval` gate), shipping v7 pkg + v6 route code and breaking prod `/api/ask` (0-byte stream); this migrates the ask code to v7 (prod hotfix #170). Two v7 runtime breaking changes, handled per the bundled migration guide: (1) v7 **rejects `role:'system'` in `messages` by default** → added `allowSystemInMessages: true` (safe: `SYSTEM_TEXT` is a trusted server-side constant; the visitor question is the separate user message), preserving the `providerOptions.anthropic.cacheControl` ephemeral prompt-cache attachment; (2) v7 **removed `providerMetadata.anthropic.cacheCreationInputTokens`** → cache breakdown now read from the provider-agnostic `usage.inputTokenDetails.{cacheReadTokens,cacheWriteTokens}`, so the second provider-metadata promise (+ `isAnthropicCacheMeta` guard / `metaResolved` log field) was removed. Budget-refund fail-closed posture unchanged (gated on `usage` alone). Validated by the `ai-eval` CI job against the real AI Gateway. Follow-up: make an `ai`-package **major** bump set `ai=true` so a breaking SDK change can never skip `ai-eval` again. See `lib/ask/stream.ts` + `__tests__/ask-cache-metric.test.ts`. _Reversible: revert the code migration; the v7 package pin stays (from #169). Medium reversibility (prod AI path)._

## 2026-07-02 - psi-refresh reliability: deadline-budgeted retry + consecutive-failure alert gating

- **2026-07-02** · **PSI-refresh reliability: deadline-budgeted retry + consecutive-failure alert gating.** Measured ~30% daily psi-refresh failure across 2026-06-12 to 2026-07-01 (6 of ~20 runs): 4 mobile audits exceeded the 45s timeout, 2 hits upstream PSI 500 lighthouseError responses. Fix: (a) deadline-budgeted transient-error retry for `429` and `5xx` on the cron path, bounded by `PSI_STRATEGY_BUDGET_MS=50s` under the Hobby 60s job ceiling, and (b) per-strategy consecutive-failure alert gating (threshold 3) replacing alert-on-anyFailed to eliminate non-actionable alert noise from transient failures. Before/after: alert email fired on 1 failure; now fires only after 3 consecutive per-strategy failures. The mobile-45s-timeout class is accepted as a Hobby-tier ceiling limit (the only real fix is raising `maxDuration` via Vercel Pro). See the retry loop in `lib/lighthouse-scores.ts` (`PSI_STRATEGY_BUDGET_MS`, `isRetryablePsiError`), the alert gating in `app/api/psi-refresh/route.ts` (`PSI_CONSEC_KEY`, `PSI_ALERT_THRESHOLD`), and `__tests__/psi-refresh*.test.ts` + `__tests__/lighthouse-scores.test.ts`. _Reversible: remove the retry loop in `lib/lighthouse-scores.ts` and the `PSI_CONSEC_KEY`/`PSI_ALERT_THRESHOLD` gating in `app/api/psi-refresh/route.ts`, restoring the alert-on-anyFailed gate; high reversibility._

## 2026-06-21 - detect-changes pathspecs hoisted to a manifest + a stale-pathspec meta-gate

- **2026-06-21** - **detect-changes pathspecs hoisted to a manifest + a stale-pathspec meta-gate.** The job's git pathspecs moved from an inline ci.yml shell block to `scripts/detect-changes-paths.mjs` (single source of truth), the shell was replaced by `scripts/detect-changes.mjs` (a plain-node runner; behavior proven identical by a temp-git-repo parity test over 11 scenarios incl. the exclude + browserslist/pnpm render-slice edges), and `scripts/check-detect-changes-paths.mjs` now fails CI (exit 2) when any pathspec stops resolving. Closes the fail-open-on-stale-path class (the lib/eval ai-filter gap) for this job; continuous with the #154/#160/#162 vestigial-gate hardening. _Reversible: revert the ci.yml step to the inline shell and delete the three scripts; no persisted state._

## 2026-06-20 - Semgrep fixture self-test is now a BLOCKING CI step

- **2026-06-20** - **Semgrep fixture self-test is now a BLOCKING CI step (closes the 2026-06-19 ADR TODO b).** The vendored, content-pinned rules (`.semgrep/owasp-top-ten.yml` `child-process-shell-injection`, `.semgrep/secrets.yml` `hardcoded-stripe-secret-key`) were never exercised in CI: a clean tree makes the report-only scan's "0 findings" identical whether the rules work or are silently broken (the same vestigial-gate failure as the Stryker runner). Fix: `scripts/check-semgrep-fixture.mjs` copies `tests/fixtures/semgrep/{vulnerable,clean}.ts` to a temp dir (temp because `.semgrepignore` excludes `tests/fixtures/`), scans with `--config .semgrep` ONLY (no registry packs - deterministic/offline so a blocking gate cannot flake on registry drift), and asserts each vendored rule fires on `vulnerable.ts` and `clean.ts` is quiet (per-rule + clean=0, stronger than the TODO's literal ">=1 finding"). Gating split: job-level `continue-on-error` removed; the new self-test step is blocking; the report-only scan step keeps a step-level `continue-on-error` so its FINDINGS stay non-blocking until the FP-rate baseline is recorded. Exit 1 = rule regression, exit 2 = infra (semgrep missing/crash) so a broken runner is not misread as a rule break. Verified by the negative proof: breaking a vendored rule locally makes `pnpm lint:semgrep:selftest` exit 1 naming the rule. _Reversible: restore the job-level `continue-on-error`, drop the self-test step, delete the script + test + alias - one revert commit._

## 2026-06-20 - Mutation runs go incremental + cached

- **2026-06-20** · **Stryker mutation runs go incremental + cached (weekly job sped up ~36 min to ~2 min).** `stryker.config.mjs` sets `incremental: true` + `incrementalFile: '.stryker-incremental.json'` (overriding Stryker's `reports/stryker-incremental.json` default so the config, the `actions/cache` path, and `.gitignore` all name one file). `mutation.yml` caches that file with the evolving-cache pattern (`key: stryker-incremental-${{ github.run_id }}` + `restore-keys: stryker-incremental-`), so a run restores the prior file, Stryker re-tests only changed mutants, and the post-step saves the updated file. The report stays complete, so the score, the de-masking guard, and the weekly trend are unchanged. **Proven via two `workflow_dispatch` runs:** run 1 (cache miss, full) 36 min; run 2 (cache hit, incremental) 2 min, with the run log confirming `Cache restored from key: stryker-incremental-<run1 id>` and `1976 of 1976 mutant result(s) are reused`. The cache is NOT keyed on the lockfile on purpose (Stryker self-heals an incompatible incremental file by rebuilding, so lockfile-keying would force a full run on every Dependabot bump for no correctness gain). Accepted failure mode: a 7-day GitHub cache eviction at the weekly boundary, or the first weekly run on `main` after merge, is a correct full run that re-seeds the cache. The `run_id`-suffixed cache entries are small (~200KB JSON) and rely on GitHub LRU; churn is negligible (~52/year). _Reversible: remove `incremental`/`incrementalFile`, the cache step, and the gitignore line; the job reverts to the full ~36 min weekly run. Non-blocking weekly job, nothing downstream depends on its runtime._

## 2026-06-20 - Spec `clarify` convention

- **2026-06-20** · **`## Clarifications resolved` spec-template convention (handbook-routed, smallest platform-gaps unit).** Every spec now carries a `## Clarifications resolved` section listing the open design questions the brainstorming interview closed and their resolutions, in the form `<question> -> <resolution>`. Documented in `docs/handbook/knowledge-architecture.md`'s spec→plan pipeline section; several specs in this program already use the shape implicitly, so the convention names existing practice rather than adding ceremony. The failure mode it addresses: a spec that omits what the interview decided forces a reader (or the architect gate) to replay the interview to recover intent. **Deliberately scoped down from the rev.1 design:** the proposed `tasks` artifact was DROPPED because the plans in the plans directory already ARE stable-ID checkbox task lists mapping one-to-one to commits, so a separate artifact would duplicate and fragment the working single-plan pattern; and the convention was routed to the handbook rather than a `.claude/` skill or rule (a skill fires on action triggers, a rule needs a file-scope trigger, and this is a passive reference convention), adding zero `.claude/` surface and zero CLAUDE.md prose. _Reversible: revert the single `docs/handbook/knowledge-architecture.md` edit; no code, no production impact, no `.claude/` change._

## 2026-06-20 — claude-review is the sole AI PR reviewer (Copilot dropped)

- **2026-06-20** — **GitHub Copilot review dropped; `/claude-review` (claude[bot]) is now the sole AI reviewer.** Copilot's bot errored GitHub-side across an entire working session while claude-review carried the real review load, so the user made claude-review the standing reviewer. The convergence loop re-requests only `/claude-review`; nothing requests `copilot-pull-request-reviewer` anymore. _Reversible: re-add a Copilot request step + restore the copilot-gate._
- **2026-06-20** — **The merge reviewer-gate is now a claude[bot] Approve verdict, and it is STRICTER than the old Copilot gate.** `scripts/check-copilot-approval.ts` (which only checked that a Copilot review *existed*) is replaced by `scripts/check-claude-approval.ts` (`pnpm claude-gate`, called by `ready-to-merge`): it passes only when the latest `/claude-review` overview verdict is **Approve** AND the review is non-stale (the SHA stated in the overview is a prefix of HEAD). Pure `parseClaudeVerdict` (bold-verdict-only, so prose mentioning "approve" never counts) + `extractReviewedSha` (tolerant of the 3 observed overview SHA formats) are unit-tested in `__tests__/scripts/check-claude-approval.test.ts`. Branch protection never required Copilot (`required_reviews: null`), so the repo's own gate was the only blocker. _Reversible: revert the gate script + ready-to-merge wiring._
- **2026-06-20** — **`pr-metrics` now counts `/claude-review` cycles** (issue comments matching `/claude-review`) instead of Copilot review-request timeline events; >2 cycles is still the "thinking-inversion/TDD failed upstream" signal. Copilot references were also purged from the operational surface (`ready-for-pr`, `bash-guard`, `pre-push` comment, the `merge`/`ready-for-pr`/`pr-metrics` command docs, the `review-convergence` + `pr-merge-gate` skills, and CLAUDE.md). Stylistic "Copilot-style" descriptors in the claude-review prompt/comments are kept — they describe the inline-review FORMAT, not a dependency. _Reversible: the metric + text changes are self-contained._

## 2026-06-20 — Mutation runner fix: load vitest-runner under pnpm + de-masking guard

- **2026-06-20** — **RESOLVES the 2026-06-19 KNOWN ISSUE: the Stryker vitest runner now loads under pnpm.** Root cause (confirmed): Stryker's default plugin-discovery glob `@stryker-mutator/*` does not resolve the `.pnpm`-symlinked `@stryker-mutator/vitest-runner` in its worker process, so the runner never loaded and every weekly run produced zero signal. Fix: name the plugin explicitly via `plugins: ['@stryker-mutator/vitest-runner']` in `stryker.config.mjs` (a `// WHY:` comment marks the site). **Verified locally** with a scoped bare run (`stryker run --mutate 'lib/ip-hash.ts'`, NO `--plugins` flag, reading only the committed config): the runner loaded with zero `no TestRunner plugin` errors and produced 53 scoreable mutants (45 killed). This proves the *config* form fixes it, not just the CLI flag the reconcile used. _Reversible: remove the `plugins` line (reverts to the broken default glob)._
- **2026-06-20** — **De-masking guard added to `mutation.yml`: a runner crash now fails the job loudly; a genuine low score stays non-blocking.** The `Run mutation tests` step keeps `continue-on-error: true` (a real low score is a trend signal, not a per-commit gate), but a new guard step (no `continue-on-error`) asserts a report exists AND has ≥1 scoreable mutant — exiting non-zero with a `::error::` annotation when the runner produced nothing. This is the structural fix for *why the dead gate went unnoticed for weeks*: `continue-on-error` could not tell "tests are weak (low score)" from "the runner crashed (no score)". Now only the crash case fails. _Reversible: delete the guard step._
- **2026-06-20** — **CI proof is a `workflow_dispatch` run on the fix branch before merge.** The fix is validated in CI by dispatching `mutation.yml` on this branch and confirming (a) the job posts a REAL mutation score to the summary (not "report not generated"), and (b) the new guard passes. This is the merge precondition — the same `continue-on-error` that masked the failure means a green job alone is not proof; the posted score is.
- **2026-06-20** — **Working runner exceeds the old 30-min job timeout; bumped to 120.** The first CI-proof dispatch was CANCELLED at exactly 30:00 (`The operation was canceled`) — not a failure, the `timeout-minutes: 30` killed it mid-run. Second-order effect of the fix: the *dead* runner used to "finish" in ~7 min because it loaded no tests and mutated nothing; the *working* runner actually executes the suite against ~1986 instrumented mutants (perTest coverage), which is far slower. Fix: raise `timeout-minutes` 30 → 120 (a weekly, non-blocking job can afford it; GitHub job max is 360) and correct the stale "~10-15 min" comments. Real runtime is measured by the re-dispatched run. _Reversible: lower the timeout. Future optimization (noted, not done): Stryker `--incremental` + an `actions/cache` of the incremental file would make steady-state weekly runs fast._
- **2026-06-20** — **Guard `::error::` annotation routed to stdout.** claude-review MINOR: the guard's `node` script used `console.error('::error::…')`, but GitHub Actions parses `::error::` workflow commands only from STDOUT, so the annotation never surfaced in the checks UI (the step still failed correctly via `process.exit(1)`). Fixed to `console.log`. _Reversible: trivial._

- **2026-06-19** — **Stryker `mutate` scope narrowed to `app/api/**` + `lib/**`; `scripts/**` removed.** Mutation testing (already wired at `ffa7009`) exists to prove the test suite *catches regressions* at the highest-leverage surface: API routes and server logic, where a missed bug is a security/correctness defect rather than a UI regression. `scripts/**` is build/CI tooling — its failures surface directly in CI runs, so mutating it adds run time and maintenance drag (the precise failure mode the unit exists to avoid) for marginal signal. Narrowing aligns the config with the standing "highest-value mutation targets" comment already in `stryker.config.mjs`. _Reversible: re-add `'scripts/**/*.ts'` to the `mutate` array if scripts ever grow logic worth mutation-guarding._
- **2026-06-19** — **Mutation testing is non-blocking via the workflow lever (`continue-on-error: true`), NOT via an absent break threshold.** `stryker.config.mjs` sets `thresholds.break: 65`, which on its own would make `stryker run` exit non-zero below 65% and hard-fail the job. The job does not block because `.github/workflows/mutation.yml`'s `Run mutation tests` step carries `continue-on-error: true`, and the workflow runs on a weekly `schedule` + `workflow_dispatch` only (never on `push`/`pull_request`). The INTENDED value is **trend tracking** (uploaded report artifact + job-summary score), not per-commit enforcement — a per-commit mutation gate is slow (~10-15 min) and, before a stable baseline exists, would retrain `--no-verify` bypasses (the false-positive failure mode CLAUDE.md warns against). **Current reality (see the KNOWN ISSUE bullet below): that trend signal does NOT yet exist — the runner fails to load under pnpm and `continue-on-error` has silently masked it, so no score has ever actually been posted.** _Reversible: the gate is config-present but workflow-suppressed; flipping it on is a one-line workflow change, gated by the baseline below._
- **2026-06-19** — **Baseline / false-positive condition required before any blocking flip.** Promoting mutation testing to a blocking gate is explicitly deferred. Before removing `continue-on-error` (or adding a `push`/`pull_request` trigger), ALL must hold: (1) at least **3 consecutive weekly runs** recorded with a mutation score for the narrowed `app/api/**` + `lib/**` scope, establishing the real baseline; (2) the chosen `break` value set **at or below the observed floor minus a margin** (not the aspirational 80, not a guessed 65) so a green suite cannot fail the gate on noise; (3) a documented exclusion path for equivalent/non-killable mutants (e.g. log-string mutations) so a true-but-unkillable mutant does not force a bypass. Until all three exist, the gate stays non-blocking. _Reversible: this is the precondition record; no code changes here._
- **2026-06-20** — **KNOWN ISSUE (surfaced during this reconcile): the Stryker runner has never loaded under pnpm — the gate is currently vestigial.** `pnpm test:mutation` (bare `stryker run`) exits with `Cannot find TestRunner plugin "vitest"`: Stryker's default `plugins: ['@stryker-mutator/*']` glob does not resolve the `.pnpm`-symlinked `@stryker-mutator/vitest-runner` in its worker process. All four prior weekly runs (`2026-05-24` … `2026-06-14`) report the `Run mutation tests` step as job-level `success` ONLY because its `continue-on-error: true` masks the real `outcome: failure` (8× `no TestRunner plugin` in the `2026-06-14` log; no score in the job summary). Every prior "green" weekly run therefore produced ZERO mutation signal. The SURVIVED→KILLED liveness proof in this reconcile passed locally only by adding `--plugins '@stryker-mutator/vitest-runner'` explicitly — a flag NOT in the committed config or script. **Minimum fix, deliberately DEFERRED to a separate follow-up PR (this reconcile is scoped to the `mutate` array only, per the architect-approved plan): add `plugins: ['@stryker-mutator/vitest-runner']` to `stryker.config.mjs`, then prove via `workflow_dispatch` that `mutation.yml` posts a real score.** Recorded here so the runner fix — and a de-masking guard that surfaces a runner crash distinctly from a genuine low score (the masking is why this went unnoticed for weeks) — are tracked, not lost. _Reversible: the follow-up is additive (one config line + a guard + an ADR)._

## 2026-06-19 — Agent/prompt eval harness (platform self-eval, non-blocking)

- **2026-06-19** — **Monte-Carlo prompt-regression harness for the platform's own prompts/rules** (`feat/platform-gaps-2026-agent-eval`). Adds `lib/eval/` (shared judge, percentile, cost, Redis-publish, Monte-Carlo, calibration, A/B, target-run, grading extracted from `scripts/ask-eval.ts`), the `evals/agents/` gold corpus, `scripts/agent-eval.ts`, and a `pnpm eval:agents` runner. Rationale: the platform asserts CLAUDE.md rules are load-bearing but nothing *measured* whether a rule actually moves an agent's success rate. This harness runs a rule/prompt against a gold corpus N times, grades each run (code assertions first, then one calibrated LLM-judge call), and reports pass@k / pass^k / variance, with an A/B (`--ab`) control-vs-treatment mode that quantifies whether pruning a rule degrades behavior. It evals the **platform's own prompts**, distinct from `ask:eval` which evals the **product** (`/api/ask`). Foundation: the `wshobson plugin-eval` Monte-Carlo pattern.
- **2026-06-19** — **Cost ceiling is a hard, pre-flight budget.** `N <= 5` runs per case (env `EVAL_RUNS`, default 3, clamped), corpus <= 20 cases, `MAX_JOB_COST_USD = 2.00` per single-arm job. The runner projects cost from the pricing table BEFORE any model call and aborts over the cap (writing an aborted artifact). A/B mode runs both arms, so its effective cap doubles to `4.00` (`2 × MAX_JOB_COST_USD`) and the estimate accounts for both. Rationale: an unbounded N or a runaway corpus could silently burn Gateway credits; the cap makes the spend bounded and inspectable. _Reversible: the cap constants live in `lib/eval/budget.ts`._
- **2026-06-19** — **Calibration is a first-class gate, run before the corpus.** `runCalibration()` scores a gold set through the shared judge and gates on `MIN_CALIBRATION_AGREEMENT = 0.85` (with `CALIBRATION_ERROR_FRACTION_LIMIT = 0.5` distinguishing a drifted judge from a Gateway outage). A drifted judge fails the run before the corpus spends tokens. The shared `judge()` guarantees one judge prompt across both harnesses, so they can never drift apart.
- **2026-06-19** — **Distinct from `ask:eval` across every surface.** Separate corpus (`evals/agents/`, not `content/ask-eval-corpus.ts`), separate script, separate result file (`agent-eval-result.json`), and a separate Redis key `agent-eval:latest` (pinned + guarded by `__tests__/agent-eval-redis-key.test.ts` so the two harnesses never collide on one key). Shared *scaffolding* (`lib/eval/`), never shared *corpus*.
- **2026-06-19** — **CI job is non-blocking, manual/scheduled only — in its OWN workflow.** The `agent-eval` job lives in a dedicated `.github/workflows/agent-eval.yml` whose `on:` is `workflow_dispatch` + a weekly `schedule` cron (Mondays 06:00 UTC) ONLY. **Deviation from the plan (which said to add the job + a `schedule:` to `ci.yml`'s top-level `on:`):** a schedule on `ci.yml` fires the WHOLE suite weekly — including the paid `ai-eval` job — because the always-on jobs run on any non-`pull_request` event (and `detect-changes`' PR-context diff is undefined on a schedule event). Isolating the eval in its own workflow makes the weekly cron drive only this job and gives a stronger non-blocking guarantee than a job-level `if:`: the workflow physically cannot fire on a `pull_request`/`push`, so it can never gate a merge. It is not a required check and is in no other workflow's `needs`. Deliberately NOT on the blocking `ai-eval` path (the `ai_changed` detect-changes filter is untouched). Guarded by `__tests__/agent-eval-ci-config.test.ts` (asserts the dispatch/schedule-only triggers AND that `ci.yml` carries no schedule / no agent-eval job — the regression guard for the second-order effect). _Reversible: removing `evals/agents/` + `scripts/agent-eval.ts` + the `eval:agents` script + `.github/workflows/agent-eval.yml` + the `agent-eval:latest` key fully reverts the unit. `lib/eval/` stays as it is now a dependency of `ask:eval`._

## 2026-06-19 — Dependency audit: override undici ^7.28.0 + js-yaml ^4.2.0

- **2026-06-19** — **pnpm.overrides: undici ^7.28.0** (GHSA-vmh5-mc38-953g, GHSA-vxpw-j846-p89q, GHSA-hm92-r4w5-c3mj, GHSA-pr7r-676h-xcf6, GHSA-p88m-4jfj-68fv) + **js-yaml ^4.2.0** (GHSA-h67p-54hq-rp68). Root cause: `pnpm audit --audit-level=moderate` flagged undici 7.27.2 (transitive via jsdom, dev-only) with 3 HIGH + 2 MODERATE advisories, and js-yaml 3.14.2 (transitive via @lhci/utils) with 1 MODERATE advisory. **Decision:** both dependencies are overridable without API breakage — undici ^7.28.0 is a patch/minor-range bump within the 7.x major (jsdom is compatible), and js-yaml 3.14.2 → 4.2.0 is a major bump but @lhci/utils accepts js-yaml 4.x (it does not use the removed safeLoad API; verified by running `pnpm exec lhci --version` and `pnpm exec commitlint --version` post-override). js-yaml override eliminates the GHSA (⟵ @lhci/utils previously pinned to 3.14.2 for unrelated reasons, now patched); no lhci functionality lost. Commit `pnpm install` locks the overridden versions. **Audit result post-override:** `pnpm audit --audit-level=moderate` → exit 0 ("No known vulnerabilities found"). Typecheck + dep-pinning tests remain green. _Reversible: remove both overrides from pnpm.overrides block._

## 2026-06-19 — Static analysis: Semgrep CLI in CI (non-blocking)

- **2026-06-19** — **Semgrep CLI wired as a CI security-measuring layer** (`feat/platform-gaps-2026-semgrep`). Adds a `semgrep` job to `ci.yml`, a `scripts/run-semgrep.mjs` wrapper, a `pnpm lint:semgrep` script, and the Trail of Bits `semgrep` skill at `.claude/skills/semgrep/`. Rationale: the platform asserts "security is implicit on every change" but nothing *measured* it per-diff; the 5-agent battery *reasons about* vulnerabilities, Semgrep *measures* them (taint/injection, secrets) deterministically with no hallucination. Complement, not replacement — no battery change. **Pin:** `semgrep==1.97.0` (exact, mirroring `--frozen-lockfile`); the GitHub action and `upload-sarif` are SHA-pinned like every other action.
- **2026-06-19** — **Ruleset pinning is split by trust + drift sensitivity.** The security-load-bearing packs (OWASP Top Ten, secrets) are **vendored into `.semgrep/` and content-pinned** — repo files are the source of truth, immune to registry-side rule changes. The framework lint packs (`p/typescript`, `p/react`, `p/nextjs`) are fetched as **registry-latest (mutable — the Semgrep CLI has no pack version-pinning syntax)**; their rules can change upstream, so findings from them may drift between runs. This residual drift is **accepted** for the framework packs (lower security stakes, high upstream churn not worth vendoring), which is why the security-load-bearing OWASP/secrets rules are instead **vendored + content-pinned in `.semgrep/`** rather than pulled from the registry. Drift is **eliminated** for the security packs and accepted for the framework packs. _Reversible: re-vendor a framework pack into `.semgrep/` if its drift ever matters._
- **2026-06-19** — **Initially non-blocking** (`continue-on-error: true`) per the CLAUDE.md false-positive-budget rule. **Promotion gate (recorded here so it is objective): promote to blocking only after the false-positive rate on this codebase is measured at ≤ 10% over ≥ 3 representative PRs, with the count recorded in this ADR.** Until then the job is report-only and uploads SARIF to the code-scanning tab. **FP baseline (to be filled after the first 3 PRs):** `<total findings> / <false positives> = <rate>%` — TBD. `lint:semgrep` is intentionally **not** in `verify`/`ci:local` (avoids a Python dependency on every local run); it joins `ci:local` only at promotion. **Promotion-phase hardening TODOs (deferred to this gate, not done now):** (a) before `upload-sarif`, assert `semgrep.sarif` EXISTS and has ≥ 1 `runs[]` entry (else `core.warning`), so a registry-fetch failure that produces no SARIF is not a silently-green no-op; (b) wire the vulnerable-fixture trigger assertion into the CI `semgrep` job — scan a temp copy of `tests/fixtures/semgrep/vulnerable.ts` and assert ≥ 1 finding — so a broken vendored rule cannot ship silently (the `test` CI job has no Semgrep, so the rule-fires-correctly property is currently unverified in CI).
- **2026-06-19** — **CodeQL is NOT introduced here — it is already wired** at `.github/workflows/codeql.yml` (variant analysis, weekly + per-PR, SARIF to code-scanning). Semgrep MCP (interactive) is deferred to a later phase. "CodeQL deferred" in the spec refers to *expanding* CodeQL variant analysis, not adding it. _Reversibility (whole unit): delete the `semgrep` CI job, `scripts/run-semgrep.mjs`, `lint:semgrep`, `.semgrep/`, `.semgrepignore`, the fixtures, and `.claude/skills/semgrep/`. No production-code or bundle impact._
- **2026-06-19 (review convergence, #143)** — **CI resolves Semgrep's absolute path; invocation is PATH-independent.** Root cause of the CI `semgrep`-job failure: `run-semgrep.mjs` invoked the bare `semgrep` console script, which pip installs into an interpreter `bin/` dir that is **not** on the Node child process's PATH on hosted runners — the `--version` probe failed, the scan never ran, no SARIF was written, and `upload-sarif` then failed a second time on the missing file (masking the real cause). **Dead end (recorded so it is not retried):** a first fix added a `semgrep → python3 -m semgrep → python -m semgrep` resolver on the assumption the module form bypasses PATH; it **failed in CI** — semgrep ships a console script only (no `__main__` entrypoint), so `python -m semgrep` is not a valid invocation, and all three candidates missed. **Actual fix:** the CI install step resolves the absolute console-script path from the active interpreter (`sysconfig.get_path('scripts')`, with `site.getuserbase()/bin` and `shutil.which` fallbacks) and exports it as `SEMGREP_BIN`; the wrapper honors `SEMGREP_BIN` exactly (no fallback), so the invocation does not depend on PATH at all. The step runs `"$SEMGREP_BIN" --version` to fail early with a clear signal if resolution is wrong — which it did: once the path resolved, `--version` surfaced a second, real failure (`ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'pkg_resources'`). **Root cause:** semgrep 1.97.0 imports `pkg_resources` at runtime, and pip pulled `setuptools 82`, which **removed** `pkg_resources` (deprecated at 81). **Fix:** `pip install "setuptools<81" semgrep==1.97.0` — pin setuptools below 81 so `pkg_resources` is present. Verified locally: setuptools 80.10.2 imports `pkg_resources`, 82.0.1 raises the exact CI error (the setuptools deprecation warning itself instructs "pin to Setuptools<81"). This is an external-dep incompatibility, documented with a `# WHY:` comment at the site per the four-conditions rule. The wrapper's local-dev candidate probe (`semgrep` first) is retained for developers who have it on PATH. **F1 promotion-TODO (a) partially pulled forward:** `upload-sarif` is now guarded `if: always() && hashFiles('semgrep.sarif') != ''`, so a no-SARIF run is a clean skip rather than a masking failure; the richer "≥ 1 `runs[]` entry else `core.warning`" content assertion stays deferred to the promotion gate. Folded in 2 reviewer comment-accuracy fixes: the `--error`/`continue-on-error` rationale (a fails-open promotion trap if read literally) and the taint-source breadth note (the rule does not exclude constants). _Reversible with the whole unit._
- **2026-06-19 (review convergence, #143)** — **`ui` detect-changes filter excludes `package.json`.** This PR's `package.json` edit (adding the `lint:semgrep` script) tripped the `ui` path filter that gates the visual / Argos job, so the visual suite ran on a workflow-only PR and Argos reported a spurious "4 added" — the exact failure mode the filter's comment says it avoids (it already drops `.github/workflows/`, lighthouserc, bundle-size). **Fix:** drop `package.json` from the `ui` filter's plain path list (keep `pnpm-lock.yaml`). A scripts-only `package.json` edit cannot change a rendered pixel; most rendering-affecting dependency changes go through the lockfile, which stays in `ui`. **Gap closed (two review catches):** two `package.json` regions — `browserslist` (build target) and the `pnpm` block (`overrides` pins transitive versions) — can change emitted CSS/JS *without* a lockfile diff, so a plain exclusion would silently skip the visual gate on those edits. A first attempt used a line-grep (`git diff -- package.json | grep browserslist|...`), but review caught that a `browserslist` array-ELEMENT edit (`"chrome >= 93"` → `">= 95"`) changes a line that does not contain the key name, so the grep missed the common case. **Actual fix:** compare the fields SEMANTICALLY — `jq -cS '{browserslist,pnpm}'` of `git show BASE:package.json` vs `HEAD:package.json`; `ui_pkg_changed` re-arms `ui` when they differ. Verified locally (element edit → differs, scripts-only edit → same) and guarded by a behavioral test that the `{browserslist,pnpm}` projection discriminates those cases. `package.json` remains in the broader `app` filter, so build/e2e/perf gates still fire on it. Guarded by `__tests__/scripts/ci-ui-filter.test.ts`. _Reversible: re-add `package.json` to the `ui` list._

## 2026-06-19 — Fast-feedback hooks (Unit A)

- **2026-06-19** · **Fast-feedback hooks (Unit A).** Added two non-blocking local-session hooks: `biome-format.sh` (PostToolUse `Edit|Write`) runs `biome check --write --linter-enabled=false` on edited source files (format + import-organization only; the linter is off to keep the hook strictly mechanical — plain `check --write` runs SAFE fixes only and does not delete unused imports anyway, so linter-off is a scope choice that leaves every lint finding as a conscious pre-commit gate, not a deletion guard) at a measured ~130-140ms warm wall time on darwin (first cold run ~200ms; python3 fork + Biome startup; the python3 extraction is the repo's established hook idiom and is kept); `session-context.sh` (SessionStart) injects branch / ahead-behind / uncommitted / open-PR / CI state as `additionalContext`, `gh` bounded by `timeout 5` when `timeout` is present (stock macOS has none, in which case gh runs unbounded rather than being skipped) with a git-only fallback. Both `exit 0` always. Closes the one "fast-feedback" axis the 2026 ecosystem benchmark flagged as thin. _Reversible: delete the two `.claude/hooks/*.sh` files and their `settings.json` entries; no CI change, no data migration, no external dependency._

## 2026-06-18 — Ecosystem-benchmark follow-through: skill extraction, learning-loop foundation, AI-review pilot, Upstash MCP
- **2026-06-18** · **Copilot convergence loop extracted to a skill (CLAUDE.md shrink).** The ~10-step loop moved from an always-loaded CLAUDE.md bullet to `.claude/skills/copilot-convergence/SKILL.md`, loaded on demand when working an open PR toward merge. CLAUDE.md keeps a one-line stub pointer. Physical line count is flat (it was one long line) but a large procedure now leaves the always-loaded context, which is the real context-rot win (per the WS7 ADR's own line-count caveat). Hooks/CI still enforce the gates regardless. _Reversible: inline the skill body back into CLAUDE.md._
- **2026-06-18** · **Learning loop (M5): data foundation + analyzer + flood-mitigated auto-trigger.** (a) `review:stamp` appends each stamped cycle's resolved/justified findings to an append-only archive (`.review-findings-archive.jsonl`, gitignored) tagged with cycle SHA + commit time (`archiveRecords` in `scripts/review-findings.ts`). (b) `pnpm review:learn` (`scripts/review-learn.ts`, unit-tested) reads the archive and PROPOSES finding-classes that recurred across >= N cycles as gate candidates, most-recurrent first; graceful "no history yet" on the empty archive. (c) **Auto-trigger built on explicit opt-in, with the flood-mitigation that was the precondition for building it at all:** `.claude/hooks/learning-loop.sh` (SessionEnd, once per session, NOT per-turn, so no nagging) runs `review:learn --auto`, which appends only NEW finding-classes recurring across >= 3 cycles (stricter than the manual default of 2), capped at 3, to the gitignored `.review-learnings.md` inbox; silent unless it records something; never creates a gate, never blocks the session. The guardrails (high evidence bar + cap + append-only + human reviews the inbox) ARE the feature: they prevent the monotonic-growth flood that made a naive auto-proposer a no. _Reversible: remove the SessionEnd hook entry + `learning-loop.sh`; or the whole loop (archive append, `review-learn.ts`)._
- **2026-06-18** · **claude-code-action PILOT workflow added (`.github/workflows/claude.yml`).** Opt-in second AI reviewer that acts only on an explicit `@claude` comment AND requires the `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` secret (Max-subscription auth via `claude setup-token`, chosen over a billable `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`); inert otherwise (a `contains(...'@claude')` job guard skips the runner). Supplements, never replaces, the local 5-agent battery + Copilot loop. **Wiring complete:** action SHA-pinned to `806af32`, `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` secret set, Claude GitHub App installed. **Caveat before trusting:** measure its signal-to-noise on one pilot first (AI review is triage, not a guarantee); it requests write permissions (changes still pass through branch protection + Copilot). _Reversible: delete the workflow._
- **2026-06-18** · **Upstash MCP added to `.mcp.json` (PILOT, flagged for veto).** Configured with a read-only key reference (`${UPSTASH_READONLY_API_KEY}`): the server auto-disables every state-modifying tool when started with a read-only key, neutralizing the write/delete-database risk. **Three residual downsides, your call in review:** (1) it fails to start until `UPSTASH_EMAIL` + `UPSTASH_READONLY_API_KEY` are set, adding a startup warning to every session in this repo until then; (2) `@latest` is unpinned, against the repo's dependency-pinning convention (pin a version, or accept npx-float for a non-dependency); (3) marginal value for a solo reference-system (debugging rate-limit/KV state). This is the most likely veto candidate of this batch; if you prefer, move it to gitignored `.claude/settings.local.json` so it does not ship in project config. _Reversible: remove the `upstash` entry from `.mcp.json`._

## 2026-06-16 — PSI-refresh: write freshness key on partial success (Hobby blocks twice-daily cron)

- **2026-06-16** · **`meta:psi-last-run` now written when AT LEAST ONE strategy succeeds, not only on full success.** On 2026-06-16T03:03 UTC the mobile PSI audit exceeded `PSI_REFRESH_TIMEOUT_MS = 45s` (`lib/lighthouse-scores.ts`) and aborted; desktop succeeded. The freshness key was written only in the both-succeed branch of `app/api/psi-refresh/route.ts`, so one mobile timeout discarded desktop's successful refresh and the key went stale; after the 25h `PSI_STALE_MS` window (`app/api/healthz/route.ts`) `/api/healthz` flipped to `degraded`. Vercel Cron does **not** auto-retry on a non-2xx (it only re-fires at the next tick), so a single transient timeout degrades health for ~a full day. Mobile is the systematic failure mode: PSI applies 4× CPU + slow-4G throttling server-side, so mobile audits run 1.5-2× longer than desktop and sit closer to the 45s ceiling.
- **Falsified first attempt:** the initial fix changed the schedule `0 3 * * *` → `0 3,15 * * *` (twice daily) so a transient failure self-heals within 12h. This **failed Vercel deployment**: the Hobby plan caps crons at once per day, and a more-frequent expression is rejected at deploy validation (`Hobby accounts are limited to daily cron jobs. This cron expression would run more than once per day.`, see https://vercel.com/docs/cron-jobs/usage-and-pricing). The PR #125 Vercel check failed at 0s (rejected at creation, never built). Schedule reverted to `0 3 * * *`. Lesson: the schedule lever does not exist on Hobby; this should have been falsified before recommending it.
- **Chosen fix (deployable under once-daily + `maxDuration=60`):** decouple the health key from per-strategy freshness. `anySucceeded` (≥1 fulfilled) now gates the key write, independent of the `anyFailed` alert block. A partial failure still returns 500 + sends the Resend alert (failure stays visible) but no longer degrades healthz; a total failure (`anySucceeded === false`) still withholds the key so a genuine outage degrades. This repurposes `/api/healthz` from "both strategies fresh" to "cron alive + ≥1 strategy fresh"; persistent single-strategy failure is surfaced by the alert email, and that strategy's displayed scores still degrade independently via the `lh:scores:*` 25h cache TTL. In-handler retry was rejected (mobile legitimately needs up to 45s; a retry cannot fit the 60s budget without shrinking the per-attempt timeout, which makes mobile fail more).
- Also corrected four stale `// WHY` comments carrying the "Vercel retries on non-2xx" misconception: the `return` comment and the new key-write block in the route, `lib/lighthouse-scores.ts` (cron-path write), `app/api/healthz/route.ts` (`PSI_STALE_MS`), plus the test comment in `__tests__/psi-refresh-route.test.ts`. Tests: `__tests__/psi-refresh.test.ts` updated so partial failure now asserts the key IS written; added a total-failure boundary test asserting it is NOT.
- _Reversible: gate the key write on `!anyFailed` again (restore the both-succeed `else` branch) in `app/api/psi-refresh/route.ts`._
