## 2026-06-13 — Argos parallel builds: combine matrix jobs into one build

- **2026-06-13** · **Argos `ARGOS_PARALLEL_NONCE` added to `e2e-visual-chromium` matrix jobs.** Both `chromium` and `chromium-mobile` matrix jobs now share `ARGOS_PARALLEL_NONCE: ${{ github.run_id }}-${{ github.run_attempt }}` and `ARGOS_PARALLEL_TOTAL: ${{ strategy.job-total }}`. Without this, each job created a separate Argos build; the PR status check picked the last-completed build (mobile, 4 screenshots — hero is `test.skip` on chromium-mobile) and compared it against the reference (typically a desktop build with 5 screenshots), producing spurious "4 added, 5 removed" on every PR regardless of whether visual code changed. With the nonce, Argos waits for both shards before finalizing a single combined build (9 screenshots: 5 desktop + 4 mobile). The first CI run on main after this merge will establish the new 9-screenshot combined baseline; subsequent PR builds compare against that combined reference. _Reversible: remove `ARGOS_PARALLEL_NONCE` and `ARGOS_PARALLEL_TOTAL` from both steps in `e2e-visual-chromium` — each matrix job reverts to creating a separate Argos build._

## 2026-06-13 — PPR Task 2.2 completion: test coverage + ADR

- **2026-06-13** · **C2 perf fix — GuitarSection uses `fallback={null}` (not `<GuitarDesktop />`).** The original Task 2.2 spec called for all four dual-variant sections to use their desktop component as the Suspense fallback. GuitarSection (`components/sections/GuitarSection/GuitarSection.tsx:362`) uses `fallback={null}` instead. Reason: the GuitarDesktop component renders a dense data layout (signal chain, influences grid, ~200+ DOM nodes). When a mobile UA hit the pre-deployed static shell with the desktop fallback, the desktop variant mounted and then React had to unmount it and replace it with GuitarMobile — producing a visible layout shift (CLS spike) on the guitar section for every mobile visitor. The other four sections (ManPage, GitLog, Projects) have sufficiently similar desktop/mobile heights that their swap is within the CLS budget; Guitar's two variants differ enough in height that the swap was not. Decision: keep `fallback={null}` for GuitarSection; the static shell shows the Module header and empty content area until `GuitarContent` streams in per-request. This is acceptable because the dynamic hole fills sub-millisecond (no network I/O — `headers()` is a synchronous headers read). Note: `DawMixerSection` also uses `fallback={null}` but for a different reason (its desktop variant is a `next/dynamic` client component, not a static RSC). The C2 decision applies only to GuitarSection. _Reversible: change `GuitarSection`'s `fallback={null}` to `fallback={<GuitarDesktop />}` — expect a CLS regression on mobile for that section._

- **2026-06-13** · **Task 2.2 complete — PPR dual-variant sections fully implemented.** All four sections listed in the 2026-05-21 PPR spike ADR (ManPage, Guitar, Projects, GitLog) now follow the PPR pattern: each exports an async `*Content()` RSC that calls `getIsMobile()` → `headers()`, making it a per-request dynamic boundary; each section wraps it in `<Suspense fallback={<*Desktop />}>` (except GuitarSection — see C2 entry above). `cacheComponents: true` is set in `next.config.ts`. Test coverage: `__tests__/section-viewport-variants.test.ts` covers all 5 static fallback cases; `__tests__/section-mobile-variants.test.ts` covers all 5 dynamic mobile cases. The ManPage desktop fallback was missing from the viewport-variants test and `ManPageDesktop` lacked a `data-testid` — both fixed in this PR. _Reversible: remove `cacheComponents: true` from `next.config.ts` and the `<Suspense>` boundaries from each section to revert to full SSR per request._

## 2026-06-13 — DX: block direct pushes to main; enforce feature-branch + PR flow

- **2026-06-13** · **Direct-to-main push guard added to `.husky/pre-push`.** Three commits (464d7fc, fa8dee5, 17d70fe) were pushed directly to main in June 2026, bypassing the 5-agent battery, Copilot review, and review stamp. Root cause: the pre-push branch-name guard regex ended with `|^main$`, explicitly allowing direct main pushes. Fix: two-layer guard -- (1) stdin refspec loop blocks any push whose remote ref is `refs/heads/main` (catches `git push origin feat/x:main` and `HEAD:main` bypasses), (2) local-branch check blocks pushes made while on the local main branch (defence-in-depth for tooling that omits stdin). The original single-regex was also split to remove `|^main$`. Emergency escape hatch preserved: `git push --no-verify` remains available for emergency code rollbacks (bash-guard does not block the `--no-verify` flag, only Claude suggesting it). CLAUDE.md updated to remove the stale "Applies to direct-to-main and PR branches alike" clause and to correct the Emergency Rollback slow-path command. _Reversible: remove the stdin refspec loop and the local-branch block from `.husky/pre-push`; restore `|^main$` to the branch-name grep regex._

## 2026-06-13 — D1 Argos CI visual regression: GO decision (CI-only mode)

- **2026-06-13** · **D1 — Argos CI visual regression (GO — CI-only mode adopted).** Implemented in PR #115 (`ci/argos-eval` branch). `@argos-ci/playwright` installed; `argosScreenshot()` calls added to all five sections in `tests/visual/visual.spec.ts` inside `if (process.env.ARGOS_TOKEN)` guards; `ARGOS_TOKEN` added to GitHub Actions secrets; `e2e-visual-chromium` CI job uploads screenshots on every push. **Decisive criterion evaluation:** The original criterion required "a local macOS dev run against an existing Argos build produces zero spurious diffs" — this was the decisive test for the Darwin-vs-Linux cross-platform rendering divergence problem. Outcome: CI-only mode adopted instead. `ARGOS_TOKEN` is intentionally absent from the local dev environment, so Argos runs only in CI (Linux GitHub Actions runners). This sidesteps the cross-platform comparison entirely: Argos compares Linux-CI branch screenshots against a Linux-CI main baseline — same platform, zero spurious environment-driven diffs. **One infrastructure bug found and fixed:** `argosScreenshot()`'s `waitForReadiness` function polls indefinitely for `aria-busy='true'` elements to clear; the mid-stream visual test uses a mock `ReadableStream` that intentionally never closes, keeping `aria-busy='true'` on the streaming shell indefinitely. Fixed by a `page.evaluate` that clears `aria-busy` before the Argos capture (commit `17d70fe`) — the visual state is unaffected since `aria-busy` is an accessibility marker, not a CSS rendering property. **PNG baselines retained:** `tests/visual/visual.spec.ts-snapshots/` PNGs stay for local `snapshotLocator` runs (which use Playwright's native `toHaveScreenshot`, not Argos). Argos handles CI regressions; local PNGs handle dev-loop iteration. Removing the PNGs would break local `pnpm test:e2e` without `ARGOS_TOKEN`. _Reversible: remove `ARGOS_TOKEN` from GitHub secrets + remove `argosScreenshot()` calls from `tests/visual/visual.spec.ts`; `snapshotLocator` continues working with the retained PNGs. Remove `@argos-ci/playwright` with `pnpm remove @argos-ci/playwright`._

## 2026-06-12 — Lighthouse TTFB gate hardening; INP kept off; strip-next-polyfills.mjs re-evaluation

- **2026-06-12** · **Desktop TTFB hardened to error; mobile TTFB tightened to 1000ms warn; INP kept off; polyfill script confirmed still needed.** (1) `interaction-to-next-paint` kept `"off"` in both configs. Lighthouse simulated mode never emits the `interaction-to-next-paint` audit key on a static portfolio — the audit is absent from the LHCI report entirely (not null). LHCI's assertion engine treats a missing audit as `passed: false` with "audit not known"; any non-`"off"` assertion at `error` severity hard-fails CI every run, with zero real INP coverage gained. Real INP enforcement: TBT gate (desktop `["error", {"maxNumericValue": 200}]`, mobile `["error", {"maxNumericValue": 400}]`) is the lab-measurable causal proxy; Vercel Speed Insights provides the field signal. To gate on a real INP number, a scripted Playwright interaction (click/keystroke) would need to run before the Lighthouse audit, which is outside the current CI harness scope. (2) Desktop `server-response-time` upgraded from `["warn", {"maxNumericValue": 800}]` to `["error", {"maxNumericValue": 800}]` — measured CI TTFB: 2-5ms typical, occasional cold-start outlier ~190ms; 800ms leaves ample headroom while hard-failing genuine regressions. Note: the `server-response-time` audit reads observed devtools timing, not lantern-simulated latency — the simulated `requestLatencyMs`/`rttMs` values affect lantern-estimated load metrics (LCP, TTI, FCP) but not this audit's numeric value. (3) Mobile TTFB tightened from `["warn", {"maxNumericValue": 1800}]` to `["warn", {"maxNumericValue": 1000}]`. Observed mobile `server-response-time` on localhost is consistently 2-4ms (same observed-timing audit, same zero lantern inflation). The 1800ms cap was inert against any regression short of catastrophic; 1000ms tightens the regression ceiling to a level that catches real routing or cold-start failures without flapping. Severity kept at warn because this audit runs against a cold local server in CI, not the warm Vercel Edge, so an occasional cold-start spike above 1000ms is possible without indicating a real production regression. (4) `strip-next-polyfills.mjs` evaluated: still necessary. Next.js ships a ~14 KiB core-js polyfill bundle unconditionally (regardless of `browserslist`); Turbopack `resolveAlias` cannot intercept relative `require()` inside `node_modules`; postinstall overwrite is the sole mechanism for webpack production builds. WS1 (2026-06-04) already hardened the script to fail loudly on unexpected shapes. _Reversible: revert server-response-time assertions in `lighthouserc.json`/`lighthouserc.mobile.json`; polyfill script: no change needed._

## 2026-06-12 — fallow PIN bump 2.85.0→2.95.0; section C deny-list extended for GitHub/GitLab-posting --format values

- **2026-06-12** · **fallow PIN bump 2.85.0→2.95.0 + section C deny-list extension.** Bumped the fallow PIN from 2.85.0 to 2.95.0 (see 2026-05-30 adoption ADR for the original harness design and the full security audit). **Recon (2026-06-12):** latest published version is `2.95.0`; no postinstall scripts; `fix` remains the only file-mutating subcommand. New in 2.95.0: `--format review-github`/`pr-comment-github`/`review-gitlab`/`pr-comment-gitlab` GitHub/GitLab-posting surface — added to section C deny list in bash-guard (both `--format[[:space:]=](...)[^[:alnum:]-]` scoped form and `[[:space:]=](...)[^[:alnum:]-]` word-bounded bare-token fallback for alias flag coverage). **Fence posture:** subcommand gate (section D allow-list) is fail-closed — new subcommands blocked until reviewed; flag coverage uses the section C deny-list — new flags are allowed unless explicitly blocked (not fail-closed). Section C is defense-in-depth for known-dangerous flags; the re-audit-on-bump protocol in this ADR log is the backstop for newly introduced flags. _Reversible: revert the PIN and section C changes in `.claude/hooks/bash-guard.sh`, `.claude/settings.json`, and `.claude/skills/fallow-audit/SKILL.md`._

## 2026-06-05 — CI workflow hardening (Group A)

- **2026-06-05** · **LHCI `numberOfRuns: 3→1`; delete `vercel-preview` job; add tsbuildinfo cache, lockfile-diff audit gate, performance concurrency group, CodeQL path exclusions, harness threshold 250→275, mutation score summary.** `numberOfRuns: 3` was averaging 36 serial Lighthouse audits (6 URLs × 3 × 2 configs) at 18-24 min per CI run; current scores are ≥5-10 points above all thresholds, making the averaging redundant noise. Single-run reduces `performance` job to 6-8 min. `vercel-preview` was non-required and duplicated the `performance` job. `tsbuildinfo` cache exploits `"incremental": true` in `tsconfig.json` — self-healing on stale cache. `lockfile-diff` gate skips `pnpm audit` on PRs that don't touch `pnpm-lock.yaml` (saves 10-30s per push); requires `fetch-depth: 0` in test job to prevent fail-open on base SHA resolution. `perf-${{ github.ref }}` concurrency group cancels redundant LHCI runs on rapid pushes. CodeQL exclusions remove `tests/` from scan surface (test files only; `scripts/` was excluded in the original plan but reverted — scripts run with CI permissions and should remain within CodeQL scope). Harness threshold raised to 275 (CLAUDE.md at 232 lines; 18-line headroom was too tight for active development). `mutation-testing-report-schema` has no top-level `mutationScore` — score computed from per-mutant status counts (killed+timeout / total). Risk: `a11y=100` and `SEO=100` exact-equality gates lose averaging buffer; if they start flaking, increase `numberOfRuns` to 2 for affected URLs only. _Reversible: `numberOfRuns` back to 3 in both `lighthouserc.json` / `lighthouserc.mobile.json`; restore vercel-preview job from git history; remove cache step; remove lockfile-diff step; revert threshold; remove mutation summary step._

- **2026-06-06** · **LHCI `numberOfRuns: 1→2` (follow-up correction to the 3→1 decision above).** CI ran twice with `numberOfRuns: 1` and failed both times on the desktop performance assertion: scores of 0.89 (expected ≥0.95) and TBT >200ms (example failing run: 27051024744). The single-run removed median-run selection that dampens CI-runner-load variance; a loaded runner can spike TBT above the 200ms gate even on a correctly-performing page. Root cause: 1 run is too few to avoid single-point-of-failure on a shared GitHub Actions runner. Fix: `numberOfRuns: 2` — LHCI's `computeRepresentativeRuns` selects the representative run from the set (with 2 runs, the one closest to the median metric value), breaking the single-sample failure mode while cutting CI time from 18 min (3 runs) to ~12 min (2 runs). Note: with an even run count of 2, LHCI does not compute a true median but selects one representative from the pair; variance reduction is real but weaker than `numberOfRuns: 3`. The original risk note ("if they start flaking, increase to 2 for affected URLs only") was confirmed on the first attempt. _Reversible: `numberOfRuns` back to 3 (restores true median; requires syncing the count in the `scripts/gates-runtime.ts` header comment and LHCI inline comments), or 1 if future CI runners prove consistently low-load._

## 2026-06-06 — CI/CD Group C: gates:runtime moved from pre-push to pre-PR manual step

- **2026-06-06** · **CI/CD Group C (ci/dx-optimization): gates:runtime moved from pre-push enforcement to pre-PR manual step.** Previously enforced by `.husky/pre-push` (added in WS4): every non-docs push triggered a full `pnpm gates:runtime` run (build + dual LHCI + axe-core + E2E), 12-20 min per push, blocking the terminal while CI ran the same gates in parallel. Removed to recover 10+ hrs/week of foreground blocking time for a solo developer who watches CI regardless. The pre-push hook now runs branch-name guard, review-stamp check, API-edit backstop, and `pnpm verify` only. CI remains the authoritative gate. MTTD for perf regressions increases from 15 min (local) to 45-55 min (CI). `pnpm gates:runtime` remains available and is a documented manual pre-PR step in `pnpm ready-for-pr`. _Reversible: restore the removed lines to `.husky/pre-push` from git history (PR #109)._

## 2026-06-12 — CI/CD Group D: research complete — D2 no-go, D3 go (workers 1→2)

- **2026-06-12** · **D2 — Build cache audit (NO-GO — existing cache sufficient, no additional layer needed).** The `build` CI job already includes a `Cache Next.js build` step (present since repo init; `actions/cache` on `.next/cache`, keyed on `pnpm-lock.yaml` + source file hashes). PR #106 added a separate `Cache TypeScript build info` step for `tsbuildinfo` in the `typecheck` job. Measured `build` job duration across 3 CI runs post-Groups A-C merge with the existing `.next/cache` step active: 43s, 47s, 55s — well under the 3-minute threshold that would justify an additional caching layer. Decision: no further cache steps added beyond the existing `.next/cache` step. If a future change substantially increases build time above 3 min despite the existing cache, revisit (e.g. remote cache or split build). _Reversible: no code to revert — decision was not to add a further cache step._

- **2026-06-12** · **D3 — Playwright workers: 1→2 in CI (GO).** Shared-state audit across all test files (`tests/e2e/`, `tests/a11y/`, `design-system/**/*.e2e.ts`): no localStorage, sessionStorage, cookies, IndexedDB, or DOM-mutation shared state found. Redis credentials absent in CI (Upstash env vars not set), so API routes fail-open with no cross-test key contention. All specs are stateless reads (DOM inspection, HTTP probes) with no inter-test write dependencies. Implemented: `workers: process.env.CI ? 2 : 1` in `playwright.config.ts` — 2 workers in CI (reduces per-leg tail latency ~35%); 1 worker locally (preserves existing serial behaviour; `undefined` would require exactOptionalPropertyTypes relaxation). Wall-clock CI wait is unchanged (5 matrix legs already run in parallel); only per-leg tail latency improves. _Reversible: `workers: 1` in `playwright.config.ts`._

## 2026-06-06 — CI/CD Group D: deferred research — Argos visual CI, build cache, Playwright workers

- **2026-06-06** · **D1 — Argos CI visual regression (DEFERRED — research-first).** Current approach: two-platform Playwright screenshot baselines (`darwin` local, `linux` CI), stored in-repo, regenerated manually before pushes that touch visual code. The hypothesis is that Argos eliminates the per-push regen ceremony by diff-ing against a cloud-stored baseline. Decisive criterion before adopting: a local macOS dev run against an existing Argos build must produce zero spurious diffs on an untouched branch (the current tooling fails this — layout micro-shifts between darwin and linux snapshots are the reason two-platform baselines exist). Prerequisites: create an Argos account, install `@argos-ci/playwright`, integrate into `tests/visual/visual.spec.ts`, and run back-to-back macOS runs against a stable baseline. If zero spurious diffs: migrate to Argos and retire the in-repo PNG baselines. If spurious diffs persist: reject and document threshold. No implementation until the decisive criterion is confirmed. _Reversible: no change to make yet — this is the research gate._

- **2026-06-06** · **D2 — Build cache audit (DEFERRED — unblocked by Group A merge).** Group A (PR #104) adds a `tsbuildinfo` cache step to the `typecheck` job and reduces `numberOfRuns: 3→2` for LHCI (initial cut to 1 was corrected to 2 — see follow-up bullet in Group A above). Before adding a Next.js build cache to the `build` job, measure the baseline: record `build` and `typecheck` job durations in CI after Group A merges and runs 3-5 times. If `build` still exceeds 3 min (above the 2-min CI budget target), add `actions/cache` keyed on `pnpm-lock.yaml` + `next.config.ts` + `tsconfig*.json` for the `.next/` output. If under 3 min, defer indefinitely — cache adds invalidation surface without payoff. Decision gate: 3 CI run measurements post-Group A merge. _Reversible: cache step is additive; revert with one commit._

- **2026-06-06** · **D3 — Playwright worker count go/no-go (DEFERRED — shared-state audit required).** `playwright.config.ts` currently uses `workers: 1` (single-threaded) to avoid inter-test interference on shared Redis state (rate-limit buckets, budget counters). The hypothesis is that functional tests are isolated enough to run `workers: 2+`. Decisive criterion: audit every spec under `tests/e2e/` that exercises `/api/contact`, `/api/ask`, or rate-limit/budget state (`cross-cutting.spec.ts`, `observability-smoke.spec.ts`, `design-system-components.spec.ts`, and `design-system-pages.spec.ts`) for Redis key dependencies; if no two tests share a Redis key that produces a read-after-write within a single run, raise `workers` to 2 and measure CI wall-clock reduction. If shared state is found, isolate it with per-test key namespacing or accept `workers: 1`. No `workers` change until the audit is complete. _Reversible: `workers` change is a one-line config revert._

## 2026-06-06 — CI/CD Group B: post-deploy smoke reliability (PR #102)

- **2026-06-06** · **CI/CD Group B (PR #102, `ci/smoke-reliability`): post-deploy smoke with API probes, Redis/PSI 503 alert, SHA job summary.** Four additions to `.github/workflows/smoke.yml`: (B1) `POST {}` probe on `/api/ask` asserts exactly `400` or `429` — tighter than the prior `< 500` check, which would pass on 404 (routing regression) or 2xx (schema accepted empty body). `503` from the kill-switch intentionally fails the probe (documented operator responsibility). (B2) Same probe and assertion for `/api/contact`. (B3) When healthz returns `503` and `RESEND_API_KEY` is configured, attempts to send a Resend alert to `erikhenriquealvescunha@gmail.com` from `monitor@erikunha.dev` naming both degradation causes (Redis unavailable OR PSI data stale); if the secret is absent, logs a warning instead. The 503 body is also logged for triage. curl uses `--fail-with-body` so 4xx/5xx from Resend are captured and echoed in the CI log rather than silently swallowed. (B4) SHA job summary step (`if: always()`) reads the deployed SHA and `.status` field from `/api/healthz` and writes them to GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY; heading is tri-stated using two signals — `job.status` AND the parsed healthz `.status`: "Production Deploy Verified" (both clean), "Production Deploy Verified (monitoring degraded — healthz 503)" (`job.status=success` but healthz degraded), or "Production Deploy SHA (smoke had failures)" (job not successful — failure or cancelled). `${{ job.status }}` bound via `env:` per GitHub injection-safety convention; `.status` is collapsed to a boolean at parse time so no attacker-controlled text reaches the summary. _Reversible: revert PR #102; all four steps removed. When RESEND_API_KEY is set, the alert will attempt to send on any 503 healthz (including PSI staleness) — inform the operator before a deploy that silences alerts._

## 2026-06-05 — Font-loading: mono `font-display: swap`, and `@theme` tokens single-sourced to next/font vars

- **2026-06-05** · **Two coupled font-loading decisions (PR #98).** (1) **`font-display: optional` → `swap` on the self-hosted JetBrains Mono.** `optional` gives a ~100ms block period and NO swap period, so on a cold cache the woff2 missed that window and the browser committed to the metric-matched fallback for the entire first pageview — the custom font only appeared after a reload had cached it (the reported "wrong font on first load, correct after refresh" bug). `swap` paints the fallback immediately then swaps in the real font on load, so the first visit always lands on JetBrains Mono. The metric-matched next/font fallback keeps the swap reflow inside the CLS budget (measured ≤0.0439 vs 0.05; cold-cache + Slow-3G reproduction confirms the real face loads and swaps). (2) **`@theme` font tokens now reference next/font's `--font-*-src` vars instead of hardcoded family names.** `theme.css` previously set `--font-mono: "JetBrains Mono"` / `--font-display: "Geist Black"`, but neither literal is a loaded `@font-face` (next/font names its faces after the variable — `mono`/`display` — and the display font is actually Inter, not Geist). The custom fonts rendered ONLY because next/font's same-named override won the CSS cascade against those dead literals; a future Next/Tailwind injection-order change could silently flip that to system fonts on every load. Rewired next/font to expose `--font-mono-src`/`--font-display-src`, and the `@theme` tokens reference them (`var(--font-mono-src), monospace`) — one definition per token, deterministically bound to the self-hosted face, cascade-order independent. No render change (compiled CSS + a cross-engine cascade-guard test confirm the same faces resolve). The design-system tokens doc was corrected (`Geist Black` → `Inter 900`). _Reversible: revert PR #98 — `swap`→`optional` restores the first-load bug, and the token rewire reverts to the cascade-order-dependent literals. Not recommended on either count; both are strict improvements with no render regression._

## 2026-06-04 — Doc-drift gate (`scripts/check-doc-drift.mjs`); ARCHITECTURE.md tree is CI-verified against disk

- **2026-06-04** · **WS6 turns ARCHITECTURE.md file-tree rot into a build failure.** The §4 directory tree had drifted to 13 stale paths (the co-location migration renamed `components/client/*.client.tsx` → PascalCase co-located dirs, `content/bio.ts`→`readme.ts` / `zod-schemas.ts`→`schemas.ts`, removed `components/terminal` + `components/ui`, moved `robots.txt`/`llms.txt` into `public/`, dropped `app/contact/action.ts`). The premise came from the same audit that produced the dissolved WS0 false positive, so it was re-verified empirically against current `origin/main` before any work — and unlike WS0 it held (13 genuinely stale paths). **Design choice that makes the gate cheap and unfoolable:** every leaf in the tree is now a FULL repo-relative path (not indentation-nested), bounded by `<!-- doc-drift:start/end -->` markers placed OUTSIDE the fence (invisible in rendered Markdown). The gate strips `#` annotations + fence lines, takes the first token per line, and asserts `existsSync`. Rejected the alternative (an indentation-resolving parser that reconstructs paths from nesting depth) — it is the fragile failure surface this class of bug lives in. **Fail-loud contract:** absent/malformed markers exit 1, never a vacuous pass (the WS0 false-green lesson encoded). Wired into `verify` (ci:local) and the CI `quality-fast` job. STANDARDS.md Ch.9 was NOT touched — the audit's "Ch.9 is false" claim was itself the false positive; Ch.9 is accurate. _Reversible: drop the script + its `verify`/CI line + the doc markers; the tree reverts to prose. Not recommended — a one-directional path-existence gate on the highest-churn doc artifact is the WS6 deliverable, and it is the scoped cousin of the rejected doc meta-framework, aimed only at the tree._

## 2026-06-04 — Boot-time env accessor (`lib/env.ts`); secrets optional, not boot-required

- **2026-06-04** · **WS1 adds `lib/env.ts` as the single typed seam for application-managed env vars, but every external SECRET is `.optional()` — absence never throws at boot.** The naive "fail at boot on missing required key" pattern was rejected after an architect-gate review found it would break this deployment model: `AI_GATEWAY_API_KEY` is resolved by the AI SDK from the Vercel OIDC token and is intentionally unset on deploys (a boot throw blocks every build); `UPSTASH_*` are read as fail-open presence guards (a boot throw makes the ask route fail to import, so the fail-open path can never run); `RESEND_API_KEY` is lazy-thrown at its use site (a shared boot throw would crash the ask route on a missing *email* secret). So `lib/env.ts` fails fast at module load ONLY on a present-but-malformed value (e.g. a non-URL `UPSTASH_REDIS_REST_URL`); empty string coerces to `undefined` (`X=` means unset, e.g. `.env.local` ships `DEPLOY_SALT=`); `ASK_ENABLED`/`DEPLOY_SALT` carry no default (kill-switch stays live, salt auto-generates in prod). Use sites keep their existing lazy throws; the `lib/rate-limit.ts` fail-open posture is untouched. A standing test bans stray managed `process.env` reads in `app/` + `lib/` (scripts are out of scope by choice, not by an import limit — the `tsx` harnesses run outside the Next.js runtime and `scripts/tsconfig.eval.json` aliases `server-only`, so they could import `lib/env.ts` but keep their own direct reads by design). _Reversible: revert the PR; the migration is a source swap (`process.env.X` → `env.X`) plus one new module, no runtime-behavior change beyond the malformed-value fast-fail. Not recommended — the single audited secret-read seam + drift guard is the property WS1 exists to demonstrate._

## 2026-06-03 — Emergency rollback runbook added

- **2026-06-03** — Added rollback runbook to CLAUDE.md (fast: `vercel promote` to prior deployment, 30s; slow: `git revert HEAD && git push`, 5 min). Health check: `curl https://erikunha.dev/api/healthz | jq .sha`. Vercel deploy notifications enabled in Project Settings > Notifications (email on deploy success/failure). No code change required. _Reversible: delete the rollback section from CLAUDE.md._

## 2026-06-03 — /api/healthz design tradeoffs accepted

- **2026-06-03** — **`/api/healthz` exposes the deployed Git SHA and has no rate limit — both accepted.** (1) **SHA exposure:** returns `VERCEL_GIT_COMMIT_SHA` in the response body for rollback verification (`jq .sha`). This is a fingerprinting vector in that an attacker can know the exact deployed commit. Risk accepted: this is a public repo where all SHAs are public on GitHub; the SHA does not enable any attack that the public git history does not already enable. Mitigation if needed: gate `sha` behind an Authorization header. (2) **No rate limit:** the route calls Redis on every request with no Upstash rate limiter, unlike other handlers that use `defineHandler`. Risk accepted: `/api/healthz` is a monitoring endpoint designed for frequent polling; rate limiting it would defeat its purpose. DoS-via-Redis-cost is a real but low-probability risk for a personal portfolio. Mitigation if needed: add a short in-process TTL cache (e.g. 5s) to collapse bursts to one Redis call. _Both decisions are deliberate and documented; revisit if Redis cost spikes or attack surface materially changes._

## 2026-05-31 — Token catalog single-sourced to theme.css; vestigial JSON + `lint:no-ds-vars` gate removed

- **2026-05-31** · **The Tailwind v4 migration left the design-system docs dual-sourced and added an incoherent CI gate; both fixed.** Two problems surfaced. (1) **Gate hygiene:** `lint:no-ds-vars` (a grep banning `var(--ds-*)`) was added "because the migration removed the `--ds-*` namespace." It was useless — it scanned `.tsx/.ts/.css` (where zero `--ds-*` remained → passed vacuously) while the *real* dangling refs lived in `.mdx` inline styles it never scanned, silently dropping the stat-grid borders on `/design-system` + `/components`. A grep gate for a deleted namespace guards no ongoing generative risk; dangling CSS refs are the job of visual regression. Removed the script, both `package.json` refs, and the CI step. (2) **Dual source of truth:** plan task 2 was never completed — `ColorSwatch`/`TypeSpecimen`/`SpacingRuler` still imported `design-system/tokens/*.json` via `resolve-tokens.ts`, so the docs catalog read an orphaned JSON decoupled from the real `@theme` tokens in `theme.css`, with no drift gate (the removed `tokens:check`). Reconciled to a single source: `ColorSwatch` now parses `--color-*` from `theme.css` (new `app/design-system/_lib/theme-tokens.ts`, mirroring `contrast-check.mjs`); `TypeSpecimen`/`SpacingRuler` deleted (type/spacing/motion/z are Tailwind's standard scale now, not a hand-authored token layer); `design-system/tokens/*.json` + `resolve-tokens.ts` + the orphaned `lint-no-magic-values.allowlist.json` deleted; `tokens/page.mdx` + `enforcement/page.mdx` rewritten to the real system; stale `--ds-*` comments swept from `base.css`/`animations.css`/`Icons.tsx`/tests. Verified: typecheck clean, 698 unit tests pass, `pnpm build` SSGs all 17 pages (ColorSwatch reads theme.css at build), Playwright visual check of all 4 DS pages (desktop + mobile) confirms borders render and swatches resolve. _Reversible: `git revert` restores the JSON + components; not recommended — the single `@theme` source is the property the design system is meant to demonstrate, and the dual catalog had no drift gate._

## 2026-05-31 — Mobile LCP root-caused and FIXED: motion bootstrap moved off the hydration critical path

- **2026-05-31** · **Mobile LCP failed the gate at a deterministic ~3519 ms ceiling (5-run median; perf 89); root cause was the `body[data-motion]` bootstrap running as `<Script strategy="beforeInteractive">`, now fixed by inlining it at `<body>` start.** Supersedes the earlier "structural font-display, accepted for now" entry, whose diagnosis was WRONG. Real finding (Lantern trace): LCP element is the hero tagline `<p>`, with `observedLcp` 122 ms but **simulated LCP == TTI** exactly, Render Delay = 86% — the text-LCP paint is pinned to the interactive point. TTI was gated by the JS critical chain, not total bytes (deferring islands / removing fonts / inlining CSS all measured zero effect — confirmed with 5-run sampling after a 3-run false positive). The actual culprit: `init.js` loaded via `next/script` `beforeInteractive` injects into the `self.__next_s` queue, which executes just before `React.hydrateRoot()` (~3162 ms in simulation) — a 62 ms task (×4 CPU = ~248 ms) sitting right at the TTI gate. **Fix:** delete `<Script src="/init.js">`, inline the (minified) bootstrap as a plain `<script>` at the START of `<body>` in `app/layout.tsx`. This runs at parse (~650 ms) — earlier than `beforeInteractive`, before paint, and OFF the `hydrateRoot` critical path. Must be in `<body>` not `<head>`: it writes `document.body.dataset.motion`, and `document.body` is null while `<head>` parses (a `<head>` placement silently no-ops and reintroduces a reduced-motion CRT flash). `public/init.js` deleted (no longer loaded; avoids a dual-source drift hazard). CSP-safe: `script-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'` covers it. **Measured (5-run median, simulated mobile):** LCP 3519→**3385 ms** (5/5 runs pass <3500, spread 4 ms — the old bimodal 3222/3519 variance is gone), perf 89→**92**, FCP 2019→**1355 ms**; CLS 0, TBT <400, a11y/BP/SEO unchanged at 100/100/100. Verified: typecheck clean, 698 unit tests pass, build SSGs all pages, Playwright confirms `body[data-motion]` is set correctly (`full` default, `reduce` for stored/OS pref) before paint. `tests/e2e/cross-cutting.spec.ts` updated to assert the bootstrap's observable effect (`body[data-motion]`) instead of the now-removed `/init.js` script tag. _Reversible: restore `<Script src="/init.js">` + `public/init.js`; not recommended — the inline-at-body-start form is strictly better on both perf and the reduced-motion guarantee._

## 2026-05-31 — `/api/ask` budget reservation has a bounded transient over-count window

- **2026-05-31** — **`/api/ask` budget reservation has a bounded transient over-count window.** `reserveBudget` uses a Redis `INCRBY` followed by a separate `DECRBY` refund on rejection. N concurrent requests crossing the monthly cap can transiently exceed it by up to N reservation amounts before refunds settle. Accepted: the over-count collapses within the same request lifecycle; the Anthropic monthly hard cap ($50, external alert) is the real cost backstop; a Lua CAS increment+check would be the mitigation if per-request precision were required. _Not reversible (accepted behaviour); mitigate by replacing `pipeline().incrby()` + separate refund with a Lua script that atomically increments and returns the post-increment value for cap-checking._

## 2026-05-30 — fallow adopted as an on-demand read-only audit tool (ADAPT, not full install)

- **2026-05-30** · **Added `fallow` (circular-dep / architecture-boundary / unused-export detection) as a pinned, read-only, on-demand tool via a local `fallow-audit` skill + a `bash-guard.sh` fence + a `permissions.deny` entry.** Selected after an exhaustive discovery pass (~2,090 skills enumerated across every major catalog; see `docs/superpowers/discovery-ledger.md`) found it the single net-new ADD: it covers three checks no existing gate performs (circular dependencies, architecture-boundary violations, confident unused-export pruning) that Biome + `check-dep-pinning` + `check-bundle-size` do not. Did NOT vendor the upstream skill via `git subtree` — a `subtree pull` would clobber local edits and silently re-introduce the destructive `fix` instructions (drift). Did NOT install the destructive `fix` subcommand or the paid runtime/cloud surface. The Phase 2 audit scored it 41/60; the only veto-level findings were the file-deleting `fallow fix --yes` and the unpinned `npx` binary, both neutralized here: the hook blocks `fix`/`--fix`/`init`/`hooks`/`setup-hooks`/`migrate`/`watch`/`ci`/`coverage`/`license`/`telemetry` + cloud/upload flags + `FALLOW_*` env, and requires the exact `npx fallow@2.85.0` form. Recon confirmed 2.85.0 ships no `postinstall`/`preinstall`/`install` script (installation executes no code). Fence verified with 11 block / 6 allow test cases. **Residual risk (accepted):** (1) the compiled native binary is unauditable via static review — pinning + `npx` caps but does not eliminate supply-chain exposure (OWASP LLM03); re-audit on any version bump. (2) A `.fallowrc` with config-driven auto-fix cannot be caught by command regex; mitigated by the SKILL.md "treat `.fallowrc` as untrusted" rule and the `fix`-subcommand block. (3) Oxc parsing (no type-check) yields false-positive "unused" findings on RSC/Next magic files; mitigated by the advisory-only, manual-confirm framing in the skill. **Re-audit trigger:** any pin bump, or upstream re-publishing an install-time script. _Reversible, low cost: delete `.claude/skills/fallow-audit/`, the fallow block in `.claude/hooks/bash-guard.sh`, and the `permissions.deny` fallow entries in `.claude/settings.json`. No project dependency is added (invoked via `npx`), so no lockfile change to unwind._

## 2026-05-27 — Mobile boot animation: fixed height accepts minimum-font-size clipping trade-off

- **2026-05-27** · **`.mobile .boot` uses `height: 220px; overflow: hidden` instead of `min-height`.** The 11-line animation (8 MOBILE_LINE_SPECS + cmd + blank + dialog) at `--ds-font-size-sm: 12px` × `line-height: 1.65` = ~218px was exceeding the old `min-height: 165px`, causing ~53px of CLS as lines were appended over ~2s. Fixed height eliminates the shift by reserving the full space before JS runs. Known trade-off: users with a browser minimum font size set above 12px (accessibility accommodation) will see the cmd and dialog lines clipped under `overflow: hidden`, losing the punch line of the boot sequence. Content is decorative (no AT semantics, no aria-live) so WCAG 1.4.4/1.4.10 is not violated and Lighthouse a11y = 100 is unaffected. CLS savings on the median path outweigh this edge-case degradation. Invariant guarded by `HeroBootAnimation.test.tsx` ("MOBILE_LINE_SPECS + cmd + blank + dialog = 11 lines"). _Reversible: revert to `min-height: 220px; max-height: none` to degrade gracefully under font-size overrides at the cost of restoring CLS on slow devices._

## 2026-05-25 — Static OG image replaces broken Edge OG renderer

- **2026-05-25** · **`app/opengraph-image.tsx` deleted; static `public/og.png` (1200x630) committed instead.** Root cause: the Edge function used `new URL('../public/fonts/…', import.meta.url)` to load a woff2 font, which fails in Vercel's Edge runtime with "Unsupported OpenType signature wOF2" — the font was never embedded, producing a blank or broken OG image silently in production. Fix: one-time Playwright screenshot of the Edge-rendered design saved as a committed PNG; `app/layout.tsx` gains explicit `openGraph.images` and `twitter.images` fields pointing to `/og.png`; `metadataBase` (already set) resolves the relative URL to the absolute canonical origin for crawlers. WhatsApp, LinkedIn, Slack, Discord, Twitter/X all prefer a static, stable URL over a dynamically-rendered endpoint. `scripts/generate-og-image.ts` retained for future regeneration (requires re-adding a route; see script comment). _Reversible: restore `app/opengraph-image.tsx` with a fixed font-loading approach (e.g. inline base64 font data or remote URL); remove the committed PNG and revert the metadata `images` fields. Not recommended — the static approach is simpler and eliminates the Edge runtime failure mode entirely._

## 2026-05-24 — Component co-location migration

- **2026-05-24** · **All components in `components/` now follow the co-located folder convention.** Every component lives in `ComponentName/ComponentName.tsx` (or `.client.tsx`) with co-located `.module.css`, `.test.tsx`, `.e2e.ts`, and an `index.ts` barrel re-export. Previously: mixed state — some components fully co-located, some flat files, two `responsive/` components half-migrated (e2e in folder, tsx/css flat). Convention locked in: sub-components and lazy wrappers live inside the parent folder; only the primary named export appears in `index.ts`; no wildcard re-exports except `Icons` (19 public exports); `.client` suffix stays on client component files; `index.ts` files are never client components. Callers import via the folder name (`@/components/AppShell`) — TypeScript resolves to `index.ts` automatically. Lazy wrappers (`ContactFormLazy`, `InteractiveShellLazy`, `FooterLazy`) co-located inside their parent component folders and re-exported from the parent `index.ts`. `ManPageDesktop` and `ManPageMobile` are internal sub-components of `ManPageSection` and intentionally not re-exported. _Reversible: un-merge the folders and revert barrel files; import paths across `app/` and `__tests__/` would need updating back. Not worth reversing — convention is now the baseline._

## 2026-05-23 — Hash-based CSP: formal decision (Phase 3, Task 3.3)

- **2026-05-23** · **Hash-based CSP deferred — `script-src 'unsafe-inline'` retained.** React 19 Float + Next.js Turbopack emits build-varying inline scripts (RSC flight payload, hydration); pre-computed SHA-256 hashes break on every content edit and would require a post-build step to: (a) scan every generated HTML, (b) compute hashes, and (c) patch `proxy.ts` before the server starts — that pipeline does not exist. A nonce-based approach requires Next.js Middleware per request, which adds latency to a Vercel Edge deploy and breaks the static-generation posture (see DECISIONS.md 2026-05-19 "CSP nonce revert"). The `'unsafe-inline'` posture is documented and intentional; other CSP directives (`frame-ancestors`, `object-src`, `base-uri`) are fully locked. CSP violation observability is active via `report-uri /api/csp-report`. Revisit when Next.js ships stable nonce injection without Middleware. _Reversible: CSP is a response header in `proxy.ts`; no application code changes required._

## 2026-05-23 — Polyfill strip script re-evaluation (Phase 3, Task 3.4b)

- **2026-05-23** · **`strip-next-polyfills.mjs` retained after Turbopack 16 re-evaluation.** Verified against existing `.next/static/chunks/` build output: polyfill bundle content (guard patterns for `trimStart/End`, `flatMap`, `fromEntries`, `Array.prototype.at`, `Object.hasOwn`, `URL.canParse`) is present in 3 production chunks (`03~yq9q893hmn.js`, `0jnuq-whscmuk.js`, `0mwprd_.h.911.js`). `node_modules/next/dist/build/polyfills/polyfill-module.js` confirms the unstripped source is intact when `postinstall` has not run. The 2026-05-22 removal of `turbopack.resolveAlias` from `next.config.ts` (see that ADR) confirmed the alias only covered Turbopack dev builds; webpack production builds are unaffected by `resolveAlias` and still bundle the polyfill module. `postinstall` overwrite remains the sole mechanism stripping these ~14 KB of dead code from the production bundle. Script and `postinstall` entry in `package.json` both retained. _Reversible: delete `scripts/strip-next-polyfills.mjs` and remove `postinstall` from `package.json` if a future Next.js version extends `resolveAlias` to webpack builds or drops the polyfill bundle natively._

## 2026-05-23 — INP Lighthouse warn gate (Phase 3, Task 3.4a)

- **2026-05-23** · **`interaction-to-next-paint` changed from `"off"` to `["warn", { "maxNumericValue": 200 }]` in both `lighthouserc.json` and `lighthouserc.mobile.json`.** Rationale: the prior 2026-05-21 ADR turned INP off because LHCI's simulation mode cannot reliably fire the INP audit without scripted interactions (it reports `auditRan: 0`). A warn-level assertion is appropriate here: it surfaces any run where the simulation does produce an INP measurement above the 200ms budget, without blocking CI on the expected `null`/not-run state. The `"off"` posture prevented any INP signal from LHCI even when the simulation happened to measure it; warn captures that signal without flapping on no-interaction runs. Field INP monitoring via Vercel SpeedInsights remains the primary enforcement layer. _Reversible: revert to `"off"` in both configs._ **Superseded (2026-06-12):** This entry is superseded by the 2026-06-12 ADR. The `warn` approach was later reverted to `"off"` after verifying that the INP audit key is entirely absent from LHCI reports on a static site (not null/`auditRan: 0` as assumed here). LHCI treats a missing audit as `passed: false` (audit not known), causing perpetual CI noise warnings for `warn`-level assertions or hard CI failures for `error`-level assertions -- either way with zero real INP coverage. The `"off"` state in both configs is the correct final state. The `auditRan: 0` framing in this entry and the 2026-05-21 entry was inaccurate.

Preview source injection at MDAST stage via `remark-preview-source.mjs` — remark runs before JSX compilation so position offsets into the raw VFile text are available; recma runs after JSX compilation (only CallExpressions exist, no JSX nodes). `lib/mdx/recma-preview-source.mjs` is a deprecated no-op stub kept for reference. ## 2026-05-23 — Design system token pipeline adopted

- **2026-05-23** · **Adopted two-tier token pipeline (Style Dictionary, design-system/tokens/*.json → dist/tokens.css). Replaces app/css/_tokens.css.** Rationale: programmatic token generation from a single source-of-truth JSON structure enables scaling design token coverage across themes, components, and variants without hand-written CSS maintenance. Style Dictionary compiles tokens to CSS variables, ensuring consistency across platform and reducing drift. _Reversible: `git revert` + restore app/css/_tokens.css to source control and remove design-system/ build step from CI._

## 2026-05-22 — CSS Modules migration completed

- **2026-05-22** · **CSS Modules migration (PR #44): 8 global CSS files replaced with 30 colocated `.module.css` files; `lib/inline-css.ts` and its test deleted; `@layer` wrappers removed from `_tokens.css` and `_base.css`; `globals.css` slimmed to 2 imports (tokens + base resets only).** Rationale: CSS class hashing eliminates specificity collisions without any runtime overhead; colocated modules make component styles discoverable and deletable. LHCI `render-blocking-resources` budget raised from `maxLength: 1` to `maxLength: 2` in `lighthouserc.mobile.json` — old `lib/inline-css.ts` embedded all CSS as inline `<style>` (Lighthouse: 0 external blocking); CSS Modules + globals are served as external `<link>` stylesheets (Lighthouse: 2). Dock's `module--mobile` class check replaced with `el.tagName === 'DETAILS'` — CSS Modules hashes class names making classList-based detection unreliable. Visual regression baselines must be regenerated post-merge (class names changed; old baselines are stale). _Reversible: restore global CSS files, re-add `@layer`, restore `lib/inline-css.ts`, revert `lighthouserc.mobile.json`, rerun `pnpm test:e2e --update-snapshots`._

- **2026-05-22** · **`turbopack.resolveAlias` removed from `next.config.ts`.** Vercel CLI 54.3.0 introduced a `modifyConfig` crash (`TypeError: The "path" argument must be of type string. Received undefined`, `ERR_INVALID_ARG_TYPE`) when processing `turbopack.resolveAlias` in production builds. The alias only applied to Turbopack dev builds; `scripts/strip-next-polyfills.mjs` postinstall was always the sole polyfill-stripping mechanism for webpack production builds. `lib/polyfills-noop.ts` (the alias target) deleted as unreferenced. _Reversible: restore `lib/polyfills-noop.ts`, add `import path from 'node:path'` back to `next.config.ts`, and re-add `turbopack: { resolveAlias: { 'next/dist/build/polyfills/polyfill-module': path.resolve('./lib/polyfills-noop.ts') } }` if a future Vercel CLI fixes `modifyConfig`._

## 2026-05-22 — `ai-eval` CI job flipped to required

- **2026-05-22** · **`ai-eval` promoted from non-blocking (`continue-on-error: true`) to a required status check on `main`.** The job runs `scripts/ask-eval.ts` — an LLM-judge eval over the `/api/ask` route scoring correctness (target ≥ 0.90) and jailbreak resistance (target = 1.0). It was shipped non-blocking in PR #34 to collect baseline data before gating merges (LLM-judge scoring is non-deterministic; premature gating risks false reds). After 6 green CI runs on `main` since P1 merged (2026-05-21 → 2026-05-22), the floor is stable. Changes: (a) removed `continue-on-error: true` from `.github/workflows/ci.yml`; (b) added `ai-eval` to `main`'s branch-protection required-status-checks list via `gh api -X PUT repos/erikunha/portfolio/branches/main/protection`. _Reversible: restore `continue-on-error: true` in `ci.yml` and remove `ai-eval` from branch-protection via the same `gh api -X PUT repos/erikunha/portfolio/branches/main/protection` call._

## 2026-05-21 — Supply-chain package age gate

- **2026-05-21** · **Package age gate added (`scripts/check-pkg-age.mjs`). Superseded 2026-11-08 by pnpm `minimumReleaseAge`.** Queries the npm registry for every resolved package in `pnpm-lock.yaml` and warns/fails on any version published within the last 7 days. Rationale: newly published versions are a common supply-chain vector — attackers publish malicious versions and hope CI installs them before they are flagged. The 7-day window gives the community time to vet a release. `.npmrc` also gains `audit-level=high` to block installs with known high/critical CVEs. CI runs the script with `--warn-only` so legitimate `pnpm up --latest` runs do not hard-block the pipeline; developers run it without the flag before committing lockfile changes. _Reversible: delete `scripts/check-pkg-age.mjs`, remove the `check:pkg-age` script from `package.json`, remove the CI step, and remove `audit-level=high` from `.npmrc`._

## 2026-05-21 — Perf gates + polyfill re-eval (Phase 3, Task 3.4)

- **2026-05-21** · **TTFB assertions added to LHCI configs; INP turned off (field metric only).** `server-response-time` added as `warn` to both configs (800ms desktop, 1800ms mobile — warn-only because simulated TTFB in LHCI is highly variable). `interaction-to-next-paint` turned `"off"` in both configs: INP is a field metric that requires real user interactions; without a scripted interaction sequence, Lighthouse's lab simulation never fires the audit (`auditRan: 0`), causing spurious CI failures. INP monitoring is covered by Vercel SpeedInsights (real CrUX data). CLAUDE.md budget of < 200ms INP remains the target, enforced by field data not LHCI. _Reversible: change TTFB to error, add INP with a Puppeteer interaction script._ **Mechanism note (2026-06-12):** The `auditRan: 0` framing above was inaccurate — verified against actual LHCI report artifacts and `@lhci/utils@0.15.1` source: the `interaction-to-next-paint` key is entirely absent from the LHCI report (not present with `null` or `auditRan: 0`). LHCI treats a missing audit as `passed: false` with "audit not known"; the correct rationale for keeping INP `"off"` is that any non-`"off"` assertion at `error` severity hard-fails CI every run, and at `warn` severity produces perpetual noise warnings -- both with zero real INP coverage. See 2026-06-12 ADR for the corrected mechanism.
- **2026-05-21** · **`strip-next-polyfills.mjs` retained.** `next.config.ts` `turbopack.resolveAlias` points `polyfill-module` to `lib/polyfills-noop.ts`, but this alias only applies to Turbopack dev builds. The webpack production build (`pnpm build`) still contains all polyfill guard patterns (Array.prototype.at, Object.hasOwn, flatMap, fromEntries, trimStart, URL.canParse) — verified by grepping `.next/static/chunks/` after a clean build (32 matches, including `if (!Array.prototype.at) Array.prototype.at = function...` assignment guards). The `postinstall` hook that overwrites `node_modules/next/dist/build/polyfills/polyfill-module.js` with a comment stub is the only mechanism that strips these polyfills from the webpack production bundle. Script and `postinstall` entry in `package.json` both retained. _Reversible: delete `scripts/strip-next-polyfills.mjs` and remove the `postinstall` line from `package.json` if a future Next.js version extends `resolveAlias` to webpack builds or drops the polyfill bundle entirely._
- **2026-05-21** · **Observability scope: RUM + SpeedInsights sufficient for current phase.** `@vercel/analytics` (Web Analytics) + `@vercel/speed-insights` (SpeedInsights) are already imported and mounted in `app/layout.tsx` on Vercel deployments (`process.env.VERCEL === '1'` gate). No OpenTelemetry (`@vercel/otel`) or distributed tracing installed. Rationale: RUM metrics (CWV, LCP/INP/CLS via Speed Insights, session observability via Web Analytics) provide sufficient visibility for a portfolio with a static render model and minimal API surface. Full OTel tracing is deferred until the site gains multiple API routes with latency worth tracing (currently only `/api/ask` and `/api/contact` are instrumented; both are lightweight and fast). _Reversible: `pnpm add @vercel/otel` + add `app/instrumentation.ts` when instrumentation RoI justifies the dependency._ **Annotation 2026-11-08:** `@vercel/otel`, `langfuse-vercel` and `instrumentation.ts` have since been added behind `LANGFUSE_ENABLED`, and `@ai-sdk/otel` completes the AI SDK 7 path; see the 2026-11-08 entry on AI SDK 7 spans.
- **2026-05-21** · **Merge-gate harness retained as justified solo-repo pattern.** The bespoke `pnpm ready-to-merge` chain (`scripts/check-pr-comments.ts` + `scripts/check-branch-protection.ts` + pre-push hook) blocks merge until all GitHub review threads are resolved and tests pass. Justification at solo-developer scale: (a) AI agents drive development workflow — the gate is the AI's local check before `gh pr merge`, catching self-resolves and thread oversights pre-merge; (b) Self-resolve detection is especially valuable in AI-assisted dev: agents can accidentally resolve their own review findings, and this surfaces that pattern; (c) The chain bridges the gap between GitHub's native branch-protection enforcement (UI-only, slow feedback) and a pre-merge local gate (fast developer feedback, full suite in ~6s). The pattern is defined as "tool-agnostic" in the 2026-05-19 entry below (reversible via one commit). GitHub branch protection (`required_conversation_resolution`) remains the enforcement layer; this gate is the developer-experience layer. _Reversible: revert `scripts/check-pr-comments.ts` + `scripts/check-branch-protection.ts` + `.husky/pre-push` entries; the native GitHub enforcement stays live._

## 2026-05-21 — Security hardening (Phase 3, Task 3.3)

- **2026-05-21** · Hash-based CSP deferred — Next.js 16 static generation emits RSC flight payloads whose SHA-256 changes on every content edit; a build-time hash-extraction step (build → scan HTML → compute SHA-256 → patch proxy.ts) is required but does not exist yet. `'unsafe-inline'` stays. CSP violation observability added via `report-uri /api/csp-report` (present in all environments); `app/api/csp-report/route.ts` returns 204 to acknowledge receipt and prevent 404 noise. - **2026-05-21** · `getClientIp` proxy trust posture documented — no code change. Vercel's edge sets `x-forwarded-for` and `x-real-ip` authoritatively and strips client-injected values; the header-precedence order and local-dev 'unknown' fallback are documented inline in `lib/rate-limit.ts`. (informational; no behavior change)

## 2026-05-21 — PPR / `cacheComponents` API spike (Phase 2, Task 2.1)

- **2026-05-21** · **Trade-off accepted: UA-gated Suspense subtrees are per-request dynamic (not cacheable) for the five reworked sections.** Prior CSS-toggle approach had zero server cost per request (one static HTML with both variants). New approach: ~5 per-request async RSC resolutions per page load, each reading `headers()`. Acceptable for a portfolio with no horizontal scale requirement. Reversible by returning to CSS toggle + `export const dynamic = 'force-static'`.
- **2026-05-21** · **Spike outcome — PPR API confirmed: `cacheComponents: true` in `next.config.ts`.** Verified against Context7 Next.js 16.2.2 docs. Key finding: Next.js 16 replaces the `experimental_ppr` flag from Next.js 15 with the stable `cacheComponents: true` top-level config option. Dynamic boundaries are created by wrapping any `async` RSC that calls `headers()` / `cookies()` in a standard `<Suspense>` boundary — no special directive needed on the boundary component itself. `'use cache'` + `cacheLife()` are optional cache-lifetime annotations for the static side. Rework shape for Task 2.2: one `async` inner RSC per dual-variant section (ManPage, Guitar, Projects, GitLog) calls `headers()` to read the UA, returns the correct variant; each section wraps it in `<Suspense fallback={<DesktopVariant />}>` (desktop default matches the current CSS fallback, zero CLS). CLS risk is low: the UA is read from request headers with no async I/O, so the streaming delay for the dynamic hole is sub-millisecond. LCP is unaffected — the LCP element (Hero section) is fully static and not inside any Suspense boundary. _Reversible: revert `cacheComponents: true` and the per-section Suspense wrappers to restore the CSS-toggle force-static behaviour; pair with `export const dynamic = 'force-static'` if needed._

## 2026-05-21 — Agent surfaces: MCP `ask_erik` rate-limit scope (Phase 1, Task 1.5)

- **2026-05-21** · **MCP `ask_erik` shares one global rate-limit bucket — known, accepted limitation. Superseded 2026-11-07: the caller address is now forwarded.** `runAsk` in `lib/agent/mcp-tools.ts` builds a synthetic same-origin `Request` to reuse the real `/api/ask` POST handler but sets no `x-forwarded-for` / `x-real-ip` header, so `getClientIp` (`lib/rate-limit.ts`) returns the literal `'unknown'`. Every MCP `ask_erik` call worldwide therefore keys to the same IP and shares ONE 8/hour `rl:ask` bucket and one identical-question dedup bucket. Not mitigated: per-caller MCP limiting is disproportionate for a single-author portfolio, and the monthly Anthropic token budget cap (`reserveBudget`) is the real cost backstop. _Reversible: thread a caller-derived identity header into the synthetic Request if MCP traffic ever warrants per-caller limiting._

## 2026-05-21 — `/api/ask` migrated to the Vercel AI Gateway (Phase 1, Task 1.2)

- **2026-05-21** · **Spike outcome — PATH A: the Vercel AI Gateway exposes the cache-token breakdown, so the full migration is viable.** Task 1.1 (the P1.1 spike) confirmed against Vercel's AI Gateway docs + the AI SDK Anthropic-provider docs that `streamText` routed through the Gateway returns `cacheCreationInputTokens` and `cacheReadInputTokens` under `result.providerMetadata.anthropic`, and `inputTokens` / `outputTokens` under `result.usage`. Usage now arrives via the resolved end-of-stream promises (`result.usage` / `result.providerMetadata`) rather than a `message_start` SSE event. The PATH B fallback (keep the direct `@anthropic-ai/sdk` purely for the usage path) was therefore not needed — the cache-hit metric survives the migration intact. _Residual verification: (1) the doc-level confirmation is authoritative for the cache-token field names, but a live runtime smoke-test against a deployed preview (one warm + one cold request, asserting `cacheCreationInputTokens > 0` on the cold call) is the final check; it needs the deploy-env `AI_GATEWAY_API_KEY` and is listed as a deploy-env action. (2) The zero-refund error path depends on a load-bearing AI SDK assumption: that on a suppressed failed / init-failed stream `result.usage` RESOLVES to zeroed counts rather than REJECTING. The route's `usagePromise` `.catch` already degrades a rejection to `undefined` (treated as zero), and `settleAndPersist` gates the refund on real `usage` alone — so a rejection still settles fail-closed — but the resolves-to-zeros behavior is what makes the error-path refund a clean no-op; the same preview smoke-test should also exercise one forced-error request and confirm the budget settles without a phantom high-water mark._
- **2026-05-21** · **`/api/ask` migrated from the direct `@anthropic-ai/sdk` to the Vercel AI Gateway via the AI SDK v6 (`ai` package) `streamText`.** The Anthropic call and usage extraction are the only things that changed; every other behavior is preserved verbatim — the kill switch, per-IP rate limit, JSON-parse + 500-char clamp, the `INJECTION_RE` prompt-injection guard, the identical-question gate, the budget reserve/settle, the per-request unguessable `<q SENTINEL>` wrapper, the `STREAM_ERR_SENTINEL` error path, the mid-stream watchdog, the persisted `persistAskInteraction` log, and all response headers. The model is addressed by the plain Gateway string `anthropic/claude-haiku-4-5` (Vercel-preferred form — no `@ai-sdk/anthropic` instance wired directly); the ephemeral prompt cache is preserved by sending the system prompt as a `system`-role message carrying `providerOptions: { anthropic: { cacheControl: { type: 'ephemeral' } } }`. Rationale: the Gateway is one billing/observability/rate-limit seam in front of the model, the foundation Phase 1 builds the AI eval suite and metrics panel on. Notable mechanical change: `streamText` is synchronous and surfaces *both* stream-init and mid-stream failures through the `textStream` async iterator, so the route's former pre-stream `try/catch` around `messages.create()` collapses into the single mid-stream consumer catch — same observable contract (HTTP 200 + `STREAM_ERR_SENTINEL` body). Usage settlement (`settleBudget` + `persistAskInteraction`) now runs after the stream closes, awaiting `result.usage` / `result.providerMetadata`, because those resolve at stream end rather than on a `message_start` event. `cacheControl`, the `RESERVED_INPUT_TOKENS` budget sizing, and the cache-hit-rate metric (STANDARDS.md Ch.7) are unchanged. New deploy-env requirement: `AI_GATEWAY_API_KEY` (on Vercel, the OIDC token is used automatically). The CLAUDE.md stack line still naming the direct SDK is now superseded by this entry. `@anthropic-ai/sdk` is retained as a dependency — `lib/ask/system-prompt.ts` still uses its `TextBlockParam` type for the `SYSTEM` export, and it is the documented PATH B fallback. _Reversible: revert this commit — the direct-SDK `messages.create()` call returns; `pnpm remove ai` if desired. The cache-metric is pinned by `__tests__/ask-cache-metric.test.ts`, which is implementation-agnostic and passes on either path._

## 2026-05-21 — Reference Standards & Improvement Program (CG9)

- **2026-05-21** · **`STANDARDS.md` is the canonical engineering bar; supersedes the inline `CLAUDE.md` standards.** The "Reference standards (post-audit 2026-05-19)" list of 10 standards in `CLAUDE.md` is replaced by `STANDARDS.md` — an 11-domain-chapter prose document (Rendering & Architecture; API & Server Boundary; Performance; Testing; Reproducibility & Dependencies; Content & Type Safety; CSS & Visual System; Accessibility; Security & Privacy; Documentation & Decisions; Developer Experience). Each chapter names how the standard is held — a CI gate, a PR-review item, or culture — and a rule with no enforcement is marked as such. `CLAUDE.md` now carries a one-paragraph `## Engineering standards` pointer instead of the inline list. Rationale: a single canonical doc is easier to keep honest than a list embedded in the agent-instruction file, and the prose "how it is held" clause forbids silently-aspirational rules. Produced as deliverable 1 of the Reference Standards & Improvement Program (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-05-20-reference-standards-and-improvement-program-design.md`). _Reversible: revert the CG9 commit to restore the inline list; the standards themselves are unchanged, only their home moves._
- **2026-05-21** · **D1 — `e2e-full` split into required + non-required jobs.** The former non-required `e2e-full` matrix job is replaced by `e2e-functional` (required: chromium + webkit + mobile, runs `contact`/`ask`/`cross-cutting`/`observability-smoke`) and `e2e-visual` (intentionally non-required: runs `visual.spec.ts` only, keeps the `workflow_dispatch` baseline-refresh path). Rationale: functional cross-browser regressions must block merges; pixel-diff flakiness must not. Post-merge action: the repo owner adds `e2e-functional` to the GitHub required-status-checks list (branch protection is configured in the repo UI, not in `ci.yml`). _Reversible: re-merge the two jobs; loses the required/advisory split._
- **2026-05-21** · **D2 — bundle gate measures the honest unit.** The CI bundle gate (`scripts/check-bundle-size.mjs`) gates the gzipped client-chunk total (framework-inclusive) and its header comment honestly says so; the 43KB app-island figure is a documented *design target* tracked via `pnpm bundle:analyze` (`@next/bundle-analyzer`), not a gated number. Rationale: a gate set to a framework-inclusive value while claiming to measure app-only JS is theater — per-route app-only JS is not reliably extractable from the Turbopack manifest, so the gate measures what it can measure truthfully. _Reversible: change the gate args / comment; the design target is unaffected._
- **2026-05-21** · **D3 — `InteractiveShell` streaming renders through React.** The out-of-tree `streamSpan` (`document.createElement` + `appendChild` + per-chunk `textContent` mutation inside a React-owned `aria-live` feed) is replaced by a dedicated `streamingText` state, updated at most once per `requestAnimationFrame`. Rationale: React owns every DOM node in the feed, the `aria-live` region announces predictably, and rAF-coalescing keeps re-renders frame-bounded so INP does not regress. Enforced by `__tests__/InteractiveShell.streaming.test.ts` (asserts every feed child is React-owned). _Reversible: revert the CG4 commit; reintroduces the out-of-tree node and the reconciliation hazard._
- **2026-05-21** · **`/api/erik.json` stays bare JSON — documented envelope exemption.** `erik.json` is a machine-readable resume *document*, not an *operation*; wrapping it in the `lib/server/route.ts` `{ ok, requestId, data }` envelope would break agent crawlers expecting a plain profile. It keeps its bare-JSON body; being `force-static` it emits no `X-Request-Id` — a static route can only mint a build-time constant, which would be observability theater (Ch. 9). A route-file comment records the exemption. `STANDARDS.md` Chapter 2 documents it as the deliberate exception. _Reversible: bring it onto `defineHandler` if a future consumer needs the envelope; would be a breaking change for current crawlers._
- **2026-05-21** · **Domain canonicalized to `erikunha.dev` across current-state docs.** The audit flagged `erikunha.com.br` lingering in `CLAUDE.md`, `public/llms.txt`, and `ARCHITECTURE.md` while the code ships `.dev` (`layout.tsx` `metadataBase`, `sitemap.ts`, `robots.txt`, the SYSTEM prompt). All current-state occurrences are corrected to `erikunha.dev`; historical dated ADR text (e.g. the 2026-05-19 `robots.txt` entry below) and superseded specs/plans keep their original wording — they record history. `HANDOFF.md` carries banners pointing at `STANDARDS.md`/`ARCHITECTURE.md`/`DECISIONS.md` as authoritative. _Reversible in principle but pointless — `.dev` is the deployed domain._

## 2026-05-15 — Audit fixes

- **2026-05-15** · CSP `script-src 'unsafe-inline'` kept instead of nonces. Inline scripts (motion IIFE, scroll-restoration) exist solely to prevent FOUC and cannot be extracted without breaking their purpose. Nonce injection requires middleware and adds latency per request. Portfolio has no user-generated content vectors that would make XSS meaningful. _Revisit if any third-party script injection surface is added. Reversible: add Next.js middleware to inject per-request nonces._

- **2026-05-15** · Monthly LLM budget cap implemented: Redis key `ask:tokens:YYYY-MM`, 400K token hard cap (≈$0.40 at Haiku pricing), fail-open on Redis unavailability. _Critical for cost safety; never disable without an alternative cap._
- **2026-05-15** · Anthropic prompt caching enabled on `ask` system prompt via `cache_control: { type: 'ephemeral' }`. ~93% cheaper on cached input tokens per call. _Trivially reversible._
- **2026-05-15** · Ask rate limit corrected to `slidingWindow(8, '1 h')` — aligns with documented value (was 10/min = 600/hr). _More restrictive; budget cap is now the primary spend control._
- **2026-05-15** · Skip-to-content link added to AppShell as first focusable element (WCAG 2.4.1 Level A). Targets `#main-content` on the `<main>` element. _Non-negotiable for WCAG compliance._
